# Deferral backlog — code-repo index

A single, cross-cutting list of deferrals surfaced while reviewing or implementing this
backend. It is an **index, not a home-of-truth**: each entry is one line plus a pointer
to where the decision actually lives — an ADR's "Deferred"/"Risks" section, a TASK's
"Open follow-ups", or (for genuine substrate/design gaps) the design-repo trackers.

**Why this file exists.** The distributed convention (ADR "Deferred" / TASK "§N Open
follow-ups") gives every deferral a durable, agent-visible home-of-truth — but no
cross-cutting view; you can't ask "show me everything we've deferred." This index
supplies that view without a second source of truth (route-don't-duplicate). It resolves
under backend-only (agent) access, and it outlives the design-repo `ROADMAP.md` (which
discharges and is retired when Phase 1+2 close); it can seed the forward-task surface
(Planning v2) when that lands.

## Conventions

- **Capture here, decide there.** Add a one-line entry the moment something is deferred.
  The substantive record (rationale, options, forcing function) is authored in the *home*
  (ADR/TASK), never restated here. One line + a pointer per item.
- **Home column** names where the home-of-truth is (or will be). `fold pending` = captured
  here but not yet written into its ADR/TASK home.
- **Substrate/design gaps don't belong here.** A gap in the kernel/foundation/conventions
  routes to the design-repo trackers instead.
  <!-- OFFREPO: design-repo `../f5sign-doc/` (git remote name `innasign-doc`) — `FINDINGS.md` / `DESIGN_INCONSISTENCIES.md`; not reachable under agent-only backend access. -->
- **Discharge.** When an item is acted on (folded + done) or consciously closed, move it to
  Closed with the resolving commit/ADR.
- **Rows are ordered by ascending id** within each table. Nothing enforced this before
  2026-08-10 — it was a pattern in the base blob rather than a stated rule, so new rows drifted
  out of sequence and a reader scanning for an id could not stop at the first larger one.
- **Status vocabulary.** `Open` = deferred, home-of-truth not yet written · `Folded` = home-of-truth
  written, still deferred · `Landed` = the work shipped (discharge to Closed next) · `Closed` = moved
  to the Closed table with its resolving commit/ADR.

> ## ⚠ This index has a known gap: 2026-06-16 → 2026-07-28
>
> **Until 2026-07-28, every open entry below had been surfaced on a single day, 2026-06-16.** In the six
> weeks that followed, 15 ADRs were minted (**29 by 2026-08-18** — re-measured; [TASK-020](tasks/TASK-020-derive-the-deferral-index.md) §2 carries the command) and DBAL-only persistence, the event-log substrate, sequential PAdES, the testing
> standard, the Notification BC and E&A Stage-0 all shipped — each deferring things, **none of which was
> captured here.** The distributed half of the convention held (the deferrals are in their ADR/TASK
> homes); the index half stopped.
>
> This is recorded in the file itself, rather than only in a task, because a silently-stale index reads
> exactly like an accurate short one — so a reader who cannot see the gap has no way to know the
> "show me everything deferred" question is currently returning a wrong answer.
>
> The diagnosis, the measurements, and the fix — **generate this index from the ADR/TASK homes instead of
> hand-maintaining it** — are in [TASK-020](tasks/TASK-020-derive-the-deferral-index.md).
>
> ⚑ **The 2026-07-28/29 rows below are that repair, applied by hand — not the capture rule working.**
> They were placed in bulk by the workspace-artifact review, bulk-dated rather than as-surfaced, and they
> are **not** TASK-020's Phase-1 census, which has still not run. So the gap this notice describes is
> *narrower* than it was and **not closed**: the forcing function is still absent, and the next six weeks
> can silently repeat it. **Treat the table below as a partial view.**

## Open

> ⛔ **La fila más importante de esta tabla es [`BL-234`](BACKLOG.md) y está la primera, deliberadamente.** Esta tabla no tiene columna de prioridad y se ordena por id, así que un hallazgo de plataforma quedaría enterrado entre ~190 filas ordenadas por cuándo se encontraron. `BL-234` es el único con radio de **todos los tenants a la vez**: una transacción larga en cualquier handler para la publicación de eventos del clúster entero, en silencio y reportando éxito. ⚠ Si mueves esa fila de sitio, mueve también esta nota o bórrala — una nota que dice «está la primera» sobre una tabla donde ya no lo está es peor que no tenerla.

| ID | Deferral | Home-of-truth | Status | Surfaced |
|----|----------|---------------|--------|----------|
| BL-234 | ⛔ **UNA TRANSACCIÓN LARGA EN CUALQUIER SITIO PARA LA PUBLICACIÓN DE EVENTOS DE TODOS LOS TENANTS, y nada la acota ni avisa.** [`EventRelay`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/EventLog/EventRelay.php) solo publica filas con `sys_transaction_id < pg_snapshot_xmin(pg_current_snapshot())` — correcto y necesario, porque una transacción abierta aún puede deshacerse y podría tener filas que el relay no ve, así que publicar por delante de ella perdería eventos en silencio. ⛔ **El precio es que `pg_snapshot_xmin` lo fija la transacción abierta más vieja del CLÚSTER**, no de la base de datos: mientras una viva, el relay no drena nada para **ningún** tenant — **y reporta éxito**. ⚑ **Esto NO es un hallazgo nuevo: [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) lo acepta explícitamente** (*«a long-running transaction anywhere stalls the watermark and delivery lags; no data loss»*) y nombra tres remedios operativos — **advisory locks de escritor**, un **techo de tiempo** a las transacciones ofensivas, y **monitorización de transacciones largas** — diciendo que se recurra a ellos antes que a CDC/Debezium. ▶ **Lo que falta es exactamente eso: los tres están sin construir.** No hay nada que impida que una transacción crezca, ni nada que avise cuando pasa. El caveat está aceptado en el registro y no tiene realización. ⚠ **Dos sitios medidos donde la transacción crece sin cota, y el segundo es el frecuente:** **(1)** [`RevokeEnvelopeSessionsUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/RevokeEnvelopeSessionsUseCase.php) — el fan-out de una anulación hace `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` **sin `LIMIT`** sobre todas las sesiones del sobre y luego ~6 sentencias por sesión (3 de hidratación + upsert + hijos + append al log), todo en una transacción. **No hay tope de destinatarios por sobre en ningún sitio** — sí lo hay para campos, opciones (`FieldOccurrences`/`FieldChoices`), documentos asignados (`Assert\Count(max: 500)`) y métodos de auth (`AuthMethod::MAX_DECLARED_LIST_LENGTH`); para destinatarios no. Censado 2026-09-01. **(2)** [`UploadDocumentBytesUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Application/UseCase/Authoring/UploadDocumentBytesUseCase.php) — hace `objectStore->put()` (subida de red a MinIO/S3) **dentro** de la transacción del bus. ⛔ Su propio docblock lo reconoce (*«the bus opens its transaction around the whole use case (ADR-0010), so the transaction already spanned the put»*) **pero razonando sobre el row lock, no sobre el relay**: el análisis se hizo y no llegó hasta aquí. Este camino se recorre en cada subida de documento, no en un caso extremo. ⛔ **Por qué esta fila es la primera de la lista y no una más:** el resto de la tabla son defectos con radio dentro de un tenant o de una ruta; **este degrada una garantía de plataforma para todos los clientes a la vez, en silencio, y crece con el uso**. Y es el único sitio donde arreglar el síntoma que una auditoría encontró (trocear la revocación) habría dejado vivos los demás, incluido el más frecuente. ▶ **Orden de ataque, y el ADR ya lo dice:** primero **monitorización + techo de tiempo**, que cubren todos los sitios incluidos los que nadie ha escrito todavía y convierten esto de invisible en alertable; **después** acotar los dos casos medidos, si la medición dice que hace falta. ⚠ Trocear la revocación arregla **un** sitio y es la tentación obvia — [ADR-0059](adr/ADR-0059-terminal-envelope-closes-the-ceremony.md) §2.4 hace que sea seguro (el reactor es el registro, nunca la garantía, así que una revocación parcial converge en el reintento), lo cual la hace fácil de justificar y no la hace suficiente. ⚑ Cambiar fronteras transaccionales pide **ADR** (regla 7 del repo): el patrón de la casa para trabajo acotado es `EventRelay::relayBatch(int $limit = 100)`, cuyo bucle vive **fuera** de la transacción, en un comando de consola. | [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) §«Watermark stall» + auditoría de `feat/terminal-envelope-and-credential-retirement` (M10) | Open | 2026-09-01 |
| BL-239 | ⚑ **An async failure cannot be traced back to the request that caused it, and the mechanism that would fix it needs a column in a hash-chained evidentiary log.** The HTTP edge mints a correlation id per request and publishes it as `X-Request-Id` ([`CorrelationRequestListener`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Http/CorrelationRequestListener.php)); a synchronous failure carries it into Sentry as `request_id`. **The interesting work is not synchronous.** A request appends to `platform.event_log`, [`EventRelay`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/EventLog/EventRelay.php) drains it later in a separate process, and a worker consumes it in a third — which is where sending an email, sealing a PDF or calling DSS actually fails. Those failures are now attributable to a **tenant** and an **event type** (both stamped from the envelope since `13149c1`), but not to a request. ⚑ **The transport half is already built and proven**: [`TenantStamp`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Bus/TenantStamp.php) rides the envelope, [`SelfSerializingMessageSerializer`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Serialization/SelfSerializingMessageSerializer.php) round-trips it as an `X-Tenant-Id` AMQP header, and [`ReestablishTenantMiddleware`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Bus/ReestablishTenantMiddleware.php) lifts it back. A correlation stamp is that pattern copied — **except for where the value comes from**. ⛔ **The relay has no request context by construction.** It drains minutes later, so the id has to be **persisted on the log row** or it is gone, and `platform.event_log` ([`Version20260713000004`](../migrations/Version20260713000004.php)) has no column for one. That is the whole of the cost, and it is not an observability change: the table is append-only and each row carries `sys_commitment`, a self-hash leaf. Three questions have to be answered on the record — whether the column sits **inside** the commitment (changing what verification means for rows after the migration, so a verifier must know a row's era) or **outside** it (a field on an evidentiary log not covered by its integrity guarantee, which needs a stated reason); what `NULL` means on every pre-existing row; and what a **backfilled** value would mean, which authoring rule 6 governs. ⚠ **A cheaper substitute was considered and REJECTED on measurement, so it is not re-proposed from scratch.** `stream_id` already exists on the row and the migration comment calls it *«contextual correlation root (envelope)»* — but that describes the field's intent, not what every event puts in it. Enumerated 2026-09-02: Envelope, Session and SignatureExecution events do return the envelope, while **fifteen events use the kernel [`StreamsUnderSubject`](../src/F5Sign/Kernel/Event/StreamsUnderSubject.php) trait and stream under their own subject** — the five `Notification` events under `notificationId`, the three `IdentityAccess` ones under `clientId`/`tenantId`. Notification is precisely the asynchronous work that fails in production, so grouping on `stream_id` would file every delivery failure under itself. ▶ **What would force this**: a support conversation that needs *«which request produced this failed delivery»* and cannot answer it from tenant + event type. ▶ **What it needs**: an ADR ([ADR-0019](adr/ADR-0019-database-schema-conventions.md) / [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) are the governing pair) settling the three questions above, then the five-edit stamp recipe. Deliberately NOT done as a passenger on the observability work that surfaced it. | [ADR-0009](adr/ADR-0009-observability-at-the-boundary.md) part 4 (*«the async envelope slot is still unbuilt»*), bounded by [ADR-0019](adr/ADR-0019-database-schema-conventions.md) + [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) | Open | 2026-09-02 (scoped while building the rest of ADR-0009 parts 2/3; estimated small, measured otherwise) |
| BL-243 | **`CustodyRepository::save()` is an unconditional full-column upsert, and four services perform an irreversible substrate act before calling it.** Measured 2026-09-07 during the storage review: 13 `save()` call sites across 8 services, of which four act on the substrate first and can therefore lose a concurrent write with nothing to compensate it -- [`CustodyService::write`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/CustodyService.php) (PUT then save), [`CustodyErasureService::delete`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/CustodyErasureService.php) (hide then save, which `Custody::confirmHidden`'s own docblock already names: *"a refusal after the bytes are hidden would leave them hidden under a live custody"*), [`LockRollingService`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/LockRollingService.php) (roll then save, so the stamped instant is lost from the row) and [`CustodyDestructionService`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/CustodyDestructionService.php) (versioned delete then save, leaving a row that claims bytes which are gone). **One window is closed and the general case is not.** [`CustodyObligationService::strengthen`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/CustodyObligationService.php) now reads the bin back before saving and carries it forward (`Custody::carryDeletion`), which closes the one window with a driven reproduction: an erasure landing between the promotion sweep's load and its save used to be overwritten, reverting the bin and moving the bytes into the conserved container. That fix is per-path by construction -- it names `deleted_at` and nothing else, and it generalizes neither to the other columns nor to the other three services. **What the general fix needs, and why it was not done here:** an original-value or version check on `save()`, which means a column on `storage.custody` and a migration, on a changeset the storage manifest declares frozen; plus a decision per call site about what a refusal means once the substrate act is already done, because for at least two of them refusing is worse than losing the update -- it produces the orphan §2.14 calls the strictly worse failure, which the purge cannot reach because it fans out over rows. **Battery written 2026-09-08; the atomicity half is FIXED and the lost-update half is not.** `CustodySaveAtomicityTest` is now GREEN: one `save()` is one transaction, measured by `xmin`, since `TransactionalCustodyRepository` supplies the unit of work (it wrote root and two placements under three consecutive transaction ids before). `CustodyRowLockTest` is GREEN and gained a third test pinning that the finder holds nothing without a caller-owned transaction; `CustodyLockScopeTest` is the concurrent regression test this row said was owed. ⛔ **`CustodyLostUpdateTest` was INVERTED into a characterization test and is green while the defect is PRESENT** -- asserting the right behaviour would be permanently red, and a permanently red test stops being read. Its green is not a guarantee; when it goes red the fix has landed and the assertion must be flipped rather than deleted. That file is the trigger for closing this row. ⛔ **The lock belongs on the ROOT row**, and that is not a preference: [`Repository::save()`](../src/F5Sign/Kernel/Repository/Repository.php) records that a lock narrower than the write set makes two transactions holding different children reach for each other's rows in opposite orders -- a cycle, and the loser's committed-looking work is gone. Custody is three tables and `save()` upserts all of them; Envelope shipped exactly this bug once (ADR-0018 D1), and `SigningSession` escapes it only by being single-table, which its own test calls *"luck of shape, not design"*. ⚠ **Still owed and NOT expressible before the fix:** the lock-scope-versus-write-set regression test, two transactions holding different children, in the shape of the concurrent child-process tests under `tests/F5Sign/Session/Integration/`. It cannot go red today because with no lock at all nothing can be too narrow, and `Repository::save()` states that only a genuinely concurrent test catches that defect -- so it must land in the same change as the finder. **Found again 2026-09-25 (Evidence & Audit step-6 census): `open()` saves through the same upsert.** So [`CustodyService::open()`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/CustodyService.php) with an id that exists overwrites that row with a new custody that holds no bytes, and keeps its placement rows. [ADR-0063](adr/ADR-0063-where-storage-concerns-belong.md) §2.12 says the opposite: "the row's primary key rejects a duplicate, so a collision there is detected rather than silent". The audit trail put its claim before the custody for this reason ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.10) until [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md). It now registers last, and each issuance opens its custody under a fresh id, so it never opens an id that exists. **Not widened by ADR-0076 (2026-10-06).** `CustodyStore::writeAlongside()` acts on the substrate before it saves, as `write()` does, and loses no concurrent update. It reads the custody again under the root lock and refuses one that is not `PENDING`. Its withdrawal enters the same way, because a withdrawal saved from a stale read would revert a committed first write (`Custody::withdraw()`). The four services above are unchanged. | [ADR-0063](adr/ADR-0063-where-storage-concerns-belong.md) §2.13 (why the promotion saves first) and §2.14 (the orphan a post-act refusal creates) | Open | 2026-09-07 (storage review pass 1, from the fix-safety analysis of the promotion-window race) |
| BL-244 | **The custody identity is minted before the compare-and-swap that may reject it, and both catch sites commit the orphan while their own docblocks say they wrote nothing.** [`DocumentSigner`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/Service/DocumentSigner.php) opens the custody before appending the artifact, and the two refusals are raised in memory before any SQL, so the handler's transaction is never aborted: it commits everything written before the throw, including the PENDING custody row. Three sites reach it -- [`SignForRecipientUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/UseCase/SignForRecipientUseCase.php) returns normally from its catch, [`SealEnvelopeUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/UseCase/SealEnvelopeUseCase.php) does the same, and the retry loop re-enters the mint up to `MAX_ATTEMPTS` times, minting a fresh id each pass. ⚑ **A fourth site since [TASK-069](tasks/TASK-069-a-document-is-sealed-once-nobody-can-sign-it.md) (2026-10-23): [`SealDocumentUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/UseCase/SealDocumentUseCase.php), the early seal of one document, catches the same `ArtifactAlreadyAppendedException`.** It narrows the ordinary path: the use case returns on `DocumentView::$sealedBy` (its own seal or another's) *before* `DocumentSigner` opens any custody, so a sequential redelivery and a parked replay commit no `PENDING` row. Only a concurrent duplicate of the same execution, which read the chain before the first delivery committed, reaches the catch and commits one, as the other sites do. The same pre-check is what the other sites lack. At-least-once delivery makes this the ORDINARY path, not an edge. ⛔ **Two docblocks are false today and are part of this row's fix, not separable prose:** `SignForRecipientUseCase` says the act *"returns having written nothing"* and `SealEnvelopeUseCase` says it *"converges to a no-op"*. Both wrote a custody. ⚠ **Both cheap repairs are compromised, which is why this is a row and not a patch.** A compare-and-swap-first assertion does not close it and blurs a command port; an abandon verb either widens the audited `CustodyStore` surface or hands SignatureExecution the reclamation port. It must NOT become a third `CustodyState`. And moving `open()` beside `write()` destroys a recorded reason -- see the docblock at [`CustodyService`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/CustodyService.php). ▶ Reopen ADR-0063 §2.0.1 item 3 (the pending sweep's remedy) with this row: the sweep is the shape that can absorb it. **The shape that closes it exists since [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) (2026-10-06).** `CustodyStore::writeAlongside()` puts the bytes with no transaction open, then commits the custody's first write and the consumer's record in one boundary. When the consumer's record fails, Storage undoes the version and withdraws the custody. So a refused compare-and-swap would leave a withdrawn custody, a known state, and not a `PENDING` one. The audit trail uses it. `DocumentSigner` and `MockSignUseCase` still call `write()` inside the bus transaction, and this row closes when they move (ADR-0076 §2.7). Until then `write()` keeps its own defect: it puts the bytes before its guards and recovers nothing. | [ADR-0063](adr/ADR-0063-where-storage-concerns-belong.md) §2.0.1 item 3, and [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) §2.7 for the move | Open | 2026-09-08 (architecture pass A-10; fix deferred with D-3) |
| BL-245 | **`CustodyRepository::findLockedForUpdate()` has one caller, `writeAlongside()`, and the older sites cannot adopt it until each one chooses where its enclosing transaction opens, and both directions are blocked on a different open row.** The finder landed with the per-`save()` unit of work and is sabotage-verified: it takes `FOR UPDATE` on the ROOT, the plain read does not, and a narrowed lock reddens [`CustodyLockScopeTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Storage/Integration/CustodyLockScopeTest.php). ⛔ **A lock is only as long as the transaction enclosing it**, and with none open it is released before the finder returns -- asserted by `CustodyRowLockTest`, so a call added without a transaction reads as protection and is none. The ten read-modify-write sites all act on the substrate before they save, which is what makes the choice hard. **(A) Hold the lock across the backend round trip.** Simplest, needs no re-read, and matches ADR-0034's accepted DSS shape -- but an open transaction pins `pg_snapshot_xmin` for the whole CLUSTER, so it adds a third site to [`BL-234`](BACKLOG.md), whose three ADR-0031 remedies are all unbuilt, and it stalls event publication for every tenant while it runs. The custodian sweeps are the worst host for it, being batch loops with one substrate call per custody. ⚑ Bounding the S3 timeout caps each stall and does NOT stop it pinning the horizon. **(B) Take the lock after the round trip.** Keeps every transaction short and off the network, which is the house shape ([`PromoteSignedDocumentsCommand`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Infrastructure/Console/PromoteSignedDocumentsCommand.php) states the reason, and `EventRelay::relayBatch` puts its loop outside the transaction) -- but it needs a re-read under the lock and therefore a per-call-site answer to *what does it mean that the state moved*, which is exactly what [`BL-243`](BACKLOG.md) defers and where refusing is worse than losing the update for at least two callers. ▶ **Not a code task: BL-234 records that changing transactional boundaries asks for an ADR (repo rule 7), and this needs its own session rather than a lean.** ⚑ **What IS in force meanwhile:** the per-`save()` unit of work, so a custody save is atomic whoever calls it; only the serialization is unadopted. **First caller 2026-10-06 ([ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) §2.2, §2.4).** `CustodyStore::writeAlongside()` takes direction (B) for a new call site. Its record step and its withdrawal take the lock after the object-store call, inside a unit of work they own. Both refuse a custody that is not `PENDING`, and that refusal is this site's answer to *what does it mean that the state moved*. The ten older sites still read through `find()`, and the rest of this row holds for them. | [ADR-0063](adr/ADR-0063-where-storage-concerns-belong.md) §2.13/§2.14, with [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) §"Watermark stall" on leg (A) | Open | 2026-09-08 (architecture pass A-19; adoption deferred after the BL-234 interaction surfaced) |
| BL-246 | **The conditional transaction owner is Custody-only, and the obvious way to generalize it is the wrong axis.** [`TransactionalCustodyRepository`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Infrastructure/Persistence/Doctrine/TransactionalCustodyRepository.php) opens a unit of work only when none is active, so the bus middleware stays the single owner where it runs and nothing nests. Generalizing it was considered 2026-09-08 and deferred on measurement. ⚠ **Population is one:** only custody saves are reachable outside a bus transaction -- eight console sweeps across Storage and Envelope funnel into the custody services, while the sole direct repository save in any console command (`PromoteSignedDocumentsCommand`) is already inside `transactional()`. ⛔ **A base class typed on [`Repository`](../src/F5Sign/Kernel/Repository/Repository.php) does not work even so:** `findLockedForUpdate` is not on that contract at all (it is declared per-port on Notification, Envelope and Session), so a base could wrap `save()` and never supply the lock scope, which is the half that needs an owner above the repository; `save(AggregateRoot)` is deliberately wide per PHP LSP, so extending forces the storage port to widen and every adapter to carry the `instanceof` guard `DbalEnvelopeRepository` has; and a decorator must implement the whole port regardless, so the base saves the ten-line conditional and not the forwarding. ▶ **If it is generalized, the unit of work is the thing to extract, decoupled from `Repository`** -- it then serves any non-bus entry point and can host the lock scope. ⚑ **Related and INDEPENDENT: the port-extension census.** Of six write ports, four extend `Repository`; Notification and Custody do not. The second axis does not line up with the first: five of six adapters use `DrainsPendingEvents`, and `DbalCustodyRepository` is the only one that does not, because Custody is the only aggregate recording no events -- while Notification honours the clause without declaring the port. So extending `CustodyRepository` publishes a `save()` clause (*drains `pullPendingEvents()`, appends to the event log*) that the one adapter not implementing it would then either adopt vacuously or contradict. Notification is the cheap half if this is ever taken up; Custody is the half that needs the event-clause decision first. | [`Repository`](../src/F5Sign/Kernel/Repository/Repository.php) docblock + [ADR-0018](adr/ADR-0018-dbal-only-persistence.md) D1 | Open | 2026-09-08 (raised while fixing A-01; deferred as out of this branch's scope) |
| BL-247 | **ADR-0019 §9 promises that soft-delete filtering is DB-enforced through a view, and no reader in the repository addresses one.** §9's words are *filtering is DB-enforced, not per-query -- reads target a per-table filtered VIEW*. `storage.custody_live` is the only instance and nothing reads it: [`DbalPendingCustodies`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Infrastructure/Persistence/Doctrine/DbalPendingCustodies.php) records the reason, and it is a good one -- the `(state, opened_at)` index that makes the sweep cheap is on the base table, and restating `deleted_at IS NULL` puts the predicate where the planner can use it and where a reader can see which rows are excluded. So the convention is enforced for nobody: every read states its own predicate, which is the per-query filtering §9 exists to replace, and the view is carried, granted and now guarded for no caller. Either the readers move to the view and the index question is answered (a partial index on the base table serves a view over it, so this may cost nothing), or §9 is amended to say the view is the *available* shape rather than the addressed one. Not urgent and not cosmetic: the 2026-09-08 invoker defect was latent precisely because no reader would have noticed it. | [ADR-0019](adr/ADR-0019-database-schema-conventions.md) §9 | Open | 2026-09-08 (raised fixing the view's `security_invoker`) |

| BL-49 | **`envelope.field.value` PII classification.** The column will carry filled values — tax ids, names, base64 signature images — as plaintext `jsonb`. Consistent with the repo today (ADR-0033 is Proposed and no column is enciphered yet), but `value` is the strongest PII candidate in the schema and the window to classify it is **before** the commit-time write lands: enciphering a populated column is an expand/backfill/contract migration.  ⚑ **2026-09-21 — decided: the first write lands in PLAINTEXT, deliberately** (maintainer, [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §4 D9). The cost this row names — enciphering a populated column is expand/backfill/contract — is accepted knowingly rather than missed. The classification half is answered: the planned `FilledFieldValues` implements `Pii`, so it is redacted from what reaches the error tracker — and from nothing else: `SentryBeforeSend` is the redactor's only caller, so the column, HTTP bodies and Monolog lines stay plaintext. The row stays Open as the record that encipherment was declined rather than forgotten, the way [BL-214](BACKLOG.md) records it for the delegated evidence; the encipherment has no plan yet. ⚑ **2026-09-21, later — built as decided, and the classification is narrower than it sounds** (TASK-044 §4 D9; `6f106d8`, `0bae290`, `50c44ac` on `feat/field-values-at-commit`). The column is written in plaintext now, so this row's *"will carry"* is *carries*, and the *planned* `FilledFieldValues` above exists and implements `Pii`. What that covers is exactly that one type reaching the error tracker. The same values also cross Session as raw arrays on the commit commands (`CommitSignature::$filledFields` and its delegated twin, a `list<array{field_id, value}>` because `SessionUI` may not import the Contract type), and the read side's `FieldView::$value` is a raw scalar. Neither is `Pii`, so neither is redacted. That is why the adapter's one log line carries a count and the envelope id and never a value or a field id: by construction, not by redaction. | BL-50 | ⚠ **Nothing checks that a stored `validation.pattern` is a regular expression we can safely run — and the moment we run it, an author-supplied pattern is a self-inflicted ReDoS.** ⛔ **Amended 2026-08-07 — this row opened with *"storing it is harmless today"* and that is the clause that is false.** There are **two hazards here, on opposite sides of a repo boundary**, and collapsing them is what let the live one read as prospective. **(1) LIVE, and not ours to fix:** `f5sign-signer` `app/composables/useFieldValidation.ts` runs `new RegExp(v.pattern)` then `re.test(value)`, and its `catch` wraps only the *construction* — so a malformed pattern is handled and a syntactically valid catastrophic one (`(a+)+$`) compiles clean and hangs the signer's device. A sender authors it; a recipient pays. Verified first-hand cross-repo 2026-08-07. The remedy is a bounded matcher or an execution timeout **in the signer**, and a backend compile probe is explicitly the wrong shape for it — PCRE-validity is neither necessary nor sufficient for JS-validity. ✅ **ROUTED 2026-08-10 — the signer team has the write-up.** Nothing further is owed here from the backend side; limb (1) now tracks *their* fix, and this row stays Open only because limb (2) below is ours and unbuilt. **(2) PROSPECTIVE, and ours:** the backend never compiles it *yet*; that arrives with the commit-time validator, below. Storing it is harmless; **publishing it to an unguarded executor is not**, and we do publish it. ⚑ **The premise this item was written on has changed**: it used to add that nothing read the pattern at all, because the signing projection still built from `document_assignment_placement`. Since 2026-08-03 that projection publishes `options.validation.pattern` to the signer, so the pattern now reaches a real consumer — only the *backend-side* execution remains hypothetical — which is also why no compile probe was added, since PCRE-validity is neither necessary nor sufficient for JS-validity and a probe would reject patterns that work for the consumer. It stops being harmless at the **commit-time validator**, where we become the executor: a catastrophic-backtracking pattern authored by a sender then runs on our worker against a signer-supplied subject. The gate wanted there is *compiles under PCRE* **plus** a bounded `pcre.backtrack_limit` with an explicit failure branch — not a bare `preg_match`. The 512-character bound (`FieldValidation::PATTERN_MAX_LENGTH`) is an interim ceiling on complexity, **not** a defence: short patterns nest just fine. Must land **with** the commit-time write, not after it.  ⚑ **2026-09-21 — limb (2) is planned in [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §3 #4**, which lands it *with* the commit-time write as this row requires. The server will skip a pattern exactly where the signer does — a static rule (pattern over 200 characters, value over 4096, or its `NESTED_QUANTIFIER` heuristic) plus a compile failure — and additionally on any engine error under a bounded `pcre.backtrack_limit`, because a malformed value is recoverable and a blocked signature is not. ⚑ **Limb (1) is narrowed, not closed**: read in the signer's tree on 2026-09-21, `useFieldValidation.ts` now ships `isPatternSafe`, a static heuristic that refuses the common catastrophic shapes — but TASK-044's second audit found it misses `((a+))+`, which still hangs the device. Status moves to Folded because limb (2)'s home now exists; limb (2) closes when TASK-044 lands, and the row stays open for limb (1), which remains the signer's. ⛑ **2026-09-21, later — limb (2) is BUILT, and the row narrows to limb (1).** [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) landed the commit-time validator with the commit-time write, as this row required (`6f106d8`, on `feat/field-values-at-commit`). [`SignerPatternRule`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Service/SignerPatternRule.php) runs a `TEXT` field's pattern only where the signer runs it. It skips the rule and lets the value pass on a pattern over 200 UTF-16 units, a value over 4096, the signer's `NESTED_QUANTIFIER` heuristic, a compile failure, or any engine error. And it runs under `pcre.backtrack_limit` lowered to 100 000 and restored in `finally`, so a catastrophic pattern costs the server a bounded amount of backtracking and is skipped rather than run to exhaustion: `((a+))+`, which evades the signer's heuristic, is caught by the lowered limit (`SignerPatternRuleTest`). The paragraph above — *"planned"*, *"will skip"*, *"closes when TASK-044 lands"* — is the record of the plan. **Limb (1) is unchanged and is now the whole row**: the signer's `isPatternSafe` still misses `((a+))+`, which still hangs the device. The 2026-08-10 write-up predates that miss, so routing it to the signer is part of what limb (1) still owes. Status returns to **Open**: limb (2)'s home was why it read Folded, and limb (1)'s remedy has no home in this repository. | [ADR-0040](adr/ADR-0040-field-entity-unified-placement.md) Decision 6 + [`FieldValidation`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/FieldValidation.php) docblock + [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §3 #4 + [`SignerPatternRule`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Service/SignerPatternRule.php) docblock (limb 2, built; the `((a+))+` miss) | Open | 2026-08-03 (options slice) |
| BL-53 | ⚠ **`save()` re-upserts every child row of the aggregate, so authoring cost is quadratic in field count and the row lock is held for all of it.** Measured (PG 16.14, real kernel, `log_statement=all`): one `POST /envelopes/{id}/fields` issues **N+25** statements, slope **0.58 ms per pre-existing field**; the `BEGIN`→`COMMIT` window — during which `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the envelope serialises every concurrent author — is 10 ms at N=1, 44 ms at N=60, **154 ms at N=200**. Authoring 250 fields one POST at a time cost **19.5 s** of server time. Below ~50 fields this is invisible; a 30-page form at 5–10 fields per page *is* 150–300 fields, and the visual editor (BL-48) will POST them one at a time — so this slice is what puts the curve on the critical path. ⚑ **Same fix neutralises a second amplification:** re-binding an *unchanged* `choices` value as a parameter defeats Postgres's unchanged-TOAST-pointer optimisation, so a maxed 132 KB list costs **145 407 bytes of WAL per save vs 133 for an untouched column — 1 093×** — and the old TOAST chunks become dead. (Realistic option lists compress to ~1.7 KB and stay inline, so this needs a caller sending 255-character options; the abuse shape, not the workload.) The cheap fix is not a change-tracker: the aggregate is add+mutate only, so a dirty-set of touched `FieldId`s lets `save()` skip untouched rows.  ⚑ **2026-08-04 — measured worse, and recorded rather than fixed.** [ADR-0041](adr/ADR-0041-field-definition-and-occurrences.md) moves geometry to a child table, so `save()` goes from `N+25` statements to `N+P+25` where `P` is the total occurrence count. On today's one-rectangle-per-field corpus that is roughly **2× the slope and the lock window** (~154 ms → ~280 ms at N=200); on the corpus the feature enables it *improves*, because five rectangles are one field on the quadratic curve. The dirty-set fix now has to track a per-field **collection** rather than a scalar flag — skipping an untouched field must skip its occurrence rows with it.  ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-10 (review-2 worklist).** ⚠ **The figures do not compose.** One POST is one transaction, so 250 sequential POSTs are 250 disjoint BEGIN→COMMIT windows and the total must be **≥ their sum**. Under this row's own slope anchored at (N=1, 10 ms) that sum is 20.4–20.6 s, against a reported **19.5 s total that is supposed to contain it**. Inverting, slope 0.58 with a 19.5 s total forces a 5.79 ms intercept, not 10. Separately `10 + 0.58×199 = 125 ms` against a reported 154 ms — 0.58 is exactly the N=1→60 secant, so it under-predicts the third point by ~19%. **The mechanism and the direction are right; the numbers should be re-measured before anyone sizes work off them.**| [ADR-0018](adr/ADR-0018-dbal-only-persistence.md) (the full-upsert shape) + [ADR-0040](adr/ADR-0040-field-entity-unified-placement.md) | Open | 2026-08-03 (options slice performance review) |
| BL-55 | **No request-body ceiling at the API edge.** `#[MapRequestPayload]` materializes the whole body into a PHP array *before* any constraint runs, so the wire constraints — including the `Sequentially` short-circuit that correctly makes 65 choices cost one comparison — cannot bound the work that precedes them. Measured body→peak-memory ratio ≈13×, against `post_max_size=50M` and `memory_limit=512M`: a ~38 MB body exhausts the limit and the worker dies with a **fatal 500**, not a 422. Pre-existing and endpoint-agnostic, but `POST /fields` is the first route that *invites* a large array, so this is where the exposure becomes reachable. A per-route body cap at the edge (or in Caddy) is cheap.   ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-10 (review-2 worklist).** **The symptom is drawn one request wide, and the deployed topology is sized for exactly one.** The row closes at *"the worker dies with a fatal 500, not a 422"* — self-limiting and catchable. Prod runs php-fpm under `memory: 1g` with opcache reserving 192M and `pm.max_children=5`, and `f5sign-infra/docker-compose.prod.yml:225-232` records the reason in its own words: a heavy request near PHP's limit *"se lleva el contenedor por OOM — matando las 5 peticiones en vuelo"*. **Two concurrent large bodies exceed the cgroup before either reaches PHP's 512M** — the container-OOM outcome that sizing was chosen to avoid — and nothing throttles the second (`symfony/rate-limiter` is not installed).| [ADR-0021](adr/ADR-0021-http-request-edge.md) | Open | 2026-08-03 (options slice performance review) |
| BL-56 | **`public.current_tenant_id()` is evaluated per row and makes every plan parallel-unsafe.** It is a plpgsql `STABLE` function with `proparallel = 'u'`, so the RLS policy predicate on every tenant-scoped table pays ≈**1.1 µs per row** of call overhead and Postgres cannot choose a parallel plan. Measured on `envelope.field`: a 200 001-row scan takes **251 ms serial** with the function vs **26 ms with a parallel plan** against a literal uuid; 20 820 rows → 27–30 ms vs 5–7.5 ms. ⚑ **Not a finding against any one slice** — it is schema-wide and predates all of them — and at per-envelope scale (20–250 rows) it costs 20–275 µs, i.e. nothing. It becomes real for any broad scan of a tenant-scoped table. One-line fix when convenient: rewrite as a plain SQL function marked `STABLE PARALLEL SAFE` so the planner can inline it. The policy *predicate* itself is fine — `uuid_eq` is leakproof, so the index on `envelope_id` is still applied first (verified in every plan).   ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-10 (review-2 worklist).** ⚠ **The ≈1.1 µs/row constant is derived from a comparison that changes two variables, then applied where the second cannot contribute.** `(251−26)/200001` bundles per-call plpgsql overhead **with** a serial→parallel plan change, and no serial-with-literal control is reported. It is then applied at per-envelope scale (20–250 rows), where a parallel plan is never available — the relation sits far below `min_parallel_table_scan_size` and no override exists in `f5sign-infra` — so only the un-isolated component can apply. The direction is conservative, so nothing downstream is corrupted. ⛔ **The prescribed one-line fix is unavailable as stated**; re-derive it before scheduling the work.| [ADR-0017](adr/ADR-0017-tenant-isolation-rls-persistence-contract.md) | Open | 2026-08-03 (options slice performance review) |
| BL-59 | **The signer wire contract changed, and the frontend has not been told.** `GET /api/v1/signing/session` moved `fields[].page` + `position: {x, y, …}` to `placement: {page, origin_x, origin_y, …}`, opened `type` from a closed `enum: [SIGNATURE]` to all eleven `FieldType` members, and added `label` / `readonly` / `options`. Taken deliberately so this repo stops shipping two names for one rectangle (see ADR-0040's Context note, now resolved), but it is **breaking for any deployed signer client**, and the coordination is cross-repo so it cannot land here. Owed: notify the signer team, agree a cutover, and confirm nothing else consumes the old shape. ⚑ The old and new shapes cannot be served side by side without reintroducing exactly the divergence this closed.  ⚑ **2026-08-04 — a second break on the same surface, and the reason to bundle rather than hurry.** [ADR-0041](adr/ADR-0041-field-definition-and-occurrences.md) replaces `placement: {…}` with `occurrences: [{document_id, page, …}]` and drops the field's top-level `document_id`, on both the signer and sender surfaces. Deliberate — serving two spellings is the divergence this repo just paid to close — but it means **two un-communicated breaking changes are now outstanding on one contract**. The owed action is one cutover notice covering both, and the notice must also state the semantic the shape carries: *one entry is one answer* — a client that flattens the occurrence list back into N inputs re-creates the five-independent-values defect at its own layer. ✅ **Premise corrected, 2026-08-04 — the first break WAS absorbed.** This item claimed the frontend had not been told; reading `f5sign-signer` (`develop`) shows that is false: `app/composables/api/types.ts` already publishes `placement: {page, origin_x, origin_y, …}`, the opened `FieldType`, and the note about a mark's synthesized id. So **one** break is outstanding, not two: `placement` → `occurrences[]` and the field losing its `document_id` ([ADR-0041](adr/ADR-0041-field-definition-and-occurrences.md)). ⚑ And that one does bite: `field.document_id` is read in six places in the signer — the navigation order (`useFieldNavigation.ts`), the per-page filters (`view.vue`, `ReviewerView.vue`), progress (`useWorkspaceProgress.ts`) and `FieldListSheet` — and two of those are decisions rather than renames: how many navigation stops a field with N rectangles has, and what progress counts when a field spans two documents. Handoff written at `f5sign-signer/docs/HANDOFF-fields-occurrences.md`. ✅ **2026-08-05 — the navigation decision is answered, and the handoff is now stale in three places.** [ADR-0043](adr/ADR-0043-reading-order-navigation.md) makes the backend emit reading order, so the handoff's first “real decision” (pick a representative rectangle and a sort) is no longer the client's to make — and the signer had **already implemented the identical rule**, so nothing breaks and its comparator merely becomes redundant. What is *new* and uncommunicated: signature marks and data fields now **interleave** by position instead of every mark preceding every box; the array order **is** the navigation order on both wires (no `ordinal` on either, and the sender's key is gone); and the id tiebreak makes that order stable across reads. Separately, [ADR-0042](adr/ADR-0042-server-minted-authoring-identity.md) removes `field_id` from `POST /fields` — no consumer sends it, so it costs no cutover, but it constrains the dashboard's future editor (it cannot pre-mint ids, it cannot offer “reorder fields” since moving the box *is* reordering, and it should snap to a grid because the reading order carries no same-line tolerance). ⛑ The dashboard has no handoff document at all, which is why these land nowhere by default.  ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-10 (review-2 worklist).** **Two corrections.** (1) The cutover enumeration is incomplete: `SigningField.id` now has **two shapes** — marks synthesize `{documentId}-sig-{i}` while data fields emit a bare UUID — and the branch's own OpenAPI text says so, yet this row's list stops at `placement`/`type`/`label`/`readonly`/`options`. A client parsing `id` breaks, and the row's *"confirm nothing else consumes the old shape"* checklist would not surface it. (2) The ⚑ *"cannot be served side by side"* clause is stated as an impossibility where the repo records a **cost-based choice**: `EnvelopeJsonPresenter`'s own comment justifies a hard key replacement because keeping both *"would have put the signing rows and their placements on the wire twice"*. **"Chose not to" leaves a transition strategy open that "cannot" forecloses.**  ⚑ **The version question, recorded 2026-08-10 (review-2) — and it belongs to this cutover, not to the branch that caused it.** Neither break moved `GET /api/v1/signing/session` off `/v1`, and neither the per-BC bump [ADR-0021](adr/ADR-0021-http-request-edge.md) calls *"the intended degree of freedom"* nor the per-route override was used **or declined on the record**. The silence was the defect; this paragraph is the record. What it turns on: a bump *without* side-by-side buys no compatibility at all, only **loudness** — a stale client gets a 404 instead of a 200 whose body it cannot read — while a bump *with* side-by-side is precisely the double-wire cost `EnvelopeJsonPresenter` already declined. ⚑ And the argument nothing had stated: the signer's `develop` **already absorbed break #1 while staying on `/v1`**, so moving the URL now penalises the one consumer that kept up, in exchange for loudness aimed at a build that may not exist. ⛔ **So it turns on a fact this repo does not hold — whether a deployed signer build is live against this backend**, the same unestablished fact as BL-38. Live ⇒ loudness is worth the churn. Not live ⇒ the bump is pure cost. Decide it with the notice, not before it. ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-10: no bump.** The only deployment is a pre-alpha test, so there is no cohort a 404 would protect and the churn buys nothing — while moving the URL would penalise the signer's `develop`, which already absorbed break #1 while staying on `/v1`. **This is the whole of the version decision for both outstanding breaks**; the cutover is a notice, not a version move. ⚠ It is answered on a fact that expires: the reasoning holds *while* deployment is pre-alpha. The next breaking change to this surface re-opens the question against whatever is live then, and ADR-0021's per-BC bump is still the mechanism. | [ADR-0040](adr/ADR-0040-field-entity-unified-placement.md) Context (source-of-truth note) <!-- OFFREPO: the signer application lives in the workspace sibling `f5sign-signer/`; not reachable under agent-only backend access. --> | Open | 2026-08-03 (signer field projection) |
| BL-62 | **Read-path amplification on the token-gated prefix, with no rate limit anywhere.** `findContextForSE()` loads *every* field of the envelope and filters in PHP, so both `GET /signing/session` **and** `GET /signing/session/documents/{id}` — a request that needs one storage key — now drag every field's option list out of Postgres and construct every `FieldChoice` (two `preg_match` + `trim` + `mb_strlen` each). Measured: 500 maxed fields cost **78.9 ms CPU, 14 MB resident, 32 000 VO constructions**; at BL-53's realistic 200-field form, 29.7 ms and ~6.8 MB of jsonb per request. There is no cap on fields per envelope, `symfony/rate-limiter` is not installed, and neither Caddyfile rate-limits. A legitimate signer looping one endpoint costs one GET and buys megabytes of DB→PHP transfer. ⚑ Not BL-53 (that is the *write* path, and its dirty-set fix does not touch reads). The fix is pushing the recipient predicate into SQL — which `GetEnvelopeContextForRecipientHandler`'s own docblock already names as "a later optimization with a real cost to pay for it".  ⚑ **2026-08-04 — the exposure is now `fields × occurrences` plus one query.** [ADR-0041](adr/ADR-0041-field-definition-and-occurrences.md) adds one hoisted query per read (constant, not per field) and makes each field carry a list. The PHP-side filter also does more work now: `fieldsFor()` no longer merely *drops* an inaccessible field, it **rebuilds** each surviving one narrowed to the occurrences this recipient may see — so pushing the predicate into SQL, which this item already names as the fix, has to push the narrowing with it.  ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-10 (review-2 worklist).** ⚠ **The headline workload overstates the symptom by roughly two orders of magnitude.** *"At BL-53's realistic 200-field form, 29.7 ms and ~6.8 MB"* implies ~34 KB per field — precisely a maxed 64-option list of 255-char pairs, **the shape BL-53 itself calls "the abuse shape, not the workload"** and prices at ~1.7 KB. It also treats all 200 fields as selection fields, where `TEXT`/`DATE` carry no choice list at all. **The mechanism is verified and real** (no recipient predicate in `loadFieldViews()`, `fieldsFor()` computed even on the download path, no rate limiter); only the magnitude needs re-deriving.| [ADR-0008](adr/ADR-0008-read-side-realization.md) + [ADR-0021](adr/ADR-0021-http-request-edge.md) | Open | 2026-08-03 (signer projection security review) |
| BL-63 | **`GET /api/doc.json` is publicly reachable and unauthenticated in every environment, production included.** No `when@` gate in the route file or the config, no firewall entry, no Caddy block — while the sibling `config/routes/framework.yaml` *does* gate its routes with `when@dev`. Schema disclosure only (no data), and pre-existing, but it publishes the full API surface including the field vocabulary and every validation bound. Surfaced because this slice added ~110 lines to that document. | [ADR-0022](adr/ADR-0022-http-controller-conventions.md) | Open | 2026-08-03 (signer projection security review) |
| BL-65 | **The full-upsert still rewrites `envelope_id` from a supplied key — the mechanism behind ADR-0042's defect survives its fix.** `DbalEnvelopeRepository::upsertField()` issues `ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE`, and [`UpsertStatement`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Persistence/UpsertStatement.php) sets *every* non-conflict column from `EXCLUDED` — parent FK included. So any caller that supplies a `FieldId` naming a row of another envelope moves that row, and RLS does not object (it scopes by tenant, not by aggregate). [ADR-0042](adr/ADR-0042-server-minted-authoring-identity.md) closed the **reachable** path by removing `field_id` from the wire; it deliberately did not guard the sink, because a guard no test can reach is worse than none and a `WHERE parent = EXCLUDED.parent` variant fails *silently*. ⚠ **Unreachable today and re-openable by one feature**: an importer, a template instantiation, or BL-41's resolver if it is ever handed a caller-chosen id. Two things are owed. (1) **The audit nobody has done** — every DBAL adapter here is a full upsert on an app-minted key; enumerate the write paths and confirm none of the others takes an id from outside the server. (2) **A rule or a shape** that expresses *an app-minted key does not originate at the edge*, since nothing statically stands between the next feature and this defect. | [ADR-0042](adr/ADR-0042-server-minted-authoring-identity.md) Decision 4 + [ADR-0018](adr/ADR-0018-dbal-only-persistence.md) | Open | 2026-08-05 (field_id retirement) |
| BL-67 | ⚠ **The fields HTTP write edge is invisible to Infection, so its mutants are never attempted.** [`AddFieldHttpTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Envelope/Acceptance/AddFieldHttpTest.php) is `#[CoversNothing]` (`:48`), which is *correct* for an acceptance test under [ADR-0035](adr/ADR-0035-testing-standard.md) — but Infection **selects the tests it runs from the coverage report**, so a case that would kill a mutant is never chosen and the mutant is filed Not-Covered rather than Escaped. The suite exercises this edge thoroughly and the mutation score cannot see any of it. ⚑ **Same mechanism, and this is its third instance:** `DocumentAssignment` carried 34 not-covered mutants while being constructed 302× per suite run, closed only by moving `#[UsesClass]` to `#[CoversClass]` on a hermetic sibling. The remedy has the same shape here — a `#[CoversClass]` test at the hermetic tier — which is exactly what `AddFieldControllerTest` adds for `AddFieldController`, and is why that test is worth more than "a missing test". ⛔ **Do not fix it by putting `#[CoversClass]` on the acceptance test**: that would attribute a controller's lines to a tier ADR-0035 deliberately keeps unattributed, and would inflate covered-MSI without adding a single assertion. Gate config is `infection.json5.dist`. | [ADR-0035](adr/ADR-0035-testing-standard.md) + `infection.json5.dist` | Open | 2026-08-10 (review-2 fixes) |
| BL-68 | ⚠ **Three sibling routes disagree on what a non-finite coordinate is, and the declarative constraint that would settle it is available but unadopted.** (a) `POST /fields` refuses `INF` imperatively in [`AddFieldController`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/AddFieldController.php) and returns **400**; the two placement routes refuse the same input through `FieldPlacement`'s own guard and now return **422**. Same payload, same semantic error, two statuses. (b) The reason the first one is hand-rolled was recorded as *"there is no declarative `finite` constraint"* — false: `symfony/validator` v7.4.14 ships `finite-float` and `finite-number` (`Constraints/TypeValidator.php:33-34`). ⛔ **Adopting it is NOT a tidy-up.** `finite-number` is `is_int \|\| is_float && is_finite`, so it rejects the numeric **strings** (`"100"`) that `Type('numeric')` plus the controller's `is_numeric` admit today — a tightening of the published contract — and it moves the refusal from 400 to 422. Both are client-visible, so this rides the `dashboard-sf` cascade (backend first) rather than landing on its own. ⚑ Worth doing together: converging the three routes on 422 *and* the type is one decision, and doing either half alone leaves the inconsistency in a different place. The behaviour is not at risk meanwhile — the guard is held at three tiers (`FieldPlacementTest`, `AddFieldHttpTest`, and both placement-controller tests). | [ADR-0029](adr/ADR-0029-domain-exception-http-mapping.md) + `FieldPlacement` | Open | 2026-08-10 (review-2 fixes) |
| BL-69 | ⚠ **Six write-edge authoring methods answer a replayed request six different ways, and the aggregate rests on an unstated invariant — *a client cannot name an existing id*.** Re-measured against this tree 2026-08-10: `Recipient::assign()` returns the existing pair on an identical replay and **throws** naming which half diverged — the one fully-worked answer, and the template · `Document::appendArtifact()` **throws** on any repeat (`ArtifactAlreadyAppendedException`) · `Envelope::addStep()` returns the existing step and **silently ignores a divergent `minSignatures`** · `Envelope::addField()` returns the existing field, **raises on divergent geometry** and swallows type, `required`, label, recipient and options · **`Envelope::addDocument()` and `Envelope::addRecipient()` do not check for an existing id at all and OVERWRITE** — a replayed `RecipientId` resets that recipient's outcome, email and role. ⚑ **Enumeration completed 2026-09-01, and the two facts it was missing have the widest blast radius of any on the list.** It stopped at *"outcome, email and role"*, written before [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md) put `declared_claimable` and `claimed_at` through the same overwrite — and this row **was** re-read on 2026-08-28, to add the seventh method below, without this sentence being re-read with it. `Recipient::create()` writes `claimedAt: null` **by construction** (*"a slot is born open"* — its own comment), so a replay does not merely reset a value: it returns an **already-claimed** slot to unclaimed, and, where the replayed body still says `claimable: true`, re-opens it to be claimed by a **different** party. That is exactly the second occupant [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.4 makes the claim once-only to prevent, and they would inherit the first one's deterministic [`SigningSessionId`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Contract/Type/SigningSessionId.php), its access trace and its **passed gate**. Where the body says `claimable: false`, the slot instead becomes an ordinary recipient carrying no contact, which no route can then name. ⚠ **Latent on the same terms as the rest of this bullet and no louder** — `AddRecipientController` mints the id with `RecipientId::generate()` — but the two writes genuinely do overlap: `addRecipient()` opens with `guardDraft()` and `Envelope::claimRecipient()` demands **no** `sentAt`, only a party who could still act, so a claimed slot on a `DRAFT` envelope is a reachable state. ⚑ Those last two are unreachable *today* only because their controllers always mint; they are the same latent defect, not safe by design. ⚑ **ADR-0014 is the frame, and it reframes the fix**: client-supplied retry keys are its **Layer 3, unbuilt** — `rg -n 'Idempotency-Key' src/ config/` finds only two docblocks *naming* it as absent — while Layer 1's teeth are *"a re-dispatched creation fails the constraint"*, which `ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE` disarms on every child table. **So the remedy is Layer 3 once, for every endpoint, not six private per-endpoint keys** — and doing it per-method is the one-off-exception shape that produced the ADR-0042 defect. ⛔ **This row is what closes `EnvelopeAddFieldTest::a_replay_carrying_the_same_rectangles_but_different_content_is_swallowed`**, which characterises `addField()`'s share of it green with a tripwire and says to delete rather than repair it when the comparison lands. Related but narrower: BL-48 (the field mutation surface, which is what would make the swallowing deliberate) and BL-65 (the upsert sink beneath it). ⚑ **A seventh, 2026-08-28 — and it answers correctly, which is why the enumeration is incomplete rather than wrong.** [`Recipient::claim()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Entity/Recipient.php), built by [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md), follows this row's own named template — `assign()`'s: an identical replay **returns the existing claim** and records no second fact, a divergent one **throws naming the differing field** (`RecipientNotClaimableException::claimDiverges()`). It adds one rule the template did not have and that ADR-0057 §5 bar 11 demanded before it could be written: **a field the replay does not supply is not compared** — absent means *not asserted*, so a retry that omits `locale` after a first call that sent one is identical, not divergent, which is exactly the network-timeout retry a 409 would be worst on. ⛔ **This does not narrow the row.** Seven methods now answer in seven ways rather than six, and the remedy is unchanged: ADR-0014 Layer 3 **once**, for every endpoint — a seventh private per-method key is the one-off-exception shape that produced the ADR-0042 defect. ⚠ **Count words in this row are the shape [`BL-165`](BACKLOG.md) names**; re-derive the census from the tree rather than reading *"six"* here. | fold pending — [ADR-0014](adr/ADR-0014-idempotency-three-layer.md) Layer 3 is the home once it is designed | Open | 2026-08-04 (the server-minted-id fix survey; re-measured + filed 2026-08-10; seventh method 2026-08-28) |
| BL-70 | ⚠ **No abuse control exists anywhere in the request path, and D4's 178 bits of credential entropy are the only thing standing behind machine access.** Measured 2026-08-05, not assumed: **zero** matches for `RateLimiter` across `composer.json`, `config/` and `src/` (the component is not installed), **zero** rate-limiting directives in either `f5sign-infra` Caddyfile (and Caddy ships no built-in `rate_limit` — it needs a third-party module compiled into the image), and the infra Redis DB reserved for one is unused by the backend. No lockout, no IP reputation, no probe-volume ceiling. ⚑ **Surfaced by closing [ADR-0045](adr/ADR-0045-issued-credential-format.md)'s trailer-oracle gate, and it is the half that ruling deliberately does NOT cover.** That gate closed on the finding that the control trailer *filters garbage, not adversaries* — anyone can mint a well-formed candidate for free (draw 30 base62 characters, compute the trailer, append), so every adversarial probe is well-formed by construction and reaches the digest lookup regardless. The trailer bounds work on *malformed* input, which is a resource property and not a security one. ⛔ So this row exists specifically so that *"addressed by rate limiting and network-adjacent guards"* (his framing, same day) does not stay a sentence with no owner — naming a mitigation is not building one. ⚠ Bounded rather than dismissed: current deployment is a test environment behind an access restriction (see BL-38), and the restriction is in no repo we own. **The likely home is `f5sign-infra` (edge) rather than this repo** — propose, do not land unilaterally. | fold pending — likely `f5sign-infra` edge config <!-- OFFREPO: the edge config lives in the sibling infra repo `../f5sign-infra/`; not reachable under agent-only backend access. --> | Open | 2026-08-05 (ADR-0045 gate-2 closure) |
| BL-72 | ⚠ **Registering an API client records two facts at PLATFORM scope, so a tenant-filtered audit projection cannot see its own integrator being granted access.** Measured 2026-08-05 while writing [`ApiClientProvisioningTest`](../tests/F5Sign/IdentityAccess/Integration/ApiClientProvisioningTest.php), which now pins it: both `ApiClientRegistered` and `ApiKeyIssued` land in `platform.event_log` with `tenant_id = NULL`, `actor_kind = SYSTEM`, `actor = bootstrap:provision-api-client`. ⚑ **It is a consequence, not a decision, and that is the whole reason it is filed.** [`DbalEventLog`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/EventLog/DbalEventLog.php) stamps `tenant_id` from the ambient scope and a [`SystemCommand`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Bus/SystemCommand.php) has none — by construction, since it runs above RLS on a NOBYPASSRLS role. So the NULL is not a choice anyone made about API clients; it is inherited from a mechanism built for `TenantProvisioned`, where platform scope is exactly right because the fact brings the scope into being. Here the tenant already exists and the client belongs to it. ⚑ **The tenant is not lost — it is in the `ApiClientRegistered` payload**, so the information is recoverable; what is absent is the *column* a projector filters on. ▶ **The forcing moment is [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md)**, the Evidence & Audit projector: whoever builds a per-tenant audit view decides then whether these facts must be visible in it, and if they must, the fix is a scoping decision about system commands rather than a change to this command. ⛔ Do not "fix" it by scoping provisioning to the tenant — for `ProvisionTenant` that is circular, and a rule that treats the two system commands differently needs stating before it is coded. | [ADR-0010](adr/ADR-0010-command-bus-middleware-spine.md) Rule 4 *system context* → [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) | Open | 2026-08-05 (writing `ApiClientProvisioningTest`) |
| BL-73 | ⛔ **Nothing anywhere records WHICH CREDENTIAL authenticated a request, so after a key compromise the event log cannot answer "what did this key do?".** Measured 2026-08-05 by tracing every consumer: `platform.event_log` has a single `actor` column and it held the *claimed user* ([`DbalEventLog`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/EventLog/DbalEventLog.php) classifies the command's `issuer()`, and all twelve controllers passed `actingUser()`); [`Principal::client()`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Actor/Principal.php) has **zero call sites in `src/`**; `ApiClientId` appeared outside Identity & Access in exactly two files, both of which only *classified* it; and [`RequestContextProcessor`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Logging/RequestContextProcessor.php) reads only the two legacy headers. ⚑ **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-11 after D10 (a), and the row SURVIVES with a changed premise.** `actor` now holds the verified client (`client:<uuid>`, via `ActorId`), so "the client identity is consumed by nothing after the edge" is no longer the argument — **the gap is one level down and unchanged: a client is not a credential.** `ProvisionApiClient` mints up to `MAX_LIVE_KEYS` keys per client, nothing records *which* one authenticated a request, and so the log still cannot answer "what did this key do?" after a compromise. ⚑ **Both supporting measurements moved again later the same day and neither rescues the row.** `Principal::client()` now has exactly **one** call site in `src/` — `RequestContextProcessor` stamps the client onto Monolog records — and that processor no longer reads the retired headers. ⛔ **That is operational telemetry, not evidence**, so it does not narrow this gap by any amount: Monolog is erasable, retention-managed and outside `sys_commitment`, whereas the question here is what the **eIDAS system of record** can answer after a key compromise. If anything it sharpens the row by making the two categories concrete — which is the same split D10 (d) is still open on. ⚑ **This is the real finding that `BL-71` was pointing at and mis-stating** — it is a *traceability* gap, not an attribution-vocabulary fork, and it does not turn on which `ActorKind` a fact carries. ⚑ Severity is a forensics property, not a tidiness one: the log is the eIDAS system of record, and a leaked key is currently invisible in it. ▶ **Three shapes, cheapest first, and the cheap one is deliberately listed first because the expensive one is where this instinctively goes:** (a) record an **access fact once per authenticated request** — no schema change, no commitment change; (b) carry the client on the *command* so it reaches the existing `actor` slot in composite form — cheap but abuses `Issuer::toString()`; (c) a delegation column on `event_log` — ⛔ `sys_commitment` hashes the field set, so this is a **v2 commitment scheme** on an append-only tamper-evidence log. ⛔ Do not start at (c). ⚑ **2026-08-28 — a second half of this row, surfaced by TASK-036 and by the front-end team who have to satisfy it.** The declared subject (`F5Sign-Declared-Subject`, [ADR-0047](adr/ADR-0047-declared-subject-on-machine-requests.md)) is the mechanism that would answer *"which human is behind this machine call?"*, and TASK-036's claim route is the first to make its absence concrete: it **refuses** `f5s:none`, so a claim must assert a human — and the value is then validated and **discarded**, so nothing can answer *"which dispatcher named this consignee?"*. ⛔ **The refusal therefore proves a human was ASSERTED, never that one was RECORDED**, and no docblock, `#[OA\*]` string or test may imply otherwise. ⚠ **And the grammar admits an answer that satisfies the rule without naming a party, which is the part to read before building this.** Only `f5s:` is reserved; everything else is the integrator's own namespace and we validate shape alone. The dashboard, asked for a carrier reference from a **public** QR surface where no operator is authenticated, resolves one where it can (`carrier:<opaque id>`) and otherwise sends `wb:<waybillId>` — the id of the **object acted on**, not of a party. That is legitimate and deliberately does not reopen [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.9, since the bearer of the printed waybill stays unnamed. But it means that in those cases the traceability **does not live here**: it lives in that id resolving, on their side, to a carrier and a client when somebody asks. ⛑ **From this log the two are indistinguishable**, and the distinction is the whole of what this row is about — *"we know who answers for it"* versus *"we know who to ask"*. ⚠ A second constraint from the same exchange, for whoever renders this: a carrier reference attributes **responsibility, not authorship**. It must never be published as *"claimed by TRANSPORTES ACME"* — the data does not support that sentence. **2026-11-05: that second half closes for six acts, and the row stays open for the credential.** [ADR-0097](adr/ADR-0097-the-log-is-the-documents-one-source.md) §2.10 records the header on `RECIPIENT_CLAIMED` and five other facts, encrypted when it names a person. The `carrier:` and `wb:` answers above stay as written, and no document reads the value yet. | [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) *event-log store* + [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) | Open | 2026-08-05 (tracing BL-71) |
| BL-74 | ⛔ **On the own-CA path a signature's legal assurance is exactly the assurance of an email someone clicked, and no artifact records that.** For certificates issued by our own CA the subject DN (`CN`, `EMAIL`, `O`) derives **directly from the platform user record with no independent identity proofing** — only the QTSP path performs real vetting. There is no registration-authority process, no identity-vetting record, and **no level-of-assurance field**: measured 2026-08-12, `level.of.assurance` / `LoA` / `identity proofing` / `registration authority` return no field anywhere in `src/`, `migrations/` or `config/`. ⚑ **The gap is not unrecorded — it is recorded somewhere no teammate can read.** [`Authn`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Actor/Authn.php) names it twice, both times citing a workspace-local specification that is not in this repository and never will be; **this row is the home those pointers needed**. ⚑ It spans **Identity & Access** (who the subject is and how they were verified), **Signature Execution** (which DN reaches the certificate) and **Evidence & Audit** (what the evidence package may claim) — presumably why it landed in none of them. ⚑ For an eIDAS platform this is structural rather than a missing feature: the identity claim a signature makes is stronger than anything the platform can currently evidence. ▶ **The first move is a decision, not code** — whether the own-CA path may assert an identity it did not verify, and if so what level it may claim. | fold pending — spans IAM / Signature Execution / Evidence & Audit, with no single owner today | Open | 2026-08-12 (Identity & Access long-horizon design pass) |
| BL-75 | ⚠ **Three regulatory obligations land in this domain and not one of them is recorded anywhere in-repo.** Measured 2026-08-12: `RD 311/2022` / `Esquema Nacional` / `Accessibility Act` / `nivel medio` return **zero** hits across `docs/` and `src/`. **(a) ENS (RD 311/2022), nivel medio** is the minimum to serve Spanish public administration and explicitly requires role-based access control **and access auditing**; the action item *"document the mapping between ENS controls and the architecture"* is recorded as undone, and most of that mapping lands here. **(b) The European Accessibility Act** (in force 2025-06-28) applies to login, MFA and consent UIs. **(c) RGPD data-subject-rights flows are all specified around *signers*** — there is **no flow for a dashboard user** exercising access, portability or erasure over their own record. ⚑ **Filed as ONE row rather than three, deliberately**: they are three faces of *the compliance obligations landing in this domain are unmapped*, one person would work them as one piece, and three rows would grow the provisional-id debt without adding a decision. ▶ **Start with the ENS mapping** — it is the only one of the three gated by a customer (public-administration tenants) rather than by a date, and open question 16 (impersonation invisible to the tenant) is already in tension with its access-auditing requirement. | fold pending — Identity & Access, and the ENS half reaches every BC | Open | 2026-08-12 (Identity & Access long-horizon design pass) |

The design names the missing seam `IdentityQuery` (`docs/ddd/identity-access-domain-model.md`, *Ports this domain exposes*). ⛔ **The sharp part is that the specified signature is already wrong.** It was written as `UserId → display name / email / locale`, but since the qualified-actor cutover ([ADR-0046](adr/ADR-0046-qualified-actor-reference.md)) `Envelope::$senderId` is an `ActorId`, which is `user:<uuid>` **or** `client:<uuid>` — and a machine-authored envelope names an *organization with no mailbox*. So the port cannot be a user lookup, and the honest question is what a notification addressed to a client even means: the integrator's registered contact, the declared subject, or nothing at all. ⚑ Measured 2026-08-12: `IdentityQuery` appears in exactly one place in the repository — the ports table that declares it absent — so nothing is coding against it yet and the signature is still free. | fold pending — the port is Identity & Access's; the blocked consumer is Notification | Open | 2026-08-12 (Identity & Access long-horizon design pass) |
| BL-77 | ⚠ **An envelope carries no sender contact, so there is nobody to notify about it when a machine authored it.** Measured 2026-08-12: no `senderEmail` / `sender_email` / `senderContact` field exists anywhere in `src/` or `migrations/`. ▶ **Shape sketched by him, 2026-08-12, and recorded rather than built:** an **optional** sender-contact field on the envelope. When a *user* authored it the field may be null and the contact resolves from that user's record; when the field **is** present it **takes precedence** over the resolved value; and when the envelope is **machine-authored it is required**. ⚑ **The requirement is derivable from a field the envelope already holds** — `senderId` is an `ActorId`, which distinguishes `user:<uuid>` from `client:<uuid>` ([ADR-0046](adr/ADR-0046-qualified-actor-reference.md)) — so the presence rule needs **no second flag**, and a flag would be the BL-71 shape of a field whose meaning depends on context nobody reads. The natural enforcement point is a send-time invariant, beside `ensureEveryRecipientSeesSomething()`. ⛔ **One constraint the design must clear before it is built:** an email address is PII, [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) has **no application delete path**, and that is exactly why the declared subject was forced to be an opaque identifier and **never email or name** ([ADR-0047](adr/ADR-0047-declared-subject-on-machine-requests.md)). A sender contact that reaches the event log hits the same wall. ⚑ Partner to BL-76: that row says the *port* cannot resolve a client's address; this row says the *envelope* could carry one instead. Neither is decided. | fold pending — the model half is Envelope's, the resolution half is Identity & Access's | Open | 2026-08-12 (his gap, raised on the IdentityQuery finding) |
| BL-78 | ⚠ **There is no channel to tell anyone that their API key is compromised, and no decision about whose address it would be.** If a key is exfiltrated we can revoke it — revocation is a column-level `UPDATE` grant, deliberately — but nothing in the platform can **reach the people responsible**. ▶ **The open question is placement, and it is his, stated 2026-08-12: tenant level, `ApiClient` level, or the individual key?** Each answers a different question. A *tenant* contact is the security-incident address of the organization. An *`ApiClient`* contact is the owner of one integration, which is closer to who can actually rotate it. A *per-key* contact is finer still, and it is not obviously excessive: `MAX_LIVE_KEYS` permits multiple live keys per client precisely so rotation can overlap, and two integrations may be operated by different people. ⛔ **Compounded by BL-73: nothing records WHICH key authenticated a request**, so today a compromise is both **un-diagnosable** (the log cannot say what that key did) and **un-announceable** (no address). | BL-81 | ⛔ **No revocation entry point exists anywhere in the application, and rotation is unreachable — so a leaked API key can only be killed by hand-written SQL, and `MAX_LIVE_KEYS` can never be reached.** Measured: `ApiKey::revoke()` has **zero callers in `src/`**, and `issueKey()` has exactly one — `ApiClient::register()`, minting the *first* key — so no code path issues a second key to an existing client. Nothing is lost (`platform.event_log` is the record) and the volume is operator-paced. ⚠ **What the left-alone disposition costs operations:** the queue is **never empty in production**, so a depth alert on it cannot tell these expected facts from a context whose events *should* have had a queue, which is the miss BL-32 exists for.
