On a database carried across the cutover, every historical `ENVELOPE_CREATED`, `ENVELOPE_SENT` and `ENVELOPE_VOIDED` row throws in `EventRelay::publish()`, blocks the single cursor head-of-line for `MAX_ATTEMPTS`, then is quarantined and permanently skipped — and `EventRelay::checkpoint()` returns the floor for a consumer that has never run, so a future projector replays from the floor into every one. ⚠ **Status 2026-08-14, the merge having landed — restated, because "the window closes at merge" was the pre-merge framing and it is no longer the whole answer.** The cutover is [`Version20260810000001`](../migrations/Version20260810000001.php). Any database **created after** it is clean by construction. The residual exposure is exactly: **a database that predates that migration and is carried forward rather than reset.** On 2026-08-13 the answer was that no such database existed (dev-only and resettable, his call) — but that was measured against **dev**, and it does not answer for any other environment the merge is now deployed into. ✅ **Answered 2026-08-14 (his): known and accepted — the current test database will be reset**, so no environment carries a pre-cutover row across. ▶ **What remains owed is the standing rule, not this instance:** any *future* environment must either postdate the cutover or be reset, because the symptom is not an error at deploy — it is `EventRelay::publish()` throwing on the first historical `ENVELOPE_CREATED` / `ENVELOPE_SENT` / `ENVELOPE_VOIDED` row, blocking the single cursor head-of-line for `MAX_ATTEMPTS`, then quarantining it and moving on **silently**. | BL-119 | **The strength ordering over `AuthMethod` is owed by two consumers and defined by neither.** [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.1 declines to rank the catalog and explains why the intent survives as two structural rules instead — strength is not one-dimensional, and the same `OTP_EMAIL` is strong or weak depending on the channel the invitation travelled. But two places genuinely need an order: `SubstituteAuthMethod`'s *"comparable strength"* (§2.11) and the signature-level projection. Deciding which accessibility swaps are acceptable *is* deciding an order, so that is where it has to be defined — tiered and eIDAS-anchored, with the case in front of it, rather than invented as a scalar in advance. | [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.1 + §2.11 | Open | 2026-08-17 (two-gate auth review) |

| BL-104 | ⛔ **The `Content-Type` of a stored document is whatever the client declared, it is echoed back verbatim, and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` exists nowhere.** [`UploadDocumentContentController`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/UploadDocumentContentController.php) stores `$file->getClientMimeType()` — the **declared** type, not the sniffed one (`getMimeType()`) — with no `Assert\File`, no `mimeTypes` allow-list, no `maxSize`, no `extensions` and no magic-byte check. Three download controllers then return it as-is (`'Content-Type' => $content->contentType` in `DownloadCurrentDocumentController`, `DownloadSealedDocumentController`, `DownloadSigningDocumentController`). ⚑ **And `nosniff` is absent from `src/`, `config/` and the infra Caddyfile**, so a browser is free to sniff and render. Upload an HTML payload declared as `application/pdf` and it is served back to a signer's browser as HTML. ⚑ The cheap half is the header (`nosniff` + `Content-Disposition: attachment` where a document is not meant to render inline); the real fix also derives the type from content and checks it against a closed list. ⚠ Found 2026-08-17 by a skills audit, and the reason no check caught it is worth carrying: the nearest generic rule — *"explicit Content-Type, never autodetect"* — **is satisfied by this code**. ▶ **Extended 2026-10-26 ([TASK-047](tasks/TASK-047-per-signer-evidence-document.md) security audit):** the two receipt routes (`GET /api/v1/envelopes/{id}/recipients/{recipientId}/receipt`, `GET /api/v1/signing/session/receipt`) serve a PDF with a server-set `Content-Type` and `attachment` disposition and no `nosniff`, as the audit-trail route does; whatever header decision closes this row covers them. ▶ **Extended 2026-10-26 ([TASK-070](tasks/TASK-070-a-recipient-reads-their-copy-through-a-link.md) security audit):** `GET /api/v1/copy/documents/{documentId}` ([`DownloadCopyDocumentController`](../src/F5Sign/Session/UI/Http/DownloadCopyDocumentController.php)) serves the integrator-declared `mimeType` verbatim, **inline**, with no `nosniff`: the same behaviour as `DownloadSigningDocumentController`, on a route a recipient reaches for as long as the documents are kept. It is the worst variant of this row, and whatever closes it covers this route. | fold pending — Envelope UI + a security header decision; sibling of `BL-63` | Open | 2026-08-17 (skills audit) |

| BL-110 | **A checkpoint-rewind command is the deliberate other half of quarantine recovery, and it is not being built.** [TASK-026](tasks/TASK-026-event-path-silent-drops.md) §4 D1 chose a *surgical* replay — one named `event_id` re-dispatched with the cursor left alone — because the everyday case is "one row was poison, the cause is fixed, send that one". Rewinding `platform.event_checkpoint` re-delivers **everything** after the rewind point; consumers are idempotent ([ADR-0014](adr/ADR-0014-idempotency-three-layer.md)) so it is survivable, but it is the wrong default. ▶ **What would force it: the first incident with more quarantined rows than an operator will paste by hand** — many rows parked across a window, e.g. after a bad deploy. ⚑ It needs a guard the replay does not: it must state its blast radius before acting, since the operator cannot see what falls inside the rewound range. ✅ **The condition is now met, which narrows this row rather than closing it.** It read *"the deferral is conditional on the replay existing — until TASK-026 §3.1 ships, **neither** path exists and the only recovery is a hand-written `UPDATE` against the checkpoint table in production"*. The replay shipped (`app:event-log:replay`, TASK-026 slice 1), so the everyday case — one poison row, cause fixed — is served and the hand-written `UPDATE` is no longer the only recovery. What stays deferred is exactly the broad-incident tool, and the guard named above is still owed with it. ⚑ This row is also the correct target for the pointer ADR-0031 carried as `BL-113`, which was an unrelated plaintext-credential row; corrected in the same changeset. | [TASK-026](tasks/TASK-026-event-path-silent-drops.md) §6.1 | Open | 2026-08-17 (event-path durability plan) |
| BL-111 | **The ICU-catalogs / archive-removal chain is parked, and it is one task rather than the two it was planned as.** ⚑ **Two findings, measured 2026-08-17, both against the plan rather than the code.** **(1) It cannot be split the way it was sequenced.** ICU was scheduled *before* archive removal and cannot precede it: [`TemplateSyntax::TOKEN_PATTERN`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Templating/TemplateSyntax.php) is frozen and its own docblock names the collision — *"Every archived row is stored in this syntax, and a richer pattern (**ICU's `{token}`, say**) would not parse them"* — and [`NotificationChannel::authored()`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Domain/Port/NotificationChannel.php) takes `(purpose, locale)` and **no variables**, so ICU has nothing to select a plural form with at composition time. ICU requires formatting at transmit, which is the pin change [TASK-024](tasks/TASK-024-notification-copy-to-twig.md) §4 D3 assigns to archive removal. **(2) It buys no product today.** The catalog is two purposes across two locales (`en`, `es`) since TASK-027 slice 1, and **not one slot is a count, a number or a date**. ⛔ **And the sentence that justified the whole chain is not an ICU problem at all** — *«X» ha sido firmado* wanting *firmada* needs the document title's gender, which is user-supplied and unknowable at every point in the pipeline; the fix is copy that does not agree with it ([TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) §4 D3). ▶ **What would force it: the first copy carrying a real count, number or date; or the translation process becoming the bottleneck** — a `const` in an infrastructure class is a pull request, tolerable for two purposes in two locales and not tolerable well before twenty. ⚠ Parking it does **not** park [TASK-024](tasks/TASK-024-notification-copy-to-twig.md), whose justification was re-based onto the markup itself and onto TASK-022's new purpose. | [TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) §4 D7 | Open | 2026-08-17 (Spanish-first re-scoping) |
| BL-112 | ⛔ **From a git worktree, every main-stack tooling target validates a DIFFERENT checkout and reports green for it.** `f5sign-infra`'s `docker-compose.override.yml` bind-mounts **`../f5sign-backend`** — the main checkout, hardcoded — so `make test` / `make phpstan` / `make lint` / `make composer cmd=…` run against whatever branch *that* tree is on. The worktree's edits are never in the container and **nothing warns you**: the command succeeds and the numbers look plausible. ⚑ **Measured 2026-08-17, and the failure is confident rather than silent:** a session in `f5sign-backend-develop` got `OK (16 tests)`, inferred from the count that 8 test methods in the file it had just edited were not being collected, and reported that as a defect undermining [TASK-024](tasks/TASK-024-notification-copy-to-twig.md)'s acceptance bar. Both claims were false — the container was running an unrelated branch whose copy of that file has 6 test methods; the real count in the worktree is 27. The same session reported "PHPStan green" for edits PHPStan never saw. ▶ **Half-mitigated:** the trap is now documented at the top of `CLAUDE.md`'s tooling section, and `make wt-backend src=<path>` does mount your tree. **What is still owed is the other half:** that lane runs **`composer test` only**, so `lint`, `arch`, `phpstan` and `infection` have **no worktree-aware **target**, only a documented manual `docker run` invocation — and the lane additionally dies on composer's 300s process timeout before the suite finishes, with storage tests failing by design (no MinIO). ⛔ **Corrected same day, and the correction is the more useful half of this row: none of the above was a discovery.** The `task-runner` and `task-validate-backend` skills already document all of it — that `make test` validates the other tree, that the lane is postgres-only, that storage fails on `Could not resolve host: minio`, the third `docker run` route, and even that the 300s death is **not** suite duration (~74s measured there) but `composer install` + migrations + suite sharing one process. This row originally said the four static checks had "no worktree-aware route at all", which was false and was written without reading them. ▶ **So what is actually owed is narrower than it looked:** a `make` target (or an argument to `scripts/wt-validate.sh`) so the documented route stops being hand-assembled per session — run the three static checks, which need no service, and invoke phpunit directly rather than through composer. ⚑ **The recurring failure here is not the missing target, it is entering through `CLAUDE.md` and never opening the skill that already knew** — the same "the instruction already existed and was missed" bucket `CLAUDE.md` names as by far the largest in its own review corpus. ⚠ **Cross-repo** (`f5sign-infra`), which is why it is not a drive-by. | fold pending — `f5sign-infra` `scripts/wt-validate.sh`; the warning half is landed in `CLAUDE.md`, and the detail already lives in the `task-runner` / `task-validate-backend` skills | Open | 2026-08-17 (worktree validation trap) |
| BL-120 | **The policy-override chain is a convention with no level behind it.** `signing-session-domain-model.md` states that policy constants resolve tenant -> workspace -> envelope -> platform default, and [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.8 cited it as established infrastructure until 2026-08-17. **None of the three override levels exists**: there is no policy store at tenant, envelope or anywhere else, and `workspace` is described by its own migration as an untriaged fork. So every auth constant — code length, the two thresholds, the cooldown window, both challenge TTLs, the re-send interval — is a platform default and cannot be changed by a customer. Nobody has asked to change one, which is why the resolver was not built inside an authentication slice. What was built instead is what keeps it cheap later: the ceremony reads the constants through a single point, so adding levels changes that point rather than every call site. The forcing case will be a customer wanting a longer code or a longer email TTL, and the first level worth building is probably the tenant, not the envelope. | [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.8; [TASK-021](tasks/TASK-021-two-gate-auth-model-and-access-gate.md) §2 | Open | 2026-08-17 (two-gate auth review) |
| BL-121 | ⚠ **[ADR-0019](adr/ADR-0019-database-schema-conventions.md) §5 permits `TEXT` *because* it relocates the length bound to the value object, and for six fields that relocation never happened.** §5 is explicit — *"a string unbounded everywhere is forbidden"* — yet [`Locale::of()`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Templating/Locale.php) enforces no length, and neither does `Envelope::title` nor `Recipient::fullName`. Measured 2026-08-18 across every `Envelope` request object rather than one sibling: unbounded at **all three** layers are `AddRecipientRequest::$full_name` (no constraint at all), `CreateEnvelopeRequest::$title`, and `AddDocumentRequest`'s `$name` / `$display_name` / `$mime_type`; the compliant precedent in the same tree is `AddFieldRequest::$label` / `$anchor_text` (`Length(max: 255)`) and `VoidEnvelopeRequest::$reason` (`Length(max: 1000)`). ⚑ **[TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) added a seventh and then put a ceiling on that one edge only** — `AddRecipientRequest::$locale` carries `Assert\Length(max: 35)` — so the HTTP path is bounded and any other writer is not; the hand-written `notification.tenant_default` row is exactly such a writer. The introduced defect was worse than the missing bound and is already repaired: `Version20260817000001`'s docblock cited ADR-0019 §5 *as if* the relocation had happened, which is authoring rule 2 on the surface `CLAUDE.md` names as highest-damage. ⛔ **Do not close this by bounding `Locale` alone** — one decision about `Foundation` value objects, not six drive-bys, is what the shape wants. ▶ What would force it: any of these fields becoming reachable by a lower-trust caller, or the ADR-0019 conformance census growing a bound check. | [TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) §6.7; rule is [ADR-0019](adr/ADR-0019-database-schema-conventions.md) §5 | Folded | 2026-08-18 (TASK-027 security audit) |
| BL-122 | ⚠ **A hand-typed `' en '` in `notification.tenant_default` silently gives a tenant the opposite of the language they configured.** [`DbalTenantNotificationDefaults`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Infrastructure/Persistence/DbalTenantNotificationDefaults.php) guards *blankness* (`trim($locale) !== ''`, so a `' '` row reads as absent instead of throwing inside the notification fan-out) but deliberately does **not** normalize the value: the tag reaches `Locale::of()` verbatim, matches no authored catalog key, and degrades to [TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) §4 D8's fallback — Spanish. ⛔ **The severity is from the writer, not the string handling: hand insertion is the table's ONLY writer** (§6.2 — it ships with no endpoint and no provisioning path), so the person who can type a stray space is the only person who ever writes here, and the failure is silent in both directions since Spanish is also the right answer for nearly everyone. ⚠ **Left unfixed on purpose:** `Locale` stores tags verbatim everywhere, so normalizing in one adapter would make it disagree with the type — and normalizing tag *values* is the edge of the validation D8 declined, whose legal shape is a shared `Foundation` locale registry costing an ADR. Cheap containment if it bites first: `CHECK (locale = btrim(locale))` on the column, whose SQL is still editable while the migration is unpushed (repo rule 2). ▶ What would force it: the first tenant default that does not take effect. | [TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) §6.8; the registry shape is §6.6 | Folded | 2026-08-18 (TASK-027 persistence review) |
| BL-123 | **The tenant tier of the notification locale chain is inert on delivery — shipped, not planned.** `notification.tenant_default` exists with RLS and is read once per occasion batch, but **nothing in the application writes it**: no endpoint, no provisioning path, no console command ([TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) §6.2, his call — *"si, de momento solo a mano"*). So production runs `recipient → system`, and the middle tier is exercised only by its tests and by an operator with `psql`. ⚠ **Under §4 D8 that is indistinguishable from a tier working correctly**, because the system default is also `es`: a tenant tier that never fires and one that fires right produce the same email. ⚑ Disclosed on the surfaces that would otherwise imply a configurable setting — both published `#[OA\*]` descriptions say *"no API sets a tenant default yet"*, and the frontend handoff leads with *"No construir una pantalla de ajustes del tenant contra este campo"*. ▶ What would force an endpoint: the first tenant that is not Spanish-speaking. | [TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) §6.9 (§6.2 is the decision) | Folded | 2026-08-18 (TASK-027 close) |
| BL-124 | ⚠ **`EmailNotificationChannel::fallbackLocale()` has no production caller, so the 86% covered-MSI reported for [TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) does not speak for it.** It exists so D8's fallback-completeness guard follows `DEFAULT_LOCALE` instead of restating today's value — the right design, and precisely why nothing in `src/` calls it — but Infection only mutates what a test reaches through the code under test, so the headline figure is silent about this method. What actually holds it is sabotage 4b (§5): setting `DEFAULT_LOCALE` to an unauthored `'fr'` reddens the guard's first limb. ⚑ **Filed because the number is the thing people quote**, and it will be read as covering everything that changeset added. Contrast the near-miss in the same file: `authoredPurposes()` *is* called from the guard's body, so its mutants are attempted. ⚑ Distinct mechanism from **BL-67** (acceptance tests are `#[CoversNothing]`, so Infection never selects the killing test) and **BL-89** (exercised but not attributed) — here there is no production call site at all. ▶ What would force a change: `fallbackLocale()` acquiring a second consumer, at which point it stops being a test-facing accessor and owes a normal test. | [TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) §6.10; gate config is `infection.json5.dist` under [ADR-0035](adr/ADR-0035-testing-standard.md) | Folded | 2026-08-18 (TASK-027 validation) |
| BL-125 | ⛔ **The `lang=` escape is deliberately unobservable through the public API — do not delete it on the evidence that nothing kills it.** ⚑ **Mechanism changed 2026-08-18 ([TASK-024](tasks/TASK-024-notification-copy-to-twig.md)); the reason did not, and the row is narrower now rather than discharged.** It was a hand-written `self::escapeHtml($locale->value)` inside `EmailNotificationChannel::htmlLayout()`, which no longer exists. The sink is now `<html lang="{{ view.lang }}">` in `templates/notification/layout.html.twig`, and what escapes it is Twig's autoescaping, which applies to every `.html.twig` whether or not anyone remembers it — so the guard is held by the engine instead of by a line a cleanup could delete, and the delete-me shape moved with it: the way to lose it now is to declare [`EmailLayoutView::$lang`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Infrastructure/Channel/EmailLayoutView.php) as `Twig\Markup` like every other property there, which reads as consistency and is the defect. ⚠ **Infection still cannot see it, and now cannot even report it**: there is no PHP call left to mutate, so the escaped mutant that used to stand for this row is simply absent — the *type* is the guard, and a declaration is not a mutation target. ▶ What a future guard would look like: an assertion that every `EmailLayoutView` property carrying developer-authored markup is `Markup` and every property carrying a value is not. No authored locale contains a character `htmlspecialchars` touches, and `authored()` only ever hands the layout a catalog key or `DEFAULT_LOCALE`, so no test reachable through the public API can separate the escaped call from the bare interpolation. ⚑ **It earns its place because [TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) is what first made the locale client-supplied**, and because the `html` part is archived *and fingerprinted* ([ADR-0038](adr/ADR-0038-template-archive.md)): a regression in the upstream predicate would not spoil one email, it would write attacker-controlled markup into the template archive under a pin, permanently. Containment was probed rather than reasoned — six hostile tags (XSS, attribute break, CRLF, 100k chars, numeric-string key coercion, an unauthored `fr`) all pinned `es` and rendered `lang="es"`, against an authored `en` control that still passes through as itself. ⚠ This row is a **do-not-tidy record**, not owed work: the day the escape becomes observable is the day the upstream constraint has already failed. Not on [`LOAD-BEARING.md`](LOAD-BEARING.md) §2 because nobody has proposed removing it yet; if someone does and it is argued down, it belongs there. | [TASK-027](tasks/TASK-027-spanish-email-and-the-locale-nobody-chooses.md) §6.11, plus [TASK-024](tasks/TASK-024-notification-copy-to-twig.md) §7 for the mechanism it moved to | Folded | 2026-08-18 (TASK-027 security audit) |

| BL-126 | ⛔ **A new `RecipientOutcome` case cannot be added until the Session BC decides what a signing session reports for a recipient who was never reached, and the answer may be a signer wire break.** [`SigningSessionReader::status()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/SigningSessionReader.php) matches exhaustively over `RecipientOutcome` with **no `default`**, deliberately — its docblock says a new case must break the build *rather than silently defaulting into "open, go ahead and sign"*. So the guard is working as designed; what it exposes is an undecided question. The candidates are a refusal, an existing `SessionStatus`, or a **new** one — and a new `SessionStatus` is a published change for the deployed signer app, which lives in the sibling repo `f5sign-signer`, i.e. two coordinated PRs. ⚑ This blocks [TASK-025](tasks/TASK-025-recipient-delivery-failure-and-correction.md) outright, and through it [TASK-022](tasks/TASK-022-delivered-codes-over-email.md), which depends on it because [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.7 forbids channel fallback. ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-18 for `DELIVERY_FAILED`: it maps to `AUTH_PASSED` — the recipient may still sign.** The signing token is minted *before* delivery is attempted, so the credential is the token and not the mailbox; a bounce means our channel failed, not that the holder of the link is illegitimate. No new `SessionStatus`, no wire change, no signer coordination. ⚠ **The row stays OPEN because the general question is not answered**: this settles one case, and the `match` still breaks on the next outcome anyone adds. ▶ **What would force it again: any new recipient outcome at all** — and the answer will not always be "let them through". | [TASK-025](tasks/TASK-025-recipient-delivery-failure-and-correction.md) §6.1 | Open | 2026-08-18 (verification pass, no code) |

| BL-129 | ⚠ **A replay publishes a fact and deletes its dead-letter row with no principal, no reason and no log append — and the deletion erases the only record the quarantine ever existed.** Raised by the security audit of [TASK-026](tasks/TASK-026-event-path-silent-drops.md) slice 1, 2026-08-18. `EventRelay::replayQuarantined()` can re-trigger real side effects (a notification resend reaches a real recipient) and answers none of *who replayed this, when, why*. ⚑ **This qualifies the rationale of the `f5sign.sql.rawDelete` exemption rather than contradicting it:** the *fact* survives the delete — it is in the append-only log and has just been re-published — but the *incident* does not, and `platform.event_dead_letter` is the only place it lived. ▶ **Shape when it is wanted:** an append to `platform.event_log` at replay time carrying actor + reason, rather than keeping the dead-letter row (which would re-open the permanently-red-alarm problem the delete exists to avoid). ⚠ Not an authorization gap — this repo has no CLI principal and inventing one for this command would be worse than the gap; the real boundary is who can exec the container and hold `f5sign_relay`. | [TASK-026](tasks/TASK-026-event-path-silent-drops.md) §6 | Open | 2026-08-18 (security audit of TASK-026 slice 1) |
| BL-130 | ⚠ **Six free-text fields across 8 of the 23 event types are neither pseudonymous nor references, in a table nothing can erase.** Measured 2026-08-18 by the security audit of [TASK-026](tasks/TASK-026-event-path-silent-drops.md) slice 2, over an exhaustive key-set check of all 23 frozen payload fixtures (not a sample). ADR-0031 describes payloads as *"pseudonymous / refs"*; `reservations` (in `RECIPIENT_SIGNED`, `RECIPIENT_COMPLETED`, `SESSION_COMPLETED`, `SIGNATURE_COMMITTED`), `title`, `name` and `reason` are unbounded human-authored text. ⛔ **`reservations` is the sharp one:** the eCMR incident note **typed by the signer during the ceremony**, capped only at the HTTP edge, no value object, `TEXT` column, written verbatim into the append-only log — and eCMR reservations routinely name people and vehicles. No caller discipline can prevent it because the text originates from an end user, so *"PII-free by construction"* is really *"PII-free by caller discipline"* and here there is no caller to discipline. There is no erasure path: [ADR-0032](adr/ADR-0032-evidence-pii-custody.md) is `Proposed` and the log-path cipher is unbuilt. ⚑ The finding is about the **events**, not the fixtures — all 23 fixture values are benign, and that is what makes it a claim about the grammar rather than about test data. | [ADR-0096](adr/ADR-0096-key-management-encrypts-the-logs-free-text-under-erasable-keyrings.md) §2.1, §2.12 item 3 | Folded | 2026-08-18 (security audit of TASK-026 slice 2) |
| BL-131 | **A replay's refusal paths disclose cross-tenant metadata: the guard bounds the action, not the disclosure.** `app:event-log:replay` distinguishes *not in the log* from *in the log* and prints `event_type` plus the attempt count for **any** tenant's row, without replaying anything. Within ADR-0031's accepted posture (the relay is a trusted cross-scope reader and `app:event-log:dead-letters` already prints a Tenant column), so this is a naming defect rather than a new hole — but *"only quarantined rows are replayed"* reads as if it bounds what the command **tells** you, and it does not. ▶ Cheapest honest fix is wording, not code. | [TASK-026](tasks/TASK-026-event-path-silent-drops.md) §6 | Open | 2026-08-18 (security audit of TASK-026 slice 1) |
| BL-132 | **`EventRelay::sanitizeErrorMessage()` normalises encoding but does not redact — latent, not live.** Verified 2026-08-18 rather than assumed: every current throw site was checked and none echoes a payload value (`Row` interpolates the column name and `get_debug_type`, `json_decode` carries no bytes, and the one value echo is bounded to 64 chars of an id-shaped string). ▶ **What would make it live:** a future `fromPayload()` that includes a rejected value in its message, which would write tenant free text permanently into `platform.event_dead_letter.error`. Recorded so that change is recognised as crossing a line rather than as an improved error message. | [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) | Open | 2026-08-18 (security audit of TASK-026) |
| BL-136 | **Two halves of [ADR-0052](adr/ADR-0052-twig-layout-composition.md) are held by nothing, and both are the empty-set-reads-as-all-clear shape rather than gaps somebody forgot.** **(1) *No HTTP route renders Twig* (§2.6) is unenforced, and the environment is wider than the view object.** ⚑ **Measured 2026-08-18 by the TASK-024 security audit, and it refuted three places that said otherwise**: `EmailLayoutView` is what the *channel* passes, not what a template can *reach*. TwigBundle merges `app` (`Symfony\Bridge\Twig\AppVariable`) into every template's context — `app.request`, `app.session`, `app.token` — and loads `HttpKernelExtension`, `CsrfExtension` and `DebugExtension` with **no `SandboxExtension`**. Because the command bus is `sync://`, `authored()` runs inside the caller's HTTP request, so a template reaching `app.request` would write request data into the insert-only, fingerprinted `platform.template`, permanently and unerasably. Not attacker-reachable — no template uses it — which is precisely the shape: green is the expected signal either way.  `config/packages/twig.yaml` points `default_path` at `templates/notification/`, so an application view tree is off the loader and `EmailChannelTwigWiringTest` asserts that half — but nothing stops a controller rendering `layout.html.twig`. It holds today because no controller does, so green is the expected signal and the set that would redden is empty. **(2) The `Markup`-versus-`string` contract on [`EmailLayoutView`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Infrastructure/Channel/EmailLayoutView.php) is a convention with a test, not a rule.** The property's type decides escaping (§2.5): a copy field declared `string` is double-escaped silently, a value declared `Twig\Markup` is un-escaped silently, and the two failures are opposite. `EmailNotificationChannelTest::every_authored_copy_string_survives_composition_unchanged` catches the first **only for copy that carries a character escaping touches** — today `&laquo;`/`&ldquo;` in the `lead` fields — and nothing at all catches the second, because no value is supposed to be there to observe. ⚑ Filed as one row because they are one guard: a rule over `templates/notification/` and `EmailLayoutView` could state all three — no controller may render from that path, no template may reference `app` or a bundle namespace, and every property of that class is `Markup` xor a declared value. A `SandboxExtension` policy scoped to this path is the alternative that enforces rather than detects. ⚠ Related but distinct from `BL-125` above, which is about the *one* property that must stay `string`. | [ADR-0052](adr/ADR-0052-twig-layout-composition.md) §5 + [TASK-024](tasks/TASK-024-notification-copy-to-twig.md) §7.6 | Open | 2026-08-18 (TASK-024 delivery) |

| BL-133 | ⚠ **Identity & Access events are published to the broker and match no binding, so they land in `queue.events.unroutable` for ever — measured, 39 of them.** Measured 2026-08-18 by the infra session: 39 messages, all `App.IdentityAccess.Event.*` (`ApiClientRegistered`, `ApiKeyIssued`), and **zero bindings for that BC** in `definitions.json`. ⛑ **This is one instance of [BL-32](BACKLOG.md), not a replacement for it**, and the distinction matters: BL-32 is the missing check (event-type registry vs broker bindings) that would close the *generator*; this row is what the generator produced this time. ⛔ **The right fix is NOT a queue and a binding.** Verified from the code: none of the repo's ten `Reactor` implementers consumes either event, so a queue would be one nobody drains — trading a symptom that is *named* for what it is (`unroutable`) for one that looks like it is waiting for a consumer. ⚑ **The real defect is a level up**: the relay publishes to the broker **everything** that enters the evidence log (ADR-0031), while bindings are hand-maintained per BC — so the system does not distinguish *a fact recorded for evidence* from *a fact someone must act on*, and every log-only event falls here **by construction**, with the stack green. That is why BL-32 has been "closed" three times by point patches: each patch fixed one BC and left the generator intact. ▶ **What would force it: anyone writing a consumer for credential events** — and then the queue and the binding land in the **same change** as the consumer, never before. ⚑ The infra repo's `README.md` carries the mirror of this row under a heading that says *do not add a binding to drain it*, because whoever sees the effect (`make worker-status`, `make rabbit-dlq`) is looking at infra, not backend. | Infra session + backend code census, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-134 | ⚠ **The task-runner runs its phase-3 validations in parallel, and several of those skills work by SABOTAGING source files — so they corrupt each other's runs, silently.** Observed 2026-08-18 on TASK-025: `task-validate-backend` caught `DbalEnvelopeRepository.php` carrying **another skill's** mutation mid-run and restored it, then re-established every figure on a clean tree. ⛑ **Nothing detects this**, which is the point: a suite run against someone else's half-applied sabotage produces a well-formed number for a tree that never existed, and the reds look like ordinary failures. ⚑ **The cause is that the skills changed under the orchestration, not that the orchestration was wrong.** `task-runner` §Fase 3 says to invoke them *"en paralelo (varias llamadas Agent en un solo mensaje)"*, which was correct when they only read. `doctrine-guard`, `contract-check-backend` and `task-validate-backend` now each revert a guard and watch it redden — the very technique this repo relies on — and that makes them **mutually exclusive**, not parallel. ⚠ They also share one worktree **and** one database, so serialising the agents is necessary but may not be sufficient. ▶ **What would force it: nothing — it is already firing.** The fix is either a fence in `task-runner` (sabotaging skills run sequentially) or per-skill isolation (`isolation: "worktree"` plus a private database), and the second is the one that also fixes the shared-DB half. ⚑ Recorded rather than fixed here because it is a change to the skill definitions, not to this task. | Observed on TASK-025 phase 3, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-135 | ⚠ **The ad-hoc container a worktree must use starts with a quarter of the memory the documented route has — 128M against 512M — so `composer infection` dies there and completes on the main checkout.** Measured 2026-08-18: `docker exec f5sign-php-fpm php -r 'echo ini_get("memory_limit");'` → **512M**; `docker run --rm f5sign/backend:dev` → **128M**. ⛔ **Stated as "infection is not runnable as documented" in this row's first draft, which was wrong and wrong in the expensive direction** — another session ran `make -C ../f5sign-infra composer cmd=infection` on the main checkout and it completed (3m26s, 5207 mutants, covered-MSI 86%). The documented route works; **the worktree route does not**, and a reader of the original wording would have concluded the gate is broken everywhere. Same class of imprecision [BL-112](BACKLOG.md) already had to learn. ⛔ **And the 128M route is not session folklore — it is what [`task-runner`'s](../.claude/skills/task-runner/SKILL.md) precondition 5 tells you to use** when the `wt-*` lane falls short. ⚑ **Look at what that recipe protects:** its `phpunit` line carries `-d memory_limit=-1`; its sibling **migrations** line does not, and neither does the `mkdir` helper below it. So the skill documents the low-memory route and guards **exactly one command** — every other tool anyone pipes through that same recipe inherits 128M with no override and no warning. ⚑ **The generalisation is the useful part, and it is not about Infection**: every tool run from a worktree goes through the ad-hoc container, so *all* of them get 128M instead of 512M. Infection is simply the first that needed more — `-d memory_limit=-1` does not reach the `phpunit` child it spawns, and `phpstan/tests/Rules/HermeticTierRuleTest` exhausts 128M under Xdebug coverage. ⚠ Worked around on TASK-025 with a bind-mounted `memory_limit=-1` ini, so **that task's covered-MSI was measured by a route nothing in the repo describes**; TASK-024's was measured by the documented one. ⛔ **Before editing that recipe, know where it lives — the symptom otherwise is *"I edited the file and git cannot see it"*.** `.claude/skills/task-runner/` is a **symlink into the workspace store** (`../../../ai/f5sign-backend/.claude/skills/task-runner`), as is `CLAUDE.md`. Editing through the symlink writes the real file, but it belongs to the **workspace root repo** (`/var/www/html/factor5_others/f5sign`, branch `main`), so `git add` here shows nothing. ⚑ Note the level: the **directory** is the symlink, not `SKILL.md` — `test -L` on the file says no, which is why this is easy to check and still get wrong. ▶ **What would force it: any tool crossing 128M from a worktree.** ⚑ **Two independent halves — do not fuse them.** Raising the limit **in the image** (infra) closes the *class*; adding the override to the three recipes (workspace root repo) closes the *recipe people copy* in the meantime. The second stops being needed once the first lands, but until then it is the one that gets read. ⚠ **Corrección 2026-08-18, medida por la sesión de `f5sign-infra` abriendo los ficheros que esta fila nunca abrió:** los 128 MB **no eran del lane**. `docker-compose.wt.backend.yml` ya bind-monteaba `./docker/php/php.ini` (512M) dentro de `php-fpm-wt`, con un comentario describiendo exactamente esta muerte. Los 128 MB salían del **`docker run` a mano** que una sesión de worktree se monta cuando el lane no le sirve — o sea que esto **no es un defecto independiente sino un síntoma de BL-138/§1**: el lane cojo empuja a fabricarse un contenedor propio, y ése sí arranca pelado. ⛔ Tal y como estaba escrita, esta fila mandaba a arreglar la imagen dev **cuando el lane ya cubría el caso**. ⚑ **Lo que sí sigue en pie y es nuestro**: `php -d memory_limit=-1` **no se hereda a los procesos hijo**, así que Infection relanzando `phpunit` vuelve al límite — y 512M tampoco es `-1`. Ese sub-hallazgo sobrevive intacto a la corrección. | Measured on TASK-025 phase 3 + a main-checkout counter-measurement, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18  |

| BL-137 | ⚠ **A task `Status` that under-claims breaks nothing, so nothing catches it — three instances in one night, all in the same direction.** `TASK-020` read *not started* with its census already landed; `TASK-025` read *blocked on two decisions* with both answered; `TASK-027` read *pending review, not merged* while another session had spent hours building on its code in `origin/develop`. [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) authoring rule 7 names this the *realization mirror* and says the error is always in the under-claiming direction — this is why: an over-claim gets contradicted by the tree, an under-claim just sits there. ⛑ **It is mechanically falsifiable, so it should be a check and not an eighth prose rule** — which is what CLAUDE.md itself asks you to establish before adding one. The property: *if a `Status` names a branch or commit, and that commit is an ancestor of `origin/develop`, the `Status` may not say* `not started` / `not merged` / `pending review`. `git merge-base --is-ancestor` answers it, and it is exactly the check performed by hand in `6cad1e1`. All three instances fail it. ⛔ **Do NOT mint a task for this — it already has two possible homes and a third would be the problem it describes.** [TASK-020](tasks/TASK-020-derive-the-deferral-index.md) is literally *derive the index instead of maintaining it* (stopped by decision, not by blocker); [TASK-019](tasks/TASK-019-requirement-traceability-and-per-tier-gates.md) carries traceability and the per-tier gates. ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-18: [TASK-020](tasks/TASK-020-derive-the-deferral-index.md) §9 absorbs it**, because `CLAUDE.md` already assigns two other lintable document rules there (`BL-` id uniqueness, ADR template conformance) — this is a third of the same family, and a new task would be the third place for one problem. ⚑ It also gives that task construable work again **without un-parking** what §4 stopped: a lint generates nothing, so the 34 orphan rows are untouched. ⚠ **And do not record the detection as review**: TASK-027's stale line was found by a session *building on the code*, not reviewing the record — the tree contradicted it. Depending on someone tripping over it does not scale, which is the argument for the check rather than against it. | Observed ×3 on 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-138 | ⛔ **Separating sessions by database does NOT isolate them: `pg_snapshot_xmin` is CLUSTER-wide, so one open transaction in ANY database stalls the other session's relay — and the relay reports success while draining nothing.** [`EventRelay`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/EventLog/EventRelay.php) filters on `sys_transaction_id < pg_snapshot_xmin(pg_current_snapshot())`. **Proven by controlled A/B/C, 2026-08-18**, not inferred: (A) `--filter EventRelayTest` clean → OK 9/9; (B) open one transaction in the **`postgres`** database — which contains none of our tables — with `BEGIN; SELECT txid_current(); pg_sleep(90);` and repeat → **6 of 9 red**, with the signatures `0 is identical to 2`, `-1 is identical to 1`, `actual size 0 matches expected size 1`; (C) kill that backend, repeat → OK 9/9. Occurrence proven independently by sampling `pg_stat_activity` at 1 Hz during a full-suite run: three samples showed `f5sign_test_wt` holding the horizon inside the other session's window; that run ended with 1 failure, the previous quiet-window run was **OK (1573 tests)** on the same tree. ⚠ **We had both concluded that per-database separation protected us. It protects against sharing tables and nothing else.** ⛑ **Where isolation IS real, so the row does not only say what fails** (infra session, 2026-08-18): the `wt-*` lanes bring up **their own `postgres-test` container**, which is a separate *cluster* — so those are genuinely isolated. The rule is **one cluster per session, not one database per session**; anything sharing a cluster is exposed no matter how many databases it is split across. ⚑ **In production this is the relay WORKING**: the low-water mark exists so a row whose transaction has not committed is never relayed ([ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md)). There is no product defect — the defect is a suite that assumes it owns the cluster, and goes red for something that is not the code under test. **Do not "fix" the relay.** ⛑ **The mitigation, which is the half a cause-only row would omit:** `RecipientCorrectionHttpTest` is immune by construction — it **reads the Notification tables rather than Mailpit, and pumps until the deliveries have actually settled** instead of assuming one pump suffices. `Scenario01SingleRecipientHttpTest`, `SigningLoopTest` and `EnvelopeSealLoopTest` want the same treatment. ⚠ Aggravator: Infection is the worst possible neighbour — hundreds of runs with their own transactions over minutes, not one suite. | Controlled A/B/C + 1 Hz sampling, two sessions, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |
| BL-139 | ⚠ **`eu-dss` and MinIO are shared, stateful, and have no per-session equivalent of a separate database — so two acceptance suites contend for them with no isolation available at all.** `Scenario01SingleRecipientHttpTest` seals a real PDF against `eu-dss` and uploads bytes to MinIO. Its flake signature is **distinct** from [BL-138](BACKLOG.md)'s and must not be folded into it: a Mailpit-count assertion (*"exactly the invitation has been delivered so far — actual size 0"*) and, on one intermediate run, a different assertion entirely. ⛔ **Cause NOT confirmed** — unlike BL-138, which has A/B/C. What is established is only that two sessions compete for these containers; nobody has isolated the variable. Recorded honestly rather than attributed, because the tempting attribution was made once tonight and was **wrong**: `eu-dss` was offered as the cause of the relay failures and the controlled experiment found a completely different mechanism in a different component. ⚑ **Two traps, not one. Merging them would get the wrong one "fixed".** ▶ What would settle it: an A/B/C against `eu-dss` under load, the way BL-138 was settled. | Observed across two sessions, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |
| BL-140 | ⚠ **A snapshot is not a measurement of something intermittent — and both sessions asserted it as fact tonight, in opposite directions.** Ephemeral `docker run` containers start and exit back to back, so `docker ps` lands in the gap between two and reports an empty stack that is not empty; `pg_snapshot_xmin` read once returns a clean horizon that is retained a second later. **Both were reported as evidence tonight** — one session declared the stack free on a single `docker ps`, and it was not; the other attributed a flake to its own regression on `n=1` against a transient condition, and got 8-of-8 green on repeat. ⛑ **The rule both arrived at independently: the sample count belongs with the claim.** *"I checked"* is not a measurement of an intermittent property; *"15 samples over 17 s, all clean"* is. ⚠ **And the second form is worse than the first**: a 15-iteration loop with no `sleep` completes in 2 seconds and produces fifteen samples of the same instant — an instantaneous check wearing the costume of a time series. Caught here only by reading the repeated timestamps. ⚑ Same shape as every other defect recorded tonight: a well-formed signal measuring something other than the property it is quoted for. ▶ What would force it: nothing — it is the measurement discipline behind BL-138 and BL-139 and should travel with them. | Both sessions, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-141 | ⚠ **`config/reference.php` means two different things and the written rule only covers one, so the correct habit misfires on exactly the commit where the file matters.** Every instruction in this repo says the same thing about it — *a generated artifact, revert it, not part of the change* — and that is right **when an unrelated `bin/console` run dirtied it**. ⛔ **It is wrong when a component lands**: there the regeneration IS the recorded consequence of the change. Measured 2026-08-18: installing `symfony/rate-limiter` flipped `rate_limiter.enabled`'s documented default from `false` to `true`, one line, and it was reverted three times before another session committed it. ⚑ **The failure is not carelessness, it is that only the FORM was ever written down** (*"reference.php dirty → revert"*) and never the REASON (*"revert when something unrelated to your change dirtied it"*). A rule stated as a gesture gets applied as a gesture. ⚠ Leaving it uncommitted has a concrete cost, which is why it is not merely cosmetic: **any container build in any checkout then leaves a dirty tree with no apparent cause**, and the next person goes looking for what touched it. ▶ **What would force it: the next component install** — and the same misreading, since the habit is now well-drilled. ⚑ **Whether this graduates to a line in `CLAUDE.md`'s repo-specific rules is the user's call**, deliberately not taken here: that file is instruction-surface in the workspace root repo, and its own guidance warns that each rule added makes the others marginally less likely to be read. | Two backend sessions, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-142 | ⚠ **Five signing routes the client already calls are classified by nothing, and the moment one is built it inherits a credential kind by accident.** [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.3 partitions **eight** routes; `f5sign-signer` calls five more that appear in no table — `PATCH …/fields`, `POST …/evidence`, `POST …/complete`, `…/decline` and `…/review-ack`. ⚑ **None exists in this backend today**, which is what makes this cheap: it is fixing a contract before the work rather than reclassifying something live. The signer marks the first three `@backendStatus pending`, and hides the UI of the last two in real mode precisely because they 404 — until 2026-08-18 *Decline* failed after the tap and fell into a generic toast, leaving the signer believing they had declined. ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-18 by the `f5sign-signer` session, recorded here rather than acted on**: all five are operations *after* the session opens and none is meaningful on the emailed link alone, so all five take the **session credential**. The single debatable one is `decline`, and only if a direct decline-from-email link is ever wanted — it does not exist and the signer's middleware opens a session before reaching it. ⛔ **Do not fold this into [TASK-029](tasks/TASK-029-session-credential-and-refresh.md).** That task partitions the routes that exist; widening a decided ADR table inside an implementation slice is the silent edit repo rule 7 forbids. ▶ What would force it: authoring any one of the five. Whichever task does that owns the ADR-0049 §2.3 amendment, and the answer above is the input to it, not a substitute for it. | Signer session + ADR-0049 §2.3 census, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-143 | ⚠ **Every edge refusal on the recipient surface is one undifferentiated 401, so a client cannot tell *renew and retry* from *start over* — and the one it must not get wrong is the common one.** [TASK-029](tasks/TASK-029-session-credential-and-refresh.md) ships the session credential with a fifteen-minute life, so *your credential expired, renew* becomes the **most frequent refusal on the whole API**, and it is indistinguishable at the wire from a tampered credential, a replayed retired one, and a session past its twelve-hour cap. ⚑ **The client-side workaround is real and we told the signer team to use it**: *401 on a session-credential route → renew once, retry once*. That is correct and sufficient; what it is not is *precise* — a client cannot distinguish "renew" from "the session is over, send them back through the access gate", so it will attempt a doomed renewal on every terminal refusal. ⛔ **Not fixed inside TASK-029, and the reason is not scope discipline alone.** [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.3 deliberately makes the shared 401 *"not distinguish the cases"*, so emitting a finer `code` is a decision against a written one, not a gap to fill — and the honest counter-argument is on the record too: the credential is already scoped to one recipient's session, so telling its holder that it expired discloses nothing they do not have. ⚑ Whichever way it goes, **it is additive**: a client branching on the 401 alone keeps working when a `code` appears. ⚠ Note the neighbouring work: another session is adding `TOKEN_EXPIRED` for the signing token under [TASK-028](tasks/TASK-028-opaque-signing-token.md) and found a latent defect while doing it — ~~`ApiExceptionListener`'s `HttpExceptionInterface` arm reads `STATUS_CODES` directly instead of `problemCode()`, so **any `HttpException` implementing `ProblemCoded` has its code silently ignored today**. That defect must be repaired before either code can be emitted, and it is one line.~~ ✅ **DISCHARGED 2026-08-19 — struck rather than deleted, because there genuinely was a period when it was the blocker, and that is a correct record of what changed.** In `develop` that arm now calls `$this->problemCode($throwable, $status)`, and `problemCode()` resolves `ProblemCoded` before falling back to the status default. The branch's own comment carries the date and the worked example: a limiter's 429 published as `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS`, a term the signer app maps to its *invalid link* screen. ⛔ **While this row still READ as broken, anyone about to emit a new problem code believed they inherited a repair they in fact already have** — which is the whole cost of leaving it unstruck. ⚑ **What the discharge buys is EDGE-BORN codes, and it is worth naming the boundary because it is easy to over-claim**: `OtpSendRateLimitedException`'s `OTP_SEND_COOLING_DOWN` is the first code to ride it. It does **not** buy `AUTH_METHOD_NOT_CHOSEN` ([TASK-032](tasks/TASK-032-ordered-auth-method-list.md)) — checked 2026-08-21 rather than assumed: that one is a `ConflictException`, hence a `DomainException`, so it takes the *domain* arm, which honoured `ProblemCoded` all along. The listener's own comment says as much — *"every existing implementor is a `DomainException` and takes the branch above"* — so it was publishable before the fix and depends on it for nothing. ▶ What would force it: the signer app needing to distinguish *renew* from *re-authenticate* in its UI, which is the first thing a real signer notices. ⚑ **Costed by `f5sign-signer`, 2026-08-18, and the cost is a screen that already exists.** Their `/expired` route has copy and a UI and is **unreachable for an expired link** — only `ENVELOPE_EXPIRED` lights it, while `UNAUTHORIZED` falls to `/invalid`. So the signer whose link simply lapsed, the routine no-fault case, is told the link is not valid. ⛔ **And the opaque conversion moved the balance toward distinguishing rather than away**: invalidating is now *deleting*, so an absent digest is indistinguishable from one that never existed — *unknown* has become the COMMON case, and `/invalid` insinuates tampering precisely where there almost never is any. ▶ Their suggestion, offered as a need rather than a design: keep the 401 undifferentiated and put a **non-authoritative** expiry in the emailed link itself (a query parameter, outside the credential). Client compares it to the clock — past means expired, otherwise invalid. An attacker guessing credentials holds no link, so it is no oracle. ⚠ Still a decision against ADR-0049 §2.3's deliberate single 401, so it needs that argument made, not assumed. *(⚑ This paragraph sat in a SIXTH table cell on a five-column table, which every renderer silently drops — so the costing, and the expiry-hint suggestion with it, was invisible in the rendered view from the day it was written. Folded into this cell 2026-08-21, text otherwise unchanged.)* ⛔ **`TOKEN_EXPIRED` is DECIDED, its parent task has LANDED, and the code is emitted by NOTHING — a three-way fork, and this row deliberately chooses none of it.** Verified 2026-08-21: [TASK-028](tasks/TASK-028-opaque-signing-token.md)'s `Status` is `Done`, while `grep -rn 'TOKEN_EXPIRED' src/ config/` returns **nothing** — the term lives only in two `docs/frontend-handoff/` records and in this file. ⚑ **The framing is the `f5sign-signer` session's and it is the non-obvious part: *decided but not built* is the WORST of the three states, because it is the only one in which everyone downstream believes it is resolved.** Their side already branches on `TOKEN_EXPIRED` in `useSignerNavigation` and in its store, so `/expired` lights up with **no commit of theirs** the day we emit it — the fork is entirely ours, which is exactly what makes it easy not to see from here. The three branches: **(i) emit `TOKEN_EXPIRED`** on the expired-link 401; **(ii) adopt option (d)** — the signer-side label for the non-authoritative expiry hint carried on the emailed link, costed in this row on 2026-08-18 above; **(iii) accept** that an expired link reads as *not valid* and close the question. ⛔ **They are not independent, and that dependency is what stops them being decided separately: (d) exists ONLY as a way around `/expired` being unreachable**, so choosing (i) makes (d) unnecessary *for that purpose* — anyone costing (d) on its own merits is costing a workaround for a problem the other branch removes. ⛔ **And this row's standing caveat binds (i) and (ii) alike: each is a decision against [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.3's deliberate single 401**, so that argument has to be made rather than assumed. It is not made here and nothing below decides it. ▶ What would force a choice: any one of the three actually being taken — until then the signer app ships a screen it cannot reach and we ship a code nothing emits. ⚑ **Named a defect rather than a preference by [ADR-0056](adr/ADR-0056-terminal-state-on-pre-credential-routes.md) §2.5, 2026-08-24, which states the criterion once for both**: *an undifferentiated answer is legitimate only where every branch it collapses has the same remedy.* This 401 collapses *renew* and *start over*, which are different remedies, so it falls on the defect side of that line — and the same criterion is what made the sibling case (`AUTH_PASSED` collapsing *recoverable* and *finished*) worth fixing. ⛔ **Deliberately not fixed there.** That one is a **success body** on pre-credential routes and needed no change to any refusal; this is a **refusal** on credential-bearing routes, still needs the `ApiExceptionListener` repair named above, and is still a decision against ADR-0049 §2.3's deliberate single 401. Shared criterion, separate change. | Reported to the signer team 2026-08-18 during TASK-029 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-144 | ⚠ **The session credential belongs to a LINK, and no server-side check can notice when a client forgets that.** The signing session is derived **entirely** from the bearer — there is no session id in the route, the query or the body — so a credential minted for link A, presented after the client has opened link B, returns **A's** session: A's signer, A's sender, A's envelope, A's documents. The request is perfectly well-formed and correctly scoped; there is nothing for the backend to reject. ⚑ **Measured, not hypothesised**: the `f5sign-signer` session hit it on 2026-08-18 building against [TASK-029](tasks/TASK-029-session-credential-and-refresh.md) — their `beginLoad` cleared storage but left the credentials in memory, and the next save rewrote them under the new link's key. Opening a second signing link in the same tab served another person's read-model, and a commit would have signed the wrong envelope. They fixed it on their side the same day. ⛔ **This is recorded as a CONTRACT HAZARD, not a backend defect**: deriving the session from the credential is what [ADR-0028](adr/ADR-0028-recipient-signing-token-auth.md) decided so that a caller can never name someone else's session, and that property is worth more than the hazard costs. The rule every client owes: **a credential belongs to one link; changing link discards both credentials, not just the stored copy.** ▶ What would force a change: a second integrator hitting it. The cheap mitigation nobody has costed is echoing the envelope id in the open response so a client can assert it matches the link it just opened — additive, and it makes the mismatch detectable client-side without weakening the derivation. | f5sign-signer session, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-145 | ⚠ **A redaction control that was only ever exercised against the DEV shape of a secret redacts nothing in production — reported from `f5sign-signer`, and our own shape of the same hole is different and arguably worse.** Their log redactor matched UUID v4, which is the signing token's form in mock/dev; in production the token is an HMAC string, so **nothing was redacted at all**. Fixed on their side, and they flagged it because the pattern travels. ⚑ **Measured here, not assumed**: this backend has no shape-matching redactor to hold the same bug. What it has is [`RequestContextProcessor`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Logging/RequestContextProcessor.php), whose own docblock records that ADR-0009 part 6 **allow-lists** egress fields — and that *"the allow-list has no enforcement behind it… part 6's redaction layer [has] no owner in code (`BL-29`), so the field selection below is the whole of it here."* So the failure mode is not a stale regex but an unowned control: today's safety is that one processor happens to select only non-PII fields, and nothing stops the next stamped field from being a secret. ⛔ **And the trigger is already scheduled**: [TASK-028](tasks/TASK-028-opaque-signing-token.md) changes the signing token's form again, so anything written against today's shape moves under it a second time. ▶ What would force it: giving `BL-29` an owner, or the first log line carrying a credential. This row exists so the signer's finding is not lost as a message in a thread — the lesson transfers even though the code does not. | f5sign-signer session, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-146 | ⚠ **Nothing records that a recipient ACCESSED the envelope — opening a signing session emits no fact at all, and the absence was found by someone worrying about the opposite.** The `f5sign-signer` session asked whether an automatic re-open would pollute the audit trail with access events the signer did not cause. Measured against `a7c22a9`: `SigningSession::start()` records **zero** domain events, `mintCredential()` zero, and neither `startIfAbsent()` nor `mintCredentialFor()` drains to the event log — `drainPendingEvents()` is reached only from `save()`, the commit path. So the answer was reassuring and the finding is not: **there is no recipient-access fact to pollute.** ⚑ Not introduced by [TASK-029](tasks/TASK-029-session-credential-and-refresh.md) — the ceremony never recorded one — but that task is what made someone look. ⛔ For an eIDAS product *"no access noise"* and *"no record of access"* are the same absence read from two directions, and only one reading is comforting. A signature's evidence packet plausibly wants *when the recipient first opened the envelope*, and today it cannot be reconstructed from the log. ▶ What would force it: [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) deciding what the evidence packet must contain — **written into that task's §7 as open decision 12**, which is the home of truth; this row only indexes it. ⚠ The cost is asymmetric: an access fact is cheap to start emitting now and **impossible to backfill**, so deferring it is not neutral the way deferring a projection is. | Question from f5sign-signer, measured 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |

| BL-147 | ⛔ **Pulling any branch that registered a service makes PHPStan report that service as *"not registered in the container"* — a confident, file-and-line-precise error about a world that no longer exists, and it names the OTHER person's files.** `phpstan.dist.neon`'s `symfony.containerXmlPath` reads `var/cache/dev/App_KernelDevDebugContainer.xml`, and `/var/` is **gitignored** — so the artifact never travels with a commit and is stale by construction for whoever pulls. ⚑ **Three independent hits on 2026-08-18**, which is what moves this from a documented trap to a row: two subagents during [TASK-029](tasks/TASK-029-session-credential-and-refresh.md), and the [TASK-028](tasks/TASK-028-opaque-signing-token.md) session immediately after rebasing onto it — **7 errors, all in TASK-029's files, none in its own**, while the suite was green. ⚠ **The damage is in how it reads, not in what it costs to fix.** `make sf cmd="cache:warmup"` clears it in seconds; the expensive part is that the message is specific and plausible, so the natural reading is *"the branch I just pulled is broken"* — and the person best placed to disprove that is the one who did not write the code. ▶ **What would force it: making it mechanical rather than remembered** — warm the container in the `phpstan` composer script, or have the analysis fail loudly when the dump predates `config/services.yaml` instead of reporting a missing service. Both are cheap; neither exists. ⚑ The same finding is written up for the infra side in [`docs/infra-handoff/2026-08-18-worktree-test-isolation.md`](infra-handoff/2026-08-18-worktree-test-isolation.md); this row exists because a backend session hitting the red reads BACKLOG, not an infra handoff. | Three sessions, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |
| BL-153 | ⚑ **A census written by someone who knows the system, instead of pulled from the system — three instances on 2026-08-18, in three sessions, none caught by a gate.** (1) `init-app-role.sh`'s role comment said the custodian owned *"BOTH"* funnels with `SELECT + INSERT` "and nothing else anywhere"; there were three funnels, three privileges and a schema `USAGE`. (2) That repo's *"today it owns nothing, a `DROP ROLE` suffices"* was measured **before** the migration it described and asserted as present tense. (3) [TASK-028](tasks/TASK-028-opaque-signing-token.md) §2 named **two** minting paths; there were three, and `NotifyCorrectedRecipientUseCase` went on emitting the retired encoding after the cutover — making *"every credential this system issues is opaque"* false in an ADR for several commits. ⛔ **Each list was correct when written and each was written from knowledge of the domain rather than asked of the code.** ▶ **Not proposed as an eighth authoring rule**, and the reason is [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md)'s own instruction: check whether it can be a check first. Partly it can — [`BL-151`](BACKLOG.md) turned one of these censuses into a test that enumerates `pg_proc` instead of naming three functions, which is the shape to copy. Partly it cannot: a comment in another repo has no gate, and neither does a task plan. ⚑ **For the part that cannot, the technique is mechanical rather than prose**: the third minting path was found by **deleting the class and reading the callers the compiler named**, not by re-reading the plan. Deleting the thing is how you ask the code for its own census. ⚠ This row is the dated measurement CLAUDE.md's authoring-rules footer asks for; it is closest to rule 5 aimed at prose rather than at code predicates, which that footer predicts is the usual outcome. | Three sessions, 2026-08-18 | Open | 2026-08-18 |
| BL-154 | **A RabbitMQ *policy* is the escape hatch for setting `x-dead-letter-exchange` on the four event queues without deleting them, and it is unwritten anywhere an operator would look.** [TASK-026](tasks/TASK-026-event-path-silent-drops.md) §4 D6 declared the argument in `f5sign-infra`'s `definitions.json`, which RabbitMQ applies to a queue only at declaration — so on a long-lived environment the deployed route is delete-and-recreate, and that is unacceptable the moment a queue holds messages nobody may drop. A policy sets the same argument on live queues. ⛔ **Do not verify this from the import log**: RabbitMQ 4.0's `import_definitions` keeps a pre-existing queue's old arguments, reports success and logs nothing — the check that reads the property is `make -C ../f5sign-infra rabbit-check-dlx`, and that silent success is what made the task's own deployment step fail quietly. ▶ **What would force it:** the first environment where recreating `queue.*.events` is not an option. ⚠ The change lands in `f5sign-infra`, not here; this row exists because its reader is an operator under incident pressure who has no reason to open a backend task. | [TASK-026](tasks/TASK-026-event-path-silent-drops.md) §6.3 | Folded | 2026-08-18 (indexed 2026-08-19) |

| BL-155 | **Four private copies of *"seed the smallest sent envelope"* live beside [`SeedsSentEnvelopes`](../tests/F5Sign/Support/SeedsSentEnvelopes.php), and two of them are that trait's shape exactly.** The trait was extracted while repairing the three Session fixtures [TASK-021](tasks/TASK-021-two-gate-auth-model-and-access-gate.md) turned red — before it, adding three more copies would have made seven. ⚑ **The two that are the same shape** are `LeakedLinkRefusedHttpTest` and `SessionCredentialTenantScopeTest` (the second appends a per-tenant marker to the PDF bytes, which the trait would need a parameter for). ⚠ **Two are NOT and must not be folded in**: `SigningLoopTest` grants sight instead of placing a signature, and `SigningReadEndpointsHttpTest` seeds two documents, two recipients and five fields on purpose — flattening either into the minimal shape would delete what its assertions are about. ⛔ **The cost of leaving it is the one the repo's authoring rule 3 names**: where two siblings differ, the next author cannot tell a decision from residue, and here there are five siblings. ▶ What would force it: a sixth seed, or a change to the pre-send invariants that has to be made in five places. Deliberately not done in the changeset that introduced the trait — that changeset's subject was three red fixtures, and rewriting green tests alongside them buys nothing a reviewer can check. | [`tests/F5Sign/Support/SeedsSentEnvelopes.php`](../tests/F5Sign/Support/SeedsSentEnvelopes.php) | Open | 2026-08-19 |
| BL-156 | ⛔ **`f5sign-signer`'s idle-recovery path stops working for gated envelopes, and it stops working with a 200.** They built *renewal refused → re-open with the emailed link → carry on*, which was correct while [TASK-029](tasks/TASK-029-session-credential-and-refresh.md) minted the credential pair **unconditionally** at every session open. [TASK-021](tasks/TASK-021-two-gate-auth-model-and-access-gate.md) makes that mint conditional on the access gate being open — it has to, since a recipient who returns to their link and collects a fresh credential has bypassed the gate ([ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.3) — so behind a declared gate the re-open answers **200** with `status: "AUTH_PENDING"` and **no** `access_credential` / `refresh_credential` / `expires_at` members at all. ⚑ **The members are absent rather than null on purpose**, so a client branching on presence cannot read "withheld" as "expired"; the remedy is to route the credential's ABSENCE to the auth screen instead of assuming one is there. ⛔ **This row said `status: "AUTH_PENDING"` and named that status as the discriminant until 2026-08-19, and both halves were wrong** — the controller computed `status` from the sender's declaration rather than from the session, so a recipient who had already passed the gate was told `AUTH_PENDING` on every later open; routing on it sent them to an auth screen whose descriptor answered `gate: null`, and the signer looped back to the index on a blank page. Fixed with the status now read off the session row, and `access_grant_expires_at` added beside it; see [`docs/frontend-handoff/2026-08-19-session-status-and-grant-expiry.md`](frontend-handoff/2026-08-19-session-status-and-grant-expiry.md). ⚠ **What makes this a row rather than a note in a diff: neutral envelopes are unaffected**, and every envelope authored before this slice is neutral (the migration backfills the explicit `(OPEN, NONE)` pair), so the signer's current test envelopes keep working and manual testing will not surface it. It bites the day someone authors the first `ACCESS_CODE` envelope. ▶ What would force it: the signer PR of this task's release window. Told to them in [`docs/frontend-handoff/TASK-021-two-gate-signer-auth.md`](frontend-handoff/TASK-021-two-gate-signer-auth.md), which is the home of the client-side instruction; this row exists so the break is findable from the backend side too, since the handoff's `Status` field is hand-maintained and nobody owns flipping it. ⚠ **Minted as `BL-154` on `feat/two-gate-signer-auth` and renumbered to `BL-156` when that branch merged `develop` on 2026-08-19** — `develop` had meanwhile minted its own `BL-154` (the RabbitMQ policy row, TASK-026). Nothing outside this file cited the old number, which is the only reason renumbering was available: ids are stable cross-document anchors and moving one that is cited is not an option. The lesson is the derivation, not the number — a free id has to be swept across **every** branch at the moment it is written, not read off the branch you are standing on. | [TASK-021](tasks/TASK-021-two-gate-auth-model-and-access-gate.md) §7 + [`docs/frontend-handoff/TASK-021-two-gate-signer-auth.md`](frontend-handoff/TASK-021-two-gate-signer-auth.md) | Folded | 2026-08-18 (two-gate slice, cross-repo handoff) |

| BL-159 | ⚠ **A verified signing-gate proof is transferable** — and, until 2026-08-26, never expired either. `SigningSession::consumeSigningChallengeFor()` accepts any challenge that is `isVerifiedAndUnspent()` and `authorises($set)` — it does **not** call `isLive()`, so a verified challenge outlives its own `expires_at`, and it is bound to the *session* rather than to whoever answered it. Under `(access: OPEN, signing: ACCESS_CODE)` — a configuration this slice supports and tests — party A proves the code and party B commits. ⚑ **The counterargument is real and does not fully dispose of it**: under a neutral access gate [ADR-0028](adr/ADR-0028-recipient-signing-token-auth.md) already makes possession of the link the whole of identity, so A and B are both admitted anyway and nothing was *stolen*. What is lost is narrower and is what [TASK-021](tasks/TASK-021-two-gate-auth-model-and-access-gate.md) §4 claims the signing gate buys — binding **intent to this act by the party who proved it**. Behind a declared access gate the exposure is bounded by the credential, which B does not hold. ⛔ **BOTH HALVES ARE NOW LIVE — updated 2026-08-19.** The escape clause this row leaned on has expired: [TASK-022](tasks/TASK-022-delivered-codes-over-email.md) gives a delivered code a 15-minute TTL, so a challenge now *has* a lifetime to outlive, and `consumeSigningChallengeFor()` still never calls `isLive()`. ⚠ The exposure is narrower than the headline suggests and worth stating so nobody over-reacts: it takes a **signing** gate declaring a delivered method to reach the expiry half, and the transfer half is unchanged. ▶ **What it needs**: `consumeSigningChallengeFor()` to check liveness alongside `isVerifiedAndUnspent()`. Deliberately NOT fixed in TASK-022 — that slice's scope is issuance and delivery, and narrowing a consumption guard changes the act binding, which wants its own bar rather than riding along in a slice about codes. ✅ **The EXPIRY half is discharged 2026-08-26** on `fix/signing-challenge-expiry-on-consume`: `consumeSigningChallengeFor()` now refuses a proof past its own `expires_at` with `AUTH_CHALLENGE_EXPIRED`, and passing the ACCESS gate again retires a retained signing proof outright (a product decision taken the same day, over the narrower alternative of letting the TTL alone bound it). Both guards were sabotage-checked. ⚑ **What made it urgent was not this row**: the `f5sign-signer` session hit it empirically on a live stack — verified 07:40:37, `expires_at` 07:55:28, consumed 09:10:07 — hours before this deferral was found again. An audit finding parked for a week and a field report of the same defect are the same fact arriving twice; the second one is what got it fixed, which is worth knowing about how this list actually gets read. ⛔ **The TRANSFER half stays OPEN and this row stays open for it**: the proof is still bound to the *session* rather than to whoever answered, so under `(access: OPEN, signing: ACCESS_CODE)` party A can still prove and party B commit within the TTL. The cascade narrows the window; it does not close it, because B need not re-pass ACCESS to use a session A already opened. ⚑ **2026-10-05: [ADR-0075](adr/ADR-0075-the-signing-gate-is-answered-on-the-act.md) (`Proposed`) would close the transfer half**: the signing code travels on the act (the commit, the decline and the machine signature route), which verifies and consumes it in one unit of work, so no proof waits for a second holder to spend. [TASK-052](tasks/TASK-052-the-signing-gate-is-answered-on-the-act.md) realizes it and closes this row when it lands. ADR-0075's first cut (2026-10-01) only narrowed it, by binding the waiting proof to an act. ✅ **Transfer half closed 2026-10-06 on `fix/signing-gate-on-the-act`** (not pushed, not merged): the commit, the decline and the machine signature route verify and consume the code in one unit of work, and the state in which a proof waits no longer exists. The row turns `Closed` when that branch lands. | [TASK-021](tasks/TASK-021-two-gate-auth-model-and-access-gate.md) §9 | Open until `fix/signing-gate-on-the-act` is merged (transfer half closed on it; expiry half discharged 2026-08-26) | 2026-08-19 (security audit) |
| BL-160 | ⚠ **A delivered one-time code lands at rest, unenciphered, in `notification.notification.variables`, and no open row covered it.** Notification's create-then-transmit split persists the template slots and re-reads them at transmission to render, so the plaintext has to be written down. ⛔ **This row exists because the deferral pointed somewhere that no longer holds it**: [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.8 and TASK-022 §2 both defer it to `BL-113`, which is **Resolved** and whose fix is link-specific — `Notification::forgetSecretsOnceSettled()` drops the `link` variable — and a deferral pointing at a closed row is a deferral nobody will find. ⛑ **Partly mitigated already**: TASK-022 added `code` to `SECRET_BEARING_VARIABLES`, so the same mechanism now drops it once **every** delivery is terminal, and these codes are dead within 15 minutes anyway. ▶ What is left is the window between creation and settlement — the same residue BL-113 accepted for the invitation link, and materially smaller here because the secret expires. Closing it properly means the field cipher [ADR-0033](adr/ADR-0033-pii-field-encryption-at-rest.md) proposes and nothing calls. | [TASK-022](tasks/TASK-022-delivered-codes-over-email.md) §2 | Open | 2026-08-19 |
| BL-161 | ⚠ **`LogNotificationChannel::render()` would write a delivered secret into a log line, and it is the empty-exempted-set shape rather than a live defect.** It folds `$variables->toArray()` into its `summary` part and logs it, so any purpose routed to `LOG` puts every template variable — the OTP `code`, and the invitation's `link` before it — into the application log. ⛔ **Unreachable today for one reason only**: `DefaultChannelSelector` routes every purpose to `EMAIL` and nothing to `LOG`, so the set that would leak is empty and green is the expected signal either way (the repo's authoring rule 5). The day a preference-aware selector or a debug deployment sends anything to `LOG`, it leaks — and whoever makes that change has no reason to know this predicate exists. ▶ **What it needs**: that channel to redact `Notification::SECRET_BEARING_VARIABLES` before composing its summary, which is the same list TASK-022 extended for the aggregate. ⚑ Found while adding `code` to that list — i.e. by someone standing in exactly the right place, which is not a repeatable way to find it. | [TASK-022](tasks/TASK-022-delivered-codes-over-email.md) §2 | Open | 2026-08-19 |
| BL-162 | ⚠ **A sender can declare a DELIVERED auth method on a recipient with no destination, and nothing tells them until a signer is already stuck.** `AddRecipientUseCase::guardDeclaredMethodsAreAvailable()` checks that the *deployment* can execute the method; nothing checks that *this recipient* can receive it. So `OTP_EMAIL` with `email: null` authors cleanly, sends cleanly, and fails only when the signer asks for a code — a **409 `AUTH_NO_DESTINATION`** on route 4, which is signer-facing and arrives long after the sender has moved on. ⚑ **Made reachable by [TASK-022](tasks/TASK-022-delivered-codes-over-email.md)**: before it, no delivered method was declarable at all, so the combination could not be authored. ⛔ **Narrower than it first looks, and worth stating so nobody over-scopes the fix**: a recipient with no email never receives an invitation either (`NotifyActivatedStepRecipientsUseCase` skips them, fail-closed), so they cannot open a session by the ordinary route.
