It bites where the token is delivered out of band — the sender mints one through `POST /api/v1/signing-tokens` and hands it over by another channel — which is a supported flow, not an abuse. ▶ **What it needs**: an authoring-time refusal pairing a delivered method with the contact that method needs, in the same guard that already refuses an unwired method, so the sender is told at the moment they can still fix it. ⚠ The check is per-method rather than per-recipient — `OTP_SMS` will want a phone, which does not exist on the recipient row yet ([TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md)), so building it as *"requires an email"* would have to be rewritten immediately. ⚑ **Answered 2026-08-19**: [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) §7 D4 takes the guard **per method** rather than per channel — each delivered method declares the destination it needs — precisely so it closes this row instead of half of it. | [TASK-022](tasks/TASK-022-delivered-codes-over-email.md); closed by [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) §7 D4 | Landed | 2026-08-19 |
| BL-163 | **`POST /envelopes/{id}/recipients` answers 422 or 404 depending on which check fails first, and the pair reads as *"the envelope is fine"* when it is not.** Payload validation runs before the step lookup, so a recipient posted to an envelope with **no steps** gets `404 Step not found … ordinal=1` — while the *same* request carrying an unavailable method gets a clean `422 AUTH_METHOD_UNAVAILABLE` from the same endpoint. A caller probing with the second shape concludes the envelope is healthy; the first then looks like a routing bug. ⚑ **Field signal rather than a hypothesis: two independent consumers — the signer app and the dashboard — hit it on the same day, each losing time on it, and both reported the same confusion unprompted.** ⚠ Not a defect in either status: each is individually correct, and nothing here is wrong per request. What is missing is that the *ordering* is invisible, so the endpoint's answer depends on a sequence the caller cannot see. ▶ Options, none obviously right: resolve the step before validating the payload (makes 404 win consistently, at the cost of validating later); or say which precondition failed in the 404's `detail` — it already names the ordinal, so the cheap fix may be naming that the envelope has **no steps at all** rather than that this one is missing. ⛔ Pre-existing; not introduced by [TASK-022](tasks/TASK-022-delivered-codes-over-email.md), which is only how it got noticed. | Field reports from `f5sign-signer` + `factor5-dashboard`, 2026-08-19 | Open | 2026-08-19 |
| BL-164 | ⚠ **Sixteen copy strings assert a TTL none of them can read.** ⚑ **Recounted 2026-08-25, still NOT closed — the number DOUBLED and the coverage followed it, which is the useful half.** The [gate split](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) made two purposes of `AUTH_OTP`, so email now states the figure in 3 places × 2 locales × 2 purposes = **12** strings and SMS in **4**. ⛑ **Both pairing tests were widened in the same changeset and were widened by PREDICATE, not by hand**: each iterates `NotificationPurpose::namesItsOwnChannel()`, so the new purpose arrived inside the guard rather than beside it, and a third delivered-code purpose will too. ⚠ **That is exactly what this row asks for and it still does not close it** — the two known instances are held tighter than before, and the *generic* detector is as absent as it was. ⚑ **Updated 2026-08-20, NOT closed**: this row read *"six … and a seventh and eighth are about to join them"*, and [TASK-030](tasks/TASK-030-sms-delivery-channel.md) landed the seventh and eighth, so the future tense was made true by the branch that is correcting it (the repo's authoring rule 7). **What changed is the count and the coverage; what has not changed is why the row is open.** Both channels now carry a pairing test — `AuthOtpEmailCopyTest::the_stated_expiry_is_the_ttl_the_ceremony_actually_enforces` and `SmsCopyTest::the_stated_expiry_is_the_ttl_the_ceremony_actually_enforces_for_sms`, each reading its own `SignerAuthPolicy` constant and reddening naming the number that moved — so the two known instances are held. **The generic detector is not built**, which is the deferral: a ninth string in a third channel, or a copy string stating a duration nobody thought to pair, is caught by nothing. ⚠ The SMS pairing is also weaker than it looks — it asserts the *stated* minutes match the constant, not that a slot is rendered from it, so copy re-worded to say *"cinco minutos"* in words would pass while being unpaired. [`EmailNotificationChannel`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Infrastructure/Channel/EmailNotificationChannel.php)'s `AUTH_OTP` copy states *15 minutes* in **three** places per locale — body, preheader and note, so six strings — while the value lives in [`SignerAuthPolicy::OTP_EMAIL_CHALLENGE_TTL_SECONDS`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Auth/SignerAuthPolicy.php) (`900`). Nothing pairs them: changing the constant leaves the copy asserting a duration that is no longer true, to the person who is watching the clock on it. ⚑ **The repo already knows this shape and got it right once**: the invitation copy's own docblock says it deliberately states no duration, because *"copy asserting a duration it cannot read is copy that will one day be wrong"* — the OTP copy is the same file taking the opposite choice, with no note saying why. It **doubled with the SMS channel on 2026-08-20**: [TASK-030](tasks/TASK-030-sms-delivery-channel.md) §3's copy says *5 minutos* / *5 minutes* against `OTP_SMS_CHALLENGE_TTL_SECONDS` (`300`) — one string per locale rather than three, because an SMS has a lone `body` and no preheader or note to repeat it in. ⛔ The two numbers differing is **correct** — [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.8 sets the TTL per method — so a fix must not "unify" them; what is missing is a pairing, either a slot rendered from the policy or a test that reddens when a constant and its copy disagree. ▶ Filed from [TASK-030](tasks/TASK-030-sms-delivery-channel.md) §5, which repeats the trade deliberately for consistency rather than re-arguing it. Pre-existing; the SMS channel is only how it got counted. | [TASK-030](tasks/TASK-030-sms-delivery-channel.md) copy authoring, 2026-08-19 | Open | 2026-08-19 |
| BL-165 | ⚠ **A docblock that counts its own callers or siblings goes stale by an edit in another file, and the offending diff is EMPTY where the claim lives.** Fired **three times in one session** on 2026-08-19: [`OpenGateResolver`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/OpenGateResolver.php) said *"there are two readers"* from the day it was written until there were four (the issuance path joined with [TASK-022](tasks/TASK-022-delivered-codes-over-email.md) and did not update it); [`StartSessionController::ACCESS_CREDENTIAL_LIFE`](../src/F5Sign/Session/UI/Http/StartSessionController.php) said *"the two are the same number"* while a third copy sat on the gate-passing route; and a frontend handoff asserted a six-character code length that **no signer-facing surface publishes**, against two constants that coincide at 6 today (`SignerAuthPolicy::GENERATED_CODE_DIGITS`, a length, and `MIN_SENDER_SUPPLIED_CODE_LENGTH`, a floor a national id clears at nine). ⛔ **No gate here can see any of it**, and authoring rule 1's own remedies do not fire: adding a caller retires no term to sweep for and does not change the cited symbol either — only *how many things use it* — so there is nothing to grep for and the culprit's diff never touches the file carrying the claim. ⚑ **Recorded as a candidate CHECK rather than an eighth authoring rule**, per `CLAUDE.md`'s own bar (*"before proposing an eighth rule, check whether it can be a check instead"*, and the measured finding that the largest defect bucket is *"the instruction already existed and was missed"*). The shape looks mechanical: a docblock containing a bare count word — *the two* · *both* · *two readers* · *the pair* — within a few lines of a symbol whose name `rg` finds in more files than the count claims. ▶ What would force it: the next occurrence, which on this rate is days away. Sibling of [TASK-020](tasks/TASK-020-derive-the-deferral-index.md), which already owns the *"this prose rule is lintable"* bucket. ⚠ The three instances above are all **fixed**; what is deferred is the detector, not the defects. ⚑ **The forcing function fired, 2026-08-28 — [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md) supplied at least three more, and with them the generalisation this row was missing.** The three: [`DeclaredMethodDestinations`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Application/Service/DeclaredMethodDestinations.php) said *"two callers"* where that branch made it three — and called itself *"the authoring guard"* while now running at claim time too, which is the reading that would let somebody decide the claim path may skip it; `MintSigningTokenController`'s **published** `#[OA\*]` said `code` **is** `ENVELOPE_NOT_SENT` while a second refusal code landed on the same route; and `RecipientNotCorrectableException` said *"the two"* where a third factory landed. All three fixed there. ⛔ **The generalisation, and it is what makes this row bigger than a count-word detector: the unit that needs an owner is not the FILE that changes, it is the CLAIM that changes — and claims live in files nobody is editing.** That is why both sweeps this repo already has are structurally blind to them: authoring rule 1's retired-term sweep needs a term to grep for and these retire none, and a per-worker file allowlist never opens the file, because **the offending diff is empty exactly where the claim lives**. The sweep that does find them is per-file and cheap: **re-read the whole prose block, not the lines that changed**, on every file the change gives a new caller, a new sibling, a new code or a new state — plus a separate `rg 'BL-NNN'` / `rg 'ADR-NNNN'` pass read for **tense**, since a row or a record closing elsewhere rots every sentence that named it. ⚠ **Two shapes generalise past the count word** and a detector built only on it would miss both: a published enum that *enumerates today's members*, and a **tense** claim about another record's status. | [TASK-020](tasks/TASK-020-derive-the-deferral-index.md) + the repo's authoring rule 1 | Open | 2026-08-19 (three occurrences in one session: two-gate auth follow-ups) · re-fired 2026-08-28 (TASK-036: three more, plus the generalisation) |
| BL-166 | ⚠ **There is no usage table anywhere in the schema, so nothing counts what the platform spends.** [`UsageMeterInterface`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Billing/UsageMeterInterface.php) has exactly one implementation, [`NullUsageMeter`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Billing/NullUsageMeter.php), which records nothing. What is missing is not an adapter: it is a table, its RLS contract, its migration, its append-only discipline, its idempotency constraint and its metric vocabulary — and **no ADR governs any of it**. ⚑ **Deferred deliberately on 2026-08-19** ([TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) §7 D1), against that record's own written argument that *"a metering bar asserted against a double proves the caller, not the charge, and the charge is the part that reaches an invoice"*. That record's §8 spend-alerting follow-up is the whole of the compensating control until this table exists. ▶ **Forced by** the first of: a tenant asking what they owe, or the first Twilio invoice that needs reconciling against our own records. ⚑ **A second caller landed 2026-08-20 and it forced a decision this row now owns.** [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) meters a delivered-code issuance (`DELIVERED_CODE_ISSUED`, keyed on the challenge id) from [`IssueAuthChallengeUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/IssueAuthChallengeUseCase.php) — and **after the commit rather than inside the transaction**, which is a deviation from `envelope-domain-model.md` §invariant 15 and `signing-session-domain-model.md` §14, both of which ask for metering *inside the same transaction as the metered state change*. ⛔ **The reason is structural rather than negligent**: that use case is deliberately **off the command bus** (a person is waiting on the answer, and the write must commit before the delivery is requested), so there is no ambient `DbalTransactionMiddleware` transaction to enrol in — the only in-transaction seam is `DbalSigningSessionRepository::issueAuthChallenge()`, which would put a Foundation billing port inside Session Infrastructure and move the *which metric, how much* decision out of the Application layer, against [ADR-0010](adr/ADR-0010-command-bus-middleware-spine.md) rule 3. ⛑ **What ordering still buys**: the repository throws on rollback, so a rolled-back issuance never reaches the meter call and leaves no record — the property TASK-023 §5 bar 2 actually asserts. What it does not buy is atomicity: a commit followed by a crash before that line loses the charge, which **under-bills**. ▶ **So this row inherits the question**: whoever builds the usage table decides whether that window is acceptable or whether the seam has to move, and today it is unobservable in either direction because `NullUsageMeter` records nothing. | [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) §7 D1 | Open | 2026-08-19 |
| BL-167 | ⚠ **`deptrac.yaml`'s `Vendor` collector reads like an allowlist and is not one, so the framework-independence rule holds only for the vendors somebody remembered to list.** The collector matches `^(Symfony|Doctrine|Composer|Psr|Twig|Monolog|PhpStan|Webmozart|PHPUnit|DAMA|Ramsey|Evenement|React|AsyncAws|OpenApi)\\` — and a class matching **no** layer is *uncovered*, which deptrac permits silently. ⚑ **Proven by something already in the tree, not by a hypothetical**: `Foundation/Http/SessionCredentialCodec` imports `Lcobucci\JWT`, which is absent from that regex, and `composer arch` reports **zero** violations. So today nothing would stop a **Domain** class importing an unlisted third-party library, while the same class importing Symfony would be caught — the guard is real for listed vendors and absent for the rest. ⛔ This is the empty-exempted-set shape (the repo's authoring rule 5) applied to a *gate* rather than to code: the set it silently exempts is "every vendor nobody has added yet". ⚑ **Measured 2026-08-19, and the numbers make this cheap rather than daunting**: `composer arch` reports `Violations 0` · **`Uncovered 15`** · `Allowed 4898`, and all **fifteen** are `Lcobucci\JWT\*` reached from a single class, `Foundation\Http\SessionCredentialCodec`. So the silently-exempted set today is **one library in one file**, and it is harmless *in fact* — Foundation may import Vendor, so listing it changes no verdict. **The whole risk is prospective**: the next unlisted library, in any layer including Domain — where the correct verdict would be a **violation** — passes exactly the same way, and exactly as green. ⛔ **So the defect is not the fifteen, it is that the mechanism cannot distinguish *"there is nothing to object to"* from *"I did not look"*.** Both print zero violations. ▶ **What it needs, and it is now two small steps rather than a triage project**: add the missing prefix to the collector so the count reaches zero, then **gate on the number that is already printed on every run** — fail when `Uncovered > 0`, or freeze it with a baseline the way PHPStan's is frozen. ⚑ **That the count is already in the output is the point**: nothing needed measuring, it needed *reading*. A sibling session cited `Violations 0` from a run whose very next line said `Uncovered 15` — the green was read and the number beside it was not, which is the repo's authoring rule 4's *"name where the property lives and check the harness goes there"* failing while a green run was being cited. A gate removes the need to remember. ⚑ Surfaced from [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md), which adds `giggsey/libphonenumber-for-php-lite` and found that the check which appears to govern that does not. ⚑ **A second dated reading, 2026-08-20 — added as a new line rather than by refreshing the one above, which is a stamp and not a live counter.** [TASK-030](tasks/TASK-030-sms-delivery-channel.md) added an HTTP adapter and a whole `Infrastructure/Sms/` tree: `composer arch` reports `Violations 0` · **`Uncovered 15`** · `Allowed 4898 → 4973`. The uncovered count **did not move**, which is the row's own prediction holding rather than new evidence — `symfony/http-client` is inside the collector's regex, so nothing new was silently exempted. It is a confirmation that the mechanism behaves as described, not that it is safe: the next unlisted library still passes exactly as green. ⚑ **A third dated reading, 2026-08-20, and this one MOVED the number — added as a new line rather than by refreshing the two above, which are stamps and not live counters.** [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) added `giggsey/libphonenumber-for-php-lite` behind [`LibPhoneNumberParser`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Infrastructure/Phone/LibPhoneNumberParser.php): `composer arch` reports `Violations 0` · **`Uncovered 15 → 36`** · `Allowed 4973 → 5061`. The twenty-one new uncovered classes are all `libphonenumber\*` reached from that one adapter. ⛔ **This is the row's prediction arriving with a value attached, not new evidence**: the placement is *correct* — `EnvelopeInfrastructure` is granted `Vendor`, so a covered collector would have said `Allowed` — and it passes for the wrong reason, because the prefix matches no collector at all. ⚠ **The counterfactual is what makes it worth a line**: the same import in `Envelope/Contract/Type/PhoneNumber` — where the value object lives, and where a *"keep the library behind the VO"* instinct would naturally put it — is a **violation** (`EnvelopeContract` gets `Kernel` + `Foundation` only) and would have printed exactly the same `Violations 0`. The placement was argued from `deptrac.yaml` by hand and recorded in that class's docblock, because the gate could not have told the difference. | [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) dependency review, 2026-08-19 | Open | 2026-08-19 |
| BL-168 | ⚠ **Correcting a recipient's contact does not kill the code already sent to the old one, so a delivered secret stays answerable at a destination the sender has just disowned.** [`Recipient::correctContact()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Entity/Recipient.php) sets the new email, resets the outcome to `PENDING` and increments the correction counter — and touches **nothing** in Session. Verified by both ends: the only two callers of `AuthChallenge::invalidate()` are inside [`SigningSession`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Domain/Aggregate/SigningSession.php), minting a replacement challenge and expiring one, and [`RecipientCorrected`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Event/RecipientCorrected.php)'s **only** subscriber is Notification's `OnRecipientCorrected`, which re-notifies. So the live challenge keeps its verifier hash and stays answerable for the rest of its TTL — `OTP_EMAIL_CHALLENGE_TTL_SECONDS = 900`, fifteen minutes. ⛔ **The severity is entirely in WHY the correction happened, and the two cases are indistinguishable from the row**: a typo (`juan@empersa.com` → `juan@empresa.com`) sent the code to nobody and is harmless; the wrong colleague's address sent a valid code, plus the invitation link that went to the same place, to a **third party who can still use both**. The next issuance goes to the corrected address, which is right and is also what makes this easy to miss — the visible behaviour is entirely correct. ▶ **What it needs, and the mechanism already exists rather than needing invention**: the repo solved *"the sender does something that must invalidate Session state"* for lockouts with a monotonic counter on the recipient — `resetAccessCode()` and `unlockAuth()` both do `++$this->authGeneration`, and [`RecipientAuthContext`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/RecipientAuthContext.php) already carries it and is re-read fresh at every verification precisely so a lock goes inert with no cross-context write. Three pieces: `correctContact()` raises the generation (one line); the challenge records the generation it was issued under (a column and its migration — the only new thing); verification refuses a challenge whose generation has fallen behind. ⚑ **Accept the coupling deliberately**: reusing that counter means a correction also clears an existing hard lock, which is correct — if the link reached a third party who burned the attempt budget, the real recipient should not inherit their lockout — and it cannot become a lever, since `MAX_CORRECTIONS = 3` and a generation can only ever **remove** (inert locks, dead challenges), never grant. ⛔ Do **not** store the destination on the challenge to compare it instead: [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.6 keeps addresses off that row, and a hashed address in an append-only store reopens a closed debate. ⚑ Adjacent but independent: [ADR-0054](adr/ADR-0054-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §2.11 makes this exposure *detectable* before a send by publishing the masked destinations, which is not a fix. | Design session on the ordered method list, 2026-08-19 → 2026-08-20; mechanism verified against `Recipient`, `SigningSession` and the `RecipientCorrected` subscriber set | Open | 2026-08-20 |
| BL-169 | ⛔ **The Twilio sandbox tier is built and has never been executed, so the hermetic tier's canned responses are a reading of the vendor's documentation rather than a record of its wire format.** [TASK-030](tasks/TASK-030-sms-delivery-channel.md) §6 wired that tier for exactly one stated reason — *"a fake that made up the error shape would lock in our guess"* — and landing without running it leaves that reason unpaid. ⚑ **What IS held**: `TwilioSmsTransportTest` proves the §3 classification is a correct function of `(status, code)`, and its sabotage (moving `21408`/`21606` into the permanent set) reddens naming both codes — so the *taxonomy* is tested. What is untested is the **premise**: that Twilio's error body really carries `code` as an integer, `message` as a string, and a 201 really carries `sid`. ⚠ One of those was already hedged during implementation — `is_int` was widened to accept a numeric string, because a strict read would have sent `"21211"` to the retry side and re-posted a permanently dead number on every ladder attempt for ever, on a channel that charges per attempt. That hedge is a guess about a guess. ⛔ `TwilioSandboxTest` does **not** pretend otherwise: it is excluded from the default suite by group and **fails loud** with its precondition written out (`rg -n 'markTestSkipped' tests/` still returns nothing repo-wide). ▶ **Forced by** the first Twilio account: it needs the free tier and nothing else — no sender-ID registration, no processor agreement. The work is one run of `--group sandbox` with `SMS_SANDBOX_DSN` set, then correcting the canned bodies against what came back. | [TASK-030](tasks/TASK-030-sms-delivery-channel.md) §6 + §9 | Open | 2026-08-20 |
| BL-170 | ⚠ **Two gates of this repository can report on something other than the code under test, and the failure reads as a specific defect in your slice.** Both bit during [TASK-030](tasks/TASK-030-sms-delivery-channel.md). **(1) Proven, and the sharp one: `make test` and `composer infection` cannot run concurrently.** Both target `f5sign_test` on the same cluster in the same `php-fpm` container. Measured 2026-08-20: with infection mid-run, `make test` returned `Tests: 2118 … Failures: 1` — `EnvelopeVerticalSliceTest … Delivery … is pinned to template …, which is not in the archive`, a plausible and entirely specific report about the slice under test. `docker compose exec php-fpm sh -c 'ps aux \| grep -c "[i]nfection"'` returned **7**; the same suite alone, before and after, was green. ⚑ It is `BL-138`'s *one cluster per session* rule arriving in a new form — not two sessions this time, but **two gates of the same session**. **(2) Observed once, mechanism unconfirmed: the compiled container config did not rebuild** after `config/services.yaml` was restored following a sabotage, so `SmsChannelWiringTest` stayed red against a source tree that was already correct until `make sf cmd="cache:clear --env=test"`. ⚠ n=1, and the likely mechanism is mtime granularity (the restore landed inside the same second as the cache write) rather than a systematic bug — recorded as an observation, **not** as a proven claim. ⛔ **The direction that matters is the opposite one**: the same staleness would let a gate report **green** against a compiled container built from config that has since changed. ▶ **What it needs**: for (1), either a lock so the two cannot overlap, or a separate database — ⚠ but note `BL-138` proves a separate *database* on the same cluster is not isolation for the relay, so the honest fix is a separate cluster or a mutex. For (2), a second occurrence before anything is built; until then the mitigation is `cache:clear` before trusting a lone red. | [TASK-030](tasks/TASK-030-sms-delivery-channel.md) §9, and `BL-138` for the cluster half | Open | 2026-08-20 |
| BL-171 | ⛔ **No personal-data column in this repository is enciphered, and TASK-023 adds a phone number to the set rather than starting to shrink it.** [ADR-0033](adr/ADR-0033-pii-field-encryption-at-rest.md) is `Proposed` with the substrate built and **zero callers**: `FieldCipher` / `KeyProvider` / `ConfigKeyProvider` are realized and unit-locked, and the applied carrier — the BC-local `RecipientFieldCipher` seam, the [`DbalEnvelopeRepository`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Infrastructure/Persistence/Doctrine/DbalEnvelopeRepository.php) call sites and the `?Email`-typed command — does not exist. ⚑ **A first cut DID exist and was reverted**, which is the part worth knowing before anyone re-plans this: that ADR's Status records it was *"built and green, then held back"* because it enciphered on write with **no backfill** and its read path **threw on legacy plaintext**. Backfill is the hard half, not the cipher. ▶ **Decided 2026-08-20 by the user**: [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) was going to be the first caller — its title said so — and §7 D5 removed it, on four grounds. (a) It would protect `recipient.phone` while `recipient.email` sits in `TEXT` beside it, and every recipient has an email where few have a phone; (b) `notification.notification_delivery.destination` is plain `TEXT` and takes the same value on **every send**, so using the protected column writes it in the clear one schema over; (c) [`ConfigKeyProvider::currentVersion()`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Crypto/ConfigKeyProvider.php) is a hardcoded `1` — **no rotation path** — and its keys are HKDF-derived rather than stored, so **crypto-shred is unavailable** (its own `Never` block: destroying the master secret strands every tenant); erasure is row deletion; (d) the managed-KMS provider ADR-0032 decision 6 calls for does not exist. ⚠ **The counter-argument is recorded because it is the reason to revisit**: a brand-new nullable column has **no legacy plaintext**, so it is the cheapest place the carrier could ever land — the exact obstacle that reverted the first cut is absent there. That advantage expires the moment real phone rows exist. ▶ **What this needs**: one carrier covering `email` and `phone` **together**, expand / backfill / contract, plus a decision on the plaintext copy in Notification and on whether a blind index is owed (ADR-0033 decision 5 foresees one; [`OtpSendKeys`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Port/OtpSendKeys.php) already hashes a destination at use time, which is the shape). Whoever lands it promotes ADR-0033 to `Accepted`. ⚠ Until then the decision is both **unrealized and unbinding** (the repo's authoring rule 7). ⚑ **2026-09-21 — two more planned additions to the plaintext set, both by decision of the maintainer**: [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §4 D9 (`envelope.field.value`, see BL-49) and [TASK-045](tasks/TASK-045-browser-reported-evidence-at-the-commit.md) §4 D6 (the `reported_*` evidence columns and `Accept-Language` on `session.signing_session`). Both classify through `Pii` and encipher nothing, the same trade [BL-214](BACKLOG.md) records. ⚑ **2026-09-21, later — the first of the two is realized**: TASK-044 writes `envelope.field.value` in plaintext since `0bae290` (on `feat/field-values-at-commit`); TASK-045's is still planned (realized since — see the ⚑ of 2026-10-14 below). ⚑ **2026-10-07 — a further planned addition, and the first that is binary**: [TASK-053](tasks/TASK-053-the-recipients-drawn-signature-on-their-mark.md) §4 D5 keeps the recipient's drawn signature in `session.signature_image.image_png`, a plaintext `BYTEA`, from the commit until the recipient's marks are sealed (then the bytes are nulled and the digest stays). [ADR-0032](adr/ADR-0032-evidence-pii-custody.md) governs it as personal evidence, and it enciphers nothing, the same trade as the rows above. A carrier landing for the set above should cover this column too; its short lifetime (cleared on `RecipientSigned`) makes it the cheapest to cover. Found by TASK-053's security audit (W1). ⚠ **Grows with SMS** (2026-10-09): [ADR-0077](adr/ADR-0077-a-recipient-declares-the-channels-that-reach-them.md) makes phones a routine contact for notifications, so many more of them will sit in clear. ⚑ **2026-10-14 — TASK-045's addition is realized** on `feat/browser-reported-evidence`: `session.signing_session` holds the browser's report in plaintext in seventeen `reported_*` columns, the observed `Accept-Language` in `signer_accept_language`, and a decline's observed IP and user agent in `signer_ip`/`signer_user_agent` (`Version20261013110756`). The report is classified `Pii` and nothing is enciphered; [ADR-0061](adr/ADR-0061-retention-deletion-and-purge.md) §2.12 and [`LIVE_SCHEMA.md`](LIVE_SCHEMA.md) list the columns. ⚑ **2026-10-14, [TASK-059](tasks/TASK-059-the-trail-shows-how-each-invitation-travelled.md):** the audit trail's `personal.invitations` entries hold a masked destination inside `PartyPersonal`; a cipher of that group will hide the invitation outcomes with their destinations, so TASK-059 §5 D2 is revisited then. ▶ **Extended 2026-10-26 ([TASK-047](tasks/TASK-047-per-signer-evidence-document.md) security audit):** a sealed receipt (PDF and attached JSON) states its recipient's name, IP, user agent, accept-language and asserted identity in clear, the same posture as the audit trail under [ADR-0033](adr/ADR-0033-pii-field-encryption-at-rest.md); `evidence_audit.issued_receipt` itself holds no personal column. A cipher of the evidence custody would have to cover both documents. ▶ **Added 2026-10-26 ([TASK-070](tasks/TASK-070-a-recipient-reads-their-copy-through-a-link.md) security audit):** `session.copy_access_attempt.source_ip` and `user_agent` are stored in clear, as `session.auth_attempt`'s are; the copy link's store holds a digest only and the copy challenges hold hashes, so these two columns are the only personal data the task adds. ⚑ **2026-11-04 — the carrier's design moved:** [ADR-0096](adr/ADR-0096-key-management-encrypts-the-logs-free-text-under-erasable-keyrings.md) §2.10 decides it (a key use under a keyring, a custody domain of its own, expand / backfill / contract) and supersedes in part ADR-0033 decision 3. It is not built, so this row's claim still holds: no column is encrypted. | [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) §7 D5 | Open | 2026-08-20 (review of TASK-023's encryption premise) |
| BL-172 | ⛔ **A recipient's phone number can be written and can never be read back, so the dashboard has no way to show a sender the number already on file.** [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) added `envelope.recipient.phone` and the two endpoints that write it (`POST /recipients`, `POST …/corrections`), and deliberately did **not** add it to [`EnvelopeJsonPresenter`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/EnvelopeJsonPresenter.php), which returns `email` per recipient and no `phone`. ⚑ **The omission is a held decision, not an oversight**: whether a phone joins the sender-facing projection is a **personal-data** question and TASK-023 §3 named it as one, so it was left for the user rather than taken inside the slice. The direction was chosen to be the cheap one to reverse — adding a key later is additive and breaks no client, removing one is a breaking change. ▶ **What it needs is an answer, not engineering**: the field, the presenter line and the `components.schemas.Recipient` entry are one commit. ⚠ **What is actually being traded**: a sender correcting a wrong number today is typing blind, because the API will not tell them what the wrong number *was* — against publishing a stronger identifier than the email beside it to every holder of a machine credential for that tenant, on a row that is [`BL-171`](BACKLOG.md)-unencrypted at rest. ⚑ The masked form is a third option and is already built for the signer's side ([`MaskedDestination`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Service/MaskedDestination.php)) — it would let a sender recognise the number without the response carrying it. ⛔ Until this is decided the dashboard cannot build a recipient-edit screen that shows the current number, and `docs/frontend-handoff/TASK-023-recipient-phone.md` tells them so rather than leaving them to discover it. ✅ **Answered 2026-08-20 by the user: the phone is published WHOLE, exactly as `email` beside it.** ⚑ **The deciding argument is coherence, and it is the same one §7 D5 used to leave the column unenciphered**: this route already publishes the address in the clear to every holder of a machine credential for the tenant, so masking the phone next to it protects nothing and buys an asymmetry somebody has to keep explaining. The user's words were *"de momento lo devolvería entero como el mail, y ya en un futuro vemos cómo ciframos y ofuscamos todo"* — a partial measure now is worth less than a coherent carrier later, which is [`BL-171`](BACKLOG.md). ⚠ **The widening is real and was accepted knowingly**: a phone identifies a person better than an address and cannot be abandoned the way a mailbox can. What it buys is the case that was actually broken — a sender correcting a mistyped number can now see which number they typed. ⛔ **The signer's side did NOT change and must not be harmonized with this**: route 3 publishes a masked destination because it answers before anything has been proved, to whoever holds the link. Landed on `feat/recipient-phone-on-the-envelope-response`: `EnvelopeJsonPresenter`, the `components.schemas.Recipient` entry, and an `EnvelopeHttpTest` assertion that reddens if either projection drifts toward the other. | [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) §3 — the PII decision it escalated rather than took | Landed | 2026-08-20 |
| BL-173 | ⛑ **Closed on `feat/channel-selection` 2026-10-14 (pending merge): the text below describes the state BEFORE [TASK-031](tasks/TASK-031-channel-selection-beyond-email.md), which deleted the `email === null` skip.** Option (c) was taken: the invitation resolves the recipient's required `notification_channels`, and a recipient nobody can reach is recorded and shown instead of skipped. ⛔ **A recipient with a phone and no email is now authorable, passes every guard, and is then SILENTLY SKIPPED at invitation — so they are never told the envelope exists and never reach the gate their number was supplied for.** [`NotifyActivatedStepRecipientsUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Application/UseCase/NotifyActivatedStepRecipientsUseCase.php) does `if ($recipient->email === null) { continue; }` — a `continue`, not a throw, commented *"fail-closed on an unreachable recipient"*. ⚑ **That line is older than this and was correct when written**: with no phone column there was no reason to author a recipient without an address, so the branch was unreachable. [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) makes it reachable — it gives senders a reason to add a phone-only recipient — without touching the line. ⛔ **This is [`BL-162`](BACKLOG.md) one door over, and the same failure shape TASK-023 §2 argues against**: the sender is refused nothing at authoring, and the failure surfaces where nobody is watching. It is **worse** than BL-162's original case, because there is no 409 and no `DELIVERY_FAILED` — the recipient simply never appears to have been contacted, and the envelope sits at their step for ever. ⚠ **Not caught by TASK-023's own guard, deliberately and correctly**: `DeclaredMethodDestinations` asks *can this recipient receive the METHOD they declared*, and `OTP_SMS` + a mobile is a truthful yes. What is missing is a different question — *can this recipient be reached at all, by the invitation that carries the link* — which no guard asks today. ▶ **What it needs is a decision before code, and the options are not equivalent.** (a) An **authoring** guard: every recipient needs an address, full stop — cheapest, and closes it where the sender is present, but it makes an email mandatory for ever and forecloses SMS-only signing. (b) A **pre-send** guard on the envelope, beside `ensureEverySignerIsAssigned()` — refuses at `send` rather than at authoring, which is later but is where the other reachability invariants already live. (c) Make the invitation itself channel-aware, which is [TASK-031](tasks/TASK-031-channel-selection-beyond-email.md) and the only option that actually delivers SMS-only signing rather than forbidding it. ⚑ **(c) is the product answer and (a)/(b) are the safe stopgaps**; doing (b) now does not block (c) later. ⛔ Whichever is chosen, the `continue` should become loud: a recipient the platform cannot reach is not a row to skip. | [TASK-023](tasks/TASK-023-sms-codes-and-recipient-phone.md) close-out review, 2026-08-20 — found by asking what else the new column made reachable | Closed — built on `feat/channel-selection`, pending merge ([TASK-031](tasks/TASK-031-channel-selection-beyond-email.md) replaced the skip with channel resolution over a **required** `notification_channels`, [ADR-0077](adr/ADR-0077-a-recipient-declares-the-channels-that-reach-them.md) §2.1), and [TASK-056](tasks/TASK-056-delivery-facts-and-sender-visibility.md) records and shows a recipient nobody could reach ([ADR-0078](adr/ADR-0078-delivery-outcomes-come-back-as-facts.md) §2.2); the shape was hit on the dev stack on 2026-10-08 by an eCMR carrier with only a phone | 2026-08-20 |
| BL-174 | ⚑ **EP27's only end-to-end proof cannot be run by either repository alone, and neither one's gates will ever miss it.** The epic's acceptance is *"provoke a real 500 and see both events, PHP and JS, carrying the same `request_id`"* — the single check that the two integrations are one system rather than two that happen to compile. ⛔ **It is nobody's gate on purpose and that is the danger**: the backend suite goes green without it, the signer's suite goes green without it, and both sessions can report done while the thing the epic was for has never been observed once. **Update 2026-08-20 — the blocker moved, and the new one is worse.** The Sentry org (`factor-5`, region `de.sentry.io`) now exists with a `f5sign-signer` project, so "no project" is discharged; a `f5sign-backend` project still has to be created. But the signer session drove its half with real events and measured the reply: **`429 · x-sentry-rate-limits: …organization:error_usage_exceeded` — the org's ERROR quota is exhausted.** ⚠ **Transactions and spans still ingest** (a different quota bucket), which is what makes this vicious: tracing verifies fine while errors are rejected, the SDK backs off and drops them locally, and the only trace of the loss is a `client_report` item inside the envelope. ⛔ **From the application's side this is indistinguishable from a working integration that nobody is looking at** — and it is a live false-negative trap for the backend, which has not sent its first event yet: plugging in a DSN and seeing nothing arrive reads as *"the wiring is broken"* and invites dismantling code that is correct. ▶ **This cannot close until the org has error quota**, both projects exist, and the run has actually happened. Quota is a plan decision and belongs to whoever owns the Sentry account. | [ADR-0009](adr/ADR-0009-observability-at-the-boundary.md) part 4 (correlation) — the smoke is its only end-to-end bar | Open | 2026-08-20 (backend/signer cross-session design; quota measured the same day) |
| BL-175 | ⛔ **An unset `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT` does not fail and does not read as unset — the SDK seals the event as `production`.** Measured on the JS side by the signer session: `@sentry/core` carries `DEFAULT_ENVIRONMENT = "production"`, so a build whose environment variable was forgotten does not land in a visible limbo, it lands **inside production** and fires whatever alerting points there. The misconfiguration disguises itself as the good state, which is the one failure mode configuration must never have. ⚠ **This backend has two independent routes to the same place**: the PHP SDK may carry the same default (unverified — `sentry/sentry-symfony` is not installed yet, so check it against the vendor source rather than the docs), and T27.1.1's own spec sets `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT=%env(APP_ENV)%`, which resolves to `dev`/`prod`/`test` and therefore **never** produces the agreed `production`/`staging`/`development` set. ▶ When `config/packages/sentry.yaml` is written: do not derive from `APP_ENV`, refuse to boot with a DSN present and no environment, and if a fallback is kept make it a string outside the agreed three so its appearance is self-reporting. | this row until `config/packages/sentry.yaml` exists; then that file's comment plus [ADR-0009](adr/ADR-0009-observability-at-the-boundary.md) | Open | 2026-08-20 (signer session, cross-checked) |
| BL-176 | ⚠ **A recipient whose contact is deliberately unknown at authoring cannot declare a delivered auth method — so the CLAIMABLE placeholder recipient and [ADR-0054](adr/ADR-0054-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §2.3 contradict each other, and only one of them is built.** §2.3 refuses a declared method whose destination does not resolve, **at authoring**, *"because that is the only moment the sender can still fix it"* — realized in `DeclaredMethodDestinations::guard()`, now per member. A CLAIMABLE recipient is the case where that premise is false on purpose: the sender **knows** they do not know the contact yet. ⚑ **Nothing is broken today and that is precisely why this row exists**: the placeholder does not exist in this backend, so the two features have never met. ⛔ **Whoever builds it meets the contradiction head-on**, and the tempting repair — dropping or weakening the authoring guard — would re-open [`BL-162`](BACKLOG.md) for every ordinary recipient to serve a case that is not ordinary. ▶ **What it needs is a decision before code, and the options are not equivalent.** (a) Validate at the moment the placeholder is FILLED rather than at creation, which keeps the guard's promise (*a declaration is a promise about a person we can reach*) and moves when it is checked — but the refusal then lands on whoever claims the slot, not on the sender who declared it, which is the property §2.3 was choosing. (b) Refuse a delivered method on a CLAIMABLE recipient outright, so a placeholder can only be link-only or `ACCESS_CODE` until it is filled — cheapest, and forecloses the multi-drop waybill case the feature exists for. (c) Let the declaration ride and check at issuance, which is exactly the *"declare freely, filter at execution"* shape §2.3 rejects by name as the empty-exempted-set failure. ⚑ **Reported by the `factor5_dashboard_sf` session on 2026-08-20** — an EXTERNAL consumer of this API, whose gateway already refuses to build such an envelope and whose own message is the record that `assignRecipient()` is a known backend-first gap on their side. ⚠ They also closed BL-162 in their own gateway, so our 422 is a third layer rather than their only one; do not read their resilience as evidence the gap is narrow. ⚑ **DECIDED AND BUILT 2026-08-28 — option (a), by [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md) §4 D6 realizing [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.13.** The two features have now met, so the *"nothing is broken today and that is precisely why this row exists"* clause above is the state before that branch, not the state now. What shipped: `AddRecipientUseCase` **skips** `DeclaredMethodDestinations::guard()` when the request declares the slot claimable, and `ClaimRecipientUseCase` re-runs it **entire** against what the claim supplies, before the mutation, refusing with the same `RecipientCannotReceiveMethodException::forSlot()` → 422 and naming the same **first** unreachable method in declaration order. ⛔ **Keyed on the declaration and on nothing else** — the tempting repair this row warned about, keying the deferral on *"has no contact yet"*, would reopen `BL-162` for every ordinary recipient, so the authoring refusal for an undeclared contactless recipient is **exactly as it was**. Both arms are one test (`AddRecipientUseCaseTest::the_declaration_defers_the_destination_guard_and_nothing_else_does`), the pair rather than either arm, because arm B *is* the BL-162 regression guard. ⛑ **The property (a) was chosen against is paid for and named, not glossed**: the refusal now lands on whoever calls the claim rather than on the sender who declared — but it lands on the **integrator**, synchronously, on the call that caused it, which is strictly earlier than the case §2.3's guard was written against (*"at the gate — to the signer, long after the sender has moved on"*). ▶ What is left here is not a decision: tell the `factor5_dashboard_sf` gateway that our side now accepts the declaration, since their refusal to build such an envelope predates it. ⚑ **2026-10-20 — [ADR-0083](adr/ADR-0083-a-recipients-gates-are-editable-until-they-enter.md) ([TASK-064](tasks/TASK-064-the-gates-are-editable-until-the-recipient-enters.md)) leaves option (a) as it is and moves what the claim checks against**: a slot's gates may be replaced until the recipient first opens their link, so the claim re-runs the guard against the declaration stored when the claim runs, which may no longer be the one authored. The change defers the guard for an unclaimed slot, keyed on `Recipient::isUnclaimedSlot()` (the declaration, and no claim yet), never on *"has no contact yet"*. ⚑ **2026-11-04 — [TASK-078](tasks/TASK-078-the-machine-routes-refuse-an-unclaimed-slot.md)**: the deferral assumed nothing reaches a slot before its claim, and a machine key could (`BL-528`). The three machine routes now refuse it, so a call made since leaves the slot unopened for the guard re-run at the claim; a slot stamped earlier is `BL-205`'s note. | [ADR-0054](adr/ADR-0054-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §2.3, **resolved by** [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.13 + [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md) §4 D6 | Decided and built by TASK-036 (merged); the row stays Open until the gateway is told | 2026-08-20 |
| BL-177 | ⛔ **Every request DTO on this API silently IGNORES unknown fields, so a client that adopts a new field against a deployment that predates it gets a `201` and a declaration that was never applied — and for an AUTH field that is a silently open door.** Measured 2026-08-20 by the `factor5_dashboard_sf` session against the running stack, not reasoned: `POST /recipients` with `auth.access_methods` answered **201**, and the stored row read `{"access_method": null, "signing_method": null}`. The `phone` field vanished the same way. ⚑ **Not created by [TASK-032](tasks/TASK-032-ordered-auth-method-list.md), and older than it**: nothing configures serializer strictness anywhere (`grep ALLOW_EXTRA_ATTRIBUTES config/ src/` → nothing) and **20** controllers use `#[MapRequestPayload]`, whose default is to drop what it does not recognise. What TASK-032 changes is that the ignored field is now **security-relevant** — the same generator as [`BL-173`](BACKLOG.md): a branch that was correct while unreachable, made reachable by a slice that never touched it. ⛔ **The window is the dangerous part and it is unavoidable**: between publishing a contract and deploying it, clients start sending the new field, and that is exactly when an older deployment answers `201` to a declaration it discarded. ⚠ **This is WORSE than the shape [ADR-0054](adr/ADR-0054-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §2.3 rejects by name** (*declare freely, filter at execution*) — it does not filter at execution either; nothing anywhere ever says no. ▶ **What it needs is a decision before code.** (a) **Reject unknown fields globally** (`ALLOW_EXTRA_ATTRIBUTES => false` on the payload denormalizer) — closes the whole class for all 20 endpoints and is a **breaking change to a published contract**: every client sending a stray field starts getting 422, which is an edge-wide behavioural decision and reads as an ADR rather than a patch. (b) **Reject unknown fields on the AUTH object only**, which is where the failure is a door rather than a lost preference — narrower, and leaves the class open everywhere else, including wherever the next security field lands. ⚑ **(b) gained a second case on 2026-08-21 and is no longer one option among four equals.** [TASK-032](tasks/TASK-032-ordered-auth-method-list.md) put a **third** field on that same `auth` object — `selection`, the presentation mode — and it inherits this row exactly as `access_methods` does. ⚠ Its failure is quieter and worth stating, because *quieter* is what makes it worse to find: a discarded `selection` does not leave a door open, it leaves the signer in `ESCALATION` when the sender declared `FREE_CHOICE` — **silently, and with nobody able to notice**, since both are legitimate states and neither errors. ⛔ So the pattern, not the instance, is the thing: **every future field on the `auth` object inherits this row**, and three is enough to stop calling that a coincidence. Reframed by the `factor5_dashboard_sf` session, which found the original defect. (c) **Version the declaration** so an old deployment refuses a payload shape it does not know, which is the honest fix and the most expensive. (d) Accept it and make the deploy order a documented precondition — cheapest, and it is what the frontend handoff does today, but a precondition nobody can check is not a control. ⚑ **Whoever decides should weigh that this API is consumed by at least one EXTERNAL client** who found this by probing rather than by being told. | fold pending — no ADR owns the edge's tolerance for unknown fields | Open | 2026-08-20 |
| BL-178 | ⚠ **Nothing bounds how many methods a sender may DECLARE, and each one carries its own send budget — so the effective per-session ceiling is the sum, and a sender sets it.** [ADR-0054](adr/ADR-0054-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §2.9 keeps the rate limiter per method deliberately (an email costs nothing, an SMS costs money on every send, so one shared ceiling would force one of the two numbers to be wrong) and §7.3 records the rejected alternative — a method-agnostic `otp_session` policy counting every issuance. The consequence is arithmetic: `[OTP_EMAIL, OTP_SMS]` is 5 + 3 = **8 sends an hour, three chargeable**, where `config/packages/rate_limiter.yaml` reads `limit: 5`. ⚑ **That comment is now written** (TASK-032), so an operator is no longer misled — this row is the *other* half, which the comment cannot fix: nobody decides the ceiling, a sender does. ⛔ **Not the same thing as `AuthMethod::MAX_DECLARED_LIST_LENGTH`**, which TASK-032 added: that bound is **derived** — no valid list can exceed the delivered set — and exists so a request-size guard has a number. It refuses nothing a sender could legitimately mean, so it is not a policy limit and must not be read as one. ▶ ADR-0054 §3.3 says it *"probably belongs to the authoring surface rather than to an invoice"*, which is the shape of the answer rather than the answer. The forcing function is the first tenant declaring three or four delivered methods across a large envelope, or the first SMS bill nobody predicted — whichever arrives first. ⚑ **2026-10-20 ([TASK-062](tasks/TASK-062-each-gate-has-its-own-code-send-budget.md), [ADR-0081](adr/ADR-0081-each-gate-has-its-own-code-send-budget.md), accepted 2026-10-20):** the session counter is now per gate, so the sum runs over each gate's methods and over the two gates: `[OTP_EMAIL, OTP_SMS]` on both gates is 5 + 5 at each gate = **20 sends an hour, ten chargeable**; the 5 + 3 = 8 above is the arithmetic before it. The row's question — nobody decides the ceiling, a sender does — is unchanged. | [TASK-032](tasks/TASK-032-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §7 — its first Open follow-up, which had no row until now | Open | 2026-08-21 |
| BL-179 | ⚠ **No harness in this suite can prove that a MIGRATED row means what an authored one means, so [TASK-032](tasks/TASK-032-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §6 bar 8 is unmet and was deliberately not claimed.** `Version20260820000003` rewrites `envelope.recipient.auth_access_gate` / `auth_signing_gate` from `TEXT` to ordered `jsonb`, and its safety argument is that the transform is **value-preserving in every aggregate state** — which is why it carries no status predicate (the repo's authoring rule 6). ⛔ **The two available tests are both worthless, and for opposite reasons.** Asserting that a backfilled row and an authored one read alike is **tautological**: they are byte-identical by construction, so the test agrees with the code whatever the migration does. Writing a pre-migration `TEXT` row and reading it back means **reimplementing the migration's `CASE` in the test**, which is a logic-duplicating double standing in for the artifact under test — the repo's authoring rule 4 names that as safe only when the real artifact has its own unit test, and a migration has none. ▶ What would actually hold it is a harness that can run **one migration over a fixture it wrote first** — i.e. migrate-to-version-N, seed, migrate-to-N+1, assert. Nothing in the suite does that today, and building it is a testing-infrastructure task rather than a line in this one. ⚑ Worth building the day a migration transforms rows **non**-trivially; this one is the cheap case and still could not be proved. ⚠ Until then the property rests on `doctrine-guard`'s five-way review of the reachable value space (2026-08-21), which is a review and not a test. | [TASK-032](tasks/TASK-032-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §6 bar 8 | Open | 2026-08-21 |
| BL-180 | ⛔ **`POST …/recipients/{id}/access-code` answers `200` with a freshly minted code on a recipient whose gates never declare `ACCESS_CODE`, so the sender is handed a working-looking secret that nothing will ever verify — and the sender is the one party with no way to find out.** Measured 2026-08-21 against the `develop` deployment by the `factor5_dashboard_sf` session, an EXTERNAL consumer: `POST …/access-code {}` on a recipient declared `OTP_EMAIL` returned `200 {"access_code":"982825"}`, and neither the recipient's `auth` object nor the signer descriptor changed. The code is dead by construction — [`RecipientAuthContext::verifierFor()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/RecipientAuthContext.php) hands back the stored hash **only** when the gate being asked declares `ACCESS_CODE`, and otherwise `null`. ⛔ **Worse than a silent no-op, because of what the endpoint is FOR**: the whole mechanic of `ACCESS_CODE` is that the sender reads the value aloud to the signer ([ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.8, *"show once to the sender"*), so the failure mode is a support call in which the sender dictates six digits and the signer types a number no verifier will ever meet. ⚑ **And it is not inert**: the call also bumps the recipient's auth generation, which really does clear a lockout, so it half-works — which is presumably why nobody noticed. `auth-unlock` is the endpoint that does that half on purpose. ⛔ **The predicate already exists, fifteen lines away in the sibling, which makes this the repo's authoring rule 3 rather than a missing feature**: [`AddRecipientController::generatedCodeFor()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/AddRecipientController.php) asks whether **either** slot `declares(ACCESS_CODE)` and mints nothing when the answer is no, with its `#[OA\*]` description already promising exactly that (*"`access_code` is present **only** when a gate declared `ACCESS_CODE`"*). Creation asks the question; reset never did. ⚠ **The fix must ask BOTH slots, and a naive one will not**: keying on the access slot alone breaks ADR-0049 §2.1's own worked example — access `DNIE_NFC`, signing `ACCESS_CODE` — where one hash on the recipient serves the gate that named the method. ⚠ **And it is a behaviour change on a published endpoint**: today's `200` becomes a `422`, which is the shape the reporter asked for and matches the other not-applicable-method refusals, but a client using the call as a disguised `auth-unlock` would start failing — they should use `auth-unlock`. ▶ **What it needs**: the sibling's predicate in [`ResetRecipientAccessCodeUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Application/UseCase/Authoring/ResetRecipientAccessCodeUseCase.php) or its aggregate method, a `422` code, the `#[OA\*]` response list, and a test on the case where the two slots **disagree** (access `OTP_EMAIL`, signing `ACCESS_CODE` → still `200`), which is the one a fixture whose variables always agree cannot catch. ⚑ The aggregate's docblock enumerates three guards it deliberately does NOT carry (`guardDraft`, `sentAt`, recipient-leg-closed) and never mentions the declared method, so the omission reads as considered when it was not — that docblock is part of the fix. | [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.8 — the reset it defines as the `ACCESS_CODE` recovery, and §2.10 which already says there is no re-send for a code we never delivered | Open | 2026-08-21 (reported by `factor5_dashboard_sf` against the `develop` deployment) |
| BL-181 | ⛔ **The published spec documents error STATUSES and almost never the `code`, so the one member consumers are told to branch on is the one they cannot enumerate — they discover it by probing production.** Measured 2026-08-21: **98 of 149** `#[OA\Response]` entries in `src/` are a bare `ref: '#/components/responses/…'` with no per-route description and therefore no code; the six shared components in `config/packages/nelmio_api_doc.yaml` carry prose (*"Malformed request or missing tenant scope."*) and **no code value**; and `ProblemDetails.code` is described generically with a single example. ⛔ **The cost is not cosmetic, and this API already contains the worked example**: `AUTH_METHOD_UNAVAILABLE` and `RECIPIENT_CANNOT_RECEIVE_METHOD` are **both 422** and mean opposite things — a deployment that cannot run the method (escalate to support) versus a field the user fills in ten seconds. A consumer who cannot read the catalog must branch on the status, which [TASK-032](tasks/TASK-032-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §9 already recorded as a liar in both directions on this very pair. ⚑ **The catalog IS statically derivable today, which makes the ask cheaper than it looks**: 19 domain refusals via [`ProblemCoded`](../src/F5Sign/Kernel/Exception/ProblemCoded.php) — 7 as literals plus 12 inside four factory-style exceptions (`AuthGateRefusedException`, `OtpNotDeliverableException`, `RecipientNotCorrectableException`, `SigningSessionUnavailableException`) — plus `VALIDATION_ERROR`, the nine status fallbacks in `ApiExceptionListener::STATUS_CODES`, and the uppercased `invalid_*` family from `ValueObjectValidationException::errorCode()`. What does not exist is anything that keeps it derived. ⚠ **And that is a deliberate bargain, not an oversight — do not spend it by mistake.** [ADR-0025](adr/ADR-0025-rfc9457-error-shape.md) states **twice** that *"the BC owns the term; the edge enumerates none of them, so a new code touches no boundary code"*. ⛑ **The tension dissolves once you separate the two enumerations**: that decision governs the **boundary code**, and it is silent on the **spec**. Emitting the catalog into OpenAPI does not require centralizing the codes back into the listener, and a repair that does is the plausible cleanup [`LOAD-BEARING.md`](LOAD-BEARING.md) warns about. ⚠ **No gate sees this**: `OpenApiSpecTest` asserts the ProblemDetails *shape* against a real error response and that every route is documented; response coverage of `code` is asserted nowhere. ▶ **What was asked, in the requester's order of utility**: (1) the `code` on each 4xx/5xx of the sender routes, even bare; (2) a published enum of the whole catalog, so a consumer maps it once instead of discovering it; (3) **the norm** — a new code is documented in the commit that introduces it, because a code that reaches production before the spec gets discovered with a user waiting. ⚑ (3) is the one that lasts and it is **lintable** rather than prose: a check that every `ProblemCoded` implementor's code appears in the spec would hold it, which is the shape `CLAUDE.md` asks for before an eighth authoring rule. ⚠ Requested by `factor5_dashboard_sf` on behalf of their user — an EXTERNAL consumer, who meanwhile shipped a **partial and self-declaring** mapping (the eight codes they had seen, a status fallback for the rest, and a comment saying the list is not an enumeration of our catalog because they cannot enumerate it). | [ADR-0025](adr/ADR-0025-rfc9457-error-shape.md) — the `code` member it reserves as the per-condition taxonomy, and the enumeration bargain it strikes at the boundary | Open | 2026-08-21 (external consumer, measured across eleven routes they call; generalized here to all 149 responses) |
| BL-182 | ⛔ **The `access_code` cross-validation reads two of the four declaration slots, so the SAME declaration is accepted in the singular spelling and refused in the plural — and the refusal message tells the sender to declare what they already declared.** Measured 2026-08-21 against the `develop` deployment by `factor5_dashboard_sf`, same envelope, same step, only the spelling changed: `{"access_method":"ACCESS_CODE"}` + `access_code` → **201**; `{"access_methods":["ACCESS_CODE"]}` + `access_code` → **422 `VALIDATION_ERROR`**, *"access_code was supplied but neither gate declares ACCESS_CODE … Set auth.access_method or auth.signing_method"*. [`AddRecipientRequest::validateAccessCodeHasAGate()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/Request/AddRecipientRequest.php) builds `$declared = [$this->auth?->access_method, $this->auth?->signing_method]` and never consults `access_methods` / `signing_methods`. ⚑ **The message incriminates the predicate**: it names the two singular fields and no plural one, so it was written before the lists existed and was not revisited when [TASK-032](tasks/TASK-032-ordered-auth-method-list.md) added them — the repo's authoring rule 1, *grep the symbol whose behaviour changed, not only a term you retired*, on a re-cut that retired no name. ⛔ **It breaks the equivalence the contract itself asserts.** `AuthRequirementRequest` publishes the two spellings as *"two spellings of one declaration"* and refuses sending both; here there is an operation one accepts and the other rejects, so a client adopting the plural — which is what the frontend handoff asks of them — **loses the ability to set an access code at all**. ⚠ And because `ACCESS_CODE` may not be mixed into a list of two or more (ADR-0054 §2.2), the only affected shape is the **one-member list**, which is the case that most resembles the scalar and is therefore the one nobody thinks to test. ⛑ **The docblock is the sharp part**: it argues carefully that the predicate must be *either slot* and not *the access slot*, naming ADR-0049 §2.1's `DNIE_NFC` + `ACCESS_CODE` example — the right question, asked of half the slots. ▶ **The fix is the same shape as [`BL-180`](BACKLOG.md)'s**: ask whether **any of the four** slots names `ACCESS_CODE`, ideally by building the `AuthRequirement` once and asking `declares()` — which is what the sibling `AddRecipientController::generatedCodeFor()` already does correctly, so the list-aware predicate exists in the same request's handling. ⚑ **Census of every site that decides on `ACCESS_CODE` (2026-08-21, four sites)**: `generatedCodeFor()` list-aware ✅; `RecipientAuthContext::verifierFor()` list-aware ✅; **this predicate, singular-only ❌**; `ResetRecipientAccessCodeUseCase`, which asks nothing at all ❌ (`BL-180`). There is no fifth. ⛑ **The reporter's third measured row is NOT a defect and is recorded so nobody fixes it**: plural + no `access_code` → `201` and the server mints one, which they read as a secret nobody holds. The 201 body **does** carry `access_code` — `AddRecipientController` sets it whenever it generated one — so that is ADR-0049 §2.8's *"show once to the sender"* working as designed. ⚠ **Not breaking to fix**: a 422 becomes a 201 on a request the contract already calls valid, so no client can be relying on the refusal. | [ADR-0054](adr/ADR-0054-ordered-auth-method-list.md) §2.1 — the list the scalar is now the head of, and the equivalence this predicate does not honour | Open | 2026-08-21 (external consumer, measured on three request shapes against the deployment) |
| BL-183 | ⛔ **A recipient whose leg was DELEGATED or SKIPPED is invisible to both pre-credential routes, so the system runs the whole authentication ceremony — including a billable SMS — before telling them it was never theirs to sign.** [`RecipientAuthContext`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/RecipientAuthContext.php) is the only thing routes 2 and 3 read across the Envelope boundary, and it carries `envelopeIsAlive` — a fact about the **envelope's** status — with nothing at all about the recipient's own leg. [`RecipientOutcome`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/RecipientOutcome.php) never crosses. ⚑ **Measured, not assumed**: `RecipientAuthContextReader::read()` maps `envelopeIsAlive: !$context->status->isTerminal()` and reads `$context->recipient` only for the auth requirement, the code hash, the lock generation and the locale — the outcome is never consulted, and nothing else on either route refuses. ⛔ **[ADR-0056](adr/ADR-0056-terminal-state-on-pre-credential-routes.md) does NOT cover it**, and the reason is worth stating so nobody reads that ADR as having closed this: `outcome` is projected from `SessionStatus`, the **Session's** row, which for these recipients is `AUTH_PENDING` or `AUTH_PASSED` — perfectly open. So the new member publishes `null`, which says *the leg is open*, and a client cannot degrade against a field that asserts the opposite of what is true. Graceful degradation covers the *named-but-unknown* close; this one is *invisible*. ⚑ **Severity is narrower than the ADR-0056 case and was corrected downward by `f5sign-signer`, who traced their own side**: it is not a dead end. `useSignerNavigation.errorCodeToPath()` already routes `RECIPIENT_DELEGATED` / `STEP_NOT_ACTIVE` / `ROLE_NOT_INTERACTIVE` to `/unavailable`, and the access gate knows nothing of delegation, so the recipient passes it, gets a credential, and route 6 throws `SigningSessionUnavailableException` — landing correctly, at the end. ⚑ **And that half is pinned rather than working by inertia, which is worth knowing before anyone re-measures it**: `SigningSessionReaderTest::closedOutcomes()` is a provider covering DELEGATED → `RECIPIENT_DELEGATED` and SKIPPED → `STEP_NOT_ACTIVE`, with a further case crossing DELEGATED with `IN_PERSON_HOST`, and `ApiExceptionListenerTest` holds the 409 plus the `code` the client routes on. That reader's own docblock records that both were once a `match` with a `default` arm which defaulted **into** the ceremony — so this exact class of bug already bit here and is closed. ⛔ The consequence for whoever fixes this row: the *landing* needs no repair and must not be disturbed; what is missing is only that it happens **sooner**, before a code is minted and charged. The improvement should show up as a disappearing OTP, never as a changed destination. ⚑ **And that constraint is enforced rather than merely written, on BOTH sides** — which is why it is safe to state as strongly as it is. <!-- OFFREPO: the client-side half is `f5sign-signer`'s measurement, reported 2026-08-24, and nothing in this repository can verify or re-run it; it is recorded because a cross-repo guarantee is invisible to either suite alone. --> They pin `RECIPIENT_DELEGATED`, `STEP_NOT_ACTIVE` and `ROLE_NOT_INTERACTIVE` → `/unavailable` case by case plus a sweep, and close the far end with a 409 from the read-model navigating for real. So a well-meant *"while I am in here"* change to where a delegated recipient lands goes red in four places across two repositories, which is a better guard than this sentence. ⚠ **Both halves of that were first reported the other way round — each side assumed its own coverage was missing and found it already there on looking.** The row records the corrected reading, not the first one. The cost is therefore a **useless and billable ceremony**: a real code sent to someone with nothing to verify, an OTP typed, and on `OTP_SMS` money spent on precisely the population that by definition will not sign. ⚠ **The trigger is NOT imminent — see the retraction below**, which corrects an earlier claim on this row that delegated signature was about to create the population. It is not that feature. ▶ **What it needs**: crossing the recipient's outcome into `RecipientAuthContext` and refusing — or reporting — on it at the two routes. That is a change to Envelope's published Contract, not a publication decision in Session, which is why it is a row and not an amendment. ⛔ **RETRACTED 2026-08-24, and the retraction is the useful part of this row.** For a few hours this said *owned by `TASK-033` (delegated signature)*, on the reasoning that the slice creating the population should close the hole its own feature opens. **Both halves of that were wrong, and a word did it.** `RecipientOutcome::DELEGATED` means *this recipient's leg was reassigned to someone else — terminal, not a completion, nothing left to sign*. TASK-033's fifteenth catalog member — `MACHINE_KEY_ASSERTED`, ⚑ **not `INTEGRATOR_ASSERTED`, which this row asserted in the present tense until 2026-08-31 and which exists nowhere in `src/`** (ADR-0055 §7.1 retired that shape for a declaration on the recipient) — is an **authentication method** and a positive claim — *a named third party authenticated this party* — whose recipient **passes the access gate and signs normally**, `AUTH_PASSED → COMPLETED`. ⚑ **It was called `INTEGRATOR_DELEGATED` when this confusion happened and is now `AuthMethod::INTEGRATOR_ASSERTED`, renamed on 2026-08-24 *because of* it** — the old name is kept in this sentence deliberately, as the record of what was confused with what; it is not a live symbol. The prefix was expected to disambiguate and did not, because the reader here was not reading the prefix, only the word already known from `RecipientOutcome`. Opposite meanings sharing a word. That branch adds no `SessionStatus` case either, so `terminalOutcome()`'s exhaustive `match` — cited here as the forcing function that would catch them — never fires for it. Caught by the `docs/delegated-signature` session reading their own scope against this claim; verified here against their published branch and their ADR-0055 §2.1 before retracting. ⚑ **The naming hazard this surfaced is CLOSED, and by the rename rather than by a warning**: if two sessions holding the whole context confused the two in one afternoon, the third reader would have too. Recorded in ADR-0055 §2.1 with the mechanism and the full arc — proposed, replaced, restored — so the obvious name is not re-proposed by someone who cannot see why it failed. ⛔ **And the trigger is NOT imminent, which this row also claimed.** Measured: **nothing in `src/` or in any migration ever writes `DELEGATED` or `SKIPPED`** — both arrive only by hydration (`RecipientOutcome::from()` in the two Envelope adapters), so the population is **empty today** and no built feature creates it. The defensive read in `SigningSessionReader` is correct and stays; what this row describes is **latent**, waiting on whatever first closes a leg by reassignment. It has no owner. ⚑ Prefer a predicate over a status list when it lands: `RecipientOutcome::isTerminal()` already classifies all six cases exhaustively, so the question is already answered on the other side of the boundary — what is missing is only the crossing. ⚑ **Half closed 2026-09-16, and the halves are worth separating before anyone reads this row as done.** [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) / [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) crossed **step activeness** — `RecipientAuthContext::$stepIsActive`, a required member with no default (`322ba10`) — so this row's *"carries `envelopeIsAlive` … with nothing at all about the recipient's own leg"* is no longer true as written: it carries one fact about the leg, and both pre-credential routes refuse on it before a code is minted, sent or charged (`481a41d`). ⛔ **The outcome half is untouched and this row stays `Open` for it**: `RecipientOutcome` still never crosses, so a **DELEGATED** or **SKIPPED** leg is still invisible to the descriptor and the OTP send, and the useless billable ceremony this row describes still runs for them. ⚑ The constraint above binds the remaining half unchanged — the *landing* needs no repair, and the improvement must show up as a disappearing OTP, never as a changed destination. ⚑ And the shape is now precedented rather than hypothetical: the step fact crossed as a required member on the same DTO, so the outcome half is the same edit one field over. | ADR-0056's §3.3, found reading the sibling enum during that work; severity corrected by `f5sign-signer` the same day | Open | 2026-08-24 |
| BL-184 | ⛔ **A leaked API key can already complete a signature for any neutral-gate recipient in its tenant, using three shipped routes and nothing else.** `POST /api/v1/signing-tokens` mints any `(envelope, recipient)` token under `MACHINE_KEY` with no status or existence check; `POST /api/v1/signing/session` finds the access gate neutral — which is what `AuthRequirement::fromNeutralPair()` gives **every envelope authored to date** — lands `AUTH_PASSED` and mints the session credential, the server minting the refresh half itself so the caller supplies nothing; `POST /api/v1/signing/commit` with `consent_given: true` completes the act. The output is a PDF bearing the platform seal and a visible mark naming whoever the attacker chose. ⚑ **Measured 2026-08-24 by reading the three paths, not executed.** ⚑ **Distinct from its neighbours**: `BL-38` was about *authentication* and is closed; `BL-81` is that a leaked key cannot be revoked; `BL-73` is that nothing records which credential acted — this is what the key can *do* while live, and none of the three names it. ⚠ Compounds with `BL-61` (a session opens over a DRAFT envelope), which decides whether the sequence is loud — forging on a sent envelope notifies real recipients — or silent. ⚑ **Not created by delegated signature**, which adds no capability here: whoever holds the key also authors, and an author picks a declaration they can satisfy. Surfaced while designing it. | [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) §2.8 records why an entitlement scoped to one new route would not touch it, and preserves the two candidate shapes (per-key grants, a separate credential kind) for the increment that takes the question on. ⚑ **Narrowed 2026-08-25, not closed** ([ADR-0058](adr/ADR-0058-signature-requires-a-readable-envelope.md) / [TASK-035](tasks/TASK-035-signature-requires-readable-envelope.md)): all three links now refuse an envelope no recipient could have read, so the **silent** variant is gone — a forger must send the envelope for real, which notifies its actual recipients. The chain is untouched on an envelope that **was** sent and carries the default neutral gate, which is every envelope authored without a declared gate, so the capability survives and only its stealth does not. | Open (narrowed) | 2026-08-24 (delegated-signature design; traced from source, not executed) |
| BL-185 | ⛔ **Passing a NEUTRAL access gate leaves no fact in the permanent log, so the commonest ceremony there is has no evidence trail for its own authentication.** [`SigningSession::start()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Domain/Aggregate/SigningSession.php) stamps `authenticatedAt`, `accessGatePassedAt` and `DIRECT_LINK` on the row when the gate is open — passing it *is* arriving with the link (ADR-0028) — but records **no** [`AuthGatePassed`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Contract/Event/AuthGatePassed.php), while a *declared* gate records one on every pass. So `platform.event_log` can prove how a gated recipient authenticated and cannot prove it for an ungated one, which is precisely the population an evidence pull is largest over. ⚑ **The plumbing objection is gone and the decision is now the only thing left.** That event's docblock argued the omission partly on `startIfAbsent()` not draining; since TASK-034 it drains on the insert, so a fact recorded there would reach the log. What remains is a semantic call — emitting `AUTH_GATE_PASSED` for a gate nobody was asked to pass changes what an already-published event means to every existing reader, including [`OnAuthGatePassed`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Application/Subscriber/OnAuthGatePassed.php). ⚠ **It is NOT a gap in the sender's read model**: `access_method_executed` already publishes `DIRECT_LINK` there, carried by [`SigningSessionStarted`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Contract/Event/SigningSessionStarted.php). The gap is the audit trail, whose consumer is the not-yet-built projector. | [TASK-034](tasks/TASK-034-recipient-access-trace.md) §7 — kept out of that slice because changing a published event's semantics is a decision, not a slice; [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) is the consumer that would need it | Open | 2026-08-24 (surfaced by TASK-034, which retired the mechanical half of the original argument) |
| BL-187 | **The graphometric capture's units are decided and written down NOWHERE this repo can see, and data is already being emitted under them.** Until [TASK-053](tasks/TASK-053-the-recipients-drawn-signature-on-their-mark.md), `biometric_data` and `strokes` appeared in this backend in exactly one place — [`EgressAllowList`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Observability/Redaction/EgressAllowList.php), the list of fields that must never reach a log — and `canvas_width`, `canvas_height` and `device_dpi` in none: this row said the list held all five, and it held two (corrected by TASK-053, which adds the other three). ⚑ **Since TASK-053 the signing commit receives the block** (`POST /api/v1/signing/commit`, `biometric_data`) in this row's units, checks its shape and bounds and **discards it**: nothing is kept or logged (TASK-053 §4 D7).
