The units are now written in this repo, in `BiometricCaptureShape` and in the signer handoff, but no ADR fixes them, and [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) — which will have to interpret them once they are kept — is not started. ⚑ **The signer app chose, and is emitting as of 2026-08-24** (`sentry-f4`, via `useSignatureStore.setCanvasMetrics()`): `canvas_width`/`canvas_height` **and** `strokes[].x/y` in **logical CSS px**, `device_dpi` in **real DPI = 96 × devicePixelRatio**. ⛔ **The load-bearing half is that strokes and dimensions share one unit** — a mismatch there makes the series irreproducible and nothing detects it until somebody tries to redraw a stroke, which is years after the signature it evidences. ⚠ **`device_dpi` was travelling as `1`, and `canvas_width`/`canvas_height` as `0`, in production code** — only the tests ever wrote them — so **every graphometric series captured before that date has no scale at all**. That is not repairable retroactively. ⚑ **A correction worth recording, because this backend proposed the wrong fix**: told that `1` in a field named DPI is two orders of magnitude of lie, we suggested renaming it `device_pixel_ratio`. The signer declined with the better argument — the field is the resolution header of **ISO/IEC 39794-7**, which expresses it in DPI, so renaming would have aligned the name with our value while *misaligning the field with the standard it claims to implement*. Since a CSS px is 1/96 inch by definition, `96 × DPR` gives real DPI, keeps the ISO field, and leaves the ratio recoverable by dividing. ▶ **What would force it**: TASK-014 deciding what the evidence packet holds. Whoever builds it may pick another unit — but must do so knowing there are already rows written in this one, and re-scale rather than reinterpret. | Chosen by `f5sign-signer` (`sentry-f4`) 2026-08-24; asked of this repo, which had no answer to give | Open | 2026-08-24 |
| BL-188 | ⛔ **A recipient who has signed cannot get their sealed copy back, and the refusal that stops them was written for a different question.** Behind a **declared** access gate the credential is minted only by passing the gate; once the session is `COMPLETED`, [ADR-0053](adr/ADR-0053-access-grant-recovery.md) §2.3 row 1 refuses recovery outright, reasoning *"there is no act left for a re-earned grant to reach"*. ⚑ **But retrieving your own signed document is arguably an act**, and it is the one a finished signer most plausibly wants — so that clause answers *"may they sign again?"* (no, correctly) while being read as *"may they have anything?"* (which it never argued). ⚠ **The asymmetry makes it look like a bug even though it is not one**: a recipient whose access gate is **neutral** keeps their copy, because arriving with the link is passing that door and the open still mints ([ADR-0028](adr/ADR-0028-recipient-signing-token-auth.md)). So the same product behaves differently for two recipients who both did everything right, and the difference is a setting the *sender* chose. ⚑ **Measured consequence on the client, reported by `f5sign-signer` 2026-08-24**: their `/done` renders the signing date, the envelope and document names, and the download buttons — **all** from the read-model, behind the credential. Without it that signer lands on a correct but bare screen; `document_title` from route 3 keeps it from being anonymous and nothing more. ⛔ **Deliberately NOT folded into [ADR-0056](adr/ADR-0056-terminal-state-on-pre-credential-routes.md)**, on the consuming session's own advice: a bare `/done` is strictly better than a code field that can never work, so the routing fix should not wait on this. ▶ **What it needs is a decision, not code** — it reopens an accepted ADR in its own terms, so it is a new ADR or an amendment, never a silent widening of the recovery arm. Three shapes are visible and none has been costed: let a terminal session re-pass the access gate for a **read-only** grant; have the sender's completion notification carry the sealed copy (Notification's work, not Session's, and it exists nowhere today); or decide a bare `/done` is the product and say so in ADR-0056 so it is not reopened by accident. ⚠ **Parked by Daniel 2026-08-24** — recorded rather than pursued. The cost of parking is that a bare `/done` normalises and nobody looks again. ⚑ **Unparked 2026-10-22 as the sibling of [TASK-068](tasks/TASK-068-after-the-commit-the-signer-is-told-where-their-copy-stands.md)**: that task makes the signer wait in the ceremony until their copy is ready, and a signer who closes the tab during a long wait (DSS down) has no way back. The choice between the shapes above now also decides what the waiting screen says past about 5 minutes; if the copy goes by email, the owner's wording is *"your files will reach you by email once they are ready"*. Needs its own task and ADR. | Surfaced by `f5sign-signer` while wiring ADR-0056; the ADR-0053 tension is this repo's reading | Closed 2026-10-23: closed by TASK-070 ([ADR-0088](adr/ADR-0088-a-recipients-copy-is-read-through-its-own-link.md)): a read-only copy link, its gate passed again on a record of its own. The "Parked by Daniel 2026-08-24" and "Unparked 2026-10-22" sentences above are the history; the copy-by-email wording for the waiting screen is the notices' record (TASK-071) | 2026-08-24 |
| BL-189 | ⛔ **A ceremony driven from a server is capped at ten SMS an hour for the whole integration, and the eleventh recipient is the one refused.** `OtpSendKeys` spends every send against three counters and the third is `hash($request->getClientIp())`; consumption is cheapest-blast-radius-first with a refusal short-circuiting, so under machine-driven traffic the session and destination keys pass — each recipient is a different ceremony and a different handset — and the **source** key refuses. `otp_sms_source` is 10/hour and `otp_email_source` is 30. ⚑ **The key is not defective; it is being asked a question it was never designed for** — its own docblock says it names *"an unknown number of unrelated people behind one NAT"*, and an integrator's data centre is not that, it is every recipient of one customer. ⚠ Omitting the address is **not** the workaround: `OtpSendKeys::of()` degrades a null source to a shared `'unattributed'` bucket on purpose, so that an absent address cannot be the way around the ceiling. ⛔ Nothing can distinguish the two callers today — the send route declares `Authn::SIGNING_TOKEN`, so there is no proved API client to charge, and inferring one from an address or a header would be request input wearing identity's clothes (ADR-0044 §2.3). **Not reachable in production today** (no machine-driven ceremony exists), which is why it is a deferral rather than a defect. ⚑ **2026-10-20 ([TASK-062](tasks/TASK-062-each-gate-has-its-own-code-send-budget.md), ADR-0081 §2.3, accepted 2026-10-20):** `otp_sms_source` is 30/hour since then, not 10, and a machine-driven send has been keyed on the API client since TASK-033 (`otp_machine_sms_source`, 100/hour); a send spends four counters: session (per gate), narrow and wide destination, source. Whether this row closes is not decided here. | [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) §2.12 decides it; [TASK-033](tasks/TASK-033-delegated-signature.md) §3.7 builds it. ⚑ **The fix is a key change, not a number**: §2.12 declares the limiter a **safety valve** — loose enough that meeting it means something is looping — because a valve and a commercial ceiling had been sharing one number and no value for it stays right. ⛔ **The spend control is `BL-166`, not this row**: `IssueAuthChallengeUseCase` already calls `UsageMeterInterface::record()` per issued code and `CreateEnvelopeUseCase` already consults `PlanEnforcer`, so both seams exist and are called — only the persistence is missing. Until it lands there is **no** per-integrator spend control on a paid channel, which §2.12 takes deliberately and argues. A cost alarm on the fail-open window is the companion | Open | 2026-08-24 (surfaced while designing the delegated-signature slice). ⚠ **Renumbered from `BL-183` on 2026-08-25**: this row was written when a scan over every local and remote ref returned 182 as the highest, and a different `BL-183` — the closed-leg visibility row — was at that moment **uncommitted in a sibling worktree**, invisible to any scan. That one merged to `develop` first, so this one yields; the number is re-derived here across refs **and every working tree**, which is the only sweep that would have caught it; the numbers read from `config/packages/rate_limiter.yaml`, the key shape from `OtpSendKeys`) |
| BL-190 | ⛔ **A recipient whose ROLE has no ceremony — `CERTIFIED_DELIVERY`, `IN_PERSON_HOST` — is invited, opens a session, and passes a full authentication gate before anything looks at their role.** ⚑ **Measured 2026-08-25 by enumeration, not inference**: `Role` appears in **none** of `StartSessionUseCase`, `StartSessionController`, `GetAuthDescriptorController`, [`RecipientAuthContextReader`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/RecipientAuthContextReader.php) or `SigningAccessGrantReader`. The only `ROLE_NOT_INTERACTIVE` in the codebase is [`SigningSessionReader::role()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/SigningSessionReader.php), which serves the **read-model** — fetched after the gate. And the link reaches them in the first place because [`NotifyActivatedStepRecipientsUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Application/UseCase/NotifyActivatedStepRecipientsUseCase.php) filters on activated step and non-null email **and nothing else**; its own comment says so — *"Role-differentiated inclusion (signer vs CC) is deferred"*. ⛔ **Sibling of [BL-183](BACKLOG.md), NOT a duplicate, and the pairing is the useful part of this row.** That one is the recipient's **leg outcome** (DELEGATED / SKIPPED) missing from the pre-credential routes; this is their **role**. Same cost — a real code minted, metered and, on `OTP_SMS`, charged, sent to someone who by definition will never sign — same landing (the 409 arrives correctly, only far too late), and **the same seam**: crossing the missing fact into `RecipientAuthContext` and refusing on it at routes 2 and 3. ⛑ So BL-183's constraint binds here verbatim: the *landing* needs no repair and must not be disturbed, and the improvement must show up as a **disappearing OTP**, never as a changed destination. Whoever fixes either should fix both — crossing one field and not the other is two passes over one guard. ⚠ **What this one has that BL-183 does not is a cross-repo copy consequence.** `GET /signing/session/auth` publishes `document_title` with no role check, and `f5sign-signer` renders `auth.gateFor` — *«Vas a firmar: {title}»* / *«You are signing: {title}»* — unconditionally above the code field. So the served-legal-notice addressee, the one role for whom the whole point is that they do **not** sign and that the *opening* is the legally material event ([ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.1), is told they are about to sign. ⛔ **And their side cannot fix it alone today**: the role reaches a client through `signer.role` on the read-model, which is exactly what that screen has not fetched yet. So either the copy stops presupposing the act, or the descriptor grows the role — an additive contract change nobody has asked for yet. ⚑ **The set is wider than the two non-interactive roles, which is the trap worth recording** (the repo's authoring rule 5). [`Role::signs()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/Role.php) is `true` for **`SIGNER` alone**, and `SigningSessionReader::role()` admits five roles (folding `VIEWER` into `REVIEWER`): `APPROVER`, `REVIEWER`, `VIEWER` and `EDITOR` all reach that screen, sign nothing, and read the same sentence. A fix keyed on the two non-interactive roles leaves four behind. ▶ **What would force it**: the first `CERTIFIED_DELIVERY` envelope, which is expressible but neither built nor scheduled (ADR-0049 §2.1 — the reader refusal and the missing `ACKNOWLEDGED` outcome both stand); or the first `OTP_SMS` invoice showing codes sent to non-signing roles. ⚠ Surfaced by `f5sign-signer` asking us to confirm the opposite — that `CERTIFIED_DELIVERY` could not open a session — during the [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.1 OTP-copy split. The confirmation failed, which is how the row exists. | fold pending — home is `RecipientAuthContext` alongside [BL-183](BACKLOG.md) | Open | 2026-08-25 |
| BL-191 | ⛔ **`IN_PERSON_HOST` is in the live role vocabulary, is blocking, and has no ceremony — so the one flow it names cannot be run.** The forcing case is the eCMR delivery: the carrier reaches the dock, somebody comes out of the warehouse, and that person signs **on the carrier's device**. Nobody knows their name, e-mail or phone until that second and we will never hold a contact for them, so no gate [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) defines can be passed and whoever vouches for the identity is the host rather than us. ADR-0049 §2.11 names it as one of six cases the two-gate model was not cut against — *"in-person signing, where the device belongs to the host and not the signer"* — and [`identity-access-domain-model.md`](ddd/identity-access-domain-model.md) leaves the substance open: *"What assurance does vouching confer? Unspecified in either lineage."* ⚑ **What exists, measured 2026-08-25 by enumeration**: [`Role::IN_PERSON_HOST`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/Role.php) with `isBlocking()` **true** and `signs()` **false** — it holds the step open and rubricates nothing — plus [`SigningSessionReader::role()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/SigningSessionReader.php) refusing it `roleNotInteractive`. Whoever fixes BL-190 has to refuse the role at the **gate** while leaving the **session** reachable; fixing it as a blanket role refusal makes this unbuildable behind a guard nobody remembers is there. ⚠ **It is also the first flow that would capture an identity document.** The receiver's DNI arrives as a typed value nobody verifies, and [`FieldCipher`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Crypto/FieldCipher.php) has **no production caller** (measured: the only files naming it are its own, `KeyProvider` and their two unit tests) while [ADR-0033](adr/ADR-0033-pii-field-encryption-at-rest.md) is still `Proposed` — so the substrate is here and the carrier is not. ADR-0055 §2.5 already claims to be that cipher's first caller; both want one decision and it should be taken once rather than twice. ⚠ **ADR-0055 is named unlinked on purpose** — it exists only on the unmerged `docs/delegated-signature` branch, so a link here would dangle; linkify both mentions in this row when that branch lands. ⚑ **Deliberately distinguished from *claimable*, which shares the same MVP and is not this.** Claimable fills a recipient's details after send, over the integrator's API, and everything downstream is the ceremony that already exists. This is a ceremony that does not exist, driven from our own signer portal by a party who is not the signatory. Neither record could see the other's condition when it was written, so neither is at fault and neither owns the consequence. This row owns it. ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-28: one of those two has landed, and it was not the slice this row named.** The clause above read as two pending conditions; the claimable one shipped in `b0c5eb5` on `feat/claimable-recipient-slot` as part of [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md), not TASK-037, and not from a `docs/` branch — it was pulled forward because it was a live credential leak (`BL-194`). So the enumeration two sentences up is also short by one: that fan-out carries **four** `continue`s today — step ordinal, `RecipientView::isUnclaimedSlot()`, `email === null`, and the find-or-skip — which by ADR-0057 §2.7's own counting convention is the **third** recipient-property filter. ⛔ **The threshold is therefore one condition away rather than two, and TASK-033 alone now crosses it**: `signsVia` would be the fourth filter, which is precisely the *"move the question rather than add to the list"* case. Envelope is where roles, gates, claims and delegation already live; Notification is a category-(c) supporting BC ([ADR-0037](adr/ADR-0037-notification-supporting-bc.md)) and every condition added here is a piece of another context's model in the wrong home. ⚑ **Deliberately deferred, with a named trigger rather than an open one**, because the shape depends on how the four conditions settle once both branches land, and because a cross-BC concept move reviewed as a rider on a delegated-signature PR gets reviewed by somebody thinking about delegated signature. **Act on whichever comes first: a fifth condition, or [TASK-031](tasks/TASK-031-channel-selection-beyond-email.md) deleting the `email === null` skip** — that deletion is already known to make the claimable exclusion load-bearing (`BL-194`, on the sibling branch), and it is the moment the stack stops being merely untidy. ⚠ Whoever picks it up decides then whether it needs an ADR: it likely *reduces* what Notification knows rather than opening a dependency, but repo rule 7 governs the call, not this row. ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-31: the threshold is no longer approaching, it is crossed.** The clause above read *"`signsVia` **would be** the fourth filter"* — future tense — and counted **four** `continue`s in that fan-out. So this row's trigger is now **live rather than pending**, and it is the only place the move should start from. | BL-200 | ⛔ **Presigned-direct download needs a tenant check where the URL is MINTED, and [ADR-0016](adr/ADR-0016-storage-model.md) defers presigned on capacity and integrity grounds without ever naming tenancy.** Object-storage isolation lives entirely in RLS today: the key the adapter mints is `<zone>/<tenant>/envelope/<envelope>/document/<document>/<16 random bytes>` ([`S3ObjectStore`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Storage/S3ObjectStore.php)), so **the tenant is in the key and nothing reads it back** — `put()` takes `$tenantId`, `get(string $storageKey)` takes nothing else, and the port is asymmetric ([`ObjectStore`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Storage/ObjectStore.php)). ⚑ **The obvious repair — pass the tenant to `get()` and assert the second segment — does NOTHING for the path that is actually coming.** Under presigned-direct the client fetches from object storage itself; `get()` is not in that path and never executes. The check has to sit at mint time, on whatever issues the URL. Fixing only `get()` would harden the byte-path ADR-0016 calls *provisional v1* and leave the successor open. ⛑ **The mint-time check is cheap precisely because the tenant is already in the key** — which makes the key hierarchy (is there a `client` level? does document type belong in it?) a decision that must land **before the first production PUT**: the buckets carry Object Lock COMPLIANCE for 5 years, so the layout cannot be renamed, moved or deleted for that long. ⚑ **The rule that DOES survive the successor is directional, and it is the one to write** (reformulated by the `f5sign-infra` session after the first attempt was rejected): *the storage key is never **accepted** from outside — it is only ever read from a row the caller is authorised to see.* Emitting it is safe; accepting it is the danger. And that phrasing carries the mint-time requirement rather than contradicting it: *read from an authorised row* **is** the check presigned needs. ⚠ **Do NOT write the non-directional form — 'the storage key never crosses the API boundary'.** Measured 2026-08-27 it holds — zero files under any `*/UI/`, zero presenters and zero request DTOs mention `storageKey` — but that is a property of a byte-path ADR-0016 marks *provisional* and plans to replace, not a guarantee. Recording it as an invariant would hand whoever implements presigned a rule that is false by design, and a broken rule reads as an obsolete one. ⛔ **And ADR-0016 has two decisions that were never reconciled with each other.** Its #6 mints a structured key and justifies the shape as *"operational legibility without layout coupling"* — legible for an **operator**; its #4 plans presigned-direct, which hands that same key to the **end user** inside a signed URL. The ADR notes the tension nowhere. Two consequences, both live: the key becomes external surface the day presigned lands (today it is UUIDs, so the exposure is internal identifiers — but a hierarchy that grew with **names** rather than opaque ids would publish customer or department names in URLs), and the recipient case is the sharpest, since [`SigningDocumentReader`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/SigningDocumentReader.php) serves a signer who is **not** a user of the tenant and would receive the tenant's own UUID in the URL. ⚑ **Correction to the framing this row was filed under**: the layout is **not** frozen by the first PUT. Keys are minted per object, so new objects can take a new shape tomorrow; what Object Lock freezes is *the objects already written*, which keep theirs for five years. The real cost of changing later is therefore **coexistence** — every site that parses a key must handle both shapes for at least that long. Measured 2026-08-27: exactly **one** line parses a key anywhere in `src/` — `explode('/', $storageKey, 2)[0]` in `S3ObjectStore::zoneFromKey()`, reading the zone only — so the cost is near zero today and grows with every parser added. Adding the tenant assertion is what makes the second segment load-bearing, and therefore what makes a later layout change expensive. ⚠ **Neither level under discussion exists in the domain**: `client` resolves only to [`ApiClientId`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Identity/ApiClientId.php) — an integration credential, not a customer — and `Document` carries `mimeType` and no business type at all. Putting either in the key means minting an identifier for a concept the model does not have yet. ▶ What would make any of this enforceable rather than written: a `StorageKey` value object. Today it is a bare `string` in all five call sites, so a PHPStan rule could only key on a property NAME — which goes silent on the rename (the repo's authoring rule 5). Raised by the `f5sign-infra` session while provisioning Linode Object Storage, whose limited-access keys are per-bucket with no prefix condition, so this cannot be expressed as a credential boundary the way an AWS `s3:prefix` policy would. **Updated 2026-09-04 from ADR-0063's working notes:** two of this row's premises moved. The tenant has left the object key (`ObjectKey::fromIdentities()` is `<custodyId>/<placementId>`, ADR-0063 §2.2), so *"the tenant is already in the key"* is false and prefix-based isolation is impossible by construction; the mint-time check reads the custody row, which carries the tenant. And per-tenant containers became expressible with the container reshape: on Linode they are the only way to make tenant isolation a credential boundary rather than an application one, at the cost of a bucket per tenant with Object Lock decided at creation, so provisioning a tenant becomes a storage operation that can fail. Recorded as an option this row's fix now has, not a lean. **Updated 2026-09-08 from the architecture pass:** a third premise moved, and it narrows what this row owes. *"Today it is a bare `string` in all five call sites"* is no longer the state of the tree: `ObjectKey` is a value object ([ADR-0063](adr/ADR-0063-where-storage-concerns-belong.md) 2.2), and every `ObjectStore` call site has moved across to the Storage module's own port (`config/services.yaml:447-450` records eight), leaving [`ObjectStore`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Storage/ObjectStore.php) wired for its own integration test and nothing else. The bare `string` survives only there -- `S3ObjectStore::get(string $storageKey)` at :73 and `zoneFromKey()` at :101. So what is still owed is not the value object but the **rule** that stops a key being reconstructed as a string ([ADR-0063](adr/ADR-0063-where-storage-concerns-belong.md) 5, *Enforced by*); if `ObjectStore` retires, this row's remaining surface retires with it. | [ADR-0016](adr/ADR-0016-storage-model.md) + [`ObjectStore`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Storage/ObjectStore.php) | Open | 2026-08-27 |
| BL-201 | ⚠ **Dos «30 días» independientes mantienen firmable un envelope, por coincidencia y con margen cero.** [`CreateEnvelopeController`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/CreateEnvelopeController.php) fija `expiresAt = issuedAt + 30 days` **a fuego**; el lifecycle `expire-originals-30d` borra los objetos de `originals` a los **30 días de su propio PUT**. <!-- OFFREPO: el lifecycle vive en el repo hermano de infra `../f5sign-infra/docker/minio/lifecycle-originals.json`; no alcanzable con acceso agent-only al backend. --> Y la firma lee la **punta**, que en un documento sin firmar **es el original en `originals`** ([`DocumentSigner`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/Service/DocumentSigner.php), §*Tip, not original*). Hoy no rompe porque el upload (`POST /envelopes/{id}/documents/{documentId}/content`) es una llamada **estrictamente posterior** a la creación, así que el reloj del objeto arranca igual o después que el del sobre: **el margen es exactamente (instante de subida − instante de creación), que puede ser ~0.** ⛔ **Nada conecta los dos números** — viven en repos distintos, ninguno cita al otro, y ningún test cubre la relación. ⚑ **Y `CreateEnvelope` está diseñado para que dejen de coincidir**: su docblock dice que `$expiresAt` es *"the caller's to choose"*, y el Pilar 7 §C.5 define un modelo de retención configurable en cuatro niveles. El día que la caducidad sea un parámetro mayor de 30 días, el original desaparece bajo los pies del firmante. ⛔ **Y no hay reparación**: [`Document::appendArtifact()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Entity/Document.php) lanza `ArtifactAlreadyAppendedException` si ya existe un artefacto con el mismo `ProducedBy`, y una resubida vuelve a ser `ProducedBy::upload()` — el hueco del original queda **ocupado para siempre** y el envelope, infirmable. El fallo sale como **5xx** y no como 4xx, porque `StorageException` se deja escapar a propósito (un objeto ilegible es falta de infraestructura, no resultado de dominio). ⚑ **Y el spec ya no describe lo implementado**: el Pilar 7 §B.2 pide para `originals` *"retención durante la vida del envelope + 30 días post-cierre"*, y lo que hay son 30 días planos desde el PUT — coinciden solo si la vida del sobre es ~0. ▶ Lo barato es **acoplar los dos números explícitamente** (derivar la ventana del lifecycle de la caducidad máxima admitida, o rechazar un `expiresAt` que se salga de ella) en vez de dejar que se crucen en silencio. ⚠ **Cross-repo** (`f5sign-infra`). ⚑ Encontrado analizando la unidad de purga del rediseño de retención, y **no viaja con esa decisión**: es independiente y anterior a ella. ✅ **ACTUALIZADO 2026-09-01: el segundo reloj ya no existe, así que la carrera está eliminada y no documentada.** El rediseño de contenedores dejó `f5sign-staging` y `f5sign-retained` **sin ninguna regla de lifecycle** — bajo el versioning que Object Lock impone, una `Expiration.Days` no borra: deja un delete marker. Así que el original ya no desaparece bajo los pies del firmante en ningún plazo, y la caducidad del sobre puede pasar a ser un parámetro sin acoplarla a nada. ⚠ **Y la fila NO se cierra, porque el residuo es el opuesto**: sin regla, hoy **nada** borra los bytes de `staging`. Quien lo hará es la aplicación, vía el remedio de [`PendingCustodySweep`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/PendingCustodySweep.php), que está deliberadamente sin construir — su docblock lleva las dos razones. Lo que queda de esta fila es eso, más el desajuste con el Pilar 7 §B.2 (*"retención durante la vida del envelope + 30 días post-cierre"*), que sigue sin implementarse. **Actualizado 2026-09-04:** la mitad con bytes ya tiene sweep: `app:envelope:discard-abandoned-uploads` (ADR-0062 §2.5) descarta el original de todo sobre ENVIADO que se cerró sin firma (anulado o caducado), a diario desde el custodian. Los borradores nunca enviados quedan fuera a propósito, porque nada hace cumplir su caducidad (BL-202) y descartar su original reproduciría el defecto de esta fila; decidir si un borrador caduca es de producto. **Actualizado 2026-09-29 (TASK-049):** un borrador ya no lleva fecha alguna (la vida se resuelve al enviar), así que sigue sin caducar y la decisión de producto sigue abierta. Lo que sigue abierto aquí: el remedio de `PendingCustodySweep` (filas sin bytes) y el §B.2. ⚑ Medido de paso: `expire-originals-30d` **no había expirado nada nunca, en ningún entorno** — el MinIO de dev tenía versioning encendido sobre `originals` y el object storage de producción no se había provisionado jamás. | fold pending — el remedio del sweep de `staging` ([`PendingCustodySweep`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/PendingCustodySweep.php)); ya no acoplado a `f5sign-infra` | Open | 2026-08-27 (análisis de la unidad de purga) |
| BL-202 | ⛔ **Seis de los once `EnvelopeStatus` no los escribe nadie, y `expiresAt` no hace nada.** Censado 2026-08-27 sobre cada asignación: el agregado hace **cinco** — `DRAFT` (construcción), `SENT`, `READY_TO_SEAL`, `VOIDED`, `COMPLETED`. Sin escritor quedan `SEALING`, `DECLINED`, `EXPIRED`, `CORRECTING`, `ROUTING_FAILED` y `SEALING_FAILED`; de esos, **cuatro no aparecen ni una vez en `src/`** — ni escritos ni leídos ([`EnvelopeStatus`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/EnvelopeStatus.php)). ⚑ **Lo que lo hace fila y no «aún no construido» son los dos que SÍ se leen**, porque son guardas que no pueden dispararse nunca: [`Envelope::complete()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Aggregate/Envelope.php) acepta `SEALING` como estado previo válido —rama inalcanzable, y **documentada como deliberada**—, y [`StepCompletionEvaluator`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Service/StepCompletionEvaluator.php) ramifica sobre `EXPIRED`. Es la forma que nombra la regla de autoría 5 del repo: *"un conjunto exento vacío hoy no devuelve nada y se lee como todo-en-orden; es lo contrario — nada PUEDE ponerse rojo, así que el verde es la señal esperada, no evidencia"*. ⛔ **Y el que muerde: `expiresAt` es decoración.** Se acepta, se persiste (`Version20260713000002`), se congela en el SEND como inmutable (invariante 7 de [`envelope-domain-model.md`](ddd/envelope-domain-model.md)) — **y no lo consume nada**. Un sobre pasada su fecha se queda en `SENT` indefinidamente: sigue siendo firmable, sigue mandando recordatorios, y el remitente cree razonablemente que caducó. Y [`CreateEnvelopeController`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/CreateEnvelopeController.php) la fija a `+30 días` **a fuego**, así que hoy **todos** los sobres llevan una fecha que no significa nada. ▶ Lo que hace falta no es escribir los seis: es decidir cuáles son estados reales y **cuáles sobran del enum**. Un miembro sin escritor es una promesa de comportamiento que el código no cumple, y se lee desde fuera (dashboard, OpenAPI) como si existiera. ⚑ Encontrado diseñando la promoción *stage-then-promote* del rediseño de retención: la regla de qué punta se conserva **no puede colgar de `status = 'EXPIRED'`** —que nadie escribe— sino de `expiresAt < now()`; usar el estado haría que la promoción **no ocurriera jamás para los sobres abandonados, y en verde**. ⚠ **No viaja con esa decisión**: es anterior e independiente. 2026-09-03: el primer lector de `expiresAt` existe. El predicado de promoción (`PromotableDocuments`, `Version20260908000004`) lo usa como cierre de seguridad: un sobre firmado y caducado se promociona aunque ningún estado terminal lo diga. Lo que esta fila pide, decidir qué miembros sobran del enum, sigue abierto. **Updated 2026-09-23 (TASK-046):** `DECLINED` is gone — [ADR-0070](adr/ADR-0070-an-envelope-ends-in-agreement-or-is-voided-and-sealed.md) §2.1 retired it, since an envelope that ends without agreement ends `VOIDED` with a cause. The enum has ten members; five are written, and `SEALING`, `EXPIRED`, `CORRECTING`, `ROUTING_FAILED` and `SEALING_FAILED` remain this row's question. **Updated 2026-09-28** ([ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md), TASK-048, `feat/task-048-how-an-envelope-ends`, merged into `develop` on 2026-09-30 at `e6a0af5e`): `VOIDED` is retired too, and with it the one field that answered both *which phase* and *what result* — a sender's void is now `SENT` and closed with `outcome: NOT_AGREED`, `end_cause: SENDER_VOIDED`, then `COMPLETED` with the same outcome (see [`EnvelopeStatus`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/EnvelopeStatus.php)'s own docblock). The enum now has **nine** members, not the ten the 2026-09-23 update counted, and **four** are written — `DRAFT`, `SENT`, `READY_TO_SEAL`, `COMPLETED` — not five; `SEALING`, `EXPIRED`, `CORRECTING`, `ROUTING_FAILED` and `SEALING_FAILED` are still this row's question. And the 2026-09-03 addendum above is itself superseded: `PromotableDocuments` no longer reads `expiresAt` as a security backstop at all — `Version20260928131928` re-keyed both storage funnels onto `completed_at`/`outcome` instead (every sent envelope now reaches `completed_at`, expiry included) and revoked the column's grant to the custodian, and `Version20260928142509` re-keyed them again onto whether anybody *engaged* (`docs/LIVE_SCHEMA.md`'s envelope funnels note). What this row asks — deciding which `EnvelopeStatus` members are real — is unaffected and still open. **Updated 2026-09-29** ([TASK-049](tasks/TASK-049-an-envelope-expires.md), `feat/envelope-expiry`, merged into `develop` on 2026-09-30 at `e6a0af5e`; [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.12/§2.13): **the expiry half is closed.** `expiresAt` is no longer decoration: the sender chooses a life (`expires_in_days`, 1–180, default 30, counted from the send), `Envelope::acceptsActs()` reads the date through `app_now()`, and a per-minute sweep (`app:envelope:sweep-clocks`) ends a sent envelope past its date as `COMPLETED` with `end_cause: EXPIRED` (or `AGREED` when its last step is satisfied). `EnvelopeStatus::EXPIRED` is retired, so the enum now has **eight** members and **four** are written (`DRAFT`, `SENT`, `READY_TO_SEAL`, `COMPLETED`); `SEALING`, `CORRECTING`, `ROUTING_FAILED` and `SEALING_FAILED` are what this row still asks about. Every earlier line of this row that says `expiresAt` is enforced by nothing, or that an expired envelope stays `SENT`, describes the state before that task. | fold pending — [`EnvelopeStatus`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/EnvelopeStatus.php) + [`envelope-domain-model.md`](ddd/envelope-domain-model.md) | Open | 2026-08-27 (censo de estados, diseñando stage-then-promote) |
| BL-203 | ⚠ **Two [ADR-0060](adr/ADR-0060-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) properties hold by construction and no test names either.** **(1) A renewal cannot launder a retired credential**: [`RenewSessionCredentialUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/RenewSessionCredentialUseCase.php) carries `agp` forward from the expired credential rather than re-stamping it, so a renewed credential is refused at the same three crossings as the one it replaces — but nothing asserts it, and re-stamping `agp` to `now` is precisely the change that reads like a tidy-up. The day someone makes it, retirement becomes a fifteen-minute inconvenience with every gate green. **(2) A reset does not revoke the signing token**: nothing on the reset path touches `session.signing_token`, and the nearest assertion — [`GatedSigningCeremonyHttpTest::a_reset_before_the_recipient_ever_opened_the_link_is_in_force_when_they_do`](../tests/F5Sign/Session/Acceptance/GatedSigningCeremonyHttpTest.php) — covers a recipient who never opened the link, not one whose credential was just retired, which is the population §2.6's asymmetry exists to serve. ⚑ **Both are cheap and neither is speculative**: mint → retire → renew → assert refusal; and mint → reset → re-open the same token → assert a fresh credential that reads. ⛔ Filed rather than built because ADR-0060 §5 must not claim an assertion that does not exist — under-claiming is the safe direction and this row is what keeps §5 honest. | [ADR-0060](adr/ADR-0060-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) §5 | Open | 2026-08-28 (closing TASK-039) |
| BL-204 | ⛔ **`RecipientClaimed` is the first event [ADR-0032](adr/ADR-0032-evidence-pii-custody.md) decision 7 names that actually ships, so its decision-8 window stops being hypothetical today.** ⚑ **This row was filed on 2026-08-28 saying ADR-0032 and [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) *"describe the same event with different contents"* and that one must lose. That framing was wrong and is corrected here the same day** — it was written from a summary rather than from decision 7, whose title is *"which fact carries the **ciphered** identity"*. ADR-0032 never proposed a plaintext name on the wire: its whole subject is PII **enciphered at rest in the log**, under a per-envelope DEK wrapped by a per-tenant KEK, erased by **crypto-shred** — destroy the key, keep the bytes and their commitments — with decision 8's `erased` sentinel keeping the event rehydratable afterwards. ⛑ **Crypto-shred is precisely the answer to §2.14's objection**, which is that an append-only row has no delete path. So the two records are not in conflict; they describe **different times**. ⚠ **[ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.14 is right for today and what shipped is correct**: [`FieldCipher`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Crypto/FieldCipher.php) has zero callers, [ADR-0033](adr/ADR-0033-pii-field-encryption-at-rest.md) is `Proposed` and [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) is not started, so there is no key to destroy and an identity written there would be permanent. [`RecipientClaimed`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Event/RecipientClaimed.php) therefore carries no name, e-mail or phone: four keys for a claim logged before [ADR-0083](adr/ADR-0083-a-recipients-gates-are-editable-until-they-enter.md) §2.6, and five since, adding `access_gate_was_open` (the access gate's openness at the claim, not identity; omitted when `null`), with `RecipientClaimedTest::its_payload_key_set_is_closed` asserting equality over `array_keys()` for both sets. ⛔ **What IS owed is decision 8's own open sub-decision, and this event is its first live instance.** That clause records that facts appended before the cipher exists take *Path B* with the evidential field **absent**, that [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) **forbids** copy-transform / stream-rewrite because it would mutate an immutable record and break its `sys_commitment`, and that such facts are therefore **permanently un-rehydratable**. It closes on *"caught while the log-carrier machinery is still unbuilt, which is the cheap moment to decide"* — and every claim appended from the moment TASK-036 merges until the cipher lands is one of them. ⚑ **Nothing breaks and nothing is wrong**; what changes is that the window now has traffic in it. ▶ **The trigger is whoever starts TASK-014**, and the decision is decision 8's three candidates, not a fold of one ADR into the other: a third rehydration state keyed on `schema_version` (pre-cipher = legitimately-clear), an upcaster supplying the distinction at read time, or accepting the loss and bounding it by shortening the Path-B window. ⚑ Its sibling in decision 7, `RecipientAdded`, does not exist yet (the INV-B gap), so it adds no traffic to the window. | [ADR-0032](adr/ADR-0032-evidence-pii-custody.md) decision 8's open sub-decision — its first shipped instance | Open | 2026-08-28 (closing TASK-036) |
| BL-205 | ⛔ **A claimable slot whose declared gate the claimant cannot satisfy WAS STRANDED (resolved on `feat/gates-editable-before-entry` by ADR-0083, see the 2026-10-20 note below): 422 for ever, no route could amend the gate, and the only exit was `void()` — which discards the consignor's already-collected signature.** [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.13 defers [`DeclaredMethodDestinations::guard()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Application/Service/DeclaredMethodDestinations.php) for a claimable slot and re-runs it at the claim, justified in [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md) §4 D6 as *"a refused claim consumes none of the once-only fill, so fixing the body and retrying is free."* ⛑ **That holds when the BODY is wrong. It fails when the PARTY cannot satisfy the gate**, and the difference is that a body is retryable and a person is not. ⚑ **Traced 2026-08-28 entirely through the public API, no internals**: author `claimable: true` with `auth.access_method: OTP_SMS` and no phone → **201**, because the guard is deferred; `send`; claim naming the consignee, who has a landline or no phone at all → **422 `RECIPIENT_CANNOT_RECEIVE_METHOD`**, and the guard runs *before* the mutation so the slot is still unclaimed; try the correction route → **409 `RECIPIENT_SLOT_NOT_CLAIMED`**, correctly, because a correction changes where a **named** party is reached and there is none; look for a route that amends `authRequirement` → **there is none**: [`config/routes/api/envelope.php`](../config/routes/api/envelope.php) declared 17 routes when this row was written (18 since `b9138c29`, 2026-08-31), all `GET` or `POST`, with no `PUT`, `PATCH` or `DELETE` anywhere. The leg stays `PENDING`, the step's ending rule (`Envelope::advance()`, asking `EnvelopeEndingEvaluator`) closes a step only when its blocking members' legs are closed — and the slot is one of them — so the step never closes, the envelope never seals, and `void()` is the only write left. ⚠ **The escape an integrator will actually take is worse than the deadlock**: claim the slot naming a phone they control, which unsticks the envelope and puts the wrong party in the evidence — defeating the *"who was this addressed to?"* question the whole feature exists to answer. ⚑ **For an ordinary recipient the identical authoring is refused at the `POST …/recipients` call itself**, where the sender is present and the fix costs nothing. **The deferral is not what creates the bad state; it is what moves the refusal past the point of repair** — which is the general shape worth carrying, since every future *"defer this check to a later actor"* has it. ▶ **Three candidate resolutions, recorded without choosing, because the choice is a product decision**: **(a)** a route that amends `authRequirement` **while the slot is unclaimed** — smallest, but it opens the first post-`send()` write to the gate declaration, which [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) has never contemplated and which needs its own reasoning about credential retirement; **(b)** refuse at authoring any claimable slot whose declared gate a claim could not **always** satisfy — i.e. any gate naming a method with a non-`null` `requiredDestination()` — which is enforceable in one predicate and **kills the eCMR case §2.13 and D6 exist for**, since `OTP_SMS` to a consignee is exactly the motivating shape; **(c)** accept it as a known limitation with the warning published on `AddRecipientRequest::$claimable`'s `#[OA\*]` and in the frontend handoff, so an integrator can at least see the trap before they set it. ⛔ **(c) is only honest if the warning is published**; leaving it here alone is (c) without the warning. ⚑ **2026-10-20 — decided and built, a widened (a): [ADR-0083](adr/ADR-0083-a-recipients-gates-are-editable-until-they-enter.md) ([TASK-064](tasks/TASK-064-the-gates-are-editable-until-the-recipient-enters.md)).** `PUT /api/v1/envelopes/{id}/recipients/{recipientId}/auth` replaces a recipient's gates, for every recipient and not only a slot, until they first open their link, and an unclaimed slot cannot have opened one. So `void()` is no longer the only exit: the sender narrows the slot's gate to what the claimant can receive (`[OTP_EMAIL, OTP_SMS]` to `[OTP_EMAIL]`) and the claim then passes, because it re-runs the guard on the declaration stored when it runs (`RecipientAuthRequirementStoriesHttpTest::s1_a_stranded_slot_is_narrowed_claimed_entered_and_signed`). The credential-retirement question (a) raised does not arise, because before the first opening no session or credential exists (ADR-0083 §1). The text above describes the state before that branch; the escape it warns about (claiming with a phone the integrator controls) is still possible, and is now unnecessary. ⚑ **2026-11-04 — that resolution held only while nobody called a machine route on the slot**: one call stamped `first_opened_at`, the gate change then answered `RECIPIENT_ALREADY_ENTERED`, and the slot was stranded again (`BL-528`). [TASK-078](tasks/TASK-078-the-machine-routes-refuse-an-unclaimed-slot.md) closes that door for new calls. A slot already stamped stays stranded, counted rather than repaired (its §7). | [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.13 + [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md) §8; resolved by [ADR-0083](adr/ADR-0083-a-recipients-gates-are-editable-until-they-enter.md) | Decided — built on `feat/gates-editable-before-entry`, merge pending | 2026-08-28 (post-merge audit of `feat/claimable-recipient-slot`) |
| BL-206 | ⚠ **`resetAccessCode()` and `unlockAuth()` are unguarded on an unclaimed slot, and both write `RecipientAuthUnlocked` onto the append-only log — a recorded fact about a party who does not exist.** [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §1 names all **three** sender-side post-`send()` gestures together — `correctContact()`, `resetAccessCode()`, `unlockAuth()` — as the ones that *"adjust a recipient without saying who they are"*, and §2.3 then guards exactly **one** of them. ⚑ **Measured 2026-08-28**: `Envelope::resetRecipientAccessCode()` and `Envelope::unlockRecipientAuth()` both carry a single guard, `status->isTerminal()`, and both call `recordAuthUnlock()` unconditionally; neither asks `Recipient::isUnclaimedSlot()`. So `POST …/recipients/{id}/access-code` on an unclaimed slot returns a fresh code for nobody, and `POST …/recipients/{id}/auth-unlock` records a lockout-lifted milestone against a party with no identity. ⛑ **Material harm today is low, and each reason is a separate fact that could change**: nothing subscribes to `RecipientAuthUnlocked` (`rg RecipientAuthUnlocked src/` reaches only the aggregate and the event class), `credentialsRetiredAt` is monotonic-forward so retiring a credential that was never minted is inert, and a null verifier fails closed at `SigningSession::submitAuthResponse()` (`AuthGateRefusedException::gateHasNoVerifier`). ⛔ **The asymmetry is still ours to explain or close, and explaining is a legitimate answer**: `unlockAuth()`'s own docblock already argues that a refusal keyed on state the sender cannot see would be *"a 409 with no remedy"*, and the same argument may cover this. What is not legitimate is leaving three gestures named as one family with one of them guarded and no record of why. ▶ **Whichever way it goes, the decision belongs on ADR-0057 §2.3** — which today reads as though it had considered the family and it considered one member. ⚠ The evidence half is the part that does not fail closed: [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md)'s log is append-only, so a `RecipientAuthUnlocked` written for an unclaimed slot cannot be withdrawn once TASK-014's projector starts reading it. | [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.3 | Open | 2026-08-28 (post-merge audit of `feat/claimable-recipient-slot`) |
| BL-208 | ⚠ **An `ACCESS_CODE`-gated claimable slot needs a second call nothing in the claim contract mentions, and the deferred destination guard cannot see the gap.** [`ClaimRecipientRequest`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/Request/ClaimRecipientRequest.php) carries `full_name`, `email`, `phone` and `locale` and no code; and `AuthMethod::ACCESS_CODE->requiredDestination()` is **`null`**, so `DeclaredMethodDestinations::guard()` — the whole of what [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.13 defers and re-runs at the claim — returns without asking anything. ⛑ **Not a dead end, and this row says so rather than overstating it.** The code is settable, twice over: at authoring (supplied, or server-generated and returned **once** in the 201 body) and afterwards through `POST …/recipients/{recipientId}/access-code`. ⛔ **But the natural pattern is unavailable by construction**: `AddRecipientRequest`'s own `#[OA\*]` recommends supplying a code *"only when the signer already knows the value (the common case being a national id)"* — and on a claimable slot there is no signer to know it, which is the entire premise. So the working flow is *claim, then reset the code, then transmit it out of band*, and **no part of the claim contract, ADR-0057, or the frontend handoff says so**. ⚠ The failure is quiet rather than loud: the claim returns `204`, the party is named, and the gate refuses at `SigningSession::submitAuthResponse()` with `gateHasNoVerifier` — fail-closed, correct, and surfaced to the **signer** long after the sender has moved on, which is the exact shape `DeclaredMethodDestinations` was written to prevent for delivered methods. ▶ **The question to settle**: whether the claim carries an optional `access_code` (one call, symmetric with the other gate kinds, but it puts a secret in the claim body), or whether the two-call flow is simply **published** on the claim route's `#[OA\*]` and in §2.13. ⚑ **Related but distinct from [`BL-205`](BACKLOG.md)**: there the gate is one the party *cannot* satisfy and the envelope strands; here it is one the *contract* cannot express, and the repair exists but is undocumented. ⚑ **2026-10-20 — narrowed, not closed, by [ADR-0083](adr/ADR-0083-a-recipients-gates-are-editable-until-they-enter.md) ([TASK-064](tasks/TASK-064-the-gates-are-editable-until-the-recipient-enters.md)).** A sender may now *introduce* `ACCESS_CODE` on a slot after the claim and before the recipient's first opening, through `PUT …/recipients/{recipientId}/auth` with the `access_code` the now-known party already holds, so the natural pattern exists for a slot authored without the method. It stays open because a slot that declared `ACCESS_CODE` at authoring still needs *claim, then reset* (that route refuses a supplied code for a declaration that already names the method, 422 `ACCESS_CODE_ALREADY_DECLARED`), the claim body still carries no code, and the claim route's `#[OA\*]` still does not say so. | [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.13 | Open | 2026-08-28 (post-merge audit of `feat/claimable-recipient-slot`) |
| BL-209 | ⛑ **`OpenApiSpecTest` gates the `ProblemCode` catalog globally and nothing gates it PER ROUTE, so a published `#[OA\*]` can enumerate a subset of the codes its own route emits and stay green in both directions.** The global half is real and load-bearing: that test asserts the yaml enum equals the set every `ProblemCoded` implementor carries, so a code named in the spec that nothing emits fails as hard as the reverse. What it cannot see is **which route** emits which — so a route description that says *"`code` **is** `X`"* passes while a second code reaches that same route. ⚑ **Measured on `feat/claimable-recipient-slot`, which produced two instances in one branch, both on strings emitted verbatim into the spec the frontend ratifies**: `MintSigningTokenController`'s 409 said `code` **is** `ENVELOPE_NOT_SENT` — a closed singular — while the branch added `RECIPIENT_SLOT_NOT_CLAIMED` to that exact route through its only collaborator; and `CorrectRecipientController`'s 409 enumerated two codes where a third had landed. ⛔ **Neither was reachable by any sweep**, which is why this is a check rather than a rule: the strings name no symbol the branch changed, so `rg` over the retired term, the changed symbol, or the rejected symbol all return nothing. the repo's authoring rule 1 gained a bullet for reading them per-file (`d999b0d`, root repo), and that bullet is prose doing a machine's job. ▶ **The shape**: for each route, derive the set of `ProblemCoded` codes reachable from the controller's collaborators and assert every `code` literal in its `#[OA\*]` descriptions is a member, and that no member is missing from a description that enumerates at all. ⚠ **The hard half is *"enumerates at all"***: a description that mentions no code must not be forced to list them, or every 401 and 500 grows a catalog. A cheap first cut is to key on the literal presence of a backticked `UPPER_SNAKE` code in the string. ⚑ **And `CorrectRecipientController` is the fixture that makes it discriminating**: it enumerates three codes today, so a check that only looked for *at least one* would pass it before and after the third landed. ⚠ Reachability by collaborator is an over-approximation — a controller may reach a code it can never actually raise — so the failing direction must be *"named in the route, unreachable"* as a warning and *"reachable, unnamed"* as the red. ⚑ **Second measured case, on a different branch, within minutes of the first being written — so this has now fired on two branches rather than one.** The TASK-033 session reports that the same `MintSigningTokenController` 409 says *"distinguishes **two** conditions with different remedies"* and lists two codes, while their uncommitted delegated-signature guard adds a third — **on the exact route predicted, and again unreachable by any sweep**, because the description names no symbol of theirs either. ⚠ That route's enumeration went from **one code to two to three in three days**, which is the shape that argues for a check rather than a habit. ⛑ **And the delimiting evidence, which is what makes the gap precise rather than a suspicion**: the global census DID fire for them the same day — publishing a new request property turned `OpenApiSpecTest` red until it was classified. The mechanism works; what it cannot see is that a `code` enumeration inside a `description` **string** is prose to it. ⛔ **The implementation trap, raised by that session and recorded here so the check is not built in the shape that cannot fail**: a route's reachable codes are **not** statically obvious from its controller. Their third code is thrown **two layers down**, inside `SigningTokenIssuance`, not in the controller — so an implementation that scanned only the controller's own `throw`s would have **passed their case while the stale enumeration sat in front of it**. The census must follow collaborators transitively, and the first version of it should be sabotage-checked against exactly that case: add a code thrown two layers below a controller whose description does not name it, and confirm the check reddens. | [ADR-0025](adr/ADR-0025-rfc9457-error-shape.md) *problem shape* + [ADR-0026](adr/ADR-0026-openapi-documentation.md) | Open | 2026-08-28 (proposed instead of an eighth authoring rule) |
| BL-210 | ⚑ **`RemindersSchedule` is shaped, persisted, validated — and read by nothing, which is worse than absent because it looks alive.** It is a value object with a `fromJson()` that rejects malformed input, a `settings_reminders` jsonb column and a hydrator that round-trips it; enumerate the three from [`RemindersSchedule`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/ValueObject/RemindersSchedule.php) and its column rather than from this sentence. What is missing is every consumer: [`Settings::defaultsFor()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/ValueObject/Settings.php) always builds `none()`, no endpoint accepts the field, and no worker reads it — so a customer who configured reminders would get none, silently, with the setting sitting in the database confirming they had. ⛔ **This row said [TASK-041](tasks/TASK-041-resending-a-signing-invitation.md) built *"the manual half of the feature this was shaped for"*, and that is withdrawn (2026-08-31, at that task's rename).** They are two features that shared a word: this one is a **cadence before a deadline** — *nudge 14, 7 and 1 days before expiry*, fired by a clock at a recipient who is merely slow — and TASK-041's `POST /envelopes/{id}/recipients/{recipientId}/resends` **repairs one invitation that did not arrive**, fired by a sender, at a recipient who may never have seen anything. Different trigger, different population, different copy. Calling one the half of the other made this row read as partly discharged when nothing of it exists, which is the opposite of what it was filed to say — and it is why TASK-041 shipped as a *resend*, leaving the noun here. ⛑ **What it does leave behind is machinery, which is the weaker true claim**: an occasion shape, a per-channel copy catalog and a reaction, so a cadence is a driver plus its own words rather than a feature from scratch. ▶ **The trigger is the first customer asking why reminders are configurable and never arrive** — or, sooner and cheaper, a decision to stop offering the setting until something reads it. | [TASK-041](tasks/TASK-041-resending-a-signing-invitation.md) §7 | Open | 2026-08-31 |
| BL-211 | ⛔ **Two branches can mint the same migration version, and the merge conflict is the harmless half of it.** `doctrine_migration_versions` records the version **string**, not the file it came from — so once a volume has run *either* copy, `doctrine:migrations:migrate` finds the row, reports success, and applies **neither** schema. A green migrate and a database missing the columns the code reads. ⚑ **Measured 2026-08-31, not reasoned**: [TASK-041](tasks/TASK-041-resending-a-signing-invitation.md) and `feat/delegated-signature` had each minted `Version20260831000001` — the first adding `resends_made` / `last_resent_at` to `envelope.recipient`, the second the six `asserted_*` columns of [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) to `session.signing_session` — same class name, same path, both unpushed, both aimed at `develop`. TASK-041 renumbered to `Version20260831000002` as the later of the two by authored time, and the renumber then bit **itself**: the dev volume still carried the old string and had never seen the new one, so the migration ran a second time and `make test-db-setup` died on `column "resends_made" of relation "recipient" already exists`. The remedy is `make -C ../f5sign-infra test-db-reset`; there is no code fix, and anyone who ran that branch before the renumber hits it. ⛔ **And the renumber moved the collision rather than ending it, into the direction that makes no noise** (2026-09-01): a volume that ran TASK-041 before the rename has `Version20260831000001` recorded permanently, and that string is now `feat/delegated-signature`'s. When that branch lands, `migrate` on such a volume finds the row, reports success and creates **none** of the six `asserted_*` columns — the same no-op described above, except nothing fails and no column is reported twice. So the reset is owed **before `feat/delegated-signature`**, not only before this one, and nobody reading that branch has any reason to know it. ⚑ **Structural rather than incidental**: six sibling worktrees mint `VersionYYYYMMDDNNNNNN` against one `develop`, and the `NNNNNN` counter restarts at `000001` each day **per branch** — so the first migration any two branches author on the same date collides by construction, and dating is the one thing the convention guarantees they share. ⛔ **Neither a gate nor a review can see it**: the name conflict surfaces only at merge, and the silent no-op surfaces nowhere at all — the branch that loses the race gets a green `make migrate` and a missing column. ▶ Candidates, none chosen here because the choice is a convention change: derive the suffix from something branch-unique, or a `composer` check that reddens when a migration class name already exists on another local ref. | [TASK-041](tasks/TASK-041-resending-a-signing-invitation.md) §8 | Folded | 2026-08-31 (TASK-041 implementation) |
| BL-212 | ⚠ **The minimum resend interval counts resends and nothing else, so *correct-then-resend* sends two mails inside the window it exists to prevent.** [`Recipient`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Entity/Recipient.php) refuses a resend within `MIN_RESEND_INTERVAL_SECONDS` of `last_resent_at`, and that stamp is written by the resend path only. *(Since [TASK-066](tasks/TASK-066-the-resend-interval-counts-per-channel.md) the stamp is split per channel — `email_last_resent_at` / `sms_last_resent_at`, read through `Recipient::lastResentOn()` — and the rest of this row describes the single-stamp guard it was written against; the gap itself is unchanged.)* A recipient re-invited through `POST /envelopes/{id}/recipients/{recipientId}/corrections` — the field workaround [TASK-041](tasks/TASK-041-resending-a-signing-invitation.md) exists to retire — lands with `corrections_made > 0` but `resends_made = 0` and `last_resent_at IS NULL`, so the guard sees somebody who has never been re-invited and lets the resend straight through, seconds later. ⛑ **Defensible and deliberately left**: they are different facts, a correction is itself a repair, and coupling the two counters would re-merge the two meanings TASK-041 spent a whole feature separating. ⛔ **What makes it a row is that the constant's own docblock frames the rule as *not harassing a signer*, and this pair does exactly that** — so the gap is between the rule as written and the rule as enforced, which is the kind of divergence nobody re-derives from the code. ▶ The trigger is a signer receiving two mails minutes apart and saying so. ⚠ Whoever picks it up should note the fix is not obviously *widen the guard*: a correction that repairs a genuinely wrong address **must** reach the new one immediately, so a naive coupling would block the one send that cannot wait. | [TASK-041](tasks/TASK-041-resending-a-signing-invitation.md) §7 | Folded | 2026-08-31 (TASK-041 implementation) |
| BL-213 | ⚠ **`SMALLINT` is the only ceiling on `envelope.recipient.resends_made` for a recipient reached only by email, so crossing it is a 5xx rather than a domain refusal.** Unlike `corrections_made`, which `Recipient::MAX_CORRECTIONS` bounds in the domain, nothing bounds an email-only recipient's resends by **count** — deliberately: [TASK-041](tasks/TASK-041-resending-a-signing-invitation.md) §5 D5 bounds it in **time**, on the argument that a correction rewrites who receives a document others consented to send while a resend rewrites nothing. The consequence is that the column is the last word: 32767 resends in, `save()` raises a DBAL *numeric value out of range* and the route answers 5xx with no `code` for a client to branch on, where every other refusal on it carries one. ⛑ **Loud and non-corrupting**, and remote — 32767 resends at the [`Recipient::MIN_RESEND_INTERVAL_SECONDS`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Entity/Recipient.php) floor, which at today's 60 s is roughly **23 days** of a looping integrator against one recipient — which is why it ships as a note rather than a defect. ⚠ **That floor is the only term in the figure, so re-derive it from the constant rather than quoting this number**: the row read *"roughly 341 days at the 15-minute floor"* until 2026-09-01, computed while the floor was 900 s, and the lowering to 60 s made the mitigating argument fifteen times weaker without touching a word of it. ⛔ **But nothing bounds the looping** ~~[`BL-202`](BACKLOG.md) records that `expiresAt` is enforced by nothing, so a `SENT` envelope stays re-sendable indefinitely~~ — **updated 2026-09-29 ([TASK-049](tasks/TASK-049-an-envelope-expires.md), `feat/envelope-expiry`, merged into `develop` on 2026-09-30 at `e6a0af5e`):** a resend past the envelope's date is now refused `ENVELOPE_EXPIRED` and the sweep closes the envelope within a minute of it, so the looping is bounded by the envelope's life (at most 180 days from the send, and the sender can extend it) rather than by nothing; the count ceiling is still the `SMALLINT`. ⛑ **Updated 2026-10-15 ([TASK-057](tasks/TASK-057-sms-spend-guards.md), `feat/sms-spend-guards`):** a recipient whose channels include `SMS` is now capped in the domain at `Recipient::MAX_SMS_RESENDS` (5) plus any operator grant (`resend_allowance`), refused `RECIPIENT_RESEND_CAP_REACHED`; what follows holds for the email-only recipient. ▶ The trigger is either the first sighting or a **domain** reason to cap resends at all; for an email-only recipient there is still none (the SMS cap is a spend reason, not an overflow one), and inventing a cap to avoid an integer overflow would put a business rule in the place a storage limit happened to fall. ⛑ **Updated 2026-10-19 ([TASK-060](tasks/TASK-060-a-resend-may-name-one-declared-channel.md), `feat/resend-channel`):** a recipient whose resends all name `EMAIL` is uncapped by count too, since the SMS cap now counts only resends that may go out by SMS; only the 60 s interval bounds them. | [TASK-041](tasks/TASK-041-resending-a-signing-invitation.md) §7 | Folded | 2026-08-31 (TASK-041 implementation) |
| BL-216 | **An integrator cannot register or re-key its own webhook subscription; every alta and every rotation is a support ticket.** [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.12 sizes management to a Symfony console command, deliberately, because the number of integrators is zero — and it names this as the decision most likely to be revisited first. What is missing is the authenticated HTTP surface under `/api/v1` that lets a tenant create a subscription, read its health, rotate a key and disable it. ⚠ **The key-display rule is what makes this more than convenience**: a generated key is shown **once** and never rendered again, so today a lost key is recoverable only by a human running a command. | [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.12 + [`webhook-domain-model.md`](ddd/webhook-domain-model.md) §Commands | Open | 2026-08-31 (designing the webhook BC) |
| BL-217 | **A destination narrower than the tenant cannot be expressed — not per envelope, and not per client/department when that level exists.** [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.2 puts every destination in a tenant-owned registered subscription and §7.1 rejects DocuSign's per-envelope `eventNotification`, because a URL declared inline on an aggregate has nowhere to carry signing keys, health or delivery history. ⚑ **The rejection has a real cost and this row is it**: an integrator who wants envelope A's events at one endpoint and envelope B's at another has no way to say so. ⛔ **The fix is a filter, never a second tier** — §2.2 is explicit that a future ownership level becomes another predicate on a subscription's filter, so anything that reintroduces a resolution order between levels is the wrong repair. | [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.2 + §7.1 | Open | 2026-08-31 (designing the webhook BC) |
| BL-218 | **Where a webhook signing key lives at rest is undecided, and the obvious answer has no callers.** [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.7 signs each delivery with every active key on its subscription, so the platform must hold recoverable secret material per subscription — the first time it does. [`FieldCipher`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Crypto/) exists under [ADR-0033](adr/ADR-0033-pii-field-encryption-at-rest.md), which is itself `Proposed` **and has no callers**, so choosing it here would be both this feature's first dependency on it and its first exercise. The alternatives are a secrets store or holding the material outside Postgres entirely. ⚠ **Deciding it late is the expensive order**: the storage shape is a migration, and a key column written in the clear cannot be un-written from a backup. | [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.7 + [`webhook-domain-model.md`](ddd/webhook-domain-model.md) §Persistence shape | Open | 2026-08-31 (designing the webhook BC) |
| BL-219 | **The design says a tenant is told when its subscription is disabled, and there is no surface that could tell it.** [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.10 disables a subscription after a sustained failure window and states the tenant is notified; with management console-only (BL-216) there is nothing a tenant can read, and the notice has no channel. ⛔ **The plausible repair is the wrong one**: routing it through the Notification BC would make an operational fact into a transactional message to a person, and [ADR-0037](adr/ADR-0037-notification-supporting-bc.md) forbids the Webhook BC from depending on Notification at all. ⚠ Until this is answered, a disabled subscription is silent to the only party that can fix it, which turns a recoverable outage into an indefinite one. | [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.10 + [`webhook-domain-model.md`](ddd/webhook-domain-model.md) §Open questions | Open | 2026-08-31 (designing the webhook BC) |
| BL-220 | **The webhook delivery history stores request bodies, so it stores PII, and nothing says when it is destroyed.** [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.10 keeps failed deliveries redeliverable for a retention window, and §2.5 puts the subject's data — a recipient's name, email and phone among it — inside the stored body. ⛔ **It is explicitly *not* evidence** ([`webhook-domain-model.md`](ddd/webhook-domain-model.md) §Boundary), which is what distinguishes it from anything [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) keeps and is the argument that it *should* be purgeable — but its interaction with the three clocks of [ADR-0061](adr/ADR-0061-retention-deletion-and-purge.md) is undecided, and a table nobody classified is a table the purge worker will not reach. | [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.10 + [ADR-0061](adr/ADR-0061-retention-deletion-and-purge.md) | Open | 2026-08-31 (designing the webhook BC) |
| BL-221 | ⛔ **`Uri` accepts `http://169.254.169.254/` and every private address, because it validates shape and nothing else.** [`Uri::fromString()`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/ValueObject/Uri.php) is a single `filter_var($value, FILTER_VALIDATE_URL)` — no scheme allowlist, no destination check. ⚠ **Today this is latent rather than exploitable**: the only `Uri` a customer supplies is `Settings::$callbackUrl`, which no request accepts and nothing dials. It stops being latent the moment anything dials a customer-supplied URL, which is exactly what [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.11 does. That record scopes the repair to the webhook caller and relaxes it outside production, so **the general primitive stays unsafe by default** and the next caller inherits the gap. ⚑ The question this row holds open is whether the check belongs in `Uri` (safe by default, breaks any legitimate internal use) or in each dialling adapter (composable, and forgettable). | [`Uri`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/ValueObject/Uri.php) + [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.11 | Open | 2026-08-31 (designing the webhook BC) |
| BL-222 | ⚠ **Seven of [`EnvelopeCredentialRetirementTest::RETIRES`](../tests/F5Sign/Envelope/Unit/Domain/Aggregate/EnvelopeCredentialRetirementTest.php)'s rows are registrations, not decisions.** That map's own docblock claimed the behavioural cases drove *"every `true` and every `false`"*; they do not — the cases are hand-written per gesture, so a row is green whichever answer it carries. ⚑ **Measured, not reasoned** (2026-08-31, TASK-041): flipping `resendInvitation` to `true` left the file `OK (5 tests, 18 assertions)`. Four rows now have a case — `correctRecipientContact`, `resetRecipientAccessCode`, `unlockRecipientAuth`, `resendInvitation` — and the seven bookkeeping rows do not; enumerate the split from the file rather than from this sentence. ⛔ **The census `every_recipient_addressed_gesture_is_classified` does not close the gap**: it derives the universe from the aggregate and fails on an *unclassified* gesture, which catches a missing row and says nothing about a wrong one. So the day one of the seven starts retiring — or stops — the map keeps asserting the old answer in silence. ▶ The trigger is either a gesture in that block changing what it touches, or the cheaper fix: drive the map itself, one behavioural case per row, so a wrong answer cannot be written down. | [TASK-041](tasks/TASK-041-resending-a-signing-invitation.md) §7 | Open | 2026-09-01 (TASK-041 audit) |
| BL-223 | ⛔ **Twelve of the fifteen ADRs written since the no-emoji rule landed break it, and the retrofit clause does not cover them.** [`AUTHORING.md`](adr/AUTHORING.md) convention 8 is an absolute — *"An ADR carries none"* — made absolute on 2026-08-11 precisely *"because the soft form cannot be checked and this set drifted under it"*. Measured 2026-08-31 over every post-revision ADR: **0047, 0048, 0053 and 0064 are clean; the other twelve are not, and every record from 0054 through 0062 violates it**, averaging ~18 marks. ⚑ **The hard form drifted exactly like the soft one, for the same reason: nothing checks either.** ⚠ The retrofit clause in that file covers a **pre-revision** ADR *"when it is next edited"*; these twelve were never pre-revision, so no existing rule reaches them. Two ways out and they are not equivalent: a lint that fails the build (which would make the convention real, and is the shape [`TASK-020`](tasks/TASK-020-derive-the-deferral-index.md) already owns for ADR template conformance), or amending convention 8 to describe what the set actually does. Choosing neither is choosing the second silently. | [`AUTHORING.md`](adr/AUTHORING.md) convention 8 | Open | 2026-08-31 (auditoría de la rama docs/outbound-webhooks) |
| BL-224 | **The SMS half of the receipt seam has no row; [`BL-118`](BACKLOG.md) is about email bounces only.** That row is framed entirely around an MTA-side hard bounce and never mentions SMS, status callbacks or the provider. The narrowing axis is **available in the data today** — `EnvelopeCreated` carries `senderId` as an `ActorId`, which for an integrator reads `client:<uuid>` — and the ADR settles only **how not to build it**, deliberately, because no integrator has asked and we do not yet know whether the client is even the axis they want. ⛔ **Never keyed on an `ApiKey`**: it carries `revokedAt` and exists to be rotated, so the subscription would be orphaned by the rotation §2.7 is elsewhere at pains to make seamless. ⛔ **Never resolved from the event's actor**: censused 2026-08-31, three of the eight Envelope catalog events carry one and not the same one (`senderId` on created/sent, `voidedBy` on voided) and five carry none, so that filter delivers **2 of 11** and drops the rest in silence. ⚑ **What is left to decide** is whether the mechanism is the local `envelope_id → client` projection the ADR sketches, whether pre-existing envelopes are backfilled from Envelope's read port or left to the deliver-on-unknown rule, and whether the filter ships at all. ⚠ **The one clause that is not negotiable if it is built**: a missing projection row means **deliver**, never drop — §2.8 guarantees no ordering, so a `RECIPIENT_COMPLETED` can outrun its own `ENVELOPE_CREATED` and a drop-by-default filter would lose events intermittently and unreproducibly. | [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md) §2.2 | Open | 2026-09-01 (pregunta sobre si las suscripciones deberían ir por API key) |

| BL-228 | ⛑ **Closed on `feat/channel-selection` 2026-10-14 (pending merge): the text below describes the state BEFORE [TASK-031](tasks/TASK-031-channel-selection-beyond-email.md); *"it is correct today"* is no longer true.** The email guard is gone; a resend is refused when the recipient resolves no channel (`RECIPIENT_HAS_NO_REACHABLE_CHANNEL`, or `RECIPIENT_NOTIFIED_BY_INTEGRATOR` for a declared `[]`), and a delegated recipient is refused by name. ⚠ **`Envelope::resendInvitation()`'s no-address guard asks for an *email*, and nothing can go red the day that is the wrong question.** It is correct today: [`DefaultChannelSelector`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Domain/Service/DefaultChannelSelector.php) returns `[EMAIL]` for `RECIPIENT_INVITATION_RESENT` and the reactor's SMS arm throws, so a phone-only recipient genuinely cannot be resent to.
