| BL-229 | ⚑ **The forensic half of [ADR-0060](adr/ADR-0060-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) §2.6: the superseded signing token is refused but never MARKED.** `fix/signing-link-revocation-on-correction` closed the harm — `envelope.recipient.invitations_revoked_at` compared against the token's `issued_at`, synchronously, at all four `SIGNING_TOKEN` crossings — so there is **no open security exposure here**. What is missing is the record §2.6 decided: a nullable `revoked_at` on `session.signing_token`, written through a second `SECURITY DEFINER` function (the app role holds no DML on that table, [`docs/LOAD-BEARING.md`](../docs/LOAD-BEARING.md) §1.14) and driven by a reactor. ▶ What it buys, and it is not nothing: a DBA reading `session.signing_token` today cannot see that a link was revoked, and support cannot answer *"when did this link stop working"* from that table — they must cross to `envelope.recipient` and know to. ⛔ **Only `RecipientCorrected` may drive that reactor.** §2.4 says *"`RecipientCorrected` **and** `RecipientAuthUnlocked`"*, and the second is emitted by **both** `resetRecipientAccessCode()` and `unlockRecipientAuth()` (`Envelope.php:1041`, `:1074`) — so building §2.4 as written would revoke the link on a reset, stranding the signer §2.5 exists to protect. Measured: that wiring reddens `GatedSigningCeremonyHttpTest::a_reset_before_the_recipient_ever_opened_the_link_is_in_force_when_they_do`. ⚠ Deliberately deferred rather than built with the guarantee: a reactor rides log → relay → broker → worker, and the worker is off by default, so it can never be the guarantee — only the record. | [ADR-0060](adr/ADR-0060-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) §2.6 (amended 2026-09-01) | Open | 2026-09-01 |
| BL-230 | ⚠ **A reset access code does NOT fully contain a leak when the recipient's ACCESS gate is neutral, and [`BL-158`](BACKLOG.md) was closed as though it did.** The reset retires every session credential minted before it (correct, and delivered by TASK-039). But the signing link is deliberately left working — [ADR-0060](adr/ADR-0060-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) §2.5, so the legitimate signer can come back with the new code — and under a **neutral** access gate re-opening that link mints a **fresh** credential whose `agp` is *now*, which postdates the retirement anchor. So an intruder re-opens and recovers the read-model and the document bytes; only the signature stays blocked, and only because the code sits on the signing gate. ⛑ **Where a declared `ACCESS_CODE` access gate is in force this is genuinely closed** — re-opening asks for the new code — which is why the case is configuration-dependent rather than universal. ▶ What it needs is a decision, not a patch: either the open stops re-stamping `agp` when a neutral gate proves nothing new (which strands the legitimate signer in the same configuration), or the reset stops retiring *access* credentials for recipients whose access gate is neutral (arguably right, since the reset changed the SIGNING gate's verifier and nothing about access), or the contract keeps saying voiding is the only full containment. ⚑ **The published contract on `ResetRecipientAccessCodeController` now states the condition truthfully** rather than claiming the endpoint is sufficient on its own; that string had been reversed on `feat/terminal-envelope-and-credential-retirement` into a claim that was false for the default configuration. | Audit of `feat/terminal-envelope-and-credential-retirement`, 2026-09-01 | Open | 2026-09-01 |
| BL-232 | ⚠ **`RecipientClaimed` is a `DomainEvent` and not an `AuditableEvent`, and neither [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) nor [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md) discusses the choice.** The consequence is specific rather than general: [`EnvelopeRosterDeclared`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Event/EnvelopeRosterDeclared.php) is PII-free **by construction** — recipient id, role and document ids — so on the auditable stream the row of a claimable slot is byte-identical to the row of a party named at authoring, and the fact that tells them apart is the one left out. The projector [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) will read cannot then answer *"was this party's identity supplied after the envelope was sent, and when?"*, which is the question [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §1 says the feature exists to make answerable. ⚑ **Recoverable, not lost**: `platform.event_log` stores every event ([ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md)), so what is missing is the classification, not the data. ⚑ **And there is a precedent AGAINST**: `RecipientCorrected` is not auditable either, and `AuditableEvent`'s own docblock calls the classification *"a per-event call owned by each emitting BC"* — so this may well be right. What is deferred is the **decision**, which today exists nowhere. ▶ Cheapest moment to settle it is when TASK-014 defines what the projector reads, because that is the first reader who can say whether the gap bites. | `AuditableEvent` + [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) | Open | 2026-09-01 (audit of `feat/claimable-recipient-slot`) |
| BL-233 | ⚠ **`locale` reaches the database untrimmed on both write doors, and the claim compares it byte-exactly.** `#[Assert\NotBlank(normalizer: 'trim')]` runs the normaliser **inside the validator's own check and never writes it back to the DTO** — which [`ClaimRecipientController`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/ClaimRecipientController.php) knows and handles for `full_name`, with a comment explaining exactly this, and does not for `locale`. `Locale::of()` only rejects an all-whitespace value (`trim($value) === ''`), so `" es"` is stored verbatim. Two consequences, the second only on the claim: a malformed BCP-47 tag lands in a column with almost no edit path, and `Recipient::guardReplayMatches()` compares `$locale->value` byte-exactly, so a caller who sends `" es"` then `"es"` gets `409 RECIPIENT_ALREADY_CLAIMED` where the published contract promises convergence. ⚑ **Pre-existing rather than introduced**: `AddRecipientController` does the same, so the claim mirrors the authoring path rather than opening a new hole, and a genuine byte-identical retry converges. ⛔ **The repo-wide fix is the one to prefer and it is already named**: `AddRecipientRequest::$locale`'s own comment records that ADR-0019 §5 wants the bound in the **value object**, and that `Locale::of()` enforces none — so trimming at one edge would leave the other writer and any future one unbounded. ▶ Fix in `Locale::of()`, with the length bound that comment already owes, rather than per controller. | [ADR-0019](adr/ADR-0019-database-schema-conventions.md) §5 + [`Locale`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Templating/Locale.php) | Open | 2026-09-01 (audit of `feat/claimable-recipient-slot`) |
| BL-235 | ⚠ **Nothing detects that a value crossing the wire changed, so the handoff note that announces it is owed only by memory — and on 2026-09-01 that memory failed inside the very commit that was fixing a published-contract defect.** `852b054` added `REVOKED` to the `status` enum of `POST /api/v1/signing/session` ([`StartSessionController`](../src/F5Sign/Session/UI/Http/StartSessionController.php)) and shipped no note; four files in `docs/frontend-handoff/` were touched in that same range and `git grep -l REVOKED origin/develop -- docs/frontend-handoff/` returns **nothing**, while that directory's own `README.md` demands one *"whenever a change touches … an enum whose values cross the wire"*. The signer app had no `REVOKED` branch, so the value fell through to their default — **which was the auth door**: an access-code field shown to somebody whose envelope had just been voided, on a terminal session no code can ever reach. ⛔ **The published value itself was right and must not be read as the defect**: it is unreachable today (a void is refused with 409 before that body is built) and was published anyway, on the reasoning that *"an enum missing a value its own source can return is how a client ends up in its 'unknown status' branch on the day the coupling moves"* — which is exactly what happened, and is why it was found by measurement rather than in production. What was missing is only the announcement. ⚑ **The asymmetry is the finding, and it is the consumer's, reported 2026-09-01:** *the gate is on the wrong side of the network*. Ours ([`OpenApiSpecTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Acceptance/OpenApiSpecTest.php)) runs where the truth lives and breaks the build in both directions; theirs has to fetch the truth over HTTP, so it cannot hang off their CI without coupling it to us — and the only backend it reaches is the shared stack, which serves whatever branch the MAIN checkout is sitting on rather than any ref. ▶ **What would close it, and the shape is theirs**: the trigger must be **the enum value that crosses the wire**, not the spec file — an `#[OA\*]` can change without any value changing, and a value can change somewhere that does not look like spec. It is buildable **here** and only here, needs no network, and would be consumed gladly on their side. Sibling of [`BL-209`](BACKLOG.md) (the `ProblemCode` gate is global and nothing gates it per route) and of [`BL-165`](BACKLOG.md) (a claim whose culprit diff is empty where the claim lives). ⚠ Not scoped by [TASK-036](tasks/TASK-036-claimable-recipient-slot-and-claim.md): surfaced by it, owned by nobody yet. | `docs/frontend-handoff/README.md` + [`OpenApiSpecTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Acceptance/OpenApiSpecTest.php) | Open | 2026-09-01 (cross-repo: signer measured it, backend confirmed the missing note) |
| BL-236 | ⚠ **`config/` is PHP, it is what boots the process, and NOT ONE of the three static gates reads it — so `composer lint` can report `Found 0 of N` on a tree that will not start.** Measured 2026-09-01: [`.php-cs-fixer.dist.php`](../.php-cs-fixer.dist.php)'s finder is `->in([src, tests, phpstan])`, [`phpstan.dist.neon`](../phpstan.dist.neon)'s `paths:` is `src, phpstan, tests`, and [`deptrac.yaml`](../deptrac.yaml)'s is `./src` alone. ⛔ **It fired the same day it was measured**: resolving a merge conflict left `config/routes/api/envelope.php` with a route registration missing its `->methods()->defaults();` tail — `$v1->add(…)->controller(…)` followed straight by a comment and the next `$v1->add(` — and **lint, deptrac and PHPStan all reported green**. `php -l` refuses the file outright. The lane caught it, but only at `composer install`'s post-install `cache:clear`, which is *before the first gate runs*, so the failure surfaced as a Symfony stack trace and `make … Error 255` rather than as a named gate. ⚑ **The `test` gate would also have caught it** — `RouteCollisionTest` and `RouteAuthnDeclarationTest` both load the route files — so this is not *"nothing checks config/"*; it is that **nothing checks it statically**, and the static trio is exactly what [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) offers as the quick ad-hoc re-check that needs no network. That recommendation is sound for `src/` and silently wrong for `config/`. ⚑ **And `config/` is the small half.** Counted 2026-09-01 on this tree: `config/` **5** PHP files, `public/` **1**, and `migrations/` **39** — **45** unscanned in total. ⚠ The sibling session counted **44** on `develop` the same hour, and the difference is one migration this branch's merge brings in: two correct counts of two trees, the day's recurring shape. ⛔ **The migrations are the chronic case and the syntax error was only the loud one**: outside php-cs-fixer, PSR-12 and this repo's fully-qualified-`@see` convention are **unenforced** across the 39 files that authoring rule 6 and repo rule 2 both name as the most dangerous prose surface in the codebase — *"nobody re-reads an applied migration except to reconstruct why the schema looks as it does"*. Measured: `rg '@see [A-Z]' migrations/` returns **nothing** today, so the convention holds there **by nobody having broken it** — an exempted set that is empty today, which the repo's authoring rule 5 says reads as an all-clear and is the opposite. ▶ **The fix is three lines in three files, not one, and one of the three is a decision rather than a path.** Each gate carries its own list and they share no source, so adding a directory to one leaves the other two blind. `config` and `migrations` belong in the fixer's finder and plausibly in PHPStan's `paths`; ⛔ **deptrac must NOT simply gain `migrations/`** — those 39 files belong to no declared layer, so pointing it there is a ruleset decision (39 uncovered tokens), not a path edit, and `./src` alone is likely right on purpose. PHPStan over `config/` is its own question: the route and service closures are not typed for it. ⛔ **Do not read this as *"add every directory to every gate"***: the value is that the gate an author reaches for to answer *"is my PHP well-formed"* should cover the PHP that boots the app, not that deptrac should have an opinion about a route file. | [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) § *Running tests + tooling* (the ad-hoc static re-check it recommends) | Open | 2026-09-01 (a merge resolution shipped a non-booting route file past all three static gates) |
| BL-237 | ⚑ **`first_opened_at` now stays silent for a delegated act, and silence is the WEAKER of the two honest answers — the direction of travel is a positive fact.** Closed the lie on 2026-09-02: [`AuthMethod::executedByTheRecipient()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/AuthMethod.php) gates the seal in [`Envelope::recordRecipientFirstOpen()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Aggregate/Envelope.php), so a `MACHINE_KEY_ASSERTED` admission seals the authentication and not the opening, and `signs_via` is published on the sender's read model so the pair `(MACHINE_KEY, null)` answers the question completely. ⛔ **What it does NOT do is AFFIRM anything.** This repo's own doctrine is that `null` is *unknown* and that `NOT_ATTEMPTED` is a claim ([`Recipient`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Entity/Recipient.php)); the exception that made the cheap fix defensible is that THIS field's `null` is already contractually defined as *"has not opened it"*, backfilled and renderable (TASK-034). So the composite is true today and a reader who asks only one of the two fields still gets a half-truth. ▶ **Wanted: a recorded fact saying *admitted without presence*** — a positive third state rather than an absence plus a declaration. ⚑ **The trigger that makes it worth its migration is [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md), which has not started**: an evidence projector should read a fact, not infer one by joining two columns and knowing which `AuthMethod` members imply presence. ⚠ Deliberately NOT done in the same change: it is a column, a migration, a contract addition and a line in [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) §5 — and A+ was built so this lands on top of it without undoing any of it. ⚑ Surfaced by the `factor5_dashboard_sf` integrator, who found the false seal by testing rather than by being told, and who still carries a flag of its own to label the act — which is the residue this row is about. | fold pending — [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) §2.1; consumer is [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) | Open | 2026-09-02 (delegated-signature integration) |
| BL-238 | ▶ **Un tramo de firma cuyo acto lo ejecuta un SISTEMA y no una persona — el consignatario que nadie reclama porque el hecho es *"el camión salió del almacén a las 14:32"*.** Distinto de [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md), que es un integrador firmando por una parte **nombrada**: aquí no hay parte a la que atribuir el acto. ⚑ **El encuadre es lo que vale de esta fila, porque es lo que se pierde si no se escribe**: eIDAS separa *firma electrónica* (persona física) de *sello electrónico* (persona jurídica), y un acto automático es lo segundo — el sistema del almacén atestigua en nombre de la empresa. ⛔ **Cuidado con el vocabulario: "seal" YA está cogido en este código** para el sellado final de plataforma (`READY_TO_SEAL`, `/documents/{id}/sealed`, el PAdES incremental contra DSS), así que reutilizar la palabra colisiona en cada conversación. ▶ **Lo que un tramo automático tendría que declarar en AUTORÍA**, por la misma razón que `signsVia` se declara ahí y no en la ruta (una guarda en la ruta falla abierto): que no admite puertas —una puerta sin nadie a quien desafiar es la puerta ficticia que el cargador acaba de quitarse—, que no se le acuña token, y que `access_method_executed` no aplica. ⛔ **Y la pregunta que decide si merece la pena: qué hace VERDADERO el acto.** Una evidencia que solo dice *"lo hizo el sistema"* no vale más que el `DIRECT_LINK` de hoy: sigue sin apuntar a nada. La petición tendría que **traer el hecho externo** —identificador, instante y origen— y nosotros guardarlo, o habremos cambiado una afirmación vaga por otra más elegante. ⚠ **Sin caso de uso real todavía**: se levantó discutiendo el hueco de `DIRECT_LINK` en la firma delegada, y se deja como fila y no como ADR por decisión del usuario el 2026-09-02, precisamente para no diseñar contra un ejemplo. | fold pending — vecino de [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) §2.1 y de [BL-237](BACKLOG.md); consumidor eventual [TASK-014](tasks/TASK-014-evidence-audit-projector.md) | Open | 2026-09-02 (discusión de la atribución en actos no presenciales) |
| BL-214 | ⛔ **The asserted evidence of a delegated act is stored in CLEARTEXT, and it is six columns of personal data.** [`AssertedEvidence`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Domain/ValueObject/AssertedEvidence.php) implements [`Pii`](../src/F5Sign/Kernel/Capability/Pii.php) and `Pii` **classifies without enciphering** — the only rule attached to that marker forbids an implicit plaintext read, and nothing in this repository connects it to [`FieldCipher`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Crypto/FieldCipher.php), which still has **zero callers in `src/`**. So [ADR-0033](adr/ADR-0033-pii-field-encryption-at-rest.md)'s *first caller* arrived as plaintext columns with a green build, which is the exact failure [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) §5 bar 12 was written to prevent. ⚑ **Taken deliberately rather than missed** ([TASK-033](tasks/TASK-033-delegated-signature.md) §8.3 decision 3): the bar is recorded UNMET on the record and in the value object's own docblock, so nobody reads the green board as coverage. ⛔ **The blocker is provisioning and not code, which is why it could not simply be done**: `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_SECRET` is committed **empty** and absent from `.env.dev` and `.env.test`, and [`ConfigKeyProvider`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Crypto/ConfigKeyProvider.php) throws in its **constructor** under 32 bytes — so the first code path that injects the cipher fails closed in every unprovisioned environment, including every developer's. ⚠ **Do not 'fix' that by committing a value**: repo rule 4 makes absence the pattern, and the empty string is safe precisely because it can never be a working key. ▶ **What it needs**: a dev/test secret provisioned the way `SESSION_CREDENTIAL_SECRET` is, then the six columns enciphered with the per-column AAD `FieldCipher` already supports. ⚑ **The column-per-field shape was chosen with this in mind** (ADR-0055 §2.14): each ciphertext binds to its own `(session, column)` context, which a `jsonb` blob could not. ⚠ One member is exempt by nature — `asserted_document_hashes` is a digest map and not personal data — and §2.14 records that if a future member of that collection ever is, the trade must be re-taken rather than inherited. | TASK-033 §8.6, on landing the assertion record ⚑ **Decidido 2026-09-02: se aterriza CON la laguna, deliberadamente.** El usuario la valoró contra las otras dos salidas (aprovisionar y cifrar antes; o persistir menos campos) y eligió aterrizar, porque el hueco está declarado en tres sitios —el docblock de `AssertedEvidence`, ADR-0055 §5 barra 12 y esta fila— y `Status` sigue `Proposed`, así que nada afirma una protección inexistente. ⛔ Lo que eso convierte esta fila en: el único sitio que recuerda que **la decisión fue tomada, no omitida**, y que el cifrado es una task propia cuya primera mitad es de aprovisionamiento (`FIELD_ENCRYPTION_SECRET` committeado vacío, `ConfigKeyProvider` lanza por debajo de 32 bytes) y solo la segunda de código. ⚑ **Medido 2026-09-02: la exposición REAL hoy es más estrecha que lo que esta fila describe, y por una razón que NO es un control.** El único integrador que ejerce la ruta manda tres campos y ninguno es dato personal —id opaco de su lado, instante de consentimiento y digests— y tiene un test propio que fija que los opcionales se **omiten** en vez de viajar vacíos. ⛔ Pero eso es una propiedad **de ese cliente**, no de este sistema: `AssertedEvidence` sigue admitiendo `assertedSignerIp` y `assertedSignerUserAgent` (`:102-103`, opcionales), así que la primera integración que los mande escribe IP y user-agent en claro sin que nada se lo impida ni se lo advierta. Léase como *la bala aún no ha pasado cerca*, nunca como mitigación — y por tanto no rebaja la prioridad del cifrado, solo la probabilidad de que hoy duela. | Open | 2026-08-31 |
| BL-215 | ⚠ **The delegated commit takes a row lock and nothing proves it does.** [`CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase.php) calls `findLockedForUpdate()` before committing, and [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) §2.4 is explicit that *idempotency comes from the session id **and the row lock**, and naming only the first is how this gets built wrong* — `startIfAbsent()` protects the creation of the row and nothing after it. ⛔ **But the `Integration/` tier cannot see the difference**: it runs one connection under DAMA rollback, so a locked read and an unlocked one are indistinguishable to it, and flipping `findLockedForUpdate()` to `find()` leaves the suite green. That is the same blind spot `DbalNotificationRepository`'s `FOR UPDATE` shipped through at 98.9% coverage. ⛑ **The instrument already exists** — [`ProbesRowLocks`](../tests/F5Sign/Support/ProbesRowLocks.php), used by `SigningSessionRowLockTest` — and needs a second connection plus `#[SkipDatabaseRollback]`, both of which that file demonstrates. ▶ **What it needs**: TASK-033 §6 bars 13 and 18 driven for real — two concurrent delegated calls producing ONE signature, one `ConsentRecorded` and one `SignatureCommitted`. ⚠ Until then the property is asserted by **reading the code**, not by running anything, and the branch says so rather than counting it green. ⚑ **2026-10-06, [TASK-052](tasks/TASK-052-the-signing-gate-is-answered-on-the-act.md) on `fix/signing-gate-on-the-act` (not merged)**: it rewrote this use case's commit to run in its own unit of work and built a lock probe (`SigningGateOnTheActUnitOfWorkTest`, with `ProbesRowLocks`) that shows, from a second database session, that the session row is locked when the machine commit reaches the admission. The two-concurrent-calls test this row asks for was **not** built, so the row stays open with the probe beside it as evidence. | TASK-033 §6 bars 13 and 18, unexercised on landing | Open | 2026-08-31 |
| BL-242 | **Four tables carry a time-driven column and no sweep, and the doctrine that covers them is implemented for one.** `docs/LIVE_SCHEMA.md` states it for the whole schema: the edge answers NOT SERVABLE on every request and a sweep answers NOT ACCUMULATING. Each is ADR-0063 §2.18's kind A, so nothing here fails closed; rows accumulate. Sizing and cadence per table are the call, and the notification purge is also a personal-data obligation, not housekeeping. | [ADR-0063](adr/ADR-0063-where-storage-concerns-belong.md) §2.18, [ADR-0061](adr/ADR-0061-retention-deletion-and-purge.md) §2.12 | Open | 2026-08-31 (ADR-0063 sweep census; filed 2026-09-04) |

The published 409 of `POST /api/v1/signing/session/auth/otp/send` lists `AUTH_METHOD_NOT_DECLARED`, `AUTH_METHOD_NOT_DELIVERED`, `AUTH_NO_DESTINATION`, the step refusal and *"the usual conflict cases"* — not this one. ⚑ **The code IS in the generated catalog** (33 members of the spec's `ProblemCode`) **and IS published in prose** — inside [`GetAuthDescriptorController`](../src/F5Sign/Session/UI/Http/GetAuthDescriptorController.php)'s `selection` property, which tells the client that calling the send route with no `method` is a 409 `AUTH_METHOD_NOT_CHOSEN`. So the spec states the fact on the route that cannot emit it and omits it on the route that can. ⛔ **[TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) gave the gap a second consumer** rather than causing it: the machine twin's new 409 (`481a41d`) says it runs the same issuance as the browser route *"whose 409 lists the other codes"* — a pointer written this week, at an incomplete list. | BL-250 | ⚠ **Three machine routes declare no 404 while one is the ordinary client error on them.** `machine_auth_otp_send`, `machine_auth_submit` and `commit_delegated_signature` (declared in [`config/routes/api/signing.php`](../config/routes/api/signing.php); they are **not** in `envelope.php`, and that file's own docblock records why they cannot be) all reach [`RecipientAuthContextReader::read()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/RecipientAuthContextReader.php) — or `SigningSessionNotFoundException::withId()` directly, inside [`CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase.php) — which answers **404** for an unknown envelope or recipient. All three take both ids **in the path**, so a wrong id is the commonest client mistake on them, and none declares a 404 `#[OA\Response]`. Their browser twins do. ⚑ Consequence is not cosmetic: a generated client types that 404 as unhandled, so the integrator's own error mapping has no arm for the case they will hit first. Pre-existing on all three; surfaced because [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) added a 409 to each of them and re-read the blocks. ▶ **What would force it**: the first generated client against these routes, or the per-route status gate [`BL-209`](BACKLOG.md) asks for. ⚑ **2026-11-04 — two more items for the same fold, both found by TASK-078's reviews and both left unedited there**: (1) the 409 block of `commit_delegated_signature` says *"Every act on an ended envelope gets this answer"* (`ENVELOPE_NO_LONGER_LIVE`), which is false for a `SIGNING_PLATFORM` recipient with no session, who gets the 404 this row records; (2) the handoff of [TASK-078](tasks/TASK-078-the-machine-routes-refuse-an-unclaimed-slot.md) (§2, first bullet) says a closed envelope still answers `ENVELOPE_NO_LONGER_LIVE`, which also does not hold for a relayed commit with no session. TASK-078 moves live unclaimed slots from 404 to 409 and adds no 404 state. | fold pending — the three machine controllers' `#[OA\*]`, beside [`BL-209`](BACKLOG.md) | Open | 2026-09-16 (contract-check of TASK-042, W5) |
| BL-253 | ⚠ **The auth descriptor's new 409 promises a description the route can never deliver to a recipient on a voided envelope.** [`GetAuthDescriptorController`](../src/F5Sign/Session/UI/Http/GetAuthDescriptorController.php)'s 409 tells the client *"the same link is described normally once their step is active"*. Its step guard is one of the two without an **`envelopeIsAlive` skip** — `StartSessionUseCase` is the other, and needs none because `EnvelopeNoLongerLiveException` already answers above it. What makes the descriptor different is that it has no dead-envelope refusal to yield to at all, because it reports terminal state in the **200** body ([ADR-0056](adr/ADR-0056-terminal-state-on-pre-credential-routes.md)) rather than refusing. The consequence is only in the published prose: for a dormant recipient on a **voided** envelope the step will never activate, so the promise cannot be kept and the signer is told to come back to something that is gone. ⚑ **Not a regression** — before [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) that recipient got a described gate on a cancelled envelope, which is worse — but the sentence claims more than the route can keep, and it is emitted verbatim into the spec the frontend ratifies. ▶ **What would force it**: the first recipient told to come back later on a cancelled envelope; or a copy pass over that block, which is the cheap moment to make the sentence conditional rather than absolute. | fold pending — the 409 block of `GetAuthDescriptorController`; the behaviour is [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §2.6 and is not in question | Open | 2026-09-16 (contract-check of TASK-042, §6) |

| BL-257 | ⚑ **Recorded so it is not rediscovered as a defect: the step refusal sits ABOVE the OTP limiter, by decision.** [`IssueAuthChallengeUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/IssueAuthChallengeUseCase.php) refuses a dormant step before it consumes [`FailOpenOtpSendLimiter`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Infrastructure/RateLimit/FailOpenOtpSendLimiter.php). That ordering is what [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §2.6 requires and what the route's `#[OA\*]` publishes — **nothing is sent and nothing is charged** — and it is the same shape the superseded-link refusal already had, so it is not a regression. ⚠ **The side effect is real and is the reason this is written down**: a holder of a link on a dormant step can hit the most abusable route in the system without spending any of the limiter's counters, and each attempt still costs one cross-BC read. ⛔ Changing it would mean charging the limiter for requests that cost nothing to serve, which is the opposite trade from the one ADR-0066 §2.6 made. ▶ **What would force it**: a measured abuse pattern on that route from dormant-step holders; or a limiter that counts refusals on a separate key from sends, which is a different mechanism and not a reordering. | [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §2.6 — decided, not deferred; this row is the index entry so the next audit finds the decision | Open | 2026-09-16 (security-audit of TASK-042, W3) |
| BL-258 | ⚠ **A linked git worktree lives INSIDE the main checkout, the workspace rules forbid exactly that shape, and nothing ignores it.** `worktrees/f5sign-backend/Senry` is a real worktree — `git worktree list` names it on branch `wt/senry`, dated 2026-08-20/25 — so every `git status` in the main checkout carries `?? worktrees/`, and the path is **absent from [`.gitignore`](../.gitignore)**. The workspace `CLAUDE.md` forbids it by name: a worktree goes as a **sibling** of its repo, never inside it, because one inside the checkout falls within the `../f5sign-backend` bind-mount the stack declares and shows up as untracked in the main tree. ⚑ **It contaminates no gate, verified per tool rather than assumed**: `.php-cs-fixer.dist.php` collects `{src,tests,phpstan}`, `phpstan.dist.neon`'s paths are `migrations src phpstan tests`, Infection's `source` is `src/F5Sign`, and PHPUnit's suite is `tests/` — none reaches `worktrees/`, whose 14,336 PHP files are invisible to all four. ⛔ **Pre-existing and another session's tree**: [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) left it untouched on purpose, and moving or removing it is the workspace owner's call, not a branch's. ▶ **What would force it**: a `git add -A` in the main checkout, which is the one gesture that would commit 14k foreign files (and which the workspace rules already forbid for this reason); or any future tool whose path list is broader than those four. | workspace root `CLAUDE.md` § *Worktrees* — the rule exists; what is missing is the cleanup and the `.gitignore` line | Open | 2026-09-16 (task-validate of TASK-042, N2) |
| BL-260 | ⛔ **A `SESSION_COMPLETED` fact already in `platform.event_log` at deploy time is past all nine of TASK-042's guards, and the incident's own co-cause was a relay that was behind.** Every guard [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §2.3 places sits on the **request** path: they stop the fact being written. They say nothing about a fact already written and not yet drained. When the relay does drain it, [`SignForRecipientUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/UseCase/SignForRecipientUseCase.php) still applies an out-of-turn incremental PAdES under `SEQUENTIAL_PADES` — it carries **no step predicate**, deliberately (ADR-0066 §2.5: a throw there dead-letters and the fact stays), and neither does `Envelope::recordRecipientCompletion()`. ⚠ **Not hypothetical**: the preprod incident of 2026-08-27 happened with the relay stalled behind a transaction-id epoch reset, so the window between *fact written* and *fact consumed* is exactly the window this deferral is about. ⛑ **The mitigation is operational and costs nothing**: confirm the relay has drained before deploying — compare the checkpoint row for the relay's `consumer` key against the tail of `platform.event_log`, reading the cursor column names from [`EventRelay::checkpoint()`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/EventLog/EventRelay.php) rather than from this row, because they have a history of being restated from memory. A drained relay leaves no such fact in flight. ▶ **What would force it**: deploying while the relay is behind — which nothing warns about, since the relay reports success while draining nothing ([`BL-234`](BACKLOG.md)). | [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §7 — the deploy-time check; the code placement itself is ADR-0066 §2.5 and is not in question | Open | 2026-09-16 (post-landing audit of TASK-042) |
| BL-261 | ⚠ **A recipient who signed out of turn BEFORE this fix is now permanently refused their own signed copy, and the state has no in-API repair.** Their step is `completed` but was never `activated`, and after [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §2.7 a completed step can never become the frontier — `frontierStep()` returns the lowest step **not completed**, so `activated_at` stays null for ever. The new guards then read that null: [`SigningSessionReader`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/SigningSessionReader.php) answers **409 `STEP_NOT_ACTIVE`** and [`SigningDocumentReader`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/SigningDocumentReader.php) **404**, where before this branch both served the read model and the PDF. ⛔ **And nothing upstream withholds first**: a `COMPLETED` envelope keeps serving its recipients ([ADR-0059](adr/ADR-0059-terminal-envelope-closes-the-ceremony.md) as amended, pinned by `SigningReadersRefuseUnsentEnvelopeTest::the_session_read_model_still_serves_a_completed_envelope`), so these recipients reach the step guard and are refused by it. ⚑ **The repair is operational, not urgent**: the sender can still fetch the document over the `MACHINE_KEY` routes `GET /api/v1/envelopes/{id}/documents/{documentId}/sealed` and `…/current` and send it on. The census that finds them, run as a role that can read `envelope.*`: `SELECT r.envelope_id, r.id FROM envelope.recipient r JOIN envelope.step s ON s.id = r.step_id WHERE r.outcome = 'COMPLETED' AND s.activated_at IS NULL` — expected to return the incident's envelope `01a0427d-1b4d-7643-bec3-06eb5e748d72`, and a result that does **not** contain it means the query or the environment is wrong, not that the state is clean. ▶ **What would force it**: the first such recipient asking for their copy; or the preprod repair of [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §7 bullet 2, which this census is the front half of. | [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §7 — beside the *preprod envelopes may already be corrupted* bullet, whose repair this shares | Open | 2026-09-16 (post-landing audit of TASK-042) |
| BL-262 | ⚠ **The census sees three of the nine new guards, and the repair is one more needle.** [`SigningReadersRefuseUnsentEnvelopeTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/SigningReadersRefuseUnsentEnvelopeTest.php)'s step axis classifies two universes derived from the source tree — the four `->getContextForRecipient(` crossings and the five `->issueFor(` callers — and fails naming anything unclassified. **The other six guards live in consumers of [`RecipientAuthContext`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/RecipientAuthContext.php)**, which is neither universe, so deleting one of those six reddens nothing there; ADR-0066 §5.5 declares them *held by review alone* and the [`BL-33`](BACKLOG.md) row says the same. ⛑ **The mechanical repair is cheap and shaped exactly like what is already there**: a fourth `callers()` needle on `$this->authContext->read(` — **8 call sites across 7 classes** today (`CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase` reads twice, once on each branch), each classifiable with the dispositions the axis already uses, and the per-callsite count test already written for the other universes catches a class that reads twice and guards once. ▶ **What would force it**: a new consumer of `RecipientAuthContext` that forgets to ask `stepIsActive` — which is precisely what the six are exposed to, and which no gate in the repository can see today. | [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §5.5 states the gap; this row is the repair it does not propose | Open | 2026-09-16 (post-landing audit of TASK-042) |
| BL-264 | ⚠ **The mint now tells a sender to wait for a step that a voided envelope will never activate.** [`SigningTokenIssuance::issueFor()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Service/SigningTokenIssuance.php) has four refusals — unsent, unclaimed slot, signs elsewhere, dormant step — and **no terminal-envelope refusal**, which is a standing decision its own docblock defends (ADR-0059 answers the terminal direction at the four ceremony crossings instead, and adding `isTerminal()` here would change a published **sender-side** answer, so it needs a record of its own). The new fourth refusal inherits that gap: minting for a dormant recipient of a **voided, expired or declined** envelope now answers `RECIPIENT_STEP_NOT_ACTIVE`, whose message promises *"no signing credential can be issued **until their step is active**"* — a remedy that can never arrive, when the answer the sender needs is that the envelope is gone. ⚑ **Not a regression**: before [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) the same call minted a working link over a dead envelope, which is worse. It is the **same misleading remedy [`BL-253`](BACKLOG.md) records for the auth descriptor, at a second site that row does not name** — and the pair is the argument for answering the terminal question once rather than per site. ▶ **What would force it**: a sender told to come back later for an envelope they themselves voided; or the decision `SigningTokenIssuance`'s docblock already says is owed, which would settle both sites at once. | fold pending — the terminal-at-the-mint question is declared open in `SigningTokenIssuance::issueFor()`'s docblock and needs an ADR; the misleading-remedy half is [`BL-253`](BACKLOG.md)'s shape | Open | 2026-09-16 (post-landing audit of TASK-042) |
| BL-265 | ⚑ **Latent, and recorded because the predicate that used to step over it is gone.** `Envelope::frontierStep()` returns the lowest-ordinal step **not completed**, and the step-closing predicate (until TASK-048 `advanceAfterRecipientTerminal()`, which closed a step only when `$totalCount > 0` and every recipient on it was terminal; now `Envelope::advance()` asking `EnvelopeEndingEvaluator`) closes a step only when its blocking members' legs are closed. So a step that can never complete parks the frontier **for ever**: no later step is activated, `markReadyToSeal()` is never reached, and there is no path that walks past it — where the retired `nextActivatableStepValue()`, which only ever looked **above** the step that had just closed, would have stepped over it (that stepping-over was [`BL-199`](BACKLOG.md), so this is the cost of its fix and not a defect in it). ⛔ **Unreachable today, and the two reasons are load-bearing rather than incidental**: `ensurePreSendInvariants()` refuses to send an envelope with a recipient-less step, and the roster is frozen at send — `addRecipient()` guards `DRAFT` and no remove path exists. ⚠ **Distinguish it from the deliberate park**: a member whose delivery failed keeps their leg `PENDING` on purpose, so their step stays open rather than advancing the workflow past them — that is the failure the delivery-failure state exists to prevent, and it is repairable by a resend. This row is about a step with **no** repair. ▶ **What would force it**: any feature that removes or withdraws a recipient after send; or a step whose completion predicate can fail — the quorum nuance the removed `advanceAfterRecipientTerminal()`'s own comment deferred to *BC §7 inv 16* (ADR-0073's `AT_LEAST n` step policy now carries it, TASK-050) is the nearest candidate. | [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §7 — beside the seal-frontier follow-ups; [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §2.7 owns the predicate | Open | 2026-09-16 (post-landing audit of TASK-042) |
| BL-266 | ⚠ **The delegated commit's 409 still publishes a code its use case cannot raise: `ENVELOPE_NOT_SENT`.** [`CommitDelegatedSignatureController`](../src/F5Sign/Session/UI/Http/CommitDelegatedSignatureController.php)'s block lists `ENVELOPE_NOT_SENT` and `RECIPIENT_SLOT_NOT_CLAIMED`; [`CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase.php) raises neither — its collaborators are the session repository and `RecipientAuthContextReader`, which answers an unsent envelope with a **404** and asks no slot question. ⚑ **Minted 2026-09-16 at the close of [TASK-043](tasks/TASK-043-a-refused-commit-names-its-condition.md), and it is a SPLIT rather than a new finding**: both were named inside [`BL-249`](BACKLOG.md), whose own subject — a published `ALREADY_SIGNED` that nothing emitted — that task discharged. Closing that row without opening this one would have dropped the pair from the cross-cutting view, which is the single thing this index exists to provide; [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §7 recorded them first and stays the home-of-truth. ⛔ **The rest of that block is now correct, which makes these two MORE exposed, not less**: it opens *"`code` distinguishes the conditions — branch on it"* and every other code it names is emitted by a factory, so an integrator has more reason to trust the two that are not. ▶ **What would force it**: [`BL-209`](BACKLOG.md)'s per-route code gate, which would have to classify both clauses before it could go green; or an integrator branching on either for this route. Whether they are narrowed away or realized is [TASK-033](tasks/TASK-033-delegated-signature.md)'s call, not a passing edit. **Measured 2026-09-25 (Evidence & Audit): the route signs for a claimable slot that nobody claimed.** A sent envelope whose one recipient is `claimable` with `signs_via: MACHINE_KEY` took a delegated commit with `204`, and the envelope reached `COMPLETED` with the slot unclaimed. [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) §2.3 decides that the route refuses this with a 409 naming the unclaimed state, so narrowing `RECIPIENT_SLOT_NOT_CLAIMED` away would contradict it: the repair is the refusal. The audit trail of such an envelope states `NO_SESSION_BEFORE_CLAIM` as failing. ⚑ **Narrowed 2026-11-04 by [TASK-078](tasks/TASK-078-the-machine-routes-refuse-an-unclaimed-slot.md)**: `RECIPIENT_SLOT_NOT_CLAIMED` is raised now, after the assertion checks, so what this row says of it, and the 2026-09-25 measurement, describe the state before that branch. What stays open is `ENVELOPE_NOT_SENT`, which this route answers as a 404. | [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §7, *found while scoping*; [TASK-033](tasks/TASK-033-delegated-signature.md)'s surface owns the repair | Open | 2026-09-16 (split from [`BL-249`](BACKLOG.md) at the close of TASK-043) |
| BL-267 | ⚠ **Signing-ceremony responses promise no member: objects in published responses declare no `required` (the commit's 200 does since TASK-068), so a generated client types every member optional.** Reported on 2026-09-16 by `f5sign-signer`'s contract check as eight standing warnings (a count that predates [TASK-068](tasks/TASK-068-after-the-commit-the-signer-is-told-where-their-copy-stands.md)), on the 201 of `POST /api/v1/signing-tokens`, the 200 of `POST /api/v1/signing/commit` and nested objects of the 200 of `GET /api/v1/signing/session`; the first two carried no `required` at all. ⚑ Since TASK-068 the commit's 200 declares `required: [session_id, status, signed_copy]` (the signer reads `signed_copy` straight after the commit), so what stays open is the 201 of `POST /api/v1/signing-tokens` (it still declares none) and the nested objects of the session read's 200. [ADR-0026](adr/ADR-0026-openapi-documentation.md) authors `required` only for request DTOs. | [TASK-043](tasks/TASK-043-a-refused-commit-names-its-condition.md) §7, *Two findings about this repository's published spec* | Open | 2026-09-16 (the signer's contract-snapshot regeneration, reported to TASK-043) |
| BL-268 | ⚠ **A recorded field value can be altered without trace until it is drawn into a sealed PDF.** Once [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) lands, `envelope.field.value` is written once into an ordinary column and nothing in the permanent record binds what the recipient submitted. The closing design — an HMAC in `SignatureCommitted` under a per-session key the purge destroys, not a plain hash — is in the home; **decided *not now* by the maintainer on 2026-09-21.** ⚑ Whoever revives it: the Audit Trail work (not committed on 2026-09-21) adds its own field to `SignatureCommitted` for the three-state reservations, so the digest should ride the same additive payload change rather than a second one ([TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §8). ⚑ **And a third candidate arrived the same day**: [TASK-045](tasks/TASK-045-browser-reported-evidence-at-the-commit.md) §4 D7 keeps the browser-reported evidence out of the log, and routes any anchor it needs here — so if revived, this becomes **one** commitment mechanism covering field values, reported evidence and the three-state reservations. ⚑ **2026-09-21, later — *"once TASK-044 lands"* is now**: the write is built (`0bae290`, on `feat/field-values-at-commit`), so the window this row describes opens with that deploy. | [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §4 D10 + §6 | Folded | 2026-09-21 (TASK-044 design) |
| BL-270 | **`FieldType::acceptsValidation()` admits `max_length` and `pattern` on `DATE`, on a rationale the signer contradicts.** Its docblock argues that *"a date arrives as typed characters that a length or a pattern can legitimately constrain"*; the signer uses a native date input, sends ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` and applies neither rule to a `DATE`, and [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md)'s planned commit-time validator will not either. A sender can therefore author a rule nothing will ever enforce — the silently ignored configuration `FieldOptionsNotApplicableException` exists to refuse. Narrowing it is a contract change on `POST /fields` for the dashboard; fields already authored that way stay loadable, since hydration does not re-run the applicability check. ⚑ **2026-09-21, later — built as planned**: the commit-time validator (`FieldAnswerEvaluator`, `6f106d8`) checks a `DATE` for a real calendar date and applies neither rule, so *"planned"* and *"will not"* above are the record. | [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §3 #4 + §6 | Folded | 2026-09-21 (TASK-044 design) |
| BL-271 | **EDITOR does not fill fields until the signer can submit a fill-only act.** The maintainer chose SIGNER, APPROVER and EDITOR as the filling roles on 2026-09-21; read in the signer's tree the same day, an EDITOR can edit fields but its `submit-fill` action is a placeholder that never commits, and EDITOR is not blocking — so its fields would stay empty on an envelope that completes without it. [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §4 D7 therefore makes `Role::fillsFields()` true for SIGNER and APPROVER only, and refuses data fields assigned to an EDITOR at `send()`. ▶ **What would force it**: the signer shipping a fill-only commit. Re-admitting EDITOR is then one `match` arm — relaxing a guard, so additive. | [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §4 D7 + §6 | Folded | 2026-09-21 (TASK-044 audit) |
| BL-272 | **The stored `pattern` is JS source, and the server will run it as PCRE — the two dialects still disagree in the harmful direction.** The signer runs `new RegExp(pattern)` with no flags, unanchored; [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) will run it as PCRE in `(*UTF)` mode with `D`, which closes the `\d` / `\w` / `\b` / `$` differences PHP's `/u` would open (measured 2026-09-21, PHP 8.4 / PCRE2 10.42 against Node 24). Three gaps remain where the server is stricter and a recipient is stranded on a green form: syntax only PCRE accepts (`(?i)`, `(?>…)`, possessive quantifiers), which the signer's constructor rejects and therefore skips; counts over characters outside the BMP (`^.{3,}$` against `"😀a"` — JS counts UTF-16 units); and `\s` against Unicode whitespace such as a non-breaking space. [`FieldValidation`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/FieldValidation.php)'s docblock already says PCRE-validity is neither necessary nor sufficient for JS-validity. ▶ Two repairs, one per side of the boundary: refuse PCRE-only syntax at authoring, or detect and skip it at the commit. ⚑ **2026-09-21, later — built as described** (`SignerPatternRule`, `6f106d8`): the server runs the pattern this way now, so *"will run"* above is *runs*, and the three gaps stand. | [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §3 #4 + §6 | Folded | 2026-09-21 (TASK-044 audit) |
| BL-273 | **`trusted_proxies` was not configured, so the day anything sat in front of the PHP container, `signer_ip` would silently have become the proxy's address on every signature.** It fell back to `%env(default::SYMFONY_TRUSTED_PROXIES)%` and no `.env*` file set it; that was harmless while Caddy handed requests to PHP-FPM over FastCGI, and nothing would have errored when it stopped being true. **Closed by TASK-045 (§3 #4, §4 D9):** `config/packages/framework.yaml` now sets `trusted_proxies` from `SYMFONY_TRUSTED_PROXIES` (committed empty in `.env`, so no proxy is trusted until one is named) and `trusted_headers` to `x-forwarded-for` and `x-forwarded-proto` only; `ObservedClientAddressTest` pins it. | [TASK-045](tasks/TASK-045-browser-reported-evidence-at-the-commit.md) §3 #4 + §4 D9 | Landed | 2026-09-21 (TASK-045 design) |
| BL-274 | **Server-side Geo-IP: a derived location from the signer's IP, deferred.** The browser's position (TASK-045) is a reading the signer grants or denies; a Geo-IP location would be derived, not read, and needs a licensed database (MaxMind or similar). If it ever comes it is computed on the server, which holds the IP, and recorded as derived — never in the `reported_*` columns, and never as a stand-in for a denied position (the signer's fake `GEO_IP` fallback, which TASK-045 removes, is the failure this row keeps from returning under a real name). | [TASK-045](tasks/TASK-045-browser-reported-evidence-at-the-commit.md) §4 D10 | Folded | 2026-09-21 (TASK-045 design) |
| BL-275 | ⛔ **A recipient's reservations are written in plaintext into the permanent event log, and the channel is about to carry more of them.** Since TASK-016, `reservations` rides the payload of four facts — `SIGNATURE_COMMITTED`, `SESSION_COMPLETED`, `RECIPIENT_COMPLETED` and `RECIPIENT_SIGNED` — verbatim. The log is append-only, and a payload cannot be rewritten without breaking its `sys_commitment` (ADR-0031), so every reservation written there can never be erased or enciphered afterwards; [BL-130](BACKLOG.md) already names it the sharpest of the free-text fields, because eCMR reservations name people and vehicles. ⚑ **Decided 2026-09-21 by the maintainer: kept in plaintext for now, to be revisited soon.** It matters more than it did: [ADR-0069](adr/ADR-0069-a-reservations-field-places-the-recipients-reservations.md) makes this channel the **only** home of the reservations text (the Factor5 waybill's box included), and the Audit Trail work reads them from the log. ▶ **Every signature with reservations until the change adds permanent plaintext**, which is the cost of waiting. The options, each forward-only for the rows already written: **(a)** cipher the `reservations` member inline (ADR-0032's target, which needs its key infrastructure first — the blocker [BL-214](BACKLOG.md) records); **(b)** take the text out of the payloads and keep a commitment in its place, the text living on `recipient.reservations`, where it can be enciphered or erased later, and the Audit Trail reading it from the row at seal time (ADR-0032's interim) — the same mechanism [BL-268](BACKLOG.md) describes for field values and reported evidence; **(c)** keep it. ▶ **Revisit with the Audit Trail's three-state reservations ADR**, which touches the same payload member. | [ADR-0096](adr/ADR-0096-key-management-encrypts-the-logs-free-text-under-erasable-keyrings.md) §2.1, §2.12 item 3 | Folded | 2026-09-21 (TASK-044 design review) |
| BL-276 | ⚠ **A `RESERVATIONS` field's `max_length` bounds the commit's reservations even when the recipient was never shown the field.** [ADR-0069](adr/ADR-0069-a-reservations-field-places-the-recipients-reservations.md) §2.4 bounds the text by the box *"when the committing recipient has a `RESERVATIONS` field that carries `max_length`"*, and `Envelope::recordFieldValues()` does exactly that — with no visibility condition. A field whose every rectangle sits on documents the recipient may not read is not published to the signer (ADR-0041 §6 narrows it to nothing), so the signer never had the bound to apply, and a refusal arrives as `TOO_LONG` on a `field_id` absent from its session `fields`. Factor5's box is not affected (it has a rectangle on the destinatario's own carta de porte); the shape only bites a box placed solely on unseen documents. ▶ **A decision, not a fix, and it is the maintainer's:** (a) bound only when the field is shown; (b) refuse at `send()` a `RESERVATIONS` field with no readable rectangle; (c) publish the bound to the signer even when the field is not shown. Found 2026-09-21 by the handoff review of TASK-044. | [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §6 | Open | 2026-09-21 (TASK-044 handoff review) |

| BL-278 | **On any 5xx in the commit path, a recipient's raw field values reach the error tracker as stack-frame arguments.** `CommitFieldValues::record()` (renamed `AdmitRecipientAct::commit()` by [TASK-048](tasks/TASK-048-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md), `feat/task-048-how-an-envelope-ends`, merged into `develop` on 2026-09-30 at `e6a0af5e` — the values still travel the same way, on the same call chain, under the new name), `FieldAnswerEvaluator::judge()`, `SignerPatternRule::evaluate()`, `Field::recordValue()` and the `executeStatement()` inside `DbalEnvelopeRepository::saveAdmission()` (`saveFieldValues()` until [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.1 folded it in, same TASK-048 branch) all take the values as plain scalars or arrays, the production image did not set `zend.exception_ignore_args` (default `0`), Sentry serializes frame arguments, and `SentryBeforeSend` redacts `Pii` objects — not frame variables. 4xx refusals are safe: the kernel exception families are ignored. The mechanism predates TASK-044 (ADR-0009 holds it by review alone), but TASK-044 puts tax ids on that path, and §4 D9's classification is narrower than *"redacted from the error tracker"* reads. **Fixed:** `zend.exception_ignore_args=On` in `docker/php.ini`, the file that the production image reads, and `ProductionPhpIniTest` holds that line. The development stack reads the file in `f5sign-infra` and stays off. Found 2026-09-21 by TASK-044's security audit (W1). **2026-10-07: the same fix covers a second kind of data**: [TASK-053](tasks/TASK-053-the-recipients-drawn-signature-on-their-mark.md) sends the recipient's signature image to the commit as a base64 string and holds its bytes as scalars on the commit path (`SignatureImagePng`, `CommitSignatureUseCase::checkedImage`, `PngDecoder::decodes`, `CommittedSignatureImage`, `SignForRecipientUseCase`). That task marks each such parameter `#[SensitiveParameter]`, which keeps the bytes out of a rendered frame; only the setting named above closes every frame argument at once, the image's included. Found by TASK-053's security audit (W3, infra half). | [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §6 | Closed | 2026-11-05 by `docker/php.ini` on `fix/exception-ignore-args` · surfaced 2026-09-21 (TASK-044 security audit) |
| BL-279 | **The two commit routes are not rate-limited, and a refused commit is free to repeat.** Every attempt, a 422 included, takes the envelope root `FOR UPDATE` and can run up to 512 bounded patterns while holding it; a refused commit spends no signing code by design (TASK-044 §4 D5), so it can be looped. Nothing caps the aggregate body beyond `post_max_size`. ▶ **What would force it:** a per-credential limiter on `POST /signing/commit` and the delegated signature route, on the `otp_*` model (`FailOpenOtpSendLimiter`). | BL-281 | **A voided envelope's sealed PDF cannot say it was annulled: EU DSS 6.4's REST interface writes no `/Reason`.** Measured 2026-09-23 by [TASK-046](tasks/TASK-046-a-recipient-may-decline.md) against the live service: a `reason` member anywhere in the signature parameters is refused with HTTP 500 *"Unrecognized field \"reason\""*, no remote parameter class carries one, and no converter calls `PAdESSignatureParameters.setReason`, which the PAdES library supports. So the annulment seal of [ADR-0070](adr/ADR-0070-an-envelope-ends-in-agreement-or-is-voided-and-sealed.md) §2.5 is the same invisible platform seal as the completion's, and **the PDF alone cannot tell an annulment from a completion**; until the audit trail's evidence document lands, only the API states it. ▶ The route back, chosen by the maintainer as deferred rather than rejected (ADR-0070 §7.10): extend the DSS webapp built in `f5sign-infra` (`docker/eu-dss`) to forward a reason to `setReason`, then pass *"Documento anulado el <instant>: rechazo del destinatario <recipient_id>"* / *"… por el remitente …"* — never a name, never the decline reason. An infra change, Java code carried across DSS upgrades, and a backend change in `DssPayloadMapper`. ⚠ Re-measure on each DSS upgrade: a later REST version may expose it and make the customisation unnecessary. | [TASK-046](tasks/TASK-046-a-recipient-may-decline.md) §6 | Open | 2026-09-23 (TASK-046, measured against the live DSS) |
| BL-282 | **Delegated decline is out of scope for TASK-046, and it is the normal case outside the Factor5 waybill.** An integrator declining on a recipient's behalf, as that recipient's word ([ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md)'s shape), the same way a delegated commit already exists. TASK-046 built only the recipient's own decline (§4 D2b); the delegated one is deferred, not rejected. ▶ **What would force it:** the next flow that needs a recipient declined on their behalf outside the waybill — ADR-0055 already carries the shape to extend. | [TASK-046](tasks/TASK-046-a-recipient-may-decline.md) §6 | Open | 2026-09-23 (TASK-046 design, maintainer) |

| BL-288 | **A recipient who declined cannot re-enter a `VOIDED` envelope once their session credential lapses — the same limitation `COMPLETED` already has.** The 15-minute `Authn::SESSION_CREDENTIAL` ([ADR-0050](adr/ADR-0050-session-credential-jwt-and-refresh.md)) has no renewal path once the session is terminal, so a decliner who wants to re-open the sealed annulment record after their credential expires meets a hard refusal rather than a fresh credential, exactly as a signer who already committed does today. Surfaced by TASK-046's validation pass while exercising §4 D5b (who may read the sealed document). ▶ **What would force it:** a decision on whether a terminal session should ever be re-mintable for read-only access — a broader question than decline, since it already applies to `COMPLETED`. ⚑ **[ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.15 (TASK-048, merged into `develop` on 2026-09-30 at `e6a0af5e`) narrows the premise rather than answering it**: a decliner reads nothing at all after `COMPLETED`, whatever their credential's state — the refusal this row is about no longer needs a fresh credential to fire, because §2.3's reset/unlock is refused for a decliner by name at any point. The broader re-mintable-for-read-only question stays open for a signer or an informational recipient, who §2.3/§2.15 do let back in. | [TASK-046](tasks/TASK-046-a-recipient-may-decline.md) §6 | Open | 2026-09-23 (TASK-046 validation) |
