Surfaced by TASK-046's validation pass. ▶ **What would force it:** deciding what, if anything, a `CERTIFIED_DELIVERY` recipient is shown before declining — today only the API route is reachable, with no signer-app screen behind it. | [TASK-046](tasks/TASK-046-a-recipient-may-decline.md) §6 | Open | 2026-09-23 (TASK-046 validation) |
| BL-291 | **`PromoteSignedDocumentsCommandTest`'s `#[SkipDatabaseRollback]` cases leave committed rows behind that broke a later full run on a kept lane** (`Storage write failed in f5sign-retained`). Because those cases opt out of DAMA's per-test rollback (needed to see a second connection's writes), their rows survive past the test and accumulate on a lane kept between runs rather than torn down — a later full-suite run on the same kept lane inherited state an earlier run left behind. Not a TASK-046 defect; surfaced while validating it. ▶ **What would force it:** either the test cleans up its own rows explicitly (`tearDown`), or the lane resets kept state between full runs — `f5sign-infra` is looking at the latter independently. **Measured 2026-09-28 and 2026-09-29 (Evidence & Audit), three additions.** (1) The mechanism behind the message: a cross-tenant command that runs under DAMA's rollback does its object-store work for real. It relocates another test's leftover bytes and deletes their staging copy. Then the database rolls back, and the next run finds a row that names deleted bytes. (2) `EnvelopeVoidReachesTheSessionCrossingTest` is class-level `#[SkipDatabaseRollback]` too, and leaves a voided, annulment-sealed envelope. (3) No kept lane is necessary. `make qa` runs the suite twice on one test database (the test phase, then Infection's initial run). So it can fail alone on a fresh database, and a second `make qa` without `make test-db-reset` fails. On 2026-09-29 the failing document's tenant had a UUIDv7 from inside the previous run's Infection phase. The Evidence & Audit tests that commit remove the trail records they issue (`RemovesIssuedTrails`). | [TASK-046](tasks/TASK-046-a-recipient-may-decline.md) §6 | Open | 2026-09-23 (TASK-046 validation) |
| BL-292 | **Migration `Version20260925000001`'s `down()` was never exercised dynamically** — only read structurally (reverse-order drops, `IF EXISTS` throughout, matching its own docblock). Reversing it needs the bootstrap/admin DSN, which `wt-backend-sf` could not reach in either `env=dev` (no `postgresql` service on the worktree lane) or `env=test` (the non-superuser `f5sign_app` role cannot manage `doctrine_migration_versions`) at the time of TASK-046's validation. The lane now offers `wt-backend-sf admin=1` for exactly this. ▶ **What would force it:** running `doctrine:migrations:execute Version20260925000001 --down` (then re-`up`) through `wt-backend-sf admin=1` on a disposable lane, the next time a migration's reversibility needs a live check rather than a structural read. | [TASK-046](tasks/TASK-046-a-recipient-may-decline.md) §6 | Open | 2026-09-23 (TASK-046 validation) |
| BL-293 | **`docs/ddd/signing-session-domain-model.md`'s top Status banner still says [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.5's "mint a new challenge" and its re-send route (route 4) are unbuilt — false since TASK-022 (2026-08-19/2026-09-16), over a month before TASK-046.** The same file's own realization crosswalk row ("Gate HTTP edge") and two other passages already say route 4 shipped; only the top banner paragraph was never swept when TASK-022 landed. Pre-existing, unrelated to TASK-046 — surfaced by this task's `docs-sync` pass, which correctly left it unfixed as outside its diff's conditions. ▶ **What would force it:** a one-line banner fix, the next time any task touches this file's top Status block. | [docs/ddd/signing-session-domain-model.md](ddd/signing-session-domain-model.md) | Open | 2026-09-23 (TASK-046 docs-sync) |
| BL-294 | **`OtpSent` calls `deliveryReference` the notification occasion's id, and the value is the cause token that id is derived from.** The [`OtpSent`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Contract/Event/OtpSent.php) docblock says it is "the opaque id of the notification occasion this issuance created". [`IssueAuthChallengeUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/IssueAuthChallengeUseCase.php) passes the cause token `otp-issuance:<challenge id>`, and `NotificationId::fromCause()` derives the id from it through a private namespace. A reader that joins the log to `notification.notification` with this value as an id finds nothing. **What would force it:** the first reader that joins Session's facts to Notification's by this value. | The `OtpSent` docblock | Open | 2026-09-22 (Evidence & Audit step-4 census) |
| BL-295 | **`OneTimeCodeDeliveryService` rests its rule on the premise that the call sits inside the caller's transaction, and its caller commits first.** The class docblock quotes [ADR-0051](adr/ADR-0051-notification-inbound-command-surface.md) §3.3: "the call sits inside the caller's transaction and inside a signer's HTTP request". [`IssueAuthChallengeUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/IssueAuthChallengeUseCase.php) runs off the bus and commits the challenge before it requests the delivery, a recorded choice (its "Invoked directly, not on the bus" section). The rule itself, that the call stays a database write, still holds, because the second half of the premise (a signer's HTTP request) is true. **What would force it:** an edit to the service that relies on the caller's transaction for rollback. | The [`OneTimeCodeDeliveryService`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Application/Service/OneTimeCodeDeliveryService.php) docblock | Open | 2026-09-22 (Evidence & Audit step-4 census) |
| BL-297 | **`DocumentConserved` says the fact proves a digest that its payload does not carry.** The [`DocumentConserved`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Event/DocumentConserved.php) docblock says the fact proves "they are the ones with this digest under this custody" and that "a reader of the log should not need the envelope row". The payload carries `artifact_id` and `custody_id` and no digest, so the digest needs the artifact row. Either add the digest to the payload (additive) or correct the docblock. **What would force it:** a reader of the log that must state the conserved digest without the envelope row. | The `DocumentConserved` docblock | Open | 2026-09-23 (Evidence & Audit step-5 census) |
| BL-298 | **`findSealedArtifact()` says an `EXTENSION` kind "if added" would extend its filter, and the kind exists.** The comment in [`DoctrineEnvelopeReadRepository::findSealedArtifact()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Infrastructure/Persistence/Doctrine/Read/DoctrineEnvelopeReadRepository.php) treats `EXTENSION` (LTA) as future. `ProducedByKind::EXTENSION` exists and has no producer, so the comment is residue, and nobody has decided whether an extension over the seal is what the sealed download serves. The audit trail decided it for its own read ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md), the assembler's rule for which artifact's bytes are read). **What would force it:** the first `EXTENSION` producer. | `DoctrineEnvelopeReadRepository::findSealedArtifact()` | Open | 2026-09-23 (Evidence & Audit step-5 census) |
| BL-299 | **The audit trail names the tenant by its provisioning slug, because no context holds the tenant's legal name.** The trail should state the account holder's legal name. Identity & Access holds only the name a tenant was provisioned under, and [`TenantNames`](../src/F5Sign/IdentityAccess/Contract/Query/TenantNames.php) reads that as a stop-gap; the PDF says beside the value that it is not the legal name. **What would force it:** a customer who needs the legal name on the trail. The name arrives as a field of its own, so the trail's `tenant_label` keeps its meaning. | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.5 | Folded | 2026-09-23 (maintainer, at the tenant-name read) |
| BL-300 | **`SealRequestRejected` gives "the bytes are not a PDF" as its example, and the signing engine may not answer that input with a rejection.** [`SealRequestRejected`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Contract/Exception/SealRequestRejected.php) is `PdfSealer`'s invalid-request failure. On validation, DSS answers non-PDF bytes with HTTP 500 (measured 2026-09-23), a status the adapter reads as the engine being unavailable. What it answers on signing is not measured, so the example may name a failure the seal port never reports for that input. **What would force it:** the caller that issues trails unattended and decides between retry and give-up by this distinction ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md), the trigger). | The `SealRequestRejected` docblock | Open | 2026-09-23 (Evidence & Audit seal read-back) |
| BL-301 | **`SignatureReadBack::signaturesIn()` promises to throw nothing, and a malformed response body escapes it.** [`DssHttpAdapter`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Infrastructure/Pdf/DssHttpAdapter.php)`::send()` decodes the body with `toArray(false)` after its `try`, so a 2xx body that is not JSON throws a decoding exception, and a transport failure while the body is read throws too. [`DssSignatureReadBack`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/Service/DssSignatureReadBack.php) catches only the three DSS exceptions. The port's contract ("Throws: Nothing. Each failure is a variant of the `Err`") does not hold for these inputs, and every other caller of `send()` shares the gap. **What would force it:** the trigger that issues trails unattended and must get a failure value to decide whether a trail waits ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.7). **Landed 2026-09-25 on `feat/evidence-audit`:** `send()` reads every body inside a guard. A 2xx body that is not JSON, or that the network cuts off, leaves as `DssUnavailableException`, so each port reports it as its `...ServiceUnavailable`, which a caller may wait out. A 4xx whose body cannot be read is still `DssInvalidRequestException`. | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.7; `DssHttpAdapter::send()` | Landed | 2026-09-24 (Evidence & Audit gathering review) |
| BL-302 | **The audit trail's gathering holds every signed document's final bytes in memory at once.** [`AuditTrailSources`](../src/F5Sign/EvidenceAudit/Application/Service/AuditTrailSources.php) reads each document's final bytes for the read-back and keeps them until the trail is assembled. Computed, not measured: under `memory_limit=512M` that is about seven 50 MB documents. **What would force it:** an envelope larger than that, or a lower memory limit on the process that issues trails. **Landed 2026-09-29** ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §9): the sources keep digests of the bytes, and the gathering holds one document's bytes at a time. Measured on the dev stack: before, a fatal error at the seventh document of 47 MB under `memory_limit=512M`; after, about four times one document. The force was already met: the production worker's container cap is also 512M, and a process that dies inside a delivery never parks (`BL-310`). | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §3.2 | Landed | 2026-09-24 (Evidence & Audit gathering) |
| BL-303 | **Envelope's row times keep whole seconds, while the facts about the same acts keep microseconds.** [`DbalEnvelopeRepository`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Infrastructure/Persistence/Doctrine/DbalEnvelopeRepository.php) writes an envelope's `created_at` and `sent_at`, and an artifact's `created_at`, with DBAL's `datetime_immutable` type. Its format is `Y-m-d H:i:s`, with no fractional seconds and no UTC offset, and the columns are `timestamptz(6)`. Measured on the dev database 2026-09-25: an envelope row says `10:00:32` for a creation fact at `10:00:32.840042`. So a row time can come before its own fact, rows lose their order within a second, and the value is read in the session's time zone. The audit trail takes both envelope times from the log for this reason. **What would force it:** a reader that compares a row time with a fact time, or orders rows by time within one second. **Two more, measured 2026-09-28.** Storage writes `obligation_conservation_until` with the same type ([`DbalCustodyRepository`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Infrastructure/Persistence/Doctrine/DbalCustodyRepository.php)), so a ceiling with microseconds reaches the custody truncated. And an artifact that SignatureExecution appends takes the instant of the fact that triggered it (the command's `issuedAt`), not the append time. In the Evidence & Audit exit run, an annulment seal appended at 11:02:51 had `created_at` 11:02:49, the void's instant in whole seconds. | `DbalEnvelopeRepository` | Open | 2026-09-25 (Evidence & Audit exit run) |
| BL-304 | **In production, the HTTP process holds the relay's and the provisioning's database credentials.** `f5sign-infra`'s `docker-compose.prod.yml` puts `RELAY_DATABASE_URL` and `PROVISIONING_DATABASE_URL` in the shared `x-backend-env` anchor, which php-fpm, worker, relay and custodian all merge, and [`config/packages/doctrine.yaml`](../config/packages/doctrine.yaml) declares all four connections outside any `when@` block. So php-fpm can read every tenant's event log (the relay role bypasses RLS) and create tenants and API credentials. `init-app-role.sh` says the relay's credential is confined to the relay container, which is false. In dev, `.env.dev` defines the relay DSN for every process. **What would force it:** any code execution inside php-fpm. The fix is infra's (take the two DSNs out of the anchor), with each connection declared only where it is used. | fold pending (infra compose and `doctrine.yaml`) | Open | 2026-09-21 (Evidence & Audit survey; re-measured 2026-09-25) |
| BL-305 | **The dev database container fails its next restart after any change that replaces `init-app-role.sh`, on Docker Desktop.** Both Postgres containers bind-mount the script as a single file, so a replaced file (a `git pull`, an editor save) leaves the container pointing at the old inode, and `docker compose restart` fails with "error mounting ... no such file or directory" until the container is recreated. `make test-db-reset` now recreates `postgres-test` (`f5sign-infra` `fa33953`); the dev `postgresql` container keeps the trap. Measured 2026-09-22 on Docker Desktop (WSL); plain Linux Docker is not measured. The structural fix is to bind-mount a directory into `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/`, which moves the path that `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/LOAD-BEARING.md` and migration docblocks cite. Until then: `docker compose up -d --force-recreate --no-deps postgresql`. **What would force it:** the next pull that changes the script. **A second trigger, measured 2026-09-29:** a WSL reboot made Docker's automatic restart fail every single-file bind mount of the stack (`php.ini`, the `Caddyfile`, RabbitMQ's `definitions.json` and `init-app-role.sh`), not only the Postgres ones. `make up` recovered the stack. | fold pending (infra) | Open | 2026-09-22 (Evidence & Audit step 4) |
| BL-306 | **A recipient whose signing link expired unopened is not re-invited.** The signing link lives a fixed seven days (`StoreBackedSigningTokenIssuer::LIFETIME`), while an envelope may now live up to 180 days and a step may wait longer than a week ([ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.8, §2.12). A recipient invited on day 0 who never opened the link cannot act after day 7 unless the sender resends; at the step deadline they are released `LINK_EXPIRED` (a veto step counts them as reached, maintainer 2026-09-28), so nothing false is recorded — but the party silently lost the chance to act. ▶ Requested by the maintainer on 2026-09-25: when a link expires unopened while its envelope and step are still open, **automatically send a new link, or a reminder that mints one**. To decide: which of the two; how many times; whether it respects the resend interval (`Recipient::MIN_RESEND_INTERVAL_SECONDS`) and the SMS cost ceiling; and that it never re-invites a recipient whose leg is closed or whose envelope has been closed. | ADR-0073 audit round (maintainer decision on release reasons) | Open | 2026-09-25 |
| BL-307 | **A sender cannot ask for an envelope to be conserved when nobody acted on it.** Under [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.14, an envelope that ends without agreement keeps its documents only if someone engaged — signed, declined, was admitted, or (maintainer, 2026-09-25) opened their session; otherwise the originals are discarded. An envelope whose purpose is to prove **what was sent** — a notice, an offer, pre-contractual information — whose recipients never opened it is therefore discarded, and the sender keeps only the platform's record that it was sent. ▶ Requested by the maintainer: an opt-in per envelope, **"conserve always"**, off by default, that keeps and conserves the documents whatever the recipients did. To decide: who may set it and when (authoring only, or also after send); whether it needs a legal basis recorded with it, since it keeps personal data nobody engaged with; and that it never shortens a conservation the acts rule would give. | ADR-0073 audit round (maintainer decision on what counts as acting for retention) | Open | 2026-09-25 |
| BL-308 | **A decline asks less of the recipient than a signature.** `SigningSession`'s decline requires the access gate but consumes no signing challenge, while the commit enforces the signing gate when one is declared (`hasSigningGate()`). [ADR-0070](adr/ADR-0070-an-envelope-ends-in-agreement-or-is-voided-and-sealed.md) §2.4 says a decline is "behind both gates". On a recipient whose access gate is the link alone, anyone holding the link can close that party's leg for good with a decline attributed to them; under [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md)'s `AT_LEAST` steps such a decline also stops being rare, and it counts as "someone acted" for keeping. Surfaced by the ADR-0073 security audit (2026-09-24), pre-existing since TASK-046. ▶ Reconcile the code with ADR-0070 §2.4 (a decline consumes a signing challenge when one is declared), or record why the ADR's wording is the one to change. | ADR-0073 audit round (security) | Open | 2026-09-25 |
| BL-309 | **A recipient's link validity is derived, not recorded.** The signing-token table is deliberately non-enumerable — a row-level policy keyed on a presented digest, no privilege for the application role, reads only through `session.resolve_signing_token()` — and `StoreBackedSigningTokenIssuer::issueFor()`'s callers keep only the plaintext, discarding `expiresAt`. So [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.8's release reasons derive "the link was valid at the deadline" as the last recorded invitation-sent instant plus the fixed `LIFETIME`, which is exact only while the life is fixed and every link is minted at a send. ▶ Requested by the maintainer on 2026-09-25 for later: record a "link issued" fact carrying its expiry, crossing from Session to Envelope without making the token table enumerable, so the reasons stay exact when [BL-306](BACKLOG.md) re-issues links or the life changes. | ADR-0073 third review round (maintainer decision on link validity) | Open | 2026-09-25 |
| BL-310 | **A delivery that kills the worker is delivered again and again, and never parks.** A worker process that dies inside a delivery (the kernel's kill at the production container's 512M memory cap, or a PHP fatal error) leaves the message unacknowledged. The broker delivers it again with no retry count, so Messenger never retries it or parks it on `failed`, and every handler of that fact runs again each time. The event queues are classic with no delivery limit (`f5sign-infra`, `docker/rabbitmq/definitions.json`), and production runs one worker by default (`WORKER_REPLICAS`), so the queue stops for every tenant. Any handler whose memory grows with its input can cause it: the audit trail's gathering was one (`BL-302`), and SignatureExecution's seal needs about seven times one document (measured 2026-09-29, [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §3.2). The ask for `f5sign-infra`: a delivery limit on the event queues, so that a message that kills its worker ends in the dead letter after a bounded number of deliveries. A quorum queue's `x-delivery-limit` is one way; the choice is theirs. **What would force it:** a handler whose memory grows with an input that a tenant controls, or a fatal error on one message. | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §3.2 (the consequence); the remedy is infra's | Folded | 2026-09-28 (Evidence & Audit pre-merge review) |
| BL-311 | **SignatureExecution's signing act and annulment seal recognise a redelivery only after doing the work again.** [`SignForRecipientUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/UseCase/SignForRecipientUseCase.php) and `SealVoidedEnvelopeUseCase` (retired, see below) have no check before the work. A redelivered command signs or seals the first document again through DSS and opens a custody, and only then does the append's [`ArtifactAlreadyAppendedException`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Exception/ArtifactAlreadyAppendedException.php) tell it that the act committed. The use case catches it and returns, so the command's transaction commits a custody that was opened and never written. The completion seal avoids this only because its own commit moves the envelope out of `READY_TO_SEAL`; `VOIDED` is terminal, so the annulment seal cannot use the status. The audit trail made the rerun routine: `EnvelopeVoided`'s delivery also runs the trail's issuance, and each of its retries (at most four, `max_retries` in [`messenger.yaml`](../config/packages/messenger.yaml)) runs the annulment seal again, which costs one platform seal (a DSS signing call at PAdES B-B, so no timestamp) and one custody that holds no bytes ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.10). A check before the work needs the producer of each document's tip, which `DocumentView` does not expose. **What would force it:** a sweep that must reap custodies that hold no bytes, a signing engine that bills or limits signing calls, or a sibling handler of `EnvelopeVoided` that fails often. ⚑ **The annulment half is gone since the merge of ADR-0073 (2026-09-29)**: `SealVoidedEnvelopeUseCase` and SignatureExecution's `OnEnvelopeVoided` are retired, the one seal path runs on `EnvelopeReadyToSeal` for both outcomes (ADR-0073 §2.5), and a retry of the trail's issuance no longer re-runs a seal. The signing act's half stands. Since 2026-10-01 the trail's issuance no longer runs on `EnvelopeVoided` at all ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §9), so the trail adds no rerun to the half that stands, and ADR-0072 §2.10 no longer records a cost. ⚑ **Since [TASK-053](tasks/TASK-053-the-recipients-drawn-signature-on-their-mark.md) (2026-10-07) the signing act's half is closed for a recipient who committed a signature image, and only for them**: Session clears the image's bytes on this act's own `RecipientSigned` and keeps its digest, so a redelivery that finds the image cleared knows the act landed and returns before any DSS call (TASK-053 §4 D9). A recipient who sent no image still meets the rerun this row describes. ⚑ **Since [TASK-069](tasks/TASK-069-a-document-is-sealed-once-nobody-can-sign-it.md) (2026-10-23) the early seal of a document (`SealDocumentUseCase`) is not an instance of this row**: it returns on `DocumentView::$sealedBy` before any custody is opened, so a redelivery does the work once. The envelope seal (`SealEnvelopeUseCase`) first asks whether its own execution already sealed anything, with the same effect, except in the window of [BL-465](BACKLOG.md). The phrase above, *"the one seal path runs on `EnvelopeReadyToSeal` for both outcomes"*, is true of the envelope seal only: a document can now be sealed earlier by its own execution (ADR-0087). | the remedy is SignatureExecution's, with no record yet; ADR-0072 §9 records that the trail's part is gone | Open | 2026-09-28 (Evidence & Audit pre-merge review) |
| BL-313 | **Storage texts state facts that the storage code contradicts.** (1) [`CustodyStore::open()`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Contract/CustodyStore.php) declares only `ObligationNotSatisfiableException`, and [`Custody::open()`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Domain/Aggregate/Custody.php) also refuses a custody opened conserved (`CustodyStateException::conservedAtOpen()`). (2) [`CustodyObligations::strengthen()`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Contract/CustodyObligations.php) declares two exceptions and no no-op. The aggregate does nothing when the current obligation already dominates the new one. It refuses a destroyed or deleted custody with `CustodyStateException`, and a weaker obligation with `ObligationWeakenedException`. (3) [`CustodyObligationService`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/CustodyObligationService.php) warns against `IRREVOCABLE` because it "resolves to a `COMPLIANCE` lock, which nothing can destroy at any credential level for the retention's whole length". That is the mapping that 2026-09-03 replaced: every conserved object now gets one `COMPLIANCE` lock of one roll window, whatever its enforceability ([`ObjectLockMode`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Domain/Service/ObjectLockMode.php), [ADR-0061](adr/ADR-0061-retention-deletion-and-purge.md) §2.3). (4) [`config/services.yaml`](../config/services.yaml) says `CustodyReclamation` has no consumer and stays public "until a real caller does". `DiscardAbandonedUploadsCommand` and `PruneSupersededArtifactsCommand` consume it. (5) [`PendingCustodySweep`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Application/Service/PendingCustodySweep.php) says "a sweep has no author". `SystemActorId::scheduler()` is a typed `Issuer` for a scheduled job, and `CustodyDestructionService` uses it. The class's other reason to stay off the bus (a sweep has no aggregate subject) still holds. **What would force it:** a new caller of `open()` or `strengthen()` that writes its error handling from the docblock. | fold pending | Open | 2026-09-25 (Evidence & Audit step-6 census) |
| BL-314 | **Envelope's two document downloads answer bytes that fail their digest as a 409 `CONFLICT` whose detail names the custody.** [`GetCurrentDocumentHandler`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Application/Query/GetCurrentDocumentHandler.php) and [`GetSealedDocumentHandler`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Application/Query/GetSealedDocumentHandler.php) call `CustodyStore::read()` with no catch. For bytes that do not match the recorded digest, `read()` throws `CustodyBytesCorruptedException`, and that class extends `ConflictException`. So [`ApiExceptionListener`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Http/ApiExceptionListener.php) answers 409 `CONFLICT`, with the message as `detail`: "Bytes read for custody <id> do not match its recorded digest". It logs the answer at `info`, like any other 4xx. The client gets an internal id and a state-conflict code for a storage integrity failure, and nobody is alerted. Neither [`DownloadCurrentDocumentController`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/DownloadCurrentDocumentController.php) nor [`DownloadSealedDocumentController`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/DownloadSealedDocumentController.php) lists a 409 in its `#[OA\Get]`. The audit trail's route answers the same case as 409 `AUDIT_TRAIL_UNAVAILABLE` with no custody id, and its controller logs it at `error` ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.10). **What would force it:** the first detected substitution on a document download, or an integrator that reads the 409 as a state to wait out. | fold pending | Open | 2026-09-25 (Evidence & Audit step-6 census) |
| BL-315 | **ADR-0021 and ADR-0022 describe the request edge as it was before two changes.** [ADR-0021](adr/ADR-0021-http-request-edge.md) §2 says the tenant listener runs on `kernel.request`, before routing, and rejects a missing tenant with 400. Its enforcement line cites a "missing-tenant 400" test. [`TenantRequestListener`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Http/TenantRequestListener.php) runs on `kernel.controller` and answers 401, and that test asserts 401. [ADR-0022](adr/ADR-0022-http-controller-conventions.md) calls its Cluster B, the `ActorContext` seam, "Proposed, not built", and says that controllers read a raw `X-Acting-User-Id` header. [`PrincipalContext`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Actor/PrincipalContext.php) is that seam, widened: the listener sets it, the controllers read it, and no controller reads the header. **What would force it:** a change to the request edge made from what the two ADRs say. | [ADR-0021](adr/ADR-0021-http-request-edge.md) §2 + [ADR-0022](adr/ADR-0022-http-controller-conventions.md) Cluster B | Open | 2026-09-25 (Evidence & Audit step-6 census) |
| BL-316 | **Comments in config, tests and code, and `CLAUDE.md`, state facts that the tree contradicts.** (1) [`RouteCollisionTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Integration/RouteCollisionTest.php) names a route-name prefix `se.*` that no route uses. The route files use `envelope.`, `signing.` (Session) and `evidence_audit.`. (2) [`config/routes.yaml`](../config/routes.yaml) ("Methods with the #[Route] attribute are automatically imported") and [`config/services.yaml`](../config/services.yaml) describe attribute routing. No `#[Route]` exists in `src/`, and `routes.yaml` imports only `routes/api/*.php`. (3) The bundle table in [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) omits SentryBundle and lists TwigBundle as not enabled. [`config/bundles.php`](../config/bundles.php) enables both in every environment. (4) The retry comment in [`messenger.yaml`](../config/packages/messenger.yaml) gives five delays, "2s, 6s, 18s, 54s, 60s (capped) ≈ 2.3 min". With `max_retries: 4`, `delay: 2000` and `multiplier: 3`, there are four: 2, 6, 18 and 54 s. That is about 80 s in all, and the cap never applies. (5) `services.yaml` says that handlers register through `#[AsMessageHandler]` with no explicit entries. The `_instanceof` block below it tags the same classes again, which is harmless because Messenger drops a duplicate handler name. (6) [`DbalTransactionMiddleware`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Bus/DbalTransactionMiddleware.php) says that a separate `RelayEventLogCommand` process drives the reactors. The relay only publishes to `async_events`, and the reactors run in the worker. Its conclusion holds: a reactor's dispatch is a fresh top-level command. (7) SignatureExecution's [`OnSessionCompletedHandler`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Infrastructure/Messenger/Subscriber/OnSessionCompletedHandler.php) says that its two handlers "are independent" because each gates on the signing mode "before doing any work". They share one delivery and one retry budget. The codec does not carry `HandledStamp`, so a retry that one handler causes runs the other again. The paired [`OnSessionCompleted`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/Subscriber/OnSessionCompleted.php) puts the gates in the use case and the aggregate: "each dispatches, and the mode decides which one's work survives". **What would force it:** a change to the retry policy, the routing or the handlers of `SessionCompleted`, made on a comment's word. | fold pending | Open | 2026-09-25 (Evidence & Audit step-6 census) |
| BL-317 | **`OpenApiSpecTest::every_api_v1_route_is_documented()` cannot fail for a route whose controller has no `#[OA\*]` attribute.** Nelmio puts every route's path in the spec, documented or not, so the test finds the path and passes. Measured 2026-09-25 by sabotage: with the audit-trail route's `#[OA\Get]` removed, this test stayed green. Only `every_route_that_requires_a_declared_subject_documents_the_header` failed, and it checks only the routes that require a declared subject. So an undocumented public route passes the spec tests. `BL-181` says that this test asserts "that every route is documented", which holds for the path and not for its documentation. **What would force it:** a public route shipped with no `#[OA\*]` attribute. | fold pending | Open | 2026-09-25 (Evidence & Audit step 6, a sabotage run) |
| BL-318 | **One parked message leaves five copies in `queue.events.dead-letter`.** Symfony's AMQP transport rejects the delivery on every failure, also when it schedules a retry. The event queues' dead-letter exchange keeps each rejected copy. Measured 2026-09-28 on the dev stack: one message that failed five times and parked gave five dead letters. So anything that counts dead letters (an alert, a report, an operator's triage) counts five per parked message, and four of them are retries. `BL-154` holds the dead-letter policy of these queues. **What would force it:** an alert or a report that counts dead letters. | fold pending | Open | 2026-09-28 (Evidence & Audit exit run) |
| BL-319 | **Tests and the dev workers share the broker's vhost `/`, and the traffic goes both ways.** The known direction: the dev worker consumes messages that tests publish, so [`tests/README.md`](../tests/README.md) says to run `make worker-down` first. The other direction: tests leave messages that the dev worker consumes later. On 2026-09-28 the dev broker held 501 test messages, 204 on `queue.session.events` and 297 on `queue.failed`, all from test tenants. `messenger:failed:retry --force` replays all of them. **What would force it:** a dev-stack run that replays `queue.failed`, or a dev worker left up during `make test`. The structural fix is a vhost of its own for the tests, which is infra's. | fold pending (infra) | Open | 2026-09-28 (Evidence & Audit exit run) |
| BL-320 | **`make dss-wait-tl` can answer "ready" before EU DSS serves a request.** The target runs `wait-for-tl.sh` in the `eu-dss` container, and the script waits only for a LOTL file in the trusted-list cache directory. On 2026-09-28 it said "LOTL disponible tras 0s" right after `eu-dss` started, and the container's healthcheck turned healthy about 18 s later. The cache survived the restart, so the script checked a file and not the service. A signing test that starts in that window fails on a service that is still starting. **What would force it:** a script or a CI job that runs signing tests right after it starts DSS. | fold pending (infra) | Open | 2026-09-28 (Evidence & Audit exit run) |
| BL-321 | **The audit trail's render can leave personal data in the worker's `/tmp`.** [`TypstTrailRenderer`](../src/F5Sign/EvidenceAudit/Infrastructure/Render/TypstTrailRenderer.php) writes the trail JSON (the minimised personal data) into a private job directory under `evidence_audit.render_scratch_dir` (`/tmp`), and removes it in a `finally`. Two cases leave it behind. `remove()` catches an `IOException` and goes on, and its docblock says why. A worker killed during the render never reaches the `finally`, and the worker's memory cap makes a kill a real case (`BL-310`). Nothing removes a job directory that a call left behind. **What would force it:** a retention or erasure promise that covers the worker's disk. A sweep of `trail-*` directories older than the render's timeout closes it. | fold pending | Open | 2026-09-28 (Evidence & Audit pre-merge review) |
| BL-323 | **Nothing reports an issued audit trail that the custodian did not conserve.** `app:evidence-audit:conserve-trails` raises each issued trail's custody to its envelope's conservation ceiling, every hour. If the job stops, or a tenant's read fails every hour, the trails keep the obligation they were issued with, and nothing says so. The command's non-zero exit is the only signal, and a job that does not run gives none. Promotion has this detector for signed documents: `app:envelope:report-unpromoted` runs daily and fails over a threshold ([ADR-0062](adr/ADR-0062-stage-then-promote.md) §3.3). **What would force it:** a custodian outage that nobody sees. | fold pending | Open | 2026-09-28 (Evidence & Audit pre-merge review) |
| BL-324 | **`ConserveTrailsCommand`'s per-tenant failure branch has no test.** When a tenant's read of its unconserved trails throws, [`ConserveTrailsCommand`](../src/F5Sign/EvidenceAudit/Infrastructure/Console/ConserveTrailsCommand.php) names the tenant, goes on with the other tenants, and exits non-zero. The cases of [`ConserveTrailsCommandTest`](../tests/F5Sign/EvidenceAudit/Integration/ConserveTrailsCommandTest.php) never make that read fail, and Infection reports the branch as not covered. The case that fails the run (P-TRAIL-178) goes through one trail's ceiling read, not through the tenant's read. **What would force it:** a change to the loop that stops at the first tenant that fails. ▶ **Extended 2026-10-26 by [TASK-047](tasks/TASK-047-per-signer-evidence-document.md):** the same command now also reads `evidence_audit.issued_receipt` through a second per-tenant loop (`tenants_with_unconserved_receipts()`); that loop's failure branch is equally untested, and a change that stops at the first failing tenant would break it just as silently. | fold pending | Open | 2026-09-28 (Evidence & Audit step 6, Infection) |
| BL-325 | ⚠ **A recipient invitation can still be minted for an envelope that closed a moment earlier.** [TASK-040](tasks/TASK-040-cancellation-notice.md) made `NotifyActivatedStepRecipientsUseCase`, `NotifyCorrectedRecipientUseCase` and `NotifyResentInvitationRecipientUseCase` refuse once the envelope view carries `closedAt`, but a reaction that read the envelope just before the close and saves its invitation after the ending notice read its audience is not caught: nothing serializes the two, so that recipient is invited and never told. The window is two relayed reactions wide, not a retry wide. ▶ **What would force it:** an observed invitation to a closed envelope, or a serialization point shared by the invitation reactions and the ending notice. | [TASK-040](tasks/TASK-040-cancellation-notice.md) §7 and §8 (last bullet) | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-040) |
| BL-326 | ⚠ **The sender's void reason is dual-purpose, and a phishing vector.** It is the internal record (`reason` on `EnvelopeVoided`, `end_reason` on the envelope) and, since [TASK-040](tasks/TASK-040-cancellation-notice.md), it is quoted verbatim in a platform-branded email to every recipient. It is HTML-escaped, but a URL or an instruction in it still reads as coming from the platform, and nothing enforces the split; the `#[OA\*]` string on `VoidEnvelopeRequest::$reason` is the only control. ▶ **What would force it:** a sender using the field to address recipients, or a report of a recipient acting on text in it; the fix is two fields (a contract change) or filtering what the reason may carry. | BL-333 | **`AddStepRequest::ordinal` has no upper bound** (pre-existing): a value past the INT column reaches the INSERT and answers 500 instead of 422. `min_signatures` and `deadline_hours` were bounded in [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) (`b22dd37e`); `ordinal` was outside its scope. ▶ **What would force it:** an integrator sending a large ordinal; the fix is one `Range` constraint and a 422 case. | [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) §6 | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-050) |

| BL-343 | **Invitation failures and resends carry no stored instant, so one recorded between a clock and its sweep still changes that clock's release reasons.** [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) judges the reasons at the clock's own instant (`5b5212e7`), but only the opening instant and the link's validity can be measured against it: the invitation instant is always the latest. ⛑ 2026-10-09: since TASK-056 the release snapshot carries the sent, failed and unsent instants rather than a failure boolean, but `ReleaseReasons::reasonFor()` still judges the latest one regardless of `$at`, so the residual stands. A failure or a resend that lands in the gap between a step deadline (or `expires_at`) and the sweep therefore still turns a `SENT_NO_RESPONSE` into `DELIVERY_FAILED`, or a `LINK_EXPIRED` into `SENT_NO_RESPONSE`, and in a veto step can flip `MEMBER_NOT_REACHED`. The gap is at most a minute in production. ▶ **What would force it:** store the instant of each invitation failure and resend (relates to [`BL-309`](BACKLOG.md), the recorded "link issued" fact), and judge them at `$at`; `ReleaseReasons`' docblock names the residual. | [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) §7 (Q1); `ReleaseReasons` docblock | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-050) |
| BL-344 | **A recipient's opening racing a step's deadline is argued and single-process-tested, never raced.** [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) makes an opening either recorded (and the release reason `OPENED_NO_ACT`) or not recorded (and, the member being blocking, the gate refuses and no credential is minted). What stands in for the race is the opening refusing a closed leg under the recipient row's lock (`RecipientOpeningWriteTest`, `GateRuleParityTest`) and the clock command locking the root and then every recipient row (TASK-049), so the lock order is reasoned, not measured. ▶ **What would force it:** `tests/F5Sign/Envelope/Integration/EnvelopeRaceActs.php` has no `clocks` act and `EnvelopeRootLockRaceTest` has no deadline case; add the act and a two-process case asserting both outcomes above, and that none leaves an opening recorded with a credential refused or the reverse. | [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) §7 (Declared and unproven properties) | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-050) |
| BL-345 | **A commit racing a step's deadline is argued and single-process-tested, never raced.** [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) makes a commit admitted just before the deadline count and not be released, and the reverse order refuse the commit. The single-process tests pin each order alone; nothing runs the two as competing processes, so the root-then-recipient-rows lock order of the clock command (TASK-049) is reasoned, not measured. ▶ **What would force it:** the same missing harness as [`BL-344`](BACKLOG.md) (`tests/F5Sign/Envelope/Integration/EnvelopeRaceActs.php` has no `clocks` act, `EnvelopeRootLockRaceTest` has no deadline case); add a two-process commit-versus-clock case that asserts both orders. | [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) §7 (Declared and unproven properties) | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-050) |

| BL-347 | **The waiting `AT_LEAST` step's published description is asserted by nothing.** Its ending is pinned over HTTP — `StepWithoutDeadlineExpiryHttpTest::a_non_last_at_least_step_with_no_deadline_waits_for_everyone_and_the_date_ends_it_not_agreed` (a non-last `AT_LEAST 1` step with no deadline, one member signs, the next step never activates, `NOT_AGREED` · `EXPIRED` at the date) — and at the aggregate by TASK-049's stories. What TASK-050 §5 also asks, *the published step description states it*, lives only in the `#[OA]` prose of `AddStepController` and `AddStepRequest`, and no test reads it; a rewording that dropped the warning would stay green. ▶ **What would force it:** an assertion on the emitted OpenAPI (as `OpenApiSpecTest` does for other load-bearing strings) that the step route states an `AT_LEAST` step without a deadline waits for every member. | [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) §7 (Declared and unproven properties) | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-050) |
| BL-348 | **"Same facts, same outcome" between a step deadline and an envelope expiry is not compared at the HTTP tier.** The pure census (`StepCompletionEvaluatorTest`), the aggregate stories, `EnvelopeExpirySameOutcomeTest` and `StepDeadlineGapTest` hold it; no HTTP case sets a deadline and an expiry on the same facts and compares what the routes publish. ▶ **What would force it:** an `Acceptance/` case that runs both ends on identical facts and asserts the published outcome and reasons are equal. | [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) §7 (Declared and unproven properties) | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-050) |
| BL-349 | **Close-now with a `VIEWER` step that was never activated is not exercised over HTTP.** The notice and the reads are pinned at their tiers (`NotifyCompletedEnvelopeRecipientsUseCaseTest`, `RecipientReadRulesTest`, `WhatEachRecipientReadsAfterTheCloseHttpTest`) and `CloseEnvelopeNowHttpTest` releases the pending members of a secured `AT_LEAST` step, but not this case, which is the close-now case of the copy-ready notice bar. ▶ **What would force it:** a `CloseEnvelopeNowHttpTest` case with a never-activated `VIEWER` member, asserting the notice that member gets and what they read afterwards. | [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) §7 (Declared and unproven properties) | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-050) |
| BL-350 | **"Admitted counts" is pinned at the pure evaluator tier only.** A signature admitted but not yet landed (its bytes still at DSS) while the step's other members decline keeps the minimum reachable, so the step does not end `NOT_AGREED` / `MINIMUM_UNREACHABLE` ahead of the landing (ADR-0073 §2.2: the admission instant decides, never the bytes' arrival). The pure rules hold it; no aggregate or HTTP case drives an admitted-but-unlanded commit against declines. ▶ **What would force it:** an aggregate or `Acceptance/` case that admits a commit, holds its bytes, lets the others decline, and asserts the step waits and then agrees on the landing. | [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) §7 (Declared and unproven properties) | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-050) |
| BL-351 | **Sub-second boundaries floored by `datetime_immutable`, consistent today.** DBAL's `datetime_immutable` bind floors to the second (`UtcDateTimeImmutableType`). The census at the ADR-0073 package join (TASK-050 §7, *Found at the package join*) found these consistent today but floored: `envelope.ready_to_seal_at` (floored while `closed_at`, set from the same instant, keeps microseconds; nothing compares them yet); `envelope.settings_expires_at` (expiry judged up to 1 s early after a reload; the sweep's funnel floors alike, so they agree); `session.refresh_grace_until` (the 30 s rotation grace becomes 29–30 s after a reload); `session.soft_locked_until` (the cooldown can end up to 1 s early); and `auth_attempt.attempted_at` (`ORDER BY attempted_at` has no tie-break, so two attempts in one second reload in unspecified order; nothing reads that order today). ⚑ **The credential-retirement instants need no move, and their comparison must stay as it is**: `credentials_retired_at` and `invitations_revoked_at` are compared by `getTimestamp()` on both sides against a whole-second token `issued_at` (`Envelope`, `RecipientAuthContext`, `ResolvedSigningToken`). Microseconds stored there would change nothing; switching those comparisons to `<` on the objects would decide a same-second token by sub-second noise. ▶ **What would force it:** a rule that reads one of these to the sub-second, or a boundary test that fails on the floor. Move each through an `instant()`-style binding then, not before. `step.activated_at`/`deadline_at` must move together or not at all (`step_deadline_at_chk`). | [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) §7 | Open | 2026-09-29 (ADR-0073 package join) |
| BL-352 | *(Filed as BL-310 on the ADR-0073 package branch; renumbered when develop, which had published BL-310..324 first, was merged.)* **`ChainConflictRetryStrategy`'s ~3-hour retry curve is bounded per message, not per envelope.** ADR-0073 §2.17/ADR-0015 give a lost document-chain race 183 retries (the shared curve's four, then 179 at its 60s ceiling) before parking — deliberately longer than the 2-hour pending-seal alarm's threshold. That budget is uncapped by how many recipients a step allows signing in parallel, which this repo does not itself cap: a sender with an unusually large parallel signing step could sustain legitimate chain-conflict churn that ties up shared worker/DSS capacity for hours. Not attacker-reachable without an already-admitted signing act (a valid session credential, passed admission under the root lock) — a capacity/degradation concern, not a disclosure or integrity one. Surfaced by TASK-048's security audit (round 1, W1), accepted as-is for this task. ▶ **What would force it:** a cap on concurrent parallel signers per step, or a per-envelope ceiling on total in-flight chain-conflict retries, the next time capacity planning or an incident makes the uncapped curve a live concern. | [TASK-048](tasks/TASK-048-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §7 (security audit W1) | Open | 2026-09-29 |
| BL-353 | *(Filed as BL-311 on the ADR-0073 package branch; renumbered when develop, which had published BL-310..324 first, was merged.)* **`Role::isBlocking()` has no production caller left.** Its docblock still cites "BC §7 inv 26 — only blocking roles abort an envelope on decline", but ADR-0073/TASK-048 moved agreement, routing and termination onto `EnvelopeEndingEvaluator`/`StepPolicy`, which encode blocking-ness through the step's own completion policy rather than asking this method; ⛑ Corrected 2026-10-01 (post-merge review): this read *"the sole remaining reference in the tree is a test docblock, not a call"*, which was false then — `TrailAssembler::rosterReconciled()` called it to decide which parties a reconciled roster may leave out. The audit trail's re-cut for [BL-357](BACKLOG.md) moved that check onto ADR-0073 §2.8's *"no member is left PENDING"* and removed the call. Surfaced by TASK-048's closure sweep. ▶ **What would force it:** deleting the method (or reconciling its docblock to state plainly that it is retained only as documentation of the pre-ADR-0073 predicate, if any future caller is still expected). | [TASK-048](tasks/TASK-048-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §7 | Open | 2026-09-29 |
| BL-354 | *(Filed as BL-312 on the ADR-0073 package branch; renumbered when develop, which had published BL-310..324 first, was merged.)* **`WorkflowType::SEAL_ONLY` has no zero-recipient carve-out at `send()`.** `Envelope::ensurePreSendInvariants()` refuses any envelope with zero recipients ("no recipients (BC §7 inv 2)"), unconditionally — so a `SEAL_ONLY` envelope, whose name implies a sender-only seal with no recipient ceremony, can never actually be sent with zero recipients today. Pre-existing, unrelated to TASK-048; surfaced by its closure sweep while auditing ADR-0073 §2.6's "at least one blocking member per envelope" validation. ▶ **What would force it:** deciding whether `SEAL_ONLY` is meant to admit a zero-recipient send (and adding the carve-out) or whether the workflow type's name is aspirational and the invariant is the actual product decision, the next time `SEAL_ONLY` authoring is worked on. | [TASK-048](tasks/TASK-048-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §7 | Open | 2026-09-29 |
| BL-356 | *(Filed as BL-314 on the ADR-0073 package branch; renumbered when develop, which had published BL-310..324 first, was merged.)* **`StorageLifecycleScenarioTest::bytes_are_staged_promoted_and_destroyed_on_the_clock_the_platform_undertook` fails intermittently in full-suite order, green alone every time.** Fails at `assertConserved()`'s "and its version is gone from the bucket" assertion (`versionExists` on the superseded staging placement, MinIO-backed); passes in isolation 3/3 on 2026-09-29 and on every prior rerun. Seen in three full-suite runs on `feat/task-048-how-an-envelope-ends` (2026-09-28 once; 2026-09-29 at `786c39c2` and at `1f4af49d` — the latter a clean 4164/23618 run otherwise, phpstan/lint/deptrac clean). The test was itself edited by TASK-048 (`8cd3424a`, `41e73bcb`). Suspected, unconfirmed: MinIO version-deletion visibility lag under full-suite load, or order-dependent bucket state shared across tests. ⚑ **2026-10-21, reproducible on demand:** it was the only red (besides three date-bomb classes) in the initial test run of diff-filtered Infection on `feat/resend-interval-per-channel` (`wt-backend-infection` with `--filter` over five Envelope files), two runs out of two (5507 and 1990 tests selected), while the class ran green alone on the same lane between them. That selection is a ready-made reproduction of the full-suite ordering this row asks for. Excluded there with `--test-framework-options=--exclude-filter=StorageLifecycleScenarioTest`; it covers none of the mutated files. ▶ **What would force it:** reproducing it deliberately by running the storage-lifecycle tests alongside enough MinIO-touching siblings to recreate full-suite ordering, then instrumenting the version-delete call's timing, the next time this flake recurs or storage lifecycle tests are touched. | [TASK-048](tasks/TASK-048-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §7 | Open | 2026-09-29 |
| BL-358 | **The read-to-lock race in the ending arm.** If the step deadline passes between the opening's unlocked read and its row lock, an open where the deadline ends the envelope still answers `LEG_CLOSED` instead of `ENVELOPE_NO_LONGER_LIVE`. Rare and bounded to one refusal code; the act is refused either way. ▶ **What would force it:** a client that branches on the code in that window. | [TASK-051](tasks/TASK-051-closed-answers-do-not-wait-for-the-revocation.md) §7 | Open | 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-051) |
| BL-359 | **The audit trail states no step deadline.** A step's deadline follows from its activation and the deadline that `ENVELOPE_ROSTER_DECLARED` publishes ([ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.6), so a trail whose ending's cause is `STEP_DEADLINE_MISSED` names the cause but not the date it was judged against. Left out of the trail's re-cut by the maintainer's call. **What would force it:** a tenant who needs a deadline ending's date in the evidence. | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.11 | Folded | 2026-10-01 (the trail's re-cut) |
| BL-360 | **`CLAIM_WHILE_SLOT_COULD_ACT` does not check a claim against the slot's release.** It fails a claim recorded after the party's completion or after its step closed. Since ADR-0073 a released slot can no longer be claimed (§2.8), and Envelope refuses such a claim, so a claim after a release would be an anomaly that the rule does not name. **What would force it:** a stream that holds a claim after its slot's `RECIPIENT_RELEASED`. | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.11 | Folded | 2026-10-01 (the trail's re-cut) |
| BL-361 | **Nothing compares `ENVELOPE_COMPLETED`'s `released` and `declined` lists with each party's own facts.** The completion carries a second account of how each released or declining party's leg ended. The trail states each outcome from `RECIPIENT_RELEASED` and `SESSION_DECLINED`, and does not read the lists, so a disagreement between the two goes unnamed. **Forced (2026-10-01, the re-cut's review):** the TASK-048 data migration releases members with no fact, and `app:envelope:reconcile-endings` then completes such an envelope with them in `released`. Its trail states no outcome for them, so `ROSTER_RECONCILED` fails, and it states the cause of a void recorded before ADR-0070 as not recorded, although the completion carries `end_cause` (ADR-0072 §2.9). Decide before any deployment runs the reconcile command on such envelopes. | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.9, §2.11 | Folded | 2026-10-01 (the trail's re-cut) |
| BL-362 | **The published API description carries claims that go stale, in text every integrator reads.** The descriptions in [`config/packages/nelmio_api_doc.yaml`](../config/packages/nelmio_api_doc.yaml) are emitted into the OpenAPI document. Outside the `Audit trail` tag, about thirty candidates were flagged in the Envelope, Session and signing schemas: words that date a fact ("Always false today", "the platform default is currently Spanish", "Two sources for now"), change notes ("was a member here until 2026-09-01"), counts that restate a value ("eleven of the fifteen", "Fifteen minutes"), and backlog ids that an integrator cannot open ("BL-270", "BL-272", "BL-40"). Nothing checks them against the tree, and an integrator cannot tell a stale one from a true one. Found by the hygiene pass of the audit trail's re-cut, which edited only that tag. **What would force it:** publishing the spec to an integrator outside the team. Check each claim against the tree, keep the lasting fact without the dating word, and drop the change notes and the ids. | doc hygiene (no ADR home) | Open | 2026-10-01 (the trail's re-cut, hygiene pass; filed 2026-10-05) |
| BL-363 | **`LIVE_SCHEMA.md` and the Envelope domain model carry claims that go stale, and two are already wrong.** The hygiene pass of the audit trail's re-cut flagged about 60 candidates in [`docs/LIVE_SCHEMA.md`](LIVE_SCHEMA.md) (dated notes, "today" and "until" wording, counts and migration pins in the Envelope, Session, Notification and role sections) and about 90 in [`docs/ddd/envelope-domain-model.md`](ddd/envelope-domain-model.md), all outside the passages the re-cut edited. The two that are wrong now: the `auth_selection_mode` row of `LIVE_SCHEMA.md` says "the precedent is `access_code_hash` two rows down", and that is the next row; the `CloseSigningWindow` entry of the domain model says close-now exists "as pure rules only" and that "no aggregate method or route calls it yet (TASK-050)", while its `CloseEnvelopeNow` entry says it is realized as `Envelope::closeNow()` behind `POST /api/v1/envelopes/{id}/close`. In the same model, the `EnvelopeVoided` row repeats its subscribers inside the When cell, beside the column that lists them. [`BL-99`](BACKLOG.md) is the fix for the database facts of the schema file; this row is about its prose. **What would force it:** the next edit to the Envelope or Session sections of either file. | doc hygiene (no ADR home) | Open | 2026-10-01 (the trail's re-cut, hygiene pass; filed 2026-10-05) |
| BL-364 | **The Evidence & Audit domain model still describes the audit trail as a signed XML.** The body of [`docs/ddd/evidence-audit-domain-model.md`](ddd/evidence-audit-domain-model.md) describes the Audit Trail XML signed with XAdES-B-LTA and "the evidence-projector feature". [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) replaced that design: the trail is one PDF/A-3a per envelope, sealed at PAdES B-T and made on `ENVELOPE_COMPLETED`. The banner of the document marks those passages as stale and says "The model's next feature reconciles this document in place"; no row tracked that promise until this one. **What would force it:** the next Evidence & Audit feature, as the banner says. | [`evidence-audit-domain-model.md`](ddd/evidence-audit-domain-model.md), its ADR-0072 banner | Folded | 2026-10-01 (the trail's re-cut, hygiene pass; filed 2026-10-05) |
| BL-365 | **Nothing checks for a docblock that a second docblock has detached from its declaration.** PHP binds only the last docblock before a declaration, so the first is invisible to reflection, PHPStan and the IDE, and its tags type nothing. The usual cause is a declaration inserted between a docblock and the one it describes, and the old declaration loses its text without a gate going red. It was fixed by hand three times (`a8297d31`, `dda1d063`, `5d57c4a1`) before a census on 2026-10-05 found 39 more in `src/` and `tests/`, 29 of them that shape. 38 were moved back, merged or deleted by hand; the 39th, stranded above `mintCredentialFor()`, is fixed on `refactor/persistence` instead. Reattaching is not always neutral: a stranded `@param` on `SelfSerializingMessageSerializer::decode()` was wider than the type the method inherits, and PHPStan reported two errors when it was attached, so it was deleted. **What would force it:** the next one, which nothing reports. The check is a test beside `DocumentationLinkTest` that tokenizes the same trees and fails on a doc comment whose next token, after whitespace and comments, is another doc comment, naming the file and line. | doc hygiene (no ADR home) | Open | 2026-10-05 (review of `refactor/persistence`) |
| BL-366 | **Nothing checks that a placement fits its page, and a seal that cannot succeed reads as done.** `send()` accepts a box outside its page, and a `page` past the document's last one, because the backend reads no page geometry (page count, MediaBox, CropBox, `/Rotate`). DSS then refuses the box when it seals (a 500: *"The new signature field position is outside the page dimensions!"*), after the act was admitted. Under [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.7 a step closes on the admission, so the log already reads `ENVELOPE_AGREED` and the signer was told the signature is done, while `SignForRecipient` retries a refusal that cannot change and is parked. `app:envelope:report-pending-seals` names the envelope two hours after the close; nothing names the cause. Measured end to end on the local stack on 2026-10-05 with a `/Rotate 90` page. The rotation half is fixed on `fix/rotated-page-mark` (`fieldParameters.rotation: AUTOMATIC`, pinned by `RotatedPageMarkTest` against the live DSS); the rest is this row: **(1)** a page-geometry reader whose result is stored when a document is added; **(2)** `send()` refusing a placement off its displayed page or past the last page, naming it; **(3)** `SignForRecipientUseCase` checking the box against the stored geometry before calling DSS and failing as non-retryable, logged at `error` with the envelope and the placement; **(4)** the CropBox: DSS measures from the MediaBox while a viewer shows the CropBox, so on a page whose CropBox is offset the mark shifts by that offset. Translating the box into the MediaBox's displayed frame before the call was measured to land it exactly on `/Rotate` 0, 90, 180 and 270; **(5)** undecided: what an envelope that is agreed and cannot be sealed becomes, parked and alarmed (today's behaviour, with a better signal) or the placement signed invisibly and recorded in the trail. Also undecided: `pdfinfo` (poppler, run through `symfony/process` as Typst is) or a PHP library for the reader. ⚑ **2026-10-21 — narrowed by [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) / [ADR-0085](adr/ADR-0085-recorded-values-are-form-fields-each-signature-locks.md):** on an envelope document, every signature now goes into a field of ours whose `/Rect` comes from `PageBox` (page geometry, CropBox and `/Rotate` applied by us), so **(4), the CropBox shift, is done for envelope documents** and remains only for a document signed without drawing (ADR-0085 §2.6, DSS's own coordinates). ⛔ **Behaviour change for (2) and (3):** on a drawn document DSS never sees coordinates, so an off-page placement is **no longer refused** by DSS; it is signed where `PageBox` puts it, which may fall outside the CropBox, so the mark is invisible and the act succeeds. Checking the placement (1), (2), (3) is therefore more needed, not less, and BL-269 no longer depends on it. | fold pending | Open — (4) done for envelope documents by TASK-067 (2026-10-21); (1) to (3) and (5) still open | 2026-10-05 (rotated-page sealing, measured on the local stack) |
| BL-370 | **An upload has no bound on its total duration.** The object-store client waits at most `storage.s3.idle_timeout_seconds` for each answer ([`config/packages/storage.yaml`](../config/packages/storage.yaml)), so a server that stops answering fails the write. A server that answers slowly, with no pause that long, has no limit. Since [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) the audit trail's upload holds no transaction, so a slow upload no longer holds back the event-log relay (`BL-234`). It still occupies its worker for as long as it runs. `DocumentSigner` and `MockSignUseCase` upload through `write()` inside the bus transaction, so for them a slow upload also holds back the relay. **What would force it:** an upload that trickles, or a worker pool that a few slow uploads can fill. | [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) §2.7 | Folded | 2026-10-06 (principles audit of ADR-0076) |
| BL-371 | **Storage reports a refused upload as one whose outcome is unknown.** [`S3ByteStore::put()`](../src/F5Sign/Storage/Infrastructure/Storage/S3ByteStore.php) does not tell a refusal from a lost answer. So after every failed upload, a 4xx answer included, `CustodyStore::writeAlongside()` reports the object at its key as `UNKNOWN`. A 4xx answer means that no object exists, so a reader of the report can look for a version that was never written. The tests here cannot show the split. The dev and test stacks run every role as the MinIO root user, so MinIO refuses no request for its credential. **What would force it:** a recovery that must skip the undo when nothing was written, or an operator who looks for a missing version. | [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) §2.7, §3.3 | Folded | 2026-10-06 (principles audit of ADR-0076) |
| BL-372 | **The unit of work's rollback is a recovery that does not report.** When the work of `UnitOfWork::own()` fails, its `Err` carries that failure, and a rollback that fails afterwards does not replace it ([`UnitOfWork`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Persistence/UnitOfWork.php)). The caller learns neither that the rollback failed nor the state it left. [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) §2.1 asks every recovery to return its cause, each action with its outcome, and the state it left. `CustodyStore::writeAlongside()` does not rest on this rollback, because the custody's save comes before the consumer's record. So a transaction that a failed rollback left open, and that holds a write that matters, also holds the custody as `PRESENT`. The withdrawal acts only on `PENDING` under the row lock, so it refuses that custody. A report here changes every caller of `own()`, so it is a change of its own. **What would force it:** a caller whose guarantee rests on the state that a failed rollback leaves. | [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) §1.3, §2.7 | Folded | 2026-10-06 (principles audit of ADR-0076) |
| BL-373 | **The transport retries every failure of the audit trail's issuance alike, whatever its outlook says.** [`TrailNotIssued`](../src/F5Sign/EvidenceAudit/Application/Exception/TrailNotIssued.php) carries a [`RetryOutlook`](../src/F5Sign/EvidenceAudit/Application/Exception/RetryOutlook.php), and the use case states it in the message. Messenger reads none of it: `async_events` retries each failure up to the budget of [`ChainConflictRetryStrategy`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Infrastructure/Messenger/ChainConflictRetryStrategy.php) and then moves it to `queue.failed`. A `SAME_RESULT` failure spends the whole budget, and each attempt can gather, render and seal again. [`BL-26`](BACKLOG.md) is the same gap for the signing act. **What would force it:** a failure that repeats on every attempt and costs a seal each time. | [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) §2.7 | Folded | 2026-10-06 (principles audit of ADR-0076) |
| BL-374 | **Nothing in the platform reads `queue.failed` on its own.** A message that spends its retries waits there until an operator runs `messenger:failed:retry` ([`config/packages/messenger.yaml`](../config/packages/messenger.yaml) says why `messenger:failed:show` does not work on it). No scheduled job reads the queue, so an audit trail that is never issued is visible only to an operator who looks. [`BL-319`](BACKLOG.md) is adjacent: on the dev stack the same queue also holds test traffic. **What would force it:** an ended envelope whose trail never issues, found by its tenant. | fold pending | Open | 2026-10-06 (principles audit of ADR-0076) |
| BL-375 | **A stale `PENDING` custody has no deadline, and nothing reclaims a version that a withdrawn custody still holds.** `app:storage:sweep-pending` reports a custody that stays `PENDING` and does not change it. A killed worker leaves one, and after the upload also a version under its key. A failed undo leaves a version under a withdrawn custody. Both versions are attributable by the key's prefix, `<custodyId>/`, and `app:storage:report-orphans` prints the key, but the listing port cannot list one prefix. [ADR-0063](adr/ADR-0063-where-storage-concerns-belong.md) §2.0.1 item 3's remedy becomes *withdraw the stale custody, then reclaim its prefix* (ADR-0076 §2.7). **What would force it:** versions under withdrawn custodies that accumulate, or an erasure that must reach them. | [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) §2.7, with [ADR-0063](adr/ADR-0063-where-storage-concerns-belong.md) §2.0.1 item 3 | Folded | 2026-10-06 (principles audit of ADR-0076) |
| BL-376 | **The custodian's sweeps take every due row in one run.** No query behind the scheduled jobs of Storage, Envelope and Evidence & Audit has a per-run `LIMIT` (read 2026-10-06). The `LIMIT 1` subqueries there pick one row per subject. So the size of one run is the size of the backlog, with no maximum. After an outage that backlog can be large, and the run holds the custodian's worker until it ends. **What would force it:** a run that outlasts its schedule interval. | fold pending | Open | 2026-10-06 (principles audit of ADR-0076) |
| BL-377 | **Two use cases state facts about their custody write that the code contradicts.** (1) [`UploadDocumentBytesUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Application/UseCase/Authoring/UploadDocumentBytesUseCase.php) says a crash during the write "leaves bytes at a key derivable from that row, reconcilable from either side". The key is `<custodyId>/<placementId>`, and `CustodyService::write()` mints the placement id just before the PUT. So a `PENDING` row gives only the prefix, and no port can list a prefix yet (`BL-375`). (2) [`MockSignUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/UseCase/MockSignUseCase.php) says its write is still inside the bus transaction because the post-commit point "does not exist yet". For the reason it points at `UploadDocumentBytesUseCase`, whose docblock now says that its write runs after the commit. The fact is true and the reason is not. **What would force it:** the next edit to either passage, or the move of `MockSignUseCase` to `writeAlongside()` (`BL-244`). | doc hygiene (no ADR home) | Open | 2026-10-06 (principles audit of ADR-0076) |
| BL-378 | **[ADR-0061](adr/ADR-0061-retention-deletion-and-purge.md) §2.9 calls the custodian's key unlimited, and the infrastructure scopes it.** The section's table gives the custodian path the profile `full`, and its threat table names "the custodian's unlimited key". Since 2026-09-17 the infrastructure repository's object-storage runbook gives the custodian its own scoped key. <!-- OFFREPO: `f5sign-infra` `docs/operations/object-storage.md`, the credentials section; not reachable under agent-only backend access. --> The infrastructure owns the key, so the record waits on its answer. **What would force it:** the next change to the custodian's credential, or a review of §2.9's threat table. | [ADR-0061](adr/ADR-0061-retention-deletion-and-purge.md) §2.9 | Open | 2026-10-06 (principles audit of ADR-0076) |
| BL-379 | **`phpstan/README.md` carries claims that go stale.** The hygiene pass of the purity change (2026-10-06) flagged about 80 candidates in [`phpstan/README.md`](../phpstan/README.md), all outside the passages it edited. They are dated notes, "today" and "until" wording, counts of rules and files, a commit hash, and pointers outside the repository. A reader opens the file when a rule fires, so a stale claim there misleads at that moment. **What would force it:** the next edit to a section that holds one. | doc hygiene (no ADR home) | Open | 2026-10-06 (hygiene pass of the purity change) |
| BL-380 | **Two issuances of one envelope's audit trail both do all their work, and the one that loses discards it.** Two deliveries of an envelope's end run at once only when the transport has more than one consumer. Both then gather, render, seal and upload, and Storage undoes the bytes of the issuance that loses the claim and withdraws its custody ([`IssueAuditTrailUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/EvidenceAudit/Application/UseCase/IssueAuditTrailUseCase.php), **Concurrency**). The result is correct, and the second seal and the second upload are wasted. A change that lets the second issuance learn, before it gathers, that another one is under way would let it wait or stop. Such a change must keep the use case's **Never** rule: no registry row before the bytes are written. **What would force it:** a deployment that runs more than one consumer of `async_events`. | [ADR-0076](adr/ADR-0076-a-first-write-commits-with-its-consumers-record.md) §1.2 | Open | 2026-10-06 (review of the trail's issuance) |
| BL-381 | **Nothing makes a private method static when it does not use `$this`, and `static_lambda` can break a closure that calls one.** Since `static_lambda` is on ([`.php-cs-fixer.dist.php`](../.php-cs-fixer.dist.php)), php-cs-fixer makes static every closure that does not use `$this`. A closure that calls `self::helper()` on a private instance method then fails at runtime with "Non-static method ... cannot be called statically". Neither the fixer nor PHPStan reports it, and only a test that runs the closure finds it. It happened once, in `SigningSessionDeliveredCodeTest::at()`, and the `static_lambda` change made that helper static by hand. php-cs-fixer's `static_private_method` makes such a helper static. It is a risky fixer, and a run on 2026-10-06 found 461 files to change, so it is a change of its own. **What would force it:** the next runtime failure of this kind, or a closure in production code that calls a private helper. | fold pending | Open | 2026-10-06 (the `static_lambda` change) |
| BL-382 | **A request body is capped only at 50 MB, and the commit's mixed `biometric_data` member is fully JSON-decoded before its 500-stroke / 50,000-point bounds apply, so a holder of a session credential can force a 500 against `memory_limit`.** The caps are the Caddyfile's body limit and `php.ini`; `BiometricCaptureShape` bounds the strokes only after the whole request body has been decoded. ▶ **What it needs**: a body limit on `POST /api/v1/signing/commit` close to what a valid commit weighs (a 100 KB image is about 137 KB of base64, plus strokes), set at the edge in `f5sign-infra`, or a streaming check before the decode. Low severity: it needs a live session credential and costs the attacker's own request only. Found by TASK-053's security audit (W4). | [TASK-053](tasks/TASK-053-the-recipients-drawn-signature-on-their-mark.md) §4 D7 | Open | 2026-10-07 (TASK-053 security audit) |
| BL-383 | **Envelope acceptance tests that assert recipient order flake in Infection's coverage-mode initial run, and pass in isolation and in the full suite.** Seen: `CloseEnvelopeNowHttpTest::the_sender_closes_an_envelope_secured_by_one_signature_and_releases_the_two_still_pending` and `RecipientAuthHttpTest::the_presentation_mode_is_declared_at_authoring_and_read_back`. ⚠ **Cause not confirmed**: the suspicion is a recipient read with an `ORDER BY` that has no tie-breaker, so recipients created in the same instant come back in an unspecified order under coverage's slower timing. It cost two Infection restarts (about 40 minutes) on TASK-053. ▶ **What it needs**: reproduce under `composer infection`'s initial-run settings, then either give the read a total order or make the assertion independent of it. | [TASK-053](tasks/TASK-053-the-recipients-drawn-signature-on-their-mark.md) (task-validate) | Open | 2026-10-07 (TASK-053 validation) |
| BL-385 | **The machine route takes no image, so a recipient authored without `SIMPLE` cannot finish through it.** [TASK-054](tasks/TASK-054-the-sender-chooses-how-each-recipient-signs.md) D12 refuses such a relayed act with 409 `SIGNATURE_MODE_NOT_OFFERED` and sends the recipient to the signer. **What would force it:** an integrator who needs to relay a drawn signature. | [TASK-054](tasks/tasks/TASK-054-the-sender-chooses-how-each-recipient-signs.md) §6 and D12 | fold pending | Open | 2026-10-08 |
| BL-386 | **The three signature-setting members (`signature_modes`, `biometrics_offered`, `show_name`) have no per-tenant default;** every recipient added without them signs `[SIMPLE]` (D3). **What would force it:** a tenant that wants `[DRAW]` by default. | [TASK-054](tasks/TASK-054-the-sender-chooses-how-each-recipient-signs.md) §6 | fold pending | Open | 2026-10-08 |
| BL-387 | **A `claimable` `MACHINE_KEY` recipient is reachable today (authored with both, ADR-0057 and ADR-0055 §2.2) and the D9 rule covers it, but no test pins the pair.** `Recipient`'s constructor refuses a delegated recipient whose setting is not `[SIMPLE]` with the name shown, and the claim route does not touch the setting, so a claimed one keeps it; nothing asserts that for the `claimable` + `MACHINE_KEY` combination. **Needs:** one case at the add-recipient tier and one claim-then-read case. ⚑ **Narrowed 2026-11-04 by [TASK-078](tasks/TASK-078-the-machine-routes-refuse-an-unclaimed-slot.md)**: `DelegatedCommitOnAnUnclaimedSlotHttpTest` and `ClaimedSlotOnMachineRoutesHttpTest` author the pair over HTTP, and the second claims and signs it, which `SIGNATURE_MODE_NOT_OFFERED` would refuse had the claim changed the setting. What is left is the add-recipient tier's case. | [TASK-054](tasks/TASK-054-the-sender-chooses-how-each-recipient-signs.md) §6 and D9 | fold pending | Open | 2026-10-08 |
| BL-388 | **`OpenApiSpecTest` proves a refusal code is named by SOME operation, not that it sits in the closed list of the route that can emit it.** TASK-054's fix round found the send route missing `SIGNATURE_MODES_NEED_A_MARK` while another operation named it, and the census stayed green until `is_a` discovery was added. Per-route reachability needs a PHPStan call graph that resolves the bus, which is an ADR-level decision. | [TASK-054](tasks/TASK-054-the-sender-chooses-how-each-recipient-signs.md) §5 (not yet a decision record) | fold pending | Open | 2026-10-08 |
| BL-389 | **Legal has not reviewed the optional biometric checkbox (D4), and `f5sign-docs` `Páginas Legales` still says drawing is disabled without consent.** The reading is that a PNG carries no timing or pressure and the strokes are what is biometric.
