Keeping the strokes has its own home in [TASK-053](tasks/TASK-053-the-recipients-drawn-signature-on-their-mark.md) §6. | [TASK-054](tasks/TASK-054-the-sender-chooses-how-each-recipient-signs.md) §6 and D4 | fold pending | Open | 2026-10-08 |
| BL-390 | **The ordered channel fallback covers only an IMMEDIATE refusal, because nothing ingests delivery receipts or late bounces.** Under [ADR-0077](adr/ADR-0077-a-recipient-declares-the-channels-that-reach-them.md) §2.6 an `IN_ORDER` `["SMS","EMAIL"]` moves to email only when the SMS is refused on the send call or its retries are exhausted ([ADR-0079](adr/ADR-0079-a-delivery-retries-by-what-it-carries.md)) (`TwilioSmsTransport::PERMANENT_CODES`, a permanent SMTP reply). A Twilio message accepted and then filtered by the carrier, or an email that bounces minutes later, reads as `SENT`, and `RecipientDeliveryState::DELIVERED` is unreachable because nothing can write it. Twilio status callbacks and a bounce feed are what would make the fallback cover the common loss — and what would let `SENT` mean more than "handed to a provider". ▶ Its own task: an inbound webhook route, signature verification, and the mapping onto the existing `Delivery` and trace. ⚑ **Added 2026-10-14 by [TASK-059](tasks/TASK-059-the-trail-shows-how-each-invitation-travelled.md) §3.5:** ingesting receipts retires the condition of the trail's `LimitationCode::DELIVERY_RECEIPT_NOT_RECORDED` ("a message was handed to a provider and no receipt is ingested"), so the limitation stops applying with it, and `SENT` in the evidence can become "delivered". | [ADR-0077](adr/ADR-0077-a-recipient-declares-the-channels-that-reach-them.md) §2.6 + [ADR-0079](adr/ADR-0079-a-delivery-retries-by-what-it-carries.md) §2.6 | Open | 2026-10-08 (ADR-0077 design) |
| BL-391 | **`OTP_SMS` is gated by deployment wiring alone, while an SMS notification also needs the tenant to be enabled.** [ADR-0077](adr/ADR-0077-a-recipient-declares-the-channels-that-reach-them.md) §2.4 makes SMS notifications a tenant capability (and §2.5 applies the country allowlist to `OTP_SMS` too, so the allowlist half is closed); a delivered code's channel is the declared method's ([ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.7) and `ConfiguredAuthMethodAvailability` decides it per deployment. So a tenant refused an SMS invitation may still declare an SMS code, which costs the same per message. Not decided with the re-cut on purpose: changing who may declare an authentication method is an authentication decision, and refusing `OTP_SMS` to an existing tenant would break envelopes already authored with it. ▶ Decide when the first tenant is enabled for real SMS spend. | [ADR-0077](adr/ADR-0077-a-recipient-declares-the-channels-that-reach-them.md) §3.2 | Open | 2026-10-08 (ADR-0077 design) |
| BL-392 | **The signer is not told that a code could not be delivered.** Session never learns a delivery outcome, so a signer whose SMS code failed sees nothing and must guess to resend or switch method. | BL-406 | **The relay ordered each slice by the TEXT of the transaction id, so a slice straddling a power of ten repeated facts or lost them for good.** Diagnosed and fixed 2026-10-26 on `fix/relay-orders-by-transaction-id`. Mechanism: [`EventRelay::relayBatch()`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/EventLog/EventRelay.php) and `drainPreviousEpoch()` select `sys_transaction_id::text AS sys_transaction_id` and ordered by the bare name, which PostgreSQL resolves to that text output column (`10000` before `9999`), while the cursor comparison is numeric `xid8`, so the cursor landed below rows already delivered (duplicates) or, under `LIMIT`, above rows never read (lost). Both queries now order by the qualified column `event_log.sys_transaction_id`; `EventRelayTransactionIdOrderTest` forges ids either side of a power of ten and pins each query at batch 1 and at the default batch. ⚑ **The history, kept because it was misread twice.** First seen 2026-10-13 as `EventRelayEpochResetTest::every_fact_written_across_the_boundary_is_delivered_exactly_once_at_volume` failing once under coverage-instrumented Infection ("a post-restore fact was swallowed at volume") in the initial suite of the second filtered Infection run on `feat/delivery-facts`, passing alone; it was recorded as timing or a load-exposed swallow, not told apart. On 2026-10-26 the same case failed in TASK-070's full suite with each of 36 subjects delivered three times. Neither was timing nor the branch under test: the case fails whenever the cluster's transaction counter, set by how many tests ran before it, straddles 10,000 during its drain. The text alias and the bare `ORDER BY` were in the relay since `94527121` (2026-07-01) and were copied into the epoch drain by `19bf6c74` (2026-08-27); in production it fires each time the cluster counter crosses 10^5..10^9 with a backlog in flight. Unrelated to the cluster-wide `pg_snapshot_xmin` stall of [BL-138](BACKLOG.md). | `fix/relay-orders-by-transaction-id`; first recorded in [TASK-056](tasks/TASK-056-delivery-facts-and-sender-visibility.md) §8 | Closed | 2026-10-26 (pending merge) |

The verdict rule of [ADR-0078](adr/ADR-0078-delivery-outcomes-come-back-as-facts.md) §2.1 (one verdict per occasion, a destination list) leans on the same assumption: it holds while no two deliveries of one occasion share a destination kind, true while EMAIL, SMS and LOG are the only channels. ▶ **What it needs**: key the trace by destination kind and channel, and re-cut the verdict rule with it, in whichever task first puts a second channel on one destination kind. | [TASK-056](tasks/TASK-056-delivery-facts-and-sender-visibility.md) §7 | Open — the task that adds the second channel on a kind ([TASK-031](tasks/TASK-031-channel-selection-beyond-email.md) or later) re-cuts it first | 2026-10-13 (task-close of TASK-056) |

Whether the evidence trail needs the older one wants a real case. ▶ **What it needs**: a concrete out-of-order sequence from a provider that an audit would have to show; absent that, leave it. | [TASK-056](tasks/TASK-056-delivery-facts-and-sender-visibility.md) §7 | Open — optional | 2026-10-13 (task-close of TASK-056) |

Until then, read a task's Infection result on its changed lines, as TASK-056 §8 does. | [TASK-056](tasks/TASK-056-delivery-facts-and-sender-visibility.md) §8 | Open | 2026-10-13 (after the merge of TASK-056) |

| BL-417 | **The deployment's SMS wiring is declared `true` in every environment, whatever SMS transport is configured.** `envelope.sms_notifications.wired` is a literal parameter in `config/services.yaml` (`true`), read by `ConfiguredSmsDeployment`; it is not derived from `SMS_DSN` and not set per environment, so `SMS_NOT_AVAILABLE_ON_DEPLOYMENT` can never be answered. | BL-424 | **The history has no filter by purpose or channel and no tenant-wide view.** ▶ Forced when the dashboard asks; the route is per recipient of one envelope. | [TASK-058](tasks/TASK-058-recipient-notification-history.md) §7 | Open | 2026-10-15 |

| BL-426 | **The sender is not pushed the delivery facts the history shows; it must poll.** ▶ Forced when [ADR-0064](adr/ADR-0064-outbound-integrator-webhooks.md)'s webhooks are built (unbuilt). | [TASK-058](tasks/TASK-058-recipient-notification-history.md) §7 | Open | 2026-10-15 |
| BL-427 | ⚠ **The wide OTP destination counter is keyed on the destination alone, so one tenant's envelopes to a phone can exhaust it for another tenant's recipient.** `OtpSendKeys::wideDestination` is `sha256` of the normalized destination with no tenant or envelope in it (the owner's choice, D-e of [TASK-057](tasks/TASK-057-sms-spend-guards.md), so that one phone cannot be made to receive codes without limit across envelopes), and `otp_{email,sms}_wide_destination` allows 30 an hour. A cross-tenant lockout: availability only, bounded at 30 an hour, and an operator can lift it (`app:session:clear-otp-limits`). Found by the security audit of TASK-057. ▶ Decide with the per-sender ceiling question in TASK-057 §7: a tenant-scoped wide counter, or accept it. | TASK-057 security audit, 2026-10-19 | Open — revisit with real SMS traffic (BL-169) | 2026-10-19 (task-close of TASK-057) |
| BL-428 | **The OTP limiter keys are unsalted `sha256` of low-entropy values.** A phone number or an IP address hashed without a key can be brute-forced from a read of the Redis pool that holds the counters, which recovers which destinations were sent a code. Pre-dates [TASK-057](tasks/TASK-057-sms-spend-guards.md), whose new keys follow the same shape (`OtpSendKeys`). ▶ A keyed HMAC with a secret held outside the pool would close it; a secret is a deployment variable (repo rule 4). | TASK-057 security audit, 2026-10-19 | Open — unscheduled | 2026-10-19 (task-close of TASK-057) |
| BL-429 | **An unnamed resend is classified SMS-or-not from the declaration at resend time, but delivery resolves the declaration later, so adding `SMS` in between lets one send go out by SMS uncounted.** `Recipient::mayGoOutBySms` is read when the resend is requested; `NotifyResentInvitationRecipientUseCase` resolves it afterwards. Bounded by the 60 s minimum interval on the channels that resolved when it was requested (in this window, email only: SMS was not declared, so the resend stamps no SMS interval — and the SMS that goes out late leaves the SMS stamp unset, so a named `SMS` resend right after it is not held by the interval either, only by the cap) and needs an authenticated machine caller; the same window the cap had before [TASK-060](tasks/TASK-060-a-resend-may-name-one-declared-channel.md). ▶ Closing it means carrying the classification on the event, or counting at delivery. | [TASK-060](tasks/TASK-060-a-resend-may-name-one-declared-channel.md) security audit, 2026-10-19 | Open — unscheduled | 2026-10-19 (task-close of TASK-060) |
| BL-430 | **No test sends an ending notice after a named resend.** [TASK-060](tasks/TASK-060-a-resend-may-name-one-declared-channel.md) AC-2 says the next ending notice follows the declaration; that holds because the resend leaves `notification_channels` and `notification_mode` untouched, which a test asserts, but nothing drives a notice afterwards to watch it. ▶ An acceptance or cross-BC case: named resend, then complete the envelope, then read the notice's channels. | [TASK-060](tasks/TASK-060-a-resend-may-name-one-declared-channel.md) closing review, 2026-10-19 | Open — unscheduled | 2026-10-19 (task-close of TASK-060) |
| BL-431 | **A named-SMS resend that Notification later records as unsent still counted toward the SMS cap.** The count is taken at the write ([ADR-0080](adr/ADR-0080-a-resend-may-name-one-declared-channel.md) §7.5), before Notification checks the destination again, so a resend that spent nothing can spend one of the five. ▶ Refunding would need Notification to reach back into Envelope; the cap is a spend ceiling and over-counting errs safe, so it ships as a note. | [TASK-060](tasks/TASK-060-a-resend-may-name-one-declared-channel.md) closing notes, 2026-10-19 | Open — unscheduled | 2026-10-19 (task-close of TASK-060) |
| BL-432 | **If the declaration becomes `[]` between a resend and the notification's creation, a named resend records `NOT_SENT_NO_REACHABLE_CHANNEL` where an unnamed one records `DELIVERED_BY_INTEGRATOR`.** AC-11 specifies the named behaviour; the difference is deliberate, and an integrator reading the history sees two outcomes for the same declaration. ▶ Decide whether the named path should read the `[]` reach first, as the aggregate's guard does. | [TASK-060](tasks/TASK-060-a-resend-may-name-one-declared-channel.md) closing notes, 2026-10-19 | Open — unscheduled | 2026-10-19 (task-close of TASK-060) |
| BL-433 | **The envelope reference in every email is only useful once support can search by it, and the two places that would use it are in other repositories.** [TASK-061](tasks/TASK-061-every-recipient-email-carries-the-envelope-reference.md) prints the full envelope id in every email's footer so a signer whose link fails can quote it. The dashboard's search by envelope id is decided but **on hold by its owner since 2026-10-20 (not started)**, so until it lands support receives a reference it cannot look up; the signer app's footer showing the same value is likewise built in its own repository. SMS carries no reference, by decision (segment cost). ▶ **What would force it**: the first support case where a quoted id has to be found by hand, or the dashboard owner lifting the hold. <!-- OFFREPO: the dashboard and the signer app are separate repositories; the hold is the dashboard owner's decision of 2026-10-20. --> | [TASK-061](tasks/TASK-061-every-recipient-email-carries-the-envelope-reference.md) §6 | Open (out-of-repo dependency; dashboard on hold) | 2026-10-20 |
| BL-434 | **The signing gate stays silently optional on add-recipient.** [TASK-063](tasks/TASK-063-the-access-gate-is-always-declared.md) made the access gate always declared but left the signing slot optional: a client that drops only the signing gate still authors a recipient who signs with no per-act challenge. Judged lower risk because no stranger enters through it. ▶ **What would force it**: a second incident on the signing side. The shape kept in reserve is a request-only `NONE` mapped at the edge, never a catalog member. | [TASK-063](tasks/TASK-063-the-access-gate-is-always-declared.md) §6 | Open | 2026-10-20 |
| BL-435 | **`AddRecipientRequest::validateAccessCodeHasAGate` reads only the scalar spellings.** A body that declares `ACCESS_CODE` in `access_methods` (the plural) and sends `access_code` is refused although the gate declares it. | BL-436 | **The access declaration is enforced only at the HTTP edge.** [ADR-0082](adr/ADR-0082-the-access-gate-is-always-declared.md) sections 2.5 and 7.6 keep the bus command `AddRecipient` accepting a null requirement (it writes the neutral pair), so a non-HTTP caller (an importer, a template) would author an open gate by omission with nothing to catch it; the security audit of [TASK-063](tasks/TASK-063-the-access-gate-is-always-declared.md) flagged it as a warning, with no live bypass today. ▶ **What would force it**: the first non-HTTP caller of `AddRecipient`. | [TASK-063](tasks/TASK-063-the-access-gate-is-always-declared.md) §6 | Open | 2026-10-20 |
| BL-437 | **A recipient's gates cannot be changed once they have opened their link.** [ADR-0083](adr/ADR-0083-a-recipients-gates-are-editable-until-they-enter.md) cuts editing at the recipient's first opening (`first_opened_at` set), so "the SMS never arrives" once the signer is at the gate has no repair other than a list such as `[OTP_SMS, OTP_EMAIL]`, which lets the signer move to email themselves. ▶ **What it would take** (found by the review of 2026-10-20, listed in the home): verification and `ensureLiveChallenge` refuse and retire a live challenge whose method the declaration no longer names, decided in the same request; an `ACCESS_CODE` signing challenge, which has no expiry, is retired too; the act's admission compares a declaration revision read under the envelope lock and refuses with its own 409; strengthening an open gate moves an `AUTH_PASSED` session back to pending or is refused ([`BL-230`](BACKLOG.md)'s shape); opening the gate of an `AUTH_PENDING` session is refused; any access change retires credentials ([ADR-0060](adr/ADR-0060-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) §2.5); the send budgets and the issuance interval are never cleared; a signer handoff for the new refusal and for credential retirement. | [TASK-064](tasks/TASK-064-the-gates-are-editable-until-the-recipient-enters.md) §6 | Open | 2026-10-21 |
| BL-438 | **An access-code reset can mint a code hash for a recipient whose declaration has no `ACCESS_CODE` gate.** `POST …/recipients/{recipientId}/access-code` never checks the recipient's declaration, so the hash it stores is one nothing verifies; found in the review of the gate-change slice of [TASK-064](tasks/TASK-064-the-gates-are-editable-until-the-recipient-enters.md) (pre-existing, not introduced by it). | BL-439 | **No response that carries a plaintext access code sends `Cache-Control: no-store`.** Three routes return a one-time generated `access_code` in the body and set no cache directive: add-recipient, the access-code reset, and `PUT …/recipients/{recipientId}/auth` ([TASK-064](tasks/TASK-064-the-gates-are-editable-until-the-recipient-enters.md)). The security audit of TASK-064 raised it as a warning; it was not fixed on the new route alone, because that would leave the two siblings diverging (authoring rule 3). ▶ **What it needs**: one change that sets `no-store` on all three, with a test per route. | [TASK-064](tasks/TASK-064-the-gates-are-editable-until-the-recipient-enters.md) §7 | Open | 2026-10-21 |
| BL-441 | **Nobody is told when a recipient's gates are changed.** The trail states each change, but neither the sender nor the recipient is notified, above all when `access_opened` is set (the access gate went from declared to open). | BL-444 | **The class docblock of `Version20261021095351` said a delegated or unclaimed recipient was "never resent, NULL", which was false for one resent and only later delegated.** That recipient keeps a non-null stamp; it is never read because the delegated guard fires first. ✅ Closed on `feat/resend-interval-per-channel` before its merge: the docblock now says such a row keeps whatever it held and is never read. | [TASK-066](tasks/TASK-066-the-resend-interval-counts-per-channel.md) §6 | Closed | 2026-10-21 |
| BL-445 | **An invitation in flight can escape the retirement a channel drop publishes.** Narrow async race, found by the security audit of [TASK-065](tasks/TASK-065-a-correction-reaches-only-the-contact-it-changed.md) as a warning: an invitation handler that read the old declaration, mints its token after the channel-change anchor and saves its notification after the cancel reaction has run is not retired, so a link to the dropped channel's destination survives. | BL-447 | **`RecipientCorrectionHttpTest` drains the relay with a fixed 20 pumps.** Pre-existing ([TASK-065](tasks/TASK-065-a-correction-reaches-only-the-contact-it-changed.md) inherited it from TASK-025/031): `RELAY_DRAIN_PUMPS = 20` means a stalled relay reads green on the file's negative assertions. `ContactAdditionHttpTest` and `CorrectionReachesTheChangedContactHttpTest` now drain by watermark; replace the fixed count with the same drain. ▶ Forced by: the next edit to that file. | [TASK-065](tasks/TASK-065-a-correction-reaches-only-the-contact-it-changed.md) §6, slice 04 review | Open | 2026-10-21 |

Fixed for this task with `RecipientTest` cases. Open: census the other `Recipient` (and other entity) guards asserted only from an `Envelope*Test`, and either add `#[CoversClass]` for the entity there or an entity-tier case. | [TASK-065](tasks/TASK-065-a-correction-reaches-only-the-contact-it-changed.md) §7 | Open | 2026-10-21 |

A service on the DSS libraries and PDFBox would do fill, lock and sign in one call, pick its DSS version and own the keystore. It is a new codebase and would supersede ADR-0012 and ADR-0023; deferred for the end-of-October deadline. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §6 | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 design) |
| BL-450 | **DSS 6.4 → 6.5.1.** Brings DSS-3946 (a signature field's appearance could change without invalidating the signature), a DPI change for visible signatures (DSS-3789/3799) and an image placement fix (DSS-3779) that need the mark re-measured against `PageBox`, and Spring Boot 4 in the demo, which needs Tomcat 11 in `f5sign-infra`'s image. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §6 | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 design) |
| BL-451 | **Nothing of ours checks a signature's lock.** Adobe enforces the lock and DSS does not. For each signature, compare the values of its locked fields at its `/ByteRange` with the final document: it is the test DSS cannot give and the tool for a dispute. Also the one property of [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) not repeated on the shipped files (a locked value changed after its signature shows that signature invalid; observed in the spike only). | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §4 #7, §6 | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 design) |
| BL-452 | **`RADIO` is not drawn.** One box holds all its choices, and drawing a real radio group needs a widget per choice. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §6 | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 design) |
| BL-453 | **A PDF this system cannot prepare is accepted at upload and signed without its values.** A document that already carries a signature this system did not apply, an XFA form, or one the engine cannot read is signed without drawing (an `error` log line names the envelope, the document and the reason) and its recorded values never reach it; a document signed without them can never receive them later. Refusing it at upload needs a PDF reader in Envelope. Related: [BL-366](BACKLOG.md)'s page-geometry reader (1) is the same reader. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §4 #2, §6 · [ADR-0085](adr/ADR-0085-recorded-values-are-form-fields-each-signature-locks.md) §2.6 | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 design, owner decision on unreadable PDFs) |
| BL-454 | **The `#[\SensitiveParameter]` coverage of the form engine is a hand-listed set.** `FormEngineSensitiveParametersTest::parameters()` enumerates the parameters that carry a value or a PDF; it cannot see a new one (for example a future string or array parameter in `FormRevisionPlanner`). Prefer a census of the engine's classes with fail-on-unclassified, per authoring rule 5. Same class as [BL-278](BACKLOG.md). | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §6 (security audit) | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 security audit) |
| BL-455 | **The engine child has no CPU, process-count or open-file limit.** It sets `RLIMIT_AS` (768 MB) and PHP applies a 20 s timeout, so N concurrent acts can commit N times 768 MB (a sizing question for infra), and an unhandled `memory_limit` fatal remains possible in PHP. Add `RLIMIT_CPU`, `RLIMIT_NPROC` and `RLIMIT_NOFILE`, and state the maximum PDF size. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §6 (security audit); TASK-074 removed the base64/JSON copies | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 security audit) |
| BL-456 | **A hostile or very large PDF makes its document unsignable and the envelope stuck, and the act fails on every retry.** `page_tree` in `form_engine.py` keeps its seen-set per path, so a shared `/Kids` DAG expands exponentially until memory or the timeout stops it; `MEMORY_LIMIT`, `TIMED_OUT` and `KILLED` are rethrown and replayed, never degraded to signing without drawing. The attacker is the tenant's own uploader, but it also blocks the company seal. Use a global visited set, and decide in [ADR-0085](adr/ADR-0085-recorded-values-are-form-fields-each-signature-locks.md) §2.6 whether a deterministic `MEMORY_LIMIT` on `inspect` degrades like `PDF_UNREADABLE`. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §6 (security audit) | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 security audit) |
| BL-457 | **The form engine's child process runs with no sandbox.** It has the php-fpm user's privileges and network. The script only reads and writes (pyHanko makes no OCSP or CRL fetch), so this is residual, not a defect; a seccomp or no-network profile would need infra support. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §6 (security audit) | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 security audit) |
| BL-458 | **The Dockerfile's pyHanko stage is not pinned end to end.** `FROM php:8.5-fpm-alpine` is a tag, not a digest; `apk add python3` is unpinned in three stages, and a drift of the Python minor between stages breaks the venv (`lib/python3.x`) at runtime, not at build; the venv's own `pip` and `setuptools` are unpinned and `pip` stays in the runtime image. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §6 (security audit) | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 security audit) |
| BL-459 | **The sixteen Python wheels pyHanko pulls are hash-pinned and never CVE-scanned.** `composer audit` does not cover pip and `pip-audit` was not run; the hashes prove integrity, not safety. `oscrypto` is unmaintained, and `requests`, `urllib3`, `certifi`, `lxml` and `pyyaml` are transitive packages a drawer that needs only `pyhanko.pdf_utils` does not use. arm64 hashes were not checked. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §6 (security audit) | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 security audit) |
| BL-460 | **A recipient's reservations are written in the clear at the admission, earlier than before.** `Recipient::recordAdmittedReservations` records the text when the commit is admitted instead of at completion, so a commit that never completes now keeps it; it is drawn into the stored PDF too, which is the feature. No new column, so [BL-171](BACKLOG.md) and [ADR-0033](adr/ADR-0033-pii-field-encryption-at-rest.md) (`Proposed`, `FieldCipher` has no callers) are the home; this row names the earlier-write window so it is not lost when they land. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §3 #6, §6 · [BL-171](BACKLOG.md) | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 security audit) |
| BL-461 | **Edge cases of the form engine left at low confidence by the closing review.** (a) A page whose CropBox does not intersect its MediaBox, or a `/Rotate` that is not a multiple of 90, makes `PageBox::of` throw, mapped to `MALFORMED_OUTPUT`, which blocks the act on every retry where DSS used to sign it. (b) A redelivery over the act's own tip runs the engine again and appends a new unlocked signature field before the append converges. (c) `DrawnRevisionCheck` does not refuse extra fields or signature fields in the engine's summary. (d) `FormEngineFailedException::LOCK`'s docblock is narrower than the check, and `FormEngineFailure::REQUEST_INVALID`'s is narrower than its uses. (e) `AC-16a/b` have no end-to-end case through the real script. | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) §7 | Open | 2026-10-21 (TASK-067 closing review) |
| BL-463 | **A redelivered `SESSION_COMPLETED` appends a second `RECIPIENT_SIGNED` for an approver.** `SignForRecipientUseCase` ends a repeat early only when the recipient's signature image is cleared (only if they committed one) or when an artifact of their execution is already on a chain (only if they sign a document; a signer always has one, or the use case throws `signerAssignedNoDocument`). An `APPROVER` signs no document, so it has no artifact on a chain, and it commits an image only when its signing mode and a visible mark call for one (`CommitSignatureUseCase` decides by mode, not by role); an approver who committed none has nothing cleared, so a second delivery of their `SESSION_COMPLETED` reaches `eventLog->append(new RecipientSigned(...))` again, with the same execution id (`SignatureExecutionId::fromSession`). Envelope's completion is idempotent, and the receipt's settled read takes the first row of a type and subject ([ADR-0086](adr/ADR-0086-a-recipient-who-acts-gets-a-sealed-receipt.md) §3.3), so nothing reads the duplicate wrong today; the log still states one act twice. To decide: a repeat guard keyed on the fact itself (the stream already holds a `RECIPIENT_SIGNED` for this execution). Sibling of [BL-311](BACKLOG.md) (a redelivery is recognised only after the work is redone), whose remedy may close both. **Narrowed 2026-11-05:** the use case now returns when the leg of a recipient with no assigned document is `COMPLETED`, so a repeat after the completion writes nothing. A repeat before the completion still writes a second fact, because nothing in the state differs yet. That window is what stays open. | [TASK-047](tasks/TASK-047-per-signer-evidence-document.md) AC-9d | Open | 2026-10-23 (TASK-047 claims check) |
| BL-464 | **The census of the session reader's refusals reads a ternary throw by either branch.** `CommittedRecipientMeetsOnlySealPendingTest` (TASK-068 AC-11) reads every `throw` in `SigningSessionReader` and fails on one whose expression matches none of the two spellings it knows; a throw whose expression is a ternary passes if EITHER branch uses a known spelling, so a second, unknown refusal on the other branch would go unclassified. No ternary throw exists in the reader today, so nothing is wrong yet; the guard is a property the census cannot reach, and the first one written would be the first to slip through. To decide: make the census read each branch of a conditional, or forbid a ternary throw in the reader by a PHPStan rule. | [TASK-068](tasks/TASK-068-after-the-commit-the-signer-is-told-where-their-copy-stands.md) §7 | Open | 2026-10-23 (TASK-068 slice 09 review) |
| BL-468 | **The session receipt route repeats the credential-retirement comparison inline, and its retired and closed 404s are pinned below HTTP.** `RecipientReceiptRule::forSession()` (which `GetSessionReceiptHandler` delegates to since TASK-075) decides whether the session credential is retired with a comparison written out in the rule, because the type that owns it (`RecipientAuthContext::retires()`, Session/Application) is a layer EvidenceAudit's Application may not depend on (deptrac); the comment names the source and `RecipientReceiptRuleTest` pins the boundary, so a change to the rule in Session would leave the receipt route on the old one with every gate green. Separately, the retired-credential and closed-envelope 404s of that route are tested in `GetSessionReceiptHandlerTest` and `RecipientReceiptRuleTest` only, not over HTTP (`SessionReceiptHttpTest`), and the machine route's cross-tenant 404 runs on one connection, so it cannot tell RLS from the derived id. To decide: lift the retirement rule into a Session `Contract/` type the handler may read, and add the two HTTP cases. | [TASK-047](tasks/TASK-047-per-signer-evidence-document.md) §6 #3 | Open | 2026-10-26 (TASK-047 security audit) |
| BL-469 | **The receipt's `DECLARED_NONE` reservation sentence uses "destinatario" for two people, and a sealed receipt cannot be reworded.** `assets/evidence-audit/receipt.typ` says "El destinatario declaró no formular reservas. Si firmó como destinatario en la entrega, …": the first is the envelope's recipient, the second the goods' consignee (the trail's sibling sentence says "La parte" and has no clash). Every receipt issued with the sentence keeps it for as long as its envelope, so the wording is cheapest to change before the first production issuance. To decide: "La parte" as in the trail, or "como destinatario de la mercancía". | [TASK-047](tasks/TASK-047-per-signer-evidence-document.md) §6 #4 | Closed 2026-10-29: closed by TASK-077 ("La parte declaró no formular reservas."; the second sentence keeps "destinatario en la entrega" for the consignee) | 2026-10-26 (TASK-047 closing review) |
| BL-470 | **`RecipientNotificationHistoryHttpTest::the_body_carries_exactly_the_published_keys` fails by chance when a generated id contains `550`.** It asserts the raw body never contains `550` (the provider's SMTP reply code must not leak), but the body also carries `notification_id`, a UUID derived from per-run ids, so roughly one run in thirty fails with no leak at all (seen 2026-10-26 on `feat/per-recipient-receipt`, `8c0638a0-df18-550c-…`; green on re-run). To decide: assert on the decoded fields that could carry the provider's text, or a token that cannot occur in a UUID. | [TASK-047](tasks/TASK-047-per-signer-evidence-document.md) §7 | Open | 2026-10-26 (TASK-047 final full suite) |
| BL-472 | **Some JSON that carries a credential, and the session receipt, are still served without `Cache-Control: no-store`.** ⚑ Narrowed by [TASK-072](tasks/TASK-072-the-copy-card-reads-what-the-recipient-did.md) (2026-10-27): it added `no-store` to the copy routes (open, auth, list, documents, `documents.zip`, the copy receipt) and to the copy-link mint, with `CopyRoutesAreNotStoredHttpTest` enumerating them. **Still open:** the signing session's open, the session receipt route, `MintSigningTokenController` and the copy OTP send. Before TASK-072 the copy open and auth answered a `read_credential` (a 15-minute bearer JWT), `GET /api/v1/copy` answered what a recipient may read and `GET /api/v1/copy/receipt` served a private, ETag-tagged PDF, none with `no-store`; the signing session's open and receipt routes still do not. ▶ **What it needs:** the same decision for the routes listed as still open, then a header test that enumerates the signing side as the copy side's is. Found by the security audit of [TASK-070](tasks/TASK-070-a-recipient-reads-their-copy-through-a-link.md). | [TASK-070](tasks/TASK-070-a-recipient-reads-their-copy-through-a-link.md) §6 item 5 | Open | 2026-10-26 (task-close of TASK-070) |
| BL-473 | **A holder of a copy link can lock its real recipient out of the copy for up to 24 hours, and `open` and `auth` have no per-source limiter.** The copy gate never hard-locks, so its only bound on guesses is the growing wait ([ADR-0088](adr/ADR-0088-a-recipients-copy-is-read-through-its-own-link.md) §2.4), and that wait is charged to the recipient's record, not to the source that caused it: someone who holds the link and fails five codes starts a lock of 300 s that doubles each window that ends locked, up to a day. The code-send routes are limited per source; `POST /api/v1/copy/open` and `POST /api/v1/copy/auth` are not. Declared in [ADR-0088](adr/ADR-0088-a-recipients-copy-is-read-through-its-own-link.md) §3.3. ▶ **What it needs:** a per-source budget on `auth` that is spent before the record's, or a decision that the lock-out is the price of a gate with no sender escalation. | [TASK-070](tasks/TASK-070-a-recipient-reads-their-copy-through-a-link.md) §6 item 6 | Open | 2026-10-26 (task-close of TASK-070) |
| BL-474 | **A copy link cannot be revoked on its own.** A link leaked to someone else reads until the conservation ceiling, unless the recipient's contact is corrected (which moves `invitationsRevokedAt`), and a second link minted for the same recipient does not retire the first. A read credential is retired by an access-code reset; the link is not. Declared in [ADR-0088](adr/ADR-0088-a-recipients-copy-is-read-through-its-own-link.md) §3.3. ▶ **What it needs:** a revoke action on `session.copy_link` (or a mint that retires the earlier links) and a way for the sender or the integrator to call it. | [TASK-070](tasks/TASK-070-a-recipient-reads-their-copy-through-a-link.md) §6 item 7 | Open | 2026-10-26 (task-close of TASK-070) |
| BL-475 | **The two copy reads disagree on what a missing request attribute answers.** `DownloadCopyDocumentController` answers 400 when the listener left no recipient or gate instant (as `DownloadSigningDocumentController` does, and it publishes 400); `GetCopyController` answers the generic 401 (it publishes only 200 and 401). Each answers what it publishes, and neither is reachable: the listener always sets both attributes. ▶ **What it needs:** one answer for the copy reads, and the one published set that follows. | [TASK-070](tasks/TASK-070-a-recipient-reads-their-copy-through-a-link.md) §7, contract-check W-2 | Open | 2026-10-26 (task-close of TASK-070) |
| BL-476 | **Production must inject `COPY_READ_CREDENTIAL_SECRET`, and the repo's authoring store still omits it from the absent-secret list.** The variable is absent from `.env` (pattern A) and has a dev value in `.env.dev` and `.env.test`, so a container without it fails to build: the deploy fails closed, not silently. Two coordinated changes live outside this branch: the infra repository's production environment template and compose file (PR `feat/copy-read-credential-secret`), and the pattern (A) list in the workspace root's AI store for this repository's rule 4, edited in the root repository at merge. | [TASK-070](tasks/TASK-070-a-recipient-reads-their-copy-through-a-link.md) §6 item 4 | Open | 2026-10-26 (task-close of TASK-070) |
| BL-477 | **The end of an envelope reads the whole envelope context once per recipient, about N² rows.** The seal handler reads it once and once more per viewer, the close once and once more per spared recipient, because each part-notice decision reads the envelope after taking its recipient's row lock (the repair of the race, ADR-0089 §2.3). Correct, and quadratic in the recipients of one envelope. A scoped read of one recipient and the documents they see would remove the quadratic part. ▶ **What would force it:** envelopes with tens of recipients, or the performance smoke the repo does not run yet. | [TASK-071](tasks/TASK-071-a-recipient-is-told-when-their-part-ends.md) §6 item 8 | Open | 2026-10-26 (task-close of TASK-071) |
| BL-478 | **Owner decision 2 (`RECIPIENT_PART_COMPLETE` only while the envelope is open) has no end-to-end pin.** Only `RecipientPartNoticesTest::a_close_that_landed_while_waiting_withholds_part_complete` and `PartNoticePolicyTest` hold it. On S-4's waybill an e2e is impossible because the agreement is secured once A signs and `POST /void` is refused. Reachable on an unsecured void (a step-1 signer sees only their document and signs, step 2 pending, the sender voids, the held `DocumentReadyToSeal` is released) or on S-3 with the signer's seal held across the decline. ▶ **What would force it:** a regression in that condition that the unit tier cannot see. | [TASK-071](tasks/TASK-071-a-recipient-is-told-when-their-part-ends.md) §6 item 7, §7 | Open | 2026-10-26 (task-close of TASK-071) |

| BL-492 | **A failure that the failure transport parks kept each trace string with its string arguments, so it could keep a short decrypted plaintext whole.** Messenger clears the trace array of `ErrorDetailsStamp`, and `FlattenException::$traceAsString` stays, with each string cut at `zend.exception_string_param_max_len`. Sentry received each whole argument: Sentry serializes each frame's arguments, and `SentryBeforeSend` does not remove them. A parameter marked `#[SensitiveParameter]` reaches neither. **The measure taken:** `zend.exception_ignore_args=On` in `docker/php.ini`, and `ProductionPhpIniTest` holds that line. So a trace in the production image holds no argument. The development stack and the lanes stay off. **What stays open:** an exception message can carry a plaintext, and the setting does not touch it. **The candidates not taken:** a serializer that drops traces, a static rule, a taint analysis. A `PendingText` holds its plaintext under `serialize()` and `print_r()`, as any private state does, and no message that a transport receives holds one. | [ADR-0096](adr/ADR-0096-key-management-encrypts-the-logs-free-text-under-erasable-keyrings.md) §2.11, §7.17 | Folded | 2026-10-20 · the setting landed 2026-11-05 on `fix/exception-ignore-args` |
| BL-494 | **The request path mints each keyring's first data key, so code that runs as its role can plant a key whose secret it holds.** It then reads each later value of that keyring from any copy of the log. `keyring_one_way` refuses a change of a current key that is set, so only a keyring with no current key is open to the plant. The same role reads every column of a data key, its wrapping included, and inserts data keys. So it can also keep a copy of any key's wrapping, in the store or outside it, and an erasure does not reach the copy. **The fix:** remove the authority, so that a process that holds a secret mints each data key. The landing of a data key's rotation decides the same authority. The fix also removes the copy in the store. The read of the wrapping stays, so a wrapping read before an erasure opens with the wrapping key pair until that pair is destroyed after a rotation. | [ADR-0096](adr/ADR-0096-key-management-encrypts-the-logs-free-text-under-erasable-keyrings.md) §2.11, §3.3 | Folded | 2026-10-30 |
| BL-495 | **Every backend process gets the holder of the wrapping key pair, so only an unmounted file keeps a process that encrypts from decrypting.** `DeploymentWrappingKeys::ofOneKey()` passes the pair's path to every process, and the holder of the public half alone is never built outside tests. A pair file that reaches `php-fpm` by mistake lets the request path decrypt. Every test process holds the pair, so no test can show a request path that decrypts. **The fix:** build the holder of the public half where the deployment names no pair. It changes `.env.dev`, the compose files of `f5sign-infra` and `WrappingKeyWiringTest`. | [ADR-0096](adr/ADR-0096-key-management-encrypts-the-logs-free-text-under-erasable-keyrings.md) §2.4, §2.11 | Folded | 2026-10-30 |
| BL-496 | **The storage footprint report scans all of `storage.custody` three times for each tenant.** So its daily cost is tenants times custodies. `storage.custody` has no index that leads with `tenant_id`, so `DbalTenantStorageFootprint::forTenantInScope()` applies the row-level policy after a sequential scan. Measured 2026-11-02 as `f5sign_app` in one tenant's scope, at 4,805 custodies: three sequential scans and 13 ms. It also explains most of the 26 to 35 s that each full test run adds until a test-database reset. 26 of the 32 tables with a `tenant_id` column have no such index, and this measurement covers only this query. **The fix:** an index on `storage.custody (tenant_id)`, after a check of how the code reads the other 25 tables. | fold pending | Open | 2026-11-02 |
| BL-497 | **The delegated commit keeps a blank reservation as typed, so GET envelope answers `has_reservations: true` for no text.** The signer's route turns a blank into `null` at the edge (`CommitSignatureController::normalizeReservations()`). `CommitDelegatedSignatureController` passes the blank on, and `envelope.recipient.reservations` stores it. `EnvelopeJsonPresenter::anyReservation()` reads that column with `!== null`. The fact holds `null`, because a blank is no text, so the trail and the receipt state no reservation (ADR-0096 §2.12 item 3). So one blank gives two answers. Develop stores it the same way. The effect on the signed PDF's `RESERVATIONS` box is not measured. **The fix:** turn the blank into `null` on the delegated route too, or in the aggregate. | fold pending | Open | 2026-11-03 |
| BL-498 | **`make qa` stops at its Infection stage after every test passes, because PHPUnit's child process reaches the 512 MB limit while it writes the coverage XML.** Measured 2026-11-03 on develop and on a branch of 10,633 tests: the fatal error is in `DOMDocument->saveXML()`, under `php-code-coverage/src/Util/Xml.php`. The `-d memory_limit=1536M` of `composer infection` does not reach a child process, and the lane mounts `docker/php/wt-infection.ini` for that reason. So `make wt-backend-infection` still runs, and `make qa` and `make infection` do not. **The candidates:** give the stack's Infection run the lane's ini file, or set `initialTestsPhpOptions` in `infection.json5`. Neither is tested. **Closed 2026-11-09:** `composer infection` passes `--initial-tests-php-options='-d memory_limit=1536M'`, which reaches the child process whatever the local `infection.json5` says. `composer qa` no longer runs an initial run at all: its test stage (`composer test:coverage`) writes the coverage, and `bin/infection-changed` mutates the lines changed against `origin/develop` with `--skip-initial-tests`. | `bin/infection-changed` (its header states the preconditions and exit codes) | Closed | 2026-11-03 |
| BL-499 | **`notification.notification.variables` holds the void reason, the envelope's title and the recipient's name in clear, and the log's encryption does not reach that column.** `NotifyEndedEnvelopeRecipientsUseCase` pins the three on each ending notice (`reason`, `envelope_title`, `recipient_name`), and other notices pin the title and the name. `BL-113`, `BL-160` and `BL-481` cover the secrets in those variables, and none of them names this text. So a dump of the table reads what a dump of the log does not. **The fix:** the column landing of ADR-0096 §2.10. | fold pending | Open | 2026-11-03 |
| BL-501 | **An emoji, or a CJK, Thai or Devanagari name, prints only as its code points on a sealed document.** The page is exact and the attachment keeps the text ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md), its amendment of 2026-11-04), but a reader cannot read a name in one of those scripts from the page. A font adds what it can draw: Noto Emoji is 2.0 MB and Noto Sans CJK SC is 16.4 MB for one weight. Measured 2026-11-04: no change in load time, and 1.3 to 3.4 KB more in a PDF. **The trap:** Typst draws a base and its marks from one font, so `a` with U+1F3FD stops the document when only a second font holds the modifier. The table must leave out such a mark. To decide from real usage. | fold pending | Open | 2026-11-04 |
| BL-503 | **`trail.typ` and `receipt.typ` hold 14 identical definitions, 82 lines in each, because a template imports nothing.** `DrawableTextTest` keeps them one text, so they cannot drift, but a fix must still go in both. **The fix:** a shared file with `#import`. Measured 2026-11-04 on Typst 0.15.1: with the file in the job directory, the trail and 3 receipts gave the same PDF bytes, clean and with hard text. **The trap:** `template_hash` is the SHA-256 of the template alone, so a change to the shared file changes the PDF and not the hash. The hash must cover both files, and that changes the recipe that verifies a sealed document. So this is cheapest before the first real document. Both renderers must also copy the file, because a file outside `--root` is refused. `stated`, `lbl` and `gate-lbl` read each template's `labels`, so they stay. | fold pending | Open | 2026-11-04 |
| BL-504 | **A trail or receipt states `dev` as its application version when an image build omits `SENTRY_RELEASE`, and nothing refuses that in prod.** A sealed document keeps it for good, and it is the value that lets a verifier find the template. `config/packages/evidence_audit.yaml` holds the fallback. `build-image.yml` sets the variable, so only an image built another way fails. | `evidence_audit.app_version` | Open | 2026-11-04 |
| BL-506 | **A time that enters through `datetime_immutable` loses its microseconds, so each rule must know how that time was written.** `UtcDateTimeImmutableType` writes `Y-m-d H:i:s`, while the columns are `timestamptz` and the facts keep microseconds. `BL-303`, `BL-351`, `BL-414` and `BL-479` each hold one effect. **The direction (the maintainer, 2026-11-04):** absorb it at ingestion, in one place, as the ids do. The read side must then accept each string form that a row or a fact holds, with a fraction and without one. **A census comes first, because some logic relies on whole seconds:** the credential-retirement comparisons by `getTimestamp()`, which `BL-351` says must stay, the `agp` claim, `step_deadline_at_chk`, the sweep funnels and `Obligation::dominates`. This list is not that census. **When:** after the deployment of 2026-11-07. A row written before the fix keeps whole seconds, and its fact keeps the real instant. **Done on 2026-11-05, by a text bind in `DbalNotificationRepository`:** `notification.requested_at`, and a delivery's `requested_at` and `settled_at`, because a verdict and a refusal read them. `CancelPendingInvitationsUseCase` still compares to the second. | `UtcDateTimeImmutableType` | Open | 2026-11-04 |
| BL-507 | **A refusal that crosses a correction stops the invitation to the new number.** The provider refuses the old number after the correction forgot that kind, so the refusal stamps `MOBILE_PHONE` as `FAILED` on the trace. `NotifyCorrectedRecipientUseCase` then resolves no channel and records `NOT_SENT_NO_REACHABLE_CHANNEL`. The correction stays spent, and the new number gets no message. Measured over HTTP with the real relay, and the behavior comes from before the log's new facts. **A fix:** the refusal's contact now reaches Envelope, so Envelope can compare it with the current contact and leave the trace alone. That changes what the trace records. **That fix also needs a key:** the trace's observation is the only thing that stops a repeat of a refusal, and a correction already forgets it. A redelivered `DeliveryFailed` after a correction writes a second `RECIPIENT_DESTINATION_REFUSED`, with the same contact and instant (measured at the aggregate). With the trace left alone, each repeat writes one more. | ADR-0078 §2.1 | Open | 2026-11-04 |
| BL-508 | **The dependency bump of 2026-11-05 did not take the runtime minor releases.** Runtime: `doctrine/dbal` 4.5.0, `async-aws/core`, `async-aws/s3`, `sentry/*`, `monolog/monolog`, `twig/twig` and `guzzlehttp/psr7`. `zircote/swagger-php` and `nelmio/api-doc-bundle` wait too, because they can change the published API document. The upgrade of the test dependencies on 2026-11-17 took the dev updates. It also left at their versions the runtime packages that its tools allow but do not require: `doctrine/dbal`, `doctrine/deprecations`, `webmozart/assert` 2, `symfony/polyfill-php85`, `phpdocumentor/reflection-common`, `phpdocumentor/type-resolver` and `phpstan/phpdoc-parser`. Run `composer outdated` for the versions of the day. **When:** after the deployment of 2026-11-07. | `composer.lock` at `65285573` | Open | 2026-11-05 |
| BL-509 | **An older occasion that ends after a newer occasion's verdict loses facts, because each repeat check reads an instant and no key exists.** It records no `FAILED` outcome, and no `RecipientDeliveryFailed` if none of its destinations moves. The recipient's state stays right, because the newer verdict stands. A refusal behind a newer observation, and a `SENT` that a retry reorders, have the same limit. The fix is a repeat check that reads a key. That check needs stored state from its own day on. **When:** after the deployment of 2026-11-07. | [ADR-0078](adr/ADR-0078-delivery-outcomes-come-back-as-facts.md) §3.3, [ADR-0097](adr/ADR-0097-the-log-is-the-documents-one-source.md) §2.7 | Open | 2026-11-05 |
| BL-510 | **A redelivery of a missed invitation can still send a message.** When nothing resolved, no occasion exists, so the occasion check does not stop a redelivery. If a channel has become reachable since then, that redelivery sends a message. The message is late and not false, and the earlier `NOT_SENT` outcome stands beside it. Seen by reading `NotifyActivatedStepRecipientsUseCase`. No run drove it. **When:** after the deployment of 2026-11-07. | `NotifyActivatedStepRecipientsUseCase` and its five siblings | Open | 2026-11-05 |
| BL-511 | **A packaging defect on a read path of the form engine reads as an unreadable PDF.** `load_pyhanko()` enters `pyhanko_certvalidator` without its `__init__.py`, so a pyHanko that imports a name from that package raises `ImportError`. `open_reader()`, `inspect()` and the read part of `apply()` catch it as `PDF_UNREADABLE` (exit 3), and `DocumentSigner` reads that as a property of the document: the document is signed without its drawn values, and the record blames the PDF. Measured 2026-11-12 in the dev image: the stub raises `ImportError` for `from pyhanko_certvalidator import ValidationContext`. By reading, no path of the pinned versions reaches it. At a version bump, the engine tests catch it only on the paths that a fixture reaches, and `PyHankoFormImportsTest` runs no read path. **The fix:** re-raise `ImportError` on the three read paths, so that a packaging defect ends at `FAILED` (exit 1) with its cause, with a test that pins it. | `load_pyhanko()` in [`form_engine.py`](../assets/signature-execution/form_engine.py) | Open | 2026-11-12 |
| BL-512 | **`EnvelopeRef` and `RecipientRef` accept any string.** So each route that reads a path id into them checks its format itself, as `CommitDelegatedSignatureController` does since `BL-505` closed. A check in the two types is the long-term fix. It reaches 126 call sites in `src/` (counted 2026-11-09), and the error code changes unless the label is set. | [`EnvelopeRef`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Contract/Type/EnvelopeRef.php) and [`RecipientRef`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Contract/Type/RecipientRef.php) | Open | 2026-11-12 |
| BL-520 | **The asserted signer address on the delegated commit has no format rule.** `CommitDelegatedSignatureRequest::$asserted_signer_ip` carries `Assert\Length(max: 45)` only, and the value is recorded as act evidence beside the address that the platform saw. Any string of up to 45 characters is accepted as an address. A rule such as `Assert\Ip(version: Ip::ALL)` is the fix. It changes the published contract for an integrator that sends another form, so it waits for a decision. `RecordedTextCensusTest` names this field as exempt from the NUL rule because of this row. | [`CommitDelegatedSignatureRequest`](../src/F5Sign/Session/UI/Http/Request/CommitDelegatedSignatureRequest.php) | Open | 2026-11-13 (the census of request text fields) |
| BL-525 | **Production does not yet limit the Host that the backend serves.** Caddy serves the API only under its own name, and the backend builds no absolute URL from the Host, so no exposure is known. A limit in the backend is the second guard. `config/packages/framework.yaml` now declares `trusted_hosts` from `SYMFONY_TRUSTED_HOSTS`, and `TrustedHostsHttpTest` shows that a set value refuses any other Host with a 400 before routing. The value is empty in every environment, so the guard is off. The deployment must set one anchored pattern for each name of the API site, separated by commas, and pass the variable to each service that serves HTTP. Each name that Caddy serves must have a pattern, or that name gets a 400. | [`config/packages/framework.yaml`](../config/packages/framework.yaml) | Open | 2026-11-13 |
| BL-529 | ⚠ **A session credential pair minted on an unclaimed slot before [TASK-078](tasks/TASK-078-the-machine-routes-refuse-an-unclaimed-slot.md) deploys is not withdrawn, and can still renew, commit or decline for it.** Before that branch a machine `POST …/auth` with a correct `ACCESS_CODE` on a slot returned an access and a refresh credential. [`RenewSessionCredentialUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/RenewSessionCredentialUseCase.php), [`CommitSignatureUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/CommitSignatureUseCase.php) and [`DeclineSignatureUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/DeclineSignatureUseCase.php) do not ask the slot question: they rely on no credential existing for a slot ([ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.5's upstream model), and TASK-078 closes the doors that mint one. So a pair minted before the deploy lasts at most until the session's twelve-hour cap and its last access credential's fifteen minutes, and in that window it can sign or decline for a party who does not exist. The census test classifies the machine routes of `signing.php` only, so it cannot see this. ⚠ **TASK-078 §7's production query is only an upper bound for it**: it counts rows with `first_opened_at` set on a slot, and an opening is not a minted credential (a send stamps the opening and mints no pair; only a correct submit does), so a count of 0 rules the case out and a count above 0 does not say a pair was minted. ▶ **What would force a guard** (a slot check in the three use cases, tested like the machine routes'): a deployment where a machine ceremony ran on a slot in the twelve hours before this branch deployed, or the query returning rows whose deploy date falls inside that window. Otherwise the window closes by itself. | [TASK-078](tasks/TASK-078-the-machine-routes-refuse-an-unclaimed-slot.md) §6 | Open | 2026-11-04 |

| BL-384 | **`develop`'s `composer.lock` cannot be re-resolved: `composer update --lock` fails on `symfony/psr-http-message-bridge` 8.1 against `extra.symfony.require` 7.4.\*.** TASK-053 had to change `composer.json` (`ext-gd`), so the lock's `content-hash` moved; the hash was computed by Composer's own rule (it reproduces the previous hash on the unchanged file) and written by hand, because the update could not run. The lock is therefore valid only as long as nobody asks Composer to resolve it. ▶ **What it needs**: decide whether the bridge pin or the `extra.symfony.require` constraint gives way, and regenerate the lock deliberately (repo rule 1 forbids doing it without an explicit request). | [TASK-053](tasks/TASK-053-the-recipients-drawn-signature-on-their-mark.md) §7 | Closed | 2026-11-05: `65285573` moved `symfony/psr-http-message-bridge` to v7.4.21. On that lock, `composer update --lock` with plugins exits 0 with "Nothing to modify in lock file", and the file does not change |
| BL-440 | **`composer audit --locked` reports two advisories, both pre-existing**: `symfony/http-foundation` CVE-2026-108082 (ReDoS in `HeaderUtils::split()`) and `symfony/yaml` CVE-2026-109413 (unbounded recursion in the inline lexer). The lock is untouched by [TASK-064](tasks/TASK-064-the-gates-are-editable-until-the-recipient-enters.md) and the same two are present on `develop`. ⛔ Updating `composer.lock` needs the owner's explicit request (repo rule 1), so this row waits for it. | [TASK-064](tasks/TASK-064-the-gates-are-editable-until-the-recipient-enters.md) §7 | Closed | 2026-11-05: `65285573` moved the lock. `composer audit` and `composer audit --locked` each exit 0 with "No security vulnerability advisories found." |
| BL-485 | **`composer update` with plugins cannot resolve on `develop`.** Flex restricts the `symfony/symfony` members to `7.4.*` and refuses the locked `symfony/psr-http-message-bridge` 8.1.0 (and `sentry/sentry-symfony` 5.12.0 through it), so any plugin-enabled `composer update` fails. TASK-072 refreshed the lock with `--no-plugins` (only `content-hash` and the platform list changed). Pre-existing. ▶ **What it needs:** pin or lift the bridge and sentry constraints so a plugin-enabled update resolves, then check a plugin-enabled `composer update --lock` leaves the lock unchanged. | [TASK-072](tasks/TASK-072-the-copy-card-reads-what-the-recipient-did.md) §7 deviation 01 | Closed | 2026-11-05: `65285573` moved `symfony/psr-http-message-bridge` to v7.4.21. On that lock, `composer update --lock` with plugins exits 0 with "Nothing to modify in lock file", and the file does not change |
| BL-500 | **A text with a character that DejaVu lacks stops the trail and the receipt for good.** Typst runs with `--pdf-standard a-3a` and the DejaVu fonts only, and PDF/A-3a refuses a character that the font cannot draw. Measured 2026-11-03 on `receipt.typ` with an emoji (U+1F44D), CJK and Thai, and on a minimal document with Hangul. `RendererFailed` has the outlook `SAME_RESULT`, so no retry helps. Any free text that a document states can reach it, and a signer can type it. Develop has this since 2026-09-22. **The fix:** its own PR, before any deployment with real data. The candidates: a wider font set, or a template that prints a character it cannot draw by its code point. | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md), its amendment of 2026-11-04 | Closed | 2026-11-04: each template prints a character that it cannot draw as its code point |
| BL-502 | **Not measured: what a signer's visible mark draws for a character that PT Serif lacks.** DSS draws the mark on the signed PDF in PT Serif Regular, which is a different path from the trail and the receipt, so the rule of [`BL-500`](#closed) does not reach it. The docblock of `MarkFont` says only that DSS draws "some glyph" of at most the widest advance. So a name with an emoji or a CJK character can print a wrong glyph on a signed page, and a signed page cannot be corrected. **The probe:** seal a document through the real DSS with such a name on a visible mark, then read the page and the PDF's text. Run it with a name that the font holds as the control. | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md), its amendment of 2026-11-09 | Closed | 2026-11-09: measured 2026-11-06, DSS draws no other glyph. It refuses the whole seal with a 500 ("No glyph for U+202E in font PTSerif-Regular") for U+202E, C0 and C1 controls and CJK, and the envelope stays agreed and unsealed. The mark now prints each character that PT Serif lacks as its code point, such as `[U+202E]` |
| BL-471 | **`ChannelChangeReinvitesCrossingTest::a_sent_recipient_is_not_reinvited_and_a_failed_one_is` failed in some full runs and passed alone: the failed recipient's invitation deliveries read `SMS`, `EMAIL` in place of `EMAIL`, `SMS`.** Sightings from 2026-10-26, on branches that touch neither Notification nor Envelope. **Cause, measured 2026-11-02:** the test changed the channels at the instant that the seed sends the envelope, so both invitations held one `requested_at` and `position` 0, and the read's `ORDER BY n.requested_at, d.position` set no order between them. The stored rows then followed their physical order, and the later invitation read first when its insert reused an earlier free slot of the page. Of 12 stored runs, the 2 whose `SMS` row sits physically first are the 2 full runs that failed that day, and the same rows change order under another join plan. Not a product defect: `DbalNotificationHistory` orders by `requested_at` and `id`, and a real change comes after its send. | test determinism (no ADR home) | Closed | 2026-11-02: the test now changes the channels after the send |
| BL-493 | **`RecordRecipientDecline` carries a decline reason that its use case never reads.** The reason reaches the envelope at the decline ([ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.1), and the command repeats it from Session's fact, encrypted since [ADR-0096](adr/ADR-0096-key-management-encrypts-the-logs-free-text-under-erasable-keyrings.md). **The fix:** remove the member, with its place in the command's payload and its tests. | [ADR-0096](adr/ADR-0096-key-management-encrypts-the-logs-free-text-under-erasable-keyrings.md) §2.11 | Closed | 2026-11-02 (the field went when the log's encrypt moved to the append, ADR-0096 §2.1) |
| BL-280 | **A recipient who acts receives nothing: no proof of their act and no copy of what they signed.** The maintainer wants each signer, when their own act ends, to receive their evidence document together with the signed document. **Split 2026-10-22 (owner):** the evidence document is now a sealed per-recipient **receipt**, issued for a signer, an approver or a decliner once their act is recorded (under `SEQUENTIAL_PADES`, once their signature is in the PDF) and downloadable by the integrator and inside the recipient's signing session: that half is [TASK-047](tasks/TASK-047-per-signer-evidence-document.md). What stays open here is the **delivery**: an email or SMS that reaches the recipient with their receipt (and, for a signer, their copy). An SMS cannot carry an attachment and the email channel carries none today, so it is a link behind a minted credential, as `SEALED_COPY_READY` does for an informational member (`NotifyCompletedEnvelopeRecipientsUseCase`); it is the same vehicle BL-188 needs for the signer's copy, and the only way a decliner, whose session is revoked when their decline closes the envelope, gets their receipt themselves. ▶ **What would force it:** TASK-047 landing, or BL-188 being designed. ⚑ **2026-10-26: both have happened, and the vehicle exists** since [TASK-070](tasks/TASK-070-a-recipient-reads-their-copy-through-a-link.md) ([ADR-0088](adr/ADR-0088-a-recipients-copy-is-read-through-its-own-link.md)): a copy link reads the recipient's documents and their receipt until the envelope's conservation ceiling, a decliner's receipt included through `GET /api/v1/copy/receipt`; today only an integrator mints one. The notices that mint and carry it are [TASK-071](tasks/TASK-071-a-recipient-is-told-when-their-part-ends.md), landed on `feat/recipient-notices`. | [TASK-047](tasks/TASK-047-per-signer-evidence-document.md) §6 #1 | Closed — delivered by TASK-071 (`feat/recipient-notices`, integrated in `develop` 2026-10-26; ADR-0089 accepted) | 2026-10-26 |
| BL-107 | ⛔ **23 `EVENT_TYPE` constants and no test asserts a single value — the one defect on this list whose damage cannot be repaired.** The log is permanent, append-only and upcast-only, so a wire name is frozen the instant a row carries it. `RegisterDomainEventsPass` fails the build on a **duplicate** or **missing** const, never on a **changed** one; the test that did pin the values (`DeclaredEventTypeSeedFidelityTest`) was deliberately retired at event-log stage 2; and [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md)'s replacement is recorded as *"Not enforced — golden-bytes replay conformance. **Queued, not built**."* Editing one string re-types recorded history and no rewrite can fix it (a copy-transform migration breaks `sys_commitment`). Self-maintaining fix: a checked-in frozen `event_type → FQCN` manifest plus a test asserting the live registry is a superset with **no changed value** — a new event adds one line. ✅ **Closed 2026-08-18 by TASK-026 slice 2**, and by the sibling it names rather than by the manifest it proposed. [`GoldenPayloadBytesTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Foundation/Unit/Serialization/GoldenPayloadBytesTest.php) freezes one fixture per `EVENT_TYPE` **named for that value**, so the 23 constants are pinned on disk, and its two-armed census reports a changed value from **both** sides: the new name as unfrozen, the old as orphaned. ⚑ Verified by sabotage rather than by reading — renaming `CONSENT_RECORDED` to `CONSENT_GIVEN` reddens three cases and the two census messages together identify it as a rename. That two-arm split exists *because* the sabotage found that a single assertion masked the orphan half and would have led a reader to just add the new fixture. The proposed `event_type → FQCN` manifest was **not** built and is not owed: freezing the type set catches the damage this row describes (re-typing recorded history), while a class rename that keeps the wire name is not a defect. ⚑ **Resolving commit `4e6335a`** (`32d9a11` froze the two events TASK-025 added after it). ⚠ *23* is the count on the day this closed; there are **25** today, and the census — not this row — is what keeps that number right. | [ADR-0031](adr/ADR-0031-event-log-store.md) *Enforced by* | Resolved | 2026-08-18 (discharged to Closed 2026-08-19) |
| BL-150 | **Tell `f5sign-signer` the cutover shipped so they can drop their old branch.** Done — they dropped it in `75072b5`. ⚑ **They verified the precondition instead of taking it**: rather than trusting "it is deployed", they measured a link minted that day out of Mailpit (`len=43 dots=0 prefix=f5s_st_`, matching the published pattern) before touching anything. ⚑ **And they pinned the shape by copying our published contract rather than deriving it from their own regex** — a test that builds its fixture from the expression it checks agrees with itself whatever we do. The retired format now has a rejection test. ⚠ **They deliberately did NOT narrow their runtime regex to `{36}`**, validating by family without fixing `kind` or length, because deciding which credentials exist is ours: pinning it there would turn a future `sr` or a differently-sized kind into a client-side dead end with no conversation. That is the right seam and worth not "fixing". | f5sign-signer | Resolved | 2026-08-18 |
| BL-149 | **`SIGNING_TOKEN_SECRET`'s six surfaces in `f5sign-infra`.** Done by that repo (`master` = `657abda`), after this one stopped reading the variable. Verified here rather than accepted: the four *uses* are gone from `docker-compose.prod.yml`, `.env.prod.example` and `README.md`; what remains are historical mentions — a tombstone comment, the deploy conditions, and the rotation runbook — which is what authoring rule 1 asks to keep. ⚑ **The two dangerous surfaces were the ones a grep for the name would have "fixed" by deleting**: the README's required-variables list headed *"if one is missing, step 4 refuses to render and names it"* (true only because of the `:?`, so leaving the name there would have documented a guard that no longer exists), and the rotation runbook, **rewritten rather than removed** — invalidating issued links is still an operation, it is now deleting or expiring rows, and it is **selective**, which rotating a shared secret never was. ⚠ **Verified by rendering the prod merge with the variable absent and again with it explicitly empty — not by a real `up`**, which needs `.env.prod` and registry credentials that no dev host has. That limit is stated in their commit and is the deploying operator's step. | f5sign-infra | Resolved | 2026-08-18 |
| BL-151 | **Assert that every `SECURITY DEFINER` function is owned by an unprivileged role.** Done — `SchemaConformanceTest::every_security_definer_function_is_owned_by_an_unprivileged_role()` enumerates them from `pg_proc` and refuses a superuser or `BYPASSRLS` owner. ⚑ **Enumerated, not listed**: a hardcoded set of today's three would exempt "everything not yet listed", which is the shape authoring rule 5 warns about — the fourth chokepoint inherits the assertion for free. It asserts the census is non-empty first, because a zero-length result would pass every assertion while measuring nothing. Sabotage-verified by dropping the `prosecdef` filter: reds naming `platform._event_log_field`, which is superuser-owned and correctly outside the census. ⚠ The narrower test that seeded it survives, narrowed to the half a census cannot ask — reddening when the function is **gone**, which is the answer to *"was the chokepoint quietly replaced by a table grant?"*. | TASK-028 / f5sign-infra review | Resolved | 2026-08-18 |
| BL-113 | **A live signing credential stored in plaintext, indefinitely, in `notification.notification.variables`.** Fixed on two axes, and only the pair closes it. **(1) Bounded**: [TASK-028](tasks/TASK-028-opaque-signing-token.md) made the credential expire — this row's premise was *"the token **never** expires (ADR-0049 §2.8 puts its life at the whole life of the envelope)"*, which stopped being true, so a stored link is inert after 7 days and its row is swept. **(2) Removed**: [`Notification::forgetSecretsOnceSettled()`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Domain/Aggregate/Notification.php) drops the `link` variable once **every** delivery is terminal, and the repository's upsert carries it away. ⛔ **The predicate is "every delivery terminal", deliberately NOT the `isDelivered()` fold used everywhere else in that aggregate**: under a `SINGLE` policy the occasion is delivered the moment one channel lands while a sibling may still be queued, and redacting there produces an email with an empty link days later — silent, late, and to the recipient least likely to be watched. Sabotage-verified in both directions. ⚠ **Only the credential goes**; `envelope_title` and `recipient_name` survive, because a support conversation months later needs to know what was sent to whom. ⚑ **The window is now the send itself** rather than forever: a compromise between creation and settlement still yields the links in flight. Narrowing that further means not composing the link until send, which fights ADR-0037's resolve-at-creation and was not worth it for the exposure left. | TASK-028 §6 bar 4 | Resolved | 2026-08-18 |
| BL-148 | **Close the signing-token cutover window: delete the legacy encoding branch and retire `SigningTokenCodec`.** Done — the edge now accepts one encoding, the codec and its `SigningTokenClaims` are deleted, and `SIGNING_TOKEN_SECRET` is gone from `.env.dev`, `.env.test`, `config/services.yaml` and the `.env` load-order header. ⚑ **The three surfaces that argued *from* that variable were rewritten rather than deleted**, since each used it as an example and would have lost its subject: `CLAUDE.md` repo rule 4 (both the pattern-(A) list and the "not transferable" counterexample now point at `SESSION_CREDENTIAL_SECRET`, which has the same working-dev-value property), `ConfigKeyProvider` (same repoint, plus the note that its 32-byte floor was a trio and is now a pair), and `Authn::SESSION_CREDENTIAL` (the two-separate-secrets rule survives its example — the signing token no longer has a secret at all, so the confusion it guarded against is impossible by construction). ⚠ **A third minting path was found while doing this and was NOT in the task**: `NotifyCorrectedRecipientUseCase` still minted the old encoding, so the claim "every credential this system issues is opaque" was false between the cutover and this row. Converted here. ⛔ **TWO deploy conditions outlive this row, and neither checks itself.** (a) This must not reach an environment until every pre-cutover link has expired, or outstanding invitations are refused mid-ceremony. (b) ⚑ **Raised by `f5sign-infra` and missed here**: the binding condition is the deployed **image**, not the commit on `develop`. That repo no longer injects `SIGNING_TOKEN_SECRET` into `php-fpm`, `worker` or `relay`, so a deploy pinning a `BACKEND_TAG` from **before** the cutover fails at container build — an older image still reads the variable. "Backend first, then infra" was the right order and an incomplete statement of it. Both are written together in that repo's deploy runbook. | TASK-028 | Resolved | 2026-08-18 |

Built bytes-through-backend (sync); both gates dissolved (inline hash → no validation worker; sync seal → async never needed). | [TASK-006](tasks/TASK-006-dss-stage2-real-document-seal.md) + [ADR-0016](adr/ADR-0016-storage-model.md) Decision #4 | Closed | `7609527` (2026-06-18) |
