Verified 2026-07-22: **zero** dead markdown links remain to those targets; the surviving mentions are correct historical prose (the files are deleted, the docs say so). |
| BL-47 | **`FieldValidation` + `FieldChoice` value objects.** The `validation_*` and `choices` columns shipped in `Version20260803000001` with no domain behind them. Now built and wired end to end — VOs, the `FieldType` applicability predicates, both persistence adapters, `AddField`, the edge and the presenter — so no column in `envelope.field` is unwritten except `value` / `signed_at`, which ADR-0040 Decision 6 defers by design. ADR-0019 §5's condition is discharged: `FieldChoices::MAX_CHOICES = 64`, enforced in the domain, with the column's bytes pinned by a `jsonb` read in SQL rather than by a round trip. **Two follow-ons opened rather than folded in** — BL-50 (executing a stored `pattern` safely) and BL-51 (send-time completeness) — because both belong to slices that do not exist yet.   ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-10 (review-2 worklist).** ⛔ **The closing sentence is false, and BL-51 two rows away says so.** It states that two follow-ons were *"opened rather than folded in… because both belong to slices that do not exist yet"* — but **BL-51 shipped `Closed`**, added already-closed by the very commit that rewrote this row. This matters because BL-47 is the row certifying the options work complete: a reader trusts it about what remains.| [ADR-0040](adr/ADR-0040-field-entity-unified-placement.md) Decision 5 (departure table + the bound) | Closed | 2026-08-03 (options slice) |
| BL-51 | **Send-time completeness of field options.** `addField()` accepts a selection field with no list, because the visual editor places the box before it fills it — harmless only while no data field could reach a signer. The signing projection ended that, so the hole went live and was closed in the same slice: `Envelope::ensureEveryFieldIsPlaced()` refuses an envelope carrying a `DROPDOWN`/`RADIO` with no options, beside the unresolved-geometry check. Completeness at `send()`, not at authoring.   ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-10 (review-2 worklist).** ⚠ **Stamped `Closed`, but the guard holds only on the sent path.** `send()` reaches it; `GET /api/v1/signing/session` does not. A token alone suffices (`SigningSessionId::fromScope()` derives from claims, no session row required) and nothing in the Session BC checks envelope status (**BL-61**), so a DRAFT envelope's option-less `DROPDOWN` still travels `dataFields()` untouched — that path skips only `isSignature()` and null placement — and is published as `options.choices: null`: exactly the *"list with nothing to pick"* this row says is now impossible. **The asymmetry, not the guard, is what remains open.**| [ADR-0040](adr/ADR-0040-field-entity-unified-placement.md) Decision 5 | Closed | 2026-08-03 (signer field projection)  ⚑ **The asymmetry is gone as of 2026-08-25, so this stamp now means what it says** ([TASK-035](tasks/TASK-035-signature-requires-readable-envelope.md) / [ADR-0058](adr/ADR-0058-signature-requires-a-readable-envelope.md)). `SigningSessionReader` refuses an envelope with no `sentAt`, so there is no unsent path for an option-less `DROPDOWN` to travel and `ensureEveryFieldIsPlaced()` at `send()` is the only way in. |
| BL-52 | ⚠ **`field.label` and `field.anchor_text` are silently truncated at a NUL byte.** `pdo_pgsql` hands a string parameter to Postgres as a NUL-terminated C string, so `"Nom\0bre"` stores as `"Nom"` — with **HTTP 201 and no error anywhere**. A NUL clears every guard above it: it is valid UTF-8 (so `Assert\Length` passes), it is not empty (so `NotBlank` passes), and `trim()`'s *default* charlist includes `\0`. Not an unrepairable envelope — nothing re-validates these two on hydration, so reads stay green (measured) — but a truncated `anchor_text` is a `{{Tag}}` the send-time resolver can never match, and a truncated label misstates what a signer is agreeing to. The options columns had the same hole with far worse consequences (a leading NUL bricked every read *and* write of the envelope) and it was closed in the options slice by rejecting C0 controls in the value objects. **These two have no value object to put the guard in**, which is why this is a separate item rather than a drive-by: the fix wants either a shared text guard or a `FieldLabel` / `AnchorTag` wrapper VO, and that is a shape decision ADR-0005 should answer. ⚑ Suspected repo-wide — any `'string'`-bound column fed from a JSON payload has the same mechanism; the sweep is part of the item.   ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-10 (review-2 worklist).** ✅ **CLOSED — this row described in the present tense a defect the very commit that filed it had already fixed.** `Field::create()` calls `guardText()` on both `label` and `anchor_text`, refusing `/[\x00-\x1F\x7F]/` with a `FieldNotPlaceableException` routed to 422; the guard landed in `9086b44` as a drive-by, and that commit's own docblock says *"until now the only ones on this entity without the guard"*. The row's *"these two have no value object to put the guard in"* is true, and is exactly why the guard sits on the entity instead. ⚑ Extended by `01a7d1b`: both now also carry a length bound (`Field::LABEL_MAX_LENGTH` / `ANCHOR_TEXT_MAX_LENGTH`).| [ADR-0040](adr/ADR-0040-field-entity-unified-placement.md) Decision 5 + [`FieldValidation::guardText()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/FieldValidation.php) (the guard to mirror) | Closed | 2026-08-03 (options slice security review) |
| BL-57 | ⚠ **Editable fields now render for the signer but round-trip no value — visible and unfillable, end to end.** The projection landed on 2026-08-03, so a `TEXT` or `DROPDOWN` reaches the ceremony with its geometry, label and options. Nothing downstream exists: `current_value` is a hardcoded `null` (the read side does not even `SELECT envelope.field.value`, because nothing writes it — ADR-0040 Decision 6 defers that to the commit transaction), `fields_draft` is a hardcoded `null` (BL-40), and `CommitSignature` carries only `consentGiven` / `signerIp` / `signerUserAgent` / `reservations`. So the frontend can paint editable boxes whose contents have **no read path and no write path**. Recorded because it is a gap this slice *creates visibility of* rather than one it introduces, and because "the signer receives the fields" reads as more finished than it is. ⚑ **BL-49 gates the write half** — classify `field.value` as PII *before* the column is populated; enciphering it afterwards is expand/backfill/contract.  ⚑ **2026-08-04 — the write's shape is now fixed, which is the point of having decided early.** [ADR-0041](adr/ADR-0041-field-definition-and-occurrences.md) puts **one `value` on the field**, reaching every occurrence, so the commit-time write has no reconciliation rule to get wrong: there is one slot. It also means the payload is keyed by `FieldId` and never by a rectangle. BL-49's classification therefore covers one plaintext copy per field rather than one per rectangle.  ⚑ **Corrected 2026-08-10 (review-2 worklist).** **Understated: this slice does not merely *create visibility* of the gap, it introduces both halves.** `Field`, `FieldType`, `envelope.field`, `POST /fields` and `SigningSessionReader::dataFields()` are all added or modified here. Before this branch `SigningFieldView` could only be produced by `marks()`, so `current_value: null` was vacuous — a signature mark has no value. **And nothing server-side enforces the `required` flag the projection publishes**: `CommitSignatureUseCase::execute()` never loads the envelope, so an envelope authored with required `TEXT`/`DATE` fields can complete with them unfilled. *(Traced from source; not executed.)* ⚑ **2026-09-21 — planned in [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md)**: the commit carries `filled_fields`, Envelope's port validates them against what the recipient was shown and writes them once in the commit transaction, and `required` is enforced server-side. Recording only — drawing the values onto the PDF is [BL-269](BACKLOG.md). Nothing built yet. ⛑ **CLOSED 2026-09-21 by [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) on `feat/field-values-at-commit`, on the backend side** — `6f106d8` the rules, `0bae290` the port, the narrow write and both commits, `50c44ac` the read side, and `061e293` for the refusal's list member ([ADR-0068](adr/ADR-0068-a-problem-extension-may-carry-a-list.md)). Both halves this row named exist now. **Write:** both commit routes accept `filled_fields`; `EnvelopeCommandInterface::recordFieldValues()` checks them against what the recipient was shown (`Envelope::fieldsShownTo()`) and writes them once, in the commit transaction, under the envelope's root lock; `required` is enforced server-side, `filled_fields` absent included; and a bad value answers 422 `FIELD_VALUES_INVALID` naming every bad field, after which the same signing code still commits. **Read:** both hydrations select `envelope.field.value`, the signer's `current_value` is the recorded value, and `GET /api/v1/envelopes/{id}` publishes `value` and `filled_at` per field. ⚠ **What does not close with it.** `fields_draft` is still `null` ([BL-40](BACKLOG.md)), which this row pointed at rather than owned. The deployed signer sends no `filled_fields` until it ships what TASK-044's signer handoff asks for, in one deploy with this backend (TASK-044 §3 #9, §4 D13). Drawing the values onto the PDF is [BL-269](BACKLOG.md), and the plaintext decision BL-49 asked for is recorded on BL-49. Until this close the row's status read *Folded* and its last banner *"Nothing built yet"*. | [ADR-0040](adr/ADR-0040-field-entity-unified-placement.md) Decision 6 + BL-40 / BL-49 + [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) | Closed | 2026-09-21 — TASK-044 (`061e293`, `6f106d8`, `0bae290`, `50c44ac`) on `feat/field-values-at-commit`; surfaced 2026-08-03 (signer field projection) |
| BL-58 | **One logical field, several placements — CLOSED by [ADR-0041](adr/ADR-0041-field-definition-and-occurrences.md).** The model is now a field plus a bounded list of **occurrences** (`envelope.field_occurrence`), each naming its own document, so "the same NIF on five pages" is one field with five rectangles: one definition, one navigation stop and **one `value`** — which turns "five different tax ids are storable" from unchecked into unrepresentable. Taken *now* precisely because nothing writes `value` yet (BL-57): after that column is populated, and BL-49 enciphers it, the semantics could only be retrofitted. `SIGNATURE` is capped at one occurrence and keeps fanning out as separate fields — one mark is one incremental signature (ADR-0034). The field's parent moved from a document to the envelope and `ordinal` re-scoped with it, which is what lets a field span documents. ⚠ Residue, all recorded rather than folded: the wire break (BL-59), the `save()` amplification (BL-53), and BL-45 promoted from deferred to a **prerequisite of BL-48**, since `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` cannot shrink an occurrence list. | [ADR-0041](adr/ADR-0041-field-definition-and-occurrences.md) | Closed | 2026-08-04 |
| BL-60 | ⚠ **`send()` has no *reachability* guard: a `required` field can be authored that its own recipient will never be shown.** Measured on three shapes, all of which send cleanly today — a field whose `(recipient, document)` pair has no assignment row at all; one on a document the recipient signs but may not see (`signs: true, visible: false`); and one with a NULL `recipientId`. In each case `fieldsFor()` correctly returns `[]`, so the field exists, is required, and reaches nobody. The NULL case also contradicts ADR-0040's own schema comment (`recipient_id UUID, -- NULL = … (DRAFT only)`) — nothing enforces "DRAFT only". ⚑ **Deliberately not guarded with its two siblings**, and the distinction is the point: an option-less `DROPDOWN` (BL-51) harms *now*, because the signer is shown a list they cannot answer, whereas an unreachable field is currently inert — it simply does not render. The harm arrives with the commit-time write plus a "all required fields filled" check, neither of which exists. It also needs a product call: refusing at send would forbid the *author fields first, assign recipients later* ordering that `addField()`'s nullable `recipientId` exists to permit.  ⚑ **2026-08-04 — a new shape, and it is the interesting one.** Since [ADR-0041](adr/ADR-0041-field-definition-and-occurrences.md) a field's occurrences may span documents, so reachability is no longer binary: a field can be **partially** reachable, published narrowed to the rectangles its recipient may see (which `fieldsFor()` now does). The guard this item owes has to decide the *totally* unreachable case — a field none of whose rectangles fall on a document its recipient can see — and that case is now cheap to detect, since the narrowing already computes it. ⚑ **2026-09-21 — the harm this row deferred arrives with [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md), and so does its guard** (§3 #8): a `required` data field that is **totally** unreachable — no recipient, or no occurrence on a document its recipient may read — will be refused at `send()`, and a partially reachable one will stay legal because it is published narrowed. Envelopes sent before that lands are covered at the commit instead, where an unreachable field is simply outside the recipient's key set. The worry that a send guard forbids *author fields first, assign recipients later* does not hold: the guard runs at send, by which point assignment is done; it is authoring, not send, that the nullable `recipientId` serves. ⛑ **CLOSED 2026-09-21 by [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §3 #8 on `feat/field-values-at-commit`** — `ce284c4`, on the predicate `6f106d8` added. `send()` refuses a `required` data field that is **totally** unreachable: no recipient, or not in `Envelope::fieldsShownTo()` for the recipient it names. That predicate covers all three shapes this row measured; `EnvelopeSendFieldGuardsTest` pins two of them (the null recipient, and a field only on a document its recipient signs but may not see), and the no-assignment-row shape rides on the same `restrictedTo()` narrowing. A partially reachable field stays legal and is published narrowed. The same guard refuses any data field assigned to a role that fills no fields, and a `required` field of an inert type that nothing can answer. Envelopes sent before it are protected at the commit instead, where an unreachable field is in nobody's key set and so is never demanded. ⚠ **An optional unreachable field still sends**, deliberately: it asks nothing of anyone. So *"DRAFT only"* now holds for required data fields and for nothing else, which `Field::recipientId()` and LIVE_SCHEMA's `recipient_id` row now say. Until this close the row's status read *Folded* and its last banner *"will be refused at `send()`"*. | [ADR-0040](adr/ADR-0040-field-entity-unified-placement.md) Decision 5 + BL-57 + [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) §3 #8 | Closed | 2026-09-21 — TASK-044 (`ce284c4`, on `6f106d8`) on `feat/field-values-at-commit`; surfaced 2026-08-03 (signer projection review) |

Both halves fail on measurement. **(1) The event log does not read the `Principal`.** It classifies the **command's** `issuer()`, lifted by `SetIssuerMiddleware`; `Principal::kind()` and `Principal::client()` have **zero call sites in `src/`**, so the proposed fix would change a projection nothing reads — a **no-op**. **(2) `ANONYMOUS` is unreachable on that path too**: **twelve of the thirteen** `MACHINE_KEY` routes — the Envelope controllers — call `actingUser()` and refuse **400** without one, so a client-as-itself call is rejected there before any command exists. | BL-197 | **Fold the signing gate's verification INTO the commit, so no verified-and-unspent state can exist at all.** Today the ceremony is two calls — `POST /signing/session/auth` proves the code, `POST /signing/commit` spends the proof — and everything between them is a window in which a proof sits verified and waiting. [`BL-159`](BACKLOG.md) bounds that window (expiry, plus the ACCESS cascade added 2026-08-26); this row **removes** it: if the code travels on the commit request, verification and consumption are one act and the intermediate state is unrepresentable rather than merely guarded. ⚑ **Proposed by the product owner on 2026-08-26**, on being shown the expiry fix, and it is the better shape: a guard puts a ceiling on a hole, this closes it. ⛔ **Not done then, and the reasons are the work**: (a) it is a **contract change with `f5sign-signer`** — the two routes fuse for the SIGNING gate only, so it is not a backend-local edit; (b) **retries get expensive** — the attempt budget and the two-tier lockout (`AUTH_CODE_INCORRECT` / `AUTH_COOLING_DOWN` / `AUTH_HARD_LOCKED`) live on the cheap auth route today, and a mistyped digit would cost a full commit round-trip carrying the signature payload; (c) it **interacts with idempotency** ([ADR-0014](adr/ADR-0014-idempotency-three-layer.md)) — a retried commit under the same key must neither re-verify nor re-spend, which needs designing rather than moving; (d) it **amends [ADR-0049](adr/ADR-0049-two-gate-signer-authentication.md) §2.4**, whose argument that the proof must outlive its verification is about *verification*, not about the two calls, so the amendment has to say which half it keeps. ▶ **What it needs**: an ADR amending §2.4, agreed with the signer before any code. ⚑ **2026-10-05: [ADR-0075](adr/ADR-0075-the-signing-gate-is-answered-on-the-act.md) (`Proposed`) takes this shape for both acts**, and [TASK-052](tasks/TASK-052-the-signing-gate-is-answered-on-the-act.md) realizes it. Measured that day, (b) and (c) do not hold: the commit's body is `consent`, `reservations` and `filled_fields`, which the signer already re-sends, and ADR-0014's HTTP-edge layer is not built, so a retry needs only that a closed act answers before the code is read (ADR-0074's order). (a) and (d) are paid: the signer and the integrator change in the same release window, and ADR-0075 amends ADR-0049 §2.3–§2.5. Its first cut (2026-10-01) had weighed the shape for the decline alone and left it here. ✅ **Realized 2026-10-06 on `fix/signing-gate-on-the-act`** (not pushed, not merged) by [TASK-052](tasks/TASK-052-the-signing-gate-is-answered-on-the-act.md): the code travels on the commit, the decline and the machine signature route. Original diagnosis, kept for the record: ⚠ **A signing session opens over a DRAFT envelope: nothing in the Session BC checks the envelope's status.** `EnvelopeStatus` appears **nowhere** under `src/F5Sign/Session/`; `MintSigningTokenController` mints for any `(envelope, recipient)` pair without an existence or status check, `StartSessionUseCase` has no guard, and `SigningSessionReader::status()` branches only on `RecipientOutcome` — a DRAFT envelope's recipients are `PENDING`, which reads as `AUTH_PASSED`, an open ceremony. | BL-152 | ⛔ **CORRECTED 2026-09-01: this row was marked closed on 2026-08-28 by a mechanism that did not exist, and the harm stayed open for four days.** The 08-28 close credited the credential retirement anchor — but that anchor is compared against a session credential's `agp`, and under a **neutral** access gate the session open re-stamps `agp` to *now*, so the wrong-mailbox holder simply re-opened the still-live link and was handed a credential the anchor could no longer refuse. This row offered the delete as the cheaper shape; ADR-0060 §7.3 rejects it because it destroys the answer to *"why does my link say it is invalid"* on the exact surface where that support question begins, and makes *never existed* indistinguishable from *deliberately retired*. ⚑ Built on this case — a named recipient harm — and not on *"storage makes it possible"*, exactly as this row and [TASK-028](tasks/TASK-028-opaque-signing-token.md) §4.3 both require. ⛔ **The sender-side half this row routes to the dashboard is NOT closed and does not move with it**: someone corrects an address, believes they have revoked, and now actually has — but nothing in that UI says so. That is another repo's surface. Original diagnosis, kept for the record: ⚠ **Correcting a recipient's address issues a second working credential and does not retire the first, so a link sent to the WRONG address keeps working until it expires.** [`NotifyCorrectedRecipientUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Notification/Application/UseCase/NotifyCorrectedRecipientUseCase.php) mints a fresh credential for the corrected contact — correct, since the old link went somewhere it should not have — but issuance is additive under [ADR-0045](adr/ADR-0045-issued-credential-format.md) §2.6 and nothing revokes. ⛔ **This is the case where "we did not build revocation" stops being free.** [TASK-028](tasks/TASK-028-opaque-signing-token.md) §4.3 declined revocation on the reasoning that storage makes it *possible* but not a *feature*, and that reasoning held while every credential went to an address someone had chosen deliberately. A correction is the admission that one did not. ⚑ **The exposure is now BOUNDED where it was not before**: pre-TASK-028 the token's life was the envelope's, so a mis-sent link worked forever; it is now 7 days and the row is swept. That is a narrowing, not a fix. ▶ What it needs: a `revoked_at` column and a fourth chokepoint, or a `DELETE` of the superseded digest inside the same transaction as the correction — the second is cheaper and loses the ability to say *why* a credential stopped working. ⚠ Do not build it on the "it comes for free" reasoning §4.3 rejects; build it on this case, which is a real recipient harm with a name. ⚑ **`f5sign-signer` checked and reports no impact on their side, by construction rather than by luck:** each link is handled independently and the session store discards session credentials on a token change (`beginLoad`), exactly so two live links cannot cross. Two live credentials for one recipient are two separate sessions, which is the correct behaviour *if* the old link still works. ⛔ **So the harm is sender-side and lives in the dashboard**: someone corrects an address, believes they have revoked, and has not. That is where this should be surfaced, and it is not this repo. ⛑ **2026-08-25 — the mechanism this row asks for is now decided.** [ADR-0060](adr/ADR-0060-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) (`Proposed`), with [TASK-039](tasks/TASK-039-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) as the slice. It takes **the column, not the `DELETE`** this row offers as the cheaper shape — §7.3 rejects the delete because it destroys the answer to *"why does my link say it is invalid"* on the exact surface where that support question begins, and makes *never existed* indistinguishable from *deliberately retired* at the one chokepoint that could tell them apart. ⚑ **Only a correction revokes a token**; a reset must not, or every signer whose code the sender just reset is stranded. ⛔ Built on **this** case rather than on *"storage makes it possible"*, exactly as this row instructs and as [TASK-028](tasks/TASK-028-opaque-signing-token.md) §4.3 requires. | Found converting the third minting path, TASK-028 / BL-148, 2026-08-18 | Closed | 2026-08-28 (`feat/terminal-envelope-and-credential-retirement`, commits `bb6c43c` · `a7b350f` · `44b0551`) |
| BL-158 | ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-01: closed only for recipients whose ACCESS gate declares a code, which is not the default.** The retirement of already-minted credentials is real and delivered. But the link is deliberately left working (ADR-0060 §2.5), so under a **neutral** access gate an intruder re-opens it and is minted a fresh credential whose `agp` postdates the anchor — recovering the read-model and the document bytes, with only the signature still blocked. The residual case and the three shapes a decision could take are [`BL-230`](BACKLOG.md); the endpoint's published contract now states the condition instead of claiming the reset is sufficient on its own. **CLOSED 2026-08-28 by [TASK-039](tasks/TASK-039-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) / [ADR-0060](adr/ADR-0060-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) — and deliberately NOT in the shape this row proposed.** A reset now retires every credential minted before it, so read, bytes and commit all refuse with `CREDENTIAL_RETIRED`. ⚠ **The `auth_generation` stamp this row asks for was rejected on the three grounds the row itself later recorded**, and the anchor is an instant compared against `agp` — a claim the credential already carries, so the closed claim set is untouched and no in-flight signer was evicted by the deploy. ⛑ **The asymmetry is the delivery bar, not an omission**: the reset retires the credential and leaves the signing token working, because the signer is expected back through that same link with the new code. `unlockRecipientAuth()` retires nothing at all. Original diagnosis, kept for the record: ⛔ **Resetting a recipient's access code does not evict whoever already passed the gate — and the published contract said it did.** `ResetRecipientAccessCode` replaces the hash and bumps `auth_generation`, which renders a *lockout* inert and makes the next *submission* check the new code. It does **nothing** to a session credential already minted: [`SessionCredentialListener`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Http/SessionCredentialListener.php) verifies only the JWT's signature and expiry, `SigningSession::renewWith()` never consults the generation, and `commitSignature()` reads only `envelopeIsAlive`. So a party who got in before the reset keeps **read, bytes and the ability to sign** for up to the 12-hour absolute cap. ⚑ **The root is the observation ADR-0049 §1 uses to justify minting in the first place, applied to the surface where it was not**: a proof is bound to the session, and the session is shared by every holder of the link — which is why passing a gate mints a credential rather than setting a flag. That is [`BL-87`](BACKLOG.md), answered by [ADR-0059](adr/ADR-0059-terminal-envelope-closes-the-ceremony.md). The distinction this row draws survives the correction and is the useful half: the **reset** has no equivalent, because nothing about the envelope's state answers it. The cheapest honest shape is for the credential-bearing routes to compare the recipient's current `auth_generation` against one stamped into the credential — which is an ADR decision about the credential format, not a fix to this endpoint. ⚑ **Mitigated in the meantime by prose, not by code**: the endpoint's own `#[OA\*]` now says a reset is the wrong tool once you believe someone is already in, and points at voiding. ⛑ **2026-08-25 — the mechanism this row asks for is now decided.** [ADR-0060](adr/ADR-0060-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) (`Proposed`), with [TASK-039](tasks/TASK-039-retiring-a-credential-whose-holder-changed.md) as the slice. ⚠ **It does NOT take the shape this row proposes.** Stamping `auth_generation` into the credential fails three ways: `correctRecipientContact()` never advances that counter, so it cannot see [`BL-152`](BACKLOG.md) at all; it would overload a counter whose meaning is the auth-attempt epoch with a second one, and the two diverge at `unlockRecipientAuth()` — a gesture that must bump without evicting, since it rescues a signer locked out by *someone else's* guessing; and any new claim breaks `SessionCredentialCodec`'s **closed** claim set, refusing every credential minted before the deploy. | BL-16 | **CLOSED 2026-08-11 — the counted `try` now wraps reconstruction only.** Original diagnosis, kept for the record: **a broker outage burns the relay's per-row poison counter.** [`EventRelay`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/EventLog/EventRelay.php) caught `Throwable` uniformly, so a RabbitMQ failover quarantined undelivered system-of-record facts instead of retrying them. ⚠ Discoverability trap — the method's own docblock framed the conflation as intended, so a reader passed over it. **Fixed as prescribed:** `publish()` became `reconstruct()`, which does only what can fail *because of the row* (unregistered event type, undecodable payload, a `fromPayload()` that rejects its own bytes); the `eventBus->dispatch()` moved **outside** the counted `try`, so a transport failure propagates to the caller's existing backoff with the cursor unmoved. The class docblock's "fails to reconstruct **or publish**" — the trap itself — is corrected in place with a dated note, since leaving it would re-invite the conflation. ⚑ **The test was part of the bug and is the more useful finding.** `poison_event_dead_letters_then_quarantines_without_wedging` drove the dead-letter path with `alwaysFailingBus()`, whose exception message is literally `'simulated broker outage'` — a *transport* failure standing in for a *bad row*. It therefore encoded the same conflation as the code and could never have caught it, which is why a green suite carried this for months. Genuine poison is now driven through the production path by `new EventTypeRegistry([])`, and a new sibling (`a_broker_outage_propagates_instead_of_burning_the_rows_poison_counter`) asserts the fact **survives** an outage of `MAX_ATTEMPTS` polls and relays once the broker returns. ✅ **Verified against real AMQP**, which is worth recording because the first attempt could not be: the per-worktree validation lane runs postgres only, so the seven `EventBroker*` / `EventLogBrokerRoundTrip` tests skipped the change entirely and the only defence was the argument that the dispatch call is byte-identical (same event, same stamps) with only its call site moved. Una guarda solo en el agregado rechaza la contabilidad mientras el PDF se firma igual: bytes firmados fuera de turno sin registro que los explique, que es peor que el agujero. La guarda alcanzable va en `CommitSignatureUseCase`, ANTES de emitir el hecho, cruzando la actividad del paso por el borde — que es el patrón que este repo ya usa tres veces (`documentIsWithheld`, `envelopeIsAlive`, `signsElsewhere`). ⚠ Eso retira además la frase de esta misma fila que dice *"el BC de Sesión no puede vigilarlo: es agnóstico del sobre"*: se escribió antes de que existieran esos cruces. **Standby por decisión del usuario el 2026-09-02**, con esto anotado para que el siguiente intento no arranque desde la prescripción incompleta. ⚑ **Closed 2026-09-16 by [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) / [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) on `fix/step-order-at-every-door`** — `37eae41` the predicate, `322ba10` the crossing into Session, `dc88c1c` / `9f554ba` / `481a41d` / `8b754f8` / `1e12a88` the nine refusals, `2f26190` the acceptance case. ⚑ **Read everything above as past tense from that date; | BL-38 | **CLOSED 2026-08-11 and merged into the mainline 2026-08-14 — all five acceptance criteria are met. (1) `X-Tenant-Id` no longer establishes tenant scope — it is derived from a verified credential. (2) `X-Acting-User-Id` no longer establishes the actor, and nothing a caller sends does. (3) `POST /signing-tokens` requires an authenticated sender, so issuance is gated as strongly as redemption. (4) The regression test named below runs in the default suite and is green. (5) The access restriction standing in front of this gap is external to every repo we own, and is now recorded as such in `f5sign-infra`'s prod `Caddyfile`, where it would be added. The finding below stands as written.** ⛔ **[ADR-0039](adr/ADR-0039-per-recipient-document-access.md)'s per-recipient access control is completely bypassable, and a legitimate recipient holds everything needed to do it — measured, not reasoned (2026-07-31).** A signing token's payload is `base64url(json).base64url(hmac)` — **signed, not encrypted** — so a signer reads their own `{tid, eid}` straight off their credential (`GET /signing/session` also returns `envelope.id`). Sending that tenant id as `X-Tenant-Id` on the **sender** prefix returns the full document list *and every recipient's grants* — and, since 2026-08-03, **every field of the envelope regardless of recipient**: `label`, `options.choices[].value/label`, `options.validation.pattern` and `pattern_message`, i.e. precisely the strings carrying the contents of documents that recipient was deliberately not granted sight of (`GET /envelopes/{id}`), then the bytes of any document (`…/{documentId}/current` and `…/sealed`) — the exact documents the token-gated route correctly 404s. Verified end to end against the dev stack: recipient prefix `404`, sender prefix `200` **with the bytes**, same client, same envelope. The same forgery also **mints tokens for any recipient** via `POST /signing-tokens`, so ADR-0028's *"the credential must be verified server-side"* holds on redemption but not on issuance. **RLS is not a second line** — `app.current_tenant_id` is set from the same header. ⚑ **Nothing here is a defect in ADR-0039**, whose enforcement is correct and sabotage-proven (replacing `documentsAccessibleTo()`'s body with `return $this->documents;` flips the denial to a leak; restoring restores it). The gap is one layer down: [ADR-0021](adr/ADR-0021-http-request-edge.md) says the stub headers *"MUST be replaced before any untrusted client can reach the edge"*, and [ADR-0028](adr/ADR-0028-recipient-signing-token-auth.md) shipped exactly such a client to that edge — **the precondition is already consumed and no document said so**. ADR-0039 states no threat model at all, so it reads as stronger than it is. ⚠ **Exposure, answered 2026-07-31 (his):** deployed to a **test environment with access restriction** — so this is debt, not a live incident. Note the restriction is **not in this repo**: the prod `Caddyfile` terminates TLS and proxies `/` with no `basicauth`, `forward_auth` or allowlist, so nothing here shows what narrows it, and nothing here would notice if that changed. **The fix is authentication, not another filter ⇒ this closes as part of the Identity & Access branch** (his call, same day), where it is recorded in full with acceptance criteria. The original end-to-end enumeration was recorded outside this repository and is superseded for every practical purpose by the regression test below. ✅ **Reproduced and kept 2026-08-03:** the bypass is a re-runnable regression test — [`tests/F5Sign/Acceptance/RecipientTokenSenderPrefixBypassTest.php`](../tests/F5Sign/Acceptance/RecipientTokenSenderPrefixBypassTest.php), asserting the secure expectation — plus an over-the-wire probe script kept outside this repository. ✅ **Un-parked and green in the gate**, which discharges criterion 4 above. | [ADR-0021](adr/ADR-0021-http-request-edge.md) *Auth is stubbed* → Identity & Access | Closed | 2026-08-11 — the date the row records, merged into the mainline 2026-08-14; closed in `48c80fc`, dated 2026-08-13 · surfaced 2026-07-31 (§14.9 fail-open audit) · discharged to Closed 2026-09-16 |
| BL-54 | **The field `ordinal` ceiling — CLOSED by [ADR-0043](adr/ADR-0043-reading-order-navigation.md).** There is no counter left to exhaust: the navigation order is derived from the geometry, `nextFieldOrdinal()` and `Field::MAX_ORDINAL` are gone with the column, and `FieldOrdinalExhaustedException` is deleted. Both halves close at once — the 500-vs-422 *and* the add-then-delete drift that was riding with BL-45, which was only a problem for a counter that climbed. ⚠ Original diagnosis, kept for the record: **the field `ordinal` ceiling is a 500, and BL-45 is what makes it reachable.** `Envelope::nextFieldOrdinalOn()` returns `max(ordinal) + 1` with no bound, but the column is `SMALLINT` — so field 32 768 on one document reaches Postgres as `smallint out of range`, which is neither a domain nor an HTTP exception and surfaces as a server error where the caller deserved a 422. Same shape as the `placement_page` ceiling that [`FieldPlacement::MAX_PAGE`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/FieldPlacement.php) already guards. **Unreachable today** — BL-53's quadratic curve puts 32 768 sequential POSTs at roughly 3.6 days of server time — which is why it is recorded rather than guarded now. ⚑ It stops being unreachable the moment the DRAFT delete surface (BL-45) lands: ordinals are **never renumbered** (ADR-0040 Decision 5), so add-then-delete advances the counter while the field count stays at one, and the ceiling arrives in ~32 k cheap requests instead. **Guard it in the same slice as BL-45**, not after.  ✅ **The 500-vs-422 half is CLOSED, 2026-08-04** by [ADR-0041](adr/ADR-0041-field-definition-and-occurrences.md): `Envelope::nextFieldOrdinal()` raises `FieldOrdinalExhaustedException` (422) at `Field::MAX_ORDINAL`, pinned by a unit test that reaches the ceiling through a rehydrated field rather than 32 768 requests. ⚠ **The item stays open** for the other half — the counter climbs on add-then-delete while the field count stays flat — which still rides with BL-45. Note the ordinal is now **envelope-scoped**, so the ceiling counts an envelope's fields rather than a document's: fewer allocations to reach it, which is why the guard landed now.| [ADR-0040](adr/ADR-0040-field-entity-unified-placement.md) Decision 5 (sparse, never-renumbered ordinal) + BL-45 | Closed | 2026-08-06 — the row records no date for this close, so this is the date of `e7c2fdc`, the commit that closed it (its 500-vs-422 half closed 2026-08-04, as the row says) · surfaced 2026-08-03 (options slice performance review) · discharged to Closed 2026-09-16 |
| BL-66 | ⚠ **A recipient completion advances a NEVER-SENT envelope, so every send-time guard holds on the sent path and only there.** [`Envelope::recordRecipientCompletion`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Aggregate/Envelope.php)'s only envelope-level guard is `$this->status->isTerminal()`, and `EnvelopeStatus::isTerminal()` is `COMPLETED`/`DECLINED`/`VOIDED`/`EXPIRED` — **DRAFT is not in that set**, nor are `CORRECTING`, `ROUTING_FAILED` or `SEALING_FAILED`. The guard's own comment shows it was written against the *VOIDED walk-back*, so DRAFT is **unconsidered rather than rejected**. Measured: a never-sent envelope goes straight from DRAFT to `READY_TO_SEAL`. What that skips is not incidental — `ensurePreSendInvariants()`, `pinSendArtifact()`, `EnvelopeSent` and `EnvelopeRosterDeclared` (the legally-material required-set roster, INV-B), and with them every send-time completeness guard. Two consequences already recorded elsewhere are *this* row's symptoms: a `required` field reaches the seal empty with nothing recording that the data was never collected (see BL-57 for the missing write path), and an option-less `DROPDOWN` is published to the signer, which is why **BL-51's `Closed` stamp is narrower than it reads**. ⚑ **A pre-SENT envelope is reachable from BOTH directions and neither row is sufficient alone**: BL-61 **was** the read half (nothing in the Session BC checked the envelope's status) and this is the write half — ⚑ **tense corrected 2026-09-01**: BL-61 is Closed, its pre-send direction by TASK-035 / ADR-0058 and its terminal one by TASK-038 / ADR-0059, so a reader chasing an open row for the read half finds a closed one and may reopen it as a duplicate. ⚑ Distinct from **BL-33**, which gates step-*activeness* inside the same method; this is the envelope's own status, one level up. ⚑ **BL-33 closed 2026-09-16 and did NOT land in that method**, which is worth knowing before reading the sentence above as a map: its guard sits at the nine crossings a dormant recipient reaches, above the aggregate ([ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §2.5), so *"inside the same method"* names the row's subject and not the fix's home. ⚑ Filed because no existing row named it. Sabotage-verified four ways: making `DELIVERY_FAILED` terminal reddens both the quorum test and the still-can-sign test, naming it, and reverting either completion guard reddens on its own. | [TASK-025](tasks/TASK-025-recipient-delivery-failure-and-correction.md) §6.2 | Closed | 2026-08-18 — the date the row records, closed in `8e26dc1` · surfaced 2026-08-18 · discharged to Closed 2026-09-16 |

| BL-240 | **A custodian funnel leaves the sentinel tenant in scope for the rest of the enclosing transaction.** Every `SECURITY DEFINER` funnel pins `app.current_tenant_id` to the sentinel with a transaction-local `set_config` (`Version20260908000003` for the storage funnels, `Version20260908000004` for the envelope ones). In autocommit that dies with the statement. Inside a transaction it persists, `ConnectionTenantScope::within()` reads it back as *previous* and restores it, and every later read under RLS is scoped to a tenant that owns nothing: no rows, no raise, success. Measured 2026-09-03 under the per-test rollback, with the prune sweep's discard deliberately un-scoped: the run reported *every superseded artifact reachable this run has been reclaimed* with 26 skipped and 0 discarded, while the same defect in autocommit raised on every custody. Two consequences. (1) Any test on `RlsIntegrationTestCase` that calls a funnel is no longer fail-closed afterwards, so a *no scope raises* assertion placed after a funnel call passes vacuously. (2) A sweep that enumerates through a funnel inside an enclosing transaction and then works under RLS would see nothing and report success, which is ADR-0063 §2.18's kind-B shape. Nothing in `src/` does that today; the sweeps run in autocommit. Candidates: the funnels reset the setting on exit; or `within()` refuses to restore the sentinel; or a phpstan rule against `transactional()` around a funnel port. **Closed 2026-09-03 by the first two together** (`1c23a0d4`): every funnel restores the caller's scope on exit, `ConnectionTenantScope::within()` never restores the sentinel, and `SentinelScopeRestoredTest` is a census over `pg_proc` so a new funnel that leaks is red by name. | [`ConnectionTenantScope`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Tenancy/ConnectionTenantScope.php) + `SentinelScopeRestoredTest` | Closed | 2026-09-03 — the date the row records; the `1c23a0d4` it names is in no ref of this repository, and `a8abeb9`, dated 2026-09-04, added the row already closed · surfaced 2026-09-03 (storage lifecycle scenario, sabotage of the prune scope fix) · discharged to Closed 2026-09-16 |

⛑ this sentence read *"Closed in place, as the four rows before it on this branch were; `BL-249` carries why the file's stated convention and its practice disagree"*, and `BL-249` now records how that was settled. ⚠ **The row below is what it said while open, kept as the record of the question that was asked.** ⛔ **THE SIGNER SENDS THREE DIFFERENT 409s TO ITS DONE PAGE, AND TWO OF THEM CLAIM A SIGNATURE THAT DID NOT HAPPEN.** [`CommitSignatureController`](../src/F5Sign/Session/UI/Http/CommitSignatureController.php)'s 409 on `POST /api/v1/signing/commit` publishes three uncoded conditions and says so outright: the session is **already completed**; the envelope was **voided after this recipient authenticated**; or a signing challenge was presented that **does not authorise this document set**. All three arrive as the status-derived default `CONFLICT`, because [`SigningSessionStateException`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Domain/Exception/SigningSessionStateException.php) does not implement [`ProblemCoded`](../src/F5Sign/Kernel/Exception/ProblemCoded.php) — deliberate, and the controller argues it: naming `ALREADY_SIGNED` for the other two *"replaces an omission with a false claim"*. ⛔ **The consequence is downstream and it is the dangerous half.** The signer branches `status === 409 && (code === 'ALREADY_SIGNED' \|\| code === 'CONFLICT')` and navigates to `/s/{token}/done` — *your document is signed*. For the voided envelope and the unauthorised-challenge conditions **nothing was signed**, so the one screen a signer trusts absolutely tells them the opposite of what happened. ⚠ **Reported by the signer session, which cannot fix it client-side**: the three are indistinguishable on the wire, and branching on `status` instead of `code` is what our own published prose forbids (*"Branch on `code`, and treat its absence as stop, and do not retry as-is"*). ⚑ The neighbouring delegated route publishes `ALREADY_SIGNED`, which nothing emits and the 33-member `ProblemCode` enum in [`nelmio_api_doc.yaml`](../config/packages/nelmio_api_doc.yaml) does not contain — that half is [`BL-249`](BACKLOG.md) and is not restated here. ⛔ **Partitioning this is a maintainer's decision, not a cleanup**: it means new published codes, a `ProblemCoded` taxonomy on a Domain exception, and a release coordinated with `f5sign-signer` — the signer must ship its branches before the backend starts emitting codes it would otherwise route to the generic failure screen, which is exactly the trap [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §7 records for `STEP_NOT_ACTIVE`. ▶ **What would force it**: the first support ticket from a signer shown *signed* on a cancelled envelope; or [`BL-209`](BACKLOG.md)'s per-route code gate, which has to classify these three before it can go green. | [ADR-0067](adr/ADR-0067-a-refused-commit-names-its-condition.md) — the decision, with §2.2's partition and §2.6's release order; [TASK-043](tasks/TASK-043-a-refused-commit-names-its-condition.md) §8 carries the deviations, §7 the half that stays owed in the sibling repository | Closed | 2026-09-16 — the date the row records, closed in `18960cc` on `fix/conflict-taxonomy` · surfaced 2026-09-16 (signer session's report on TASK-042) · discharged to Closed 2026-09-16 |
| BL-263 | ⛑ **CLOSED 2026-09-16 by `5143466` on `fix/step-order-at-every-door`, and the CODE is the side that moved.** The maintainer resolved the tension against the placement, not against the sentence: `refusedForPlatformRecipient()` is a refusal **of standing** by [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §2.6's own opening list — *they sign on this platform*, ADR-0055 §2.16 — so the old order had the step refusal masking one, which is the single thing that section forbids. The two `requiredForDelegatedRecipient()` shape arms moved with it on the same test the section uses everywhere: **does waiting reach the remedy?** A missing or half-formed assertion has to be fixed after the wait exactly as before it. `STEP_NOT_ACTIVE` still answers every act whose shape the route accepts — a `MACHINE_KEY` recipient asserting correctly, and a relayed act carrying none — so nothing that could legitimately reach the act lost the *wait for your step* answer. §2.6 carries the amendment, §2.3's table row and TASK-042 §3 #14 / §6.2 row 11 / §8 were inverted with it, and the frontend handoff's machine-route row was corrected because it published the old order to the integrator. ⛑ **The pin changed hands**: the second arm of `a_recipient_whose_step_has_not_been_reached_commits_no_delegated_signature` became its own case, `a_platform_recipient_on_a_dormant_step_is_refused_for_the_assertion_not_the_step`, with the opposite expectation; moving the guard back above the assertion checks reddens exactly that one, naming `STEP_NOT_ACTIVE` — measured, with the other seven green. ⚠ **The row below is what it said while open, kept as the record of the question that was asked.** ⚑ **Prose-versus-code tension for a maintainer, not a bug: the delegated commit's step refusal answers ahead of a refusal ADR-0066 §2.6 arguably calls one *of standing*.** In [`CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase.php) the step guard sits **above** both assertion refusals, so a `SIGNING_PLATFORM` recipient on a dormant step carrying an assertion is told `STEP_NOT_ACTIVE`, waits for their step, and is then refused again with `ASSERTION_NOT_ACCEPTED` — two round trips for a caller who was never going to be accepted on this route. **Deliberate** (ADR-0066 §2.3 and the guard's own docblock: *how* to act is not a useful instruction to a party whose turn has not come) and **pinned** by the second arm of `CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCaseTest::a_recipient_whose_step_has_not_been_reached_commits_no_delegated_signature`, recorded in [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §8. ⚠ **The tension is that §2.6 lists *they sign on this platform* (ADR-0055 §2.16) among the refusals of standing and then says the step refusal is placed after every one of them and *"never ahead of one"*** — while `refusedForPlatformRecipient()` is classified as a *how* answer and does sit below. Both readings are defensible; the record and the code should agree on one. ▶ **What would force it**: an integrator reporting the two-round-trip sequence; or the next edit to §2.6's list, which has to decide which side that clause is on. | [ADR-0066](adr/ADR-0066-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) §2.6 — **amended 2026-09-16**, and §2.3's delegated row with it; the behaviour is pinned by `CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCaseTest::a_platform_recipient_on_a_dormant_step_is_refused_for_the_assertion_not_the_step` | Closed | 2026-09-16 — the date the row records, closed in `dabcd98` on `fix/step-order-at-every-door` · surfaced 2026-09-16 (post-landing audit of TASK-042) · discharged to Closed 2026-09-16 |

| BL-252 | ⚠ **On `develop`, `minMsi` is within about 11 mutants of tripping; on `fix/msi-headroom` it has 643 — and the not-covered surface that ate the headroom was the prose of `#[OA\*]` attributes, which no test can ever be credited with.** ⛑ **Rewritten 2026-09-16, the same day, because the cause this row declined to name has been measured.** It opened *"`minMsi` is within about 11 mutants of tripping, and the branch that trips it will not be the one that caused it"* and said *"The cause of that drop has not been measured, and this row does not attribute it"*, offering the string concatenation in the `#[OA\*]` 409 blocks TASK-043 rewrote as *"a hypothesis, not a finding"*. **Cause**: Infection mutates the `.` operators in the concatenated prose of method-level `#[OA\*]` arguments. It does not count a method's attributes as part of the method's signature, so it can credit their mutants to a test only through executed lines, and an attribute argument is never an executed line — every such mutant scores *not covered* whatever the suite asserts. On `develop` there were **1221**, from 71 `description:` arguments in 24 controllers, and all 1221 are listed under *Not Covered* in the second run below, matched by mutant id. The drop between the two runs below was the same thing: Infection's own generator, run statically with no tests, counts 1133 of them at `37ffe1b` and 1221 at `c52cb49` (whose `src/` differs from `60a968d` by one docblock) — **88 of the 89 new mutants** — so everything else not covered fell by 9 while the prose grew. **Fix**, on `fix/msi-headroom`: a `// @infection-ignore-all` line immediately before each of the 71 arguments. Statically the mutant space drops by exactly 1221, every one a concatenation in a method-level `description:`, and no other mutant in any file changes; the generated OpenAPI document is byte-identical. **Measured** 2026-09-16 on that branch, with the same single exclusion as [`BL-251`](BACKLOG.md), from `var/infection/summary.log`: `Total: 10878` · `Killed by Test Framework: 6070` · `Errored: 3` · `Escaped: 1244` · `Timed Out: 9` · `Not Covered: 3552` — MSI **55.91%** (6082 detected where the floor needs 5439), Covered MSI **83.02%** against `minCoveredMsi: 79`. Against the second run below, the total and the not-covered count both fell by exactly 1221. The 16 escaped mutants in `SigningSessionStateException`'s message concatenations are killed by `SigningSessionStateMessageTest`. The other status changes — 12 escaped mutants now detected, 10 detected mutants now escaping, and seven moves into or out of *Timed Out* — sit in files neither the branch nor their tests touch: the two `Envelope` console commands, `EventRelay`, `UuidGenerator`, `RetryableHttpClientTrait` and `MapsFieldOccurrences`. ⚑ **This is not one of the repairs `infection.json5.dist` rules out**: it attributes no broad flow and moves no floor. It removes mutants no test can be credited with, which that file already counts as honest shrinkage — *"concats converted to sprintf"*. ⚠ **Two risks, stated because neither is solved.** (1) **It relies on installed code, not on documented behaviour.** Infection documents the annotation at class, method and statement level; it works on an argument because `IgnoreAllMutationsAnnotationReaderVisitor` checks the comments of every node it enters and stops ignoring at that node's exit — read and measured against Infection **0.31.9**. An upgrade that narrows it brings the 1221 back as *not covered*, and nothing goes red until `minMsi` does. (2) **The guard pins presence, not behaviour.** `AttributeProseMutationIgnoreTest` fails naming `file:line argument` for any concatenation in a method-level attribute argument without the annotation on it or on a node between it and the attribute — sabotaged by removing one annotation and by moving it above the method's attribute, both red naming the argument — but it cannot see whether Infection still honours what it finds. ▶ **Left open, deliberately**: constructor-parameter attributes (307 concatenation mutants on `develop`, 101 of them escaped, because Infection does count a parameter's attributes as part of the signature) are not annotated; and 487 non-concatenation mutants in method-level attributes — mostly `response:` status integers — still score *not covered* by the same mechanism. **The two runs this row first recorded**: 2026-09-16 on `fix/step-order-at-every-door` at `37ffe1b`, MSI **50.37%** (6050 of 12010 detected, 4694 not covered); then the same day on `fix/conflict-taxonomy` over the `src/` of `60a968d` (identical at `d90004b`), with one declared test exclusion, `12099 mutations`, `6052 killed`, `4773 not covered`, `1265 covered mutants were not detected`, `3 errors`, `6 time outs` — MSI **50.095%**, 6061 of 12099 detected where the floor needs 6050. ⛑ The row first recorded *"within ~45 mutants"*. Covered-MSI was 82.7% and 82.73%. `infection.json5.dist`'s calibration — *measured MSI = 1403/2673 = 52.49% (n=1). 50 leaves ~2.5pp ≈ 66 mutants of headroom* — is that file's record and is not restated here, and so is its sanctioned response: *if `minMsi` trips, the response is a sabotage triage of the NEW not-covered entries*. ▶ **What would force it**: until the branch merges, the next landing on `develop` that adds a dozen or so not-covered mutants. | [`infection.json5.dist`](../infection.json5.dist) — the calibration block is the home-of-truth and already states the sanctioned response; the annotation's scope and the half it does not pin: `AttributeProseMutationIgnoreTest`'s docblock | Closed | 2026-09-16 — resolved by the merge `2e00b0d` of `fix/msi-headroom` (`9ce0fbd`, `cf4e8ca`, `b705573`): Infection with only `DocumentationLinkTest` excluded gave MSI 55.91%, 643 mutants of headroom, Covered MSI 83.02% · surfaced 2026-09-16 (task-validate of TASK-042, N4) · discharged to Closed 2026-09-16 |

| BL-231 | ⛑ **CLOSED 2026-09-29 by TASK-050: the guard exists, and the premise "it mints NOTHING" is false by design.** `NotifyCompletedEnvelopeRecipientsUseCase` now mints one link per informational recipient at completion (the copy-ready notice, ADR-0073 §2.15, `SEALED_COPY_READY`) and carries the unclaimed-slot guard together with the delegated, no-address and redelivery guards, held by `NotifyCompletedEnvelopeRecipientsUseCaseTest` (15 cases). Read the row below as the record: its "mints nothing" and its "unreachable today" describe the tree before `c8e404c4`; a released slot is reachable now, and the guard is what stops the notice. ⚠ **`NotifyCompletedEnvelopeRecipientsUseCase` is the FOURTH consumer of the recipient roster in Notification and the only one with no slot guard, and what closes it today is an accident scheduled for removal.** The activation fan-out gained a `continue` on [`RecipientView::isUnclaimedSlot()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/View/RecipientView.php) in `b0c5eb5`; the correction fan-out is closed at source by [`Recipient::correctContact()`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Entity/Recipient.php) refusing an unclaimed slot; this one iterates **every** recipient of the envelope filtered only by `email === null`. ⚑ **It mints NOTHING** — `rg -n 'issueFor' src/F5Sign/Notification/Application/UseCase/NotifyCompletedEnvelopeRecipientsUseCase.php` returns nothing — so it does not touch ADR-0057 §2.5's credential invariant. What it would send is the completion notice (envelope title, `recipient_name: ''`) to the address of a slot nobody named. ⛔ **Unreachable today, and the reason is exactly what makes it a deferral rather than a non-issue**: an unclaimed slot is never `isTerminal()`, the step's ending rule (`Envelope::advance()`, asking `EnvelopeEndingEvaluator`; the since-removed `advanceAfterRecipientTerminal()` closed a step only on `terminalCount === totalCount`) keeps a step open while its slot's leg is open, so the envelope cannot complete while one is open. [`BL-192`](BACKLOG.md) — releasing a party from a step's required set — **is** the capability that makes it reachable, and [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md)'s row in `docs/adr/README.md` says so in those words. ▶ Whoever builds BL-192 owes this guard in the same changeset, or the completion notice starts going out the day it lands. ⚠ Recorded rather than fixed here because the guard would be **empty-exempted-set** shaped — nothing can reach it, so nothing could go red, and green would be the expected signal rather than evidence (the repo's authoring rule 5). | [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.5 + [`BL-192`](BACKLOG.md) | Closed | 2026-09-29 by `c8e404c4` on `feat/step-policy`, joined at `f48b14e2` · surfaced 2026-09-01 (audit of `feat/claimable-recipient-slot`) · discharged to Closed 2026-09-29 |
