| BL-106 | **CLOSED 2026-09-08 by `e5a1e3f3`, discharged 2026-09-29.** [`SchemaConformanceTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Integration/SchemaConformanceTest.php)`::every_bc_schema_is_owned_by_the_cross_schema_rule()` asserts that every schema in `BC_SCHEMAS` appears in `NoCrossSchemaSqlRule::OWNED_SCHEMA`, which is the check this row asked for. ⚠ **A new BC's schema is unprotected from cross-schema SQL, and the pairing that would catch it is half-built.** `NoCrossSchemaSqlRule::OWNED_SCHEMA` is a hardcoded 5-entry map and the rule only checks schemas **in** it, so a schema absent from the map is exempt in silence — its own docblock records that this already fired once with `notification`. Meanwhile `SchemaConformanceTest::BC_SCHEMAS` (4 entries) **does** fail when an unregistered schema appears and tells you to add it. So one gate notices the arrival and its partner silently does not. Cheapest fix: two lines in `SchemaConformanceTest` asserting `BC_SCHEMAS ⊆ array_values(OWNED_SCHEMA)`, which turns the silent hole red the moment the census fires. | fold pending — `phpstan/README.md` + `SchemaConformanceTest` | Closed | 2026-09-08 (`e5a1e3f3`; discharged to Closed 2026-09-29) |
| BL-328 | **The envelope-first answers are prose-only in this package; the behaviour change is a superseding record.** [TASK-050](tasks/TASK-050-a-step-says-how-much-agreement-it-needs.md) changed only the wording of `SESSION_CLOSED` on the commit, delegated commit, decline, OTP send and auth submit routes ("the recipient's leg was released", not "the sender closed the session"): an ended envelope still answers `ENVELOPE_NO_LONGER_LIVE` or `SESSION_CLOSED` depending on whether the revocation has landed ([ADR-0067](adr/ADR-0067-a-refused-commit-names-its-condition.md) §2.3), and after a step deadline a fixed `LEG_CLOSED` (`eb686c5f`). ▶ ADR-0074 supersedes ADR-0067 §2.3/§7.3 and its §2.5 replaces the fixed mapping; the row closes when TASK-051 is joined. ⛑ It was: `feat/envelope-answers-first` joined the package at `aaf15651`, merged into `develop` at `e6a0af5e` on 2026-09-30. | ADR-0074 + TASK-051 (`feat/envelope-answers-first`) | Closed | 2026-09-30 by `aaf15651` (TASK-051 joined), on `develop` at `e6a0af5e` · surfaced 2026-09-29 (task-close of TASK-050) · discharged to Closed 2026-10-01 |
| BL-192 | ⛔ **Releasing a party from a step's required set — without voiding the envelope — has no mechanism, and the class that looks like the mechanism is dead code.** Two live needs converge on it: a claimable slot ([ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md)) nobody will ever claim, and the named recipient who must be substituted mid-flight, which is [`BL-69`](BACKLOG.md)'s neighbour and the deferral ADR-0057 §2.4 records as one question with editing a recipient's identity. **It is not claimable's, which is why it is here**: the capability is the envelope's, and scoping it to the unclaimed case would produce a second mechanism the day a named party has to go. ⚑ **Measured 2026-08-25, and this is the part that makes it non-trivial**: [`StepCompletionEvaluator`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Service/StepCompletionEvaluator.php) has **zero production callers** — `grep -rn StepCompletionEvaluator src/ config/` returns only `@see` docblock references. The live predicate was then `Envelope::advanceAfterRecipientTerminal()` (since removed by TASK-048, which replaced it with `Envelope::advance()` asking `EnvelopeEndingEvaluator` over a rewritten `StepCompletionEvaluator`, ADR-0073), private, reached from `recordRecipientCompletion()` alone, and its own comment called itself a *"Slice predicate"* that *"Production extends with quorum / blocking-role nuance per BC §7 inv 16"*. It counted **every** recipient of the step and filtered on `isTerminal()` — it never consulted `Role::isBlocking()`, and it never read `isCompletionTerminal()`. ▶ **What it needs decided, each of which a design would get wrong by default:** (1) what `isTerminal()` answers for a released member — the **only** answer that decides behaviour, since that is the predicate that runs; (2) **what re-runs the predicate after a release**, because nothing does today and `markRecipientDeliveryFailed()` deliberately does not advance, so a release arriving after the step's other members are already terminal changes nothing and the step hangs anyway; (3) the **single-member step** — the eCMR shape — where removing the member from the required set leaves `$totalCount === 0` and the guard `$totalCount > 0` makes the step never close, reproducing the exact hang the release exists to end; (4) whether a released member reaching `isTerminal() === true` lets a one-party envelope walk to `READY_TO_SEAL` and **`COMPLETED` with zero signatures**, which is evidentially worse than the `void()` it was meant to avoid. ⚠ **And the cost is not domain-local.** A new [`RecipientOutcome`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Contract/Type/RecipientOutcome.php) member is a **published** value — `EnvelopeJsonPresenter` emits `outcome` raw on `GET /api/v1/envelopes/{id}` — so it widens the domain of an enum a separate frontend repo consumes, and owes a `docs/frontend-handoff/` note. Four exhaustive `match`es must answer for it, one of them `SigningSessionReader::status()` in **another BC**, which needs its own signer-facing refusal. And it is the **first writer** of a terminal-with-nothing-to-sign leg anywhere in `src/`, which makes [`BL-183`](BACKLOG.md) a precondition — keyed on the property, since [`BL-190`](BACKLOG.md) already records that a fix keyed on today's enumerated members leaves the next one behind. ⚑ Its home is INV-B territory: the roster declared at `send()` is the completeness denominator, so a release must be recorded **against** it rather than shrinking it. ⚑ **The ADR this row asked for is [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md)** (`Proposed` when this was written, `Accepted` since 2026-09-29; [TASK-048](tasks/TASK-048-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md)/049/050, `feat/task-048-how-an-envelope-ends`, merged into `develop` on 2026-09-30 at `e6a0af5e`; the ADR's own header names this row): a new `RecipientOutcome::RELEASED` (not folded into an existing terminal), with `release_reason`/`released_by` recorded **against** the roster rather than shrinking it, decided at the release from the facts then known; `StepCompletionEvaluator` becomes the predicate the aggregate calls, replacing `advanceAfterRecipientTerminal()`; the single-member-step and zero-signature-`COMPLETED` cases this row raises are ADR-0073 §2.6's `send()`-time refusals (at least one blocking member required) and §2.14's engaged/seal rule. ⛑ **Closed 2026-10-01, verified against `develop`:** `RecipientOutcome::RELEASED` is terminal, `ReleaseReason`/`ReleasedBy` record each release against the roster, `Envelope::evaluator()` runs `StepCompletionEvaluator` on every ending decision, the clocks (`ReleasedBy::STEP_DEADLINE`, `EXPIRY`) release a step's pending members before they close it — so a slot nobody claims ends as `UNCLAIMED` without a void — `send()` refuses a step with no blocking member (`SendRefusal::NO_BLOCKING_MEMBER`), and an envelope that closes without the agreement its steps ask ends `NOT_AGREED` rather than an agreed `COMPLETED`; the TASK-048 to TASK-050 handoffs carry the widened `outcome`. What this row does not answer is substituting a named recipient mid-flight, which stays with ADR-0057 §2.4's deferral. | [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.6–§2.8 (earlier: fold pending — needs its own ADR) | Closed | 2026-09-30 by ADR-0073 (TASK-048 to TASK-050), on `develop` at `e6a0af5e` · surfaced 2026-08-25 (ADR-0057 adversarial audit) · discharged to Closed 2026-10-01 |
| BL-207 | ⛔ **A claimable `IN_PERSON_HOST` or `CERTIFIED_DELIVERY` slot is refused nowhere, spends the once-only fill, and lands a leg that can never terminate — so the envelope strands.** [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.10 puts in-person signing out of scope and §2.17 calls a certified-delivery slot *"permitted by this decision and buildable by none"*; ⚑ **neither is enforced in code, measured 2026-08-28**. `Envelope::claimRecipient()` guards *could this party still act* and nothing else; `Recipient::claim()` guards `declaredClaimable`, a terminal leg and the replay comparison — **no role predicate anywhere**. So both roles author, send, and claim cleanly. Then [`SigningSessionReader::role()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Query/SigningSessionReader.php) throws `roleNotInteractive` for exactly those two, so the named party can never open a session and the leg never leaves `PENDING`; the step's ending rule (`Envelope::advance()`, asking `EnvelopeEndingEvaluator`) closes a step only when its members' legs are closed — and the envelope never seals. ⛑ Updated 2026-10-01: this sentence also said informational members held their step under `InformationalMembers::HOLD_THEIR_STEP`; TASK-050 deleted that mode, and since ADR-0073 §2.6 `send()` refuses an `IN_PERSON_HOST` or `CERTIFIED_DELIVERY` member (`RECIPIENT_ROLE_NOT_BUILT`) and the refusal reaches a claimable slot too: `Envelope::stepSnapshots()` hands the evaluator every recipient of the step, slot or not, so an envelope holding either role cannot be sent and no claim can spend its fill on one. Read from the code; the refusal itself is pinned by `EndingSendRefusalsTest`, `EnvelopeStepPolicyTest` and `StepPolicyHttpTest`. ⛑ **Closed 2026-10-01.** The warning below stands for whoever lifts that refusal: building the in-person ceremony must not simply drop `ROLE_NOT_BUILT` for `IN_PERSON_HOST`, for the reason [`BL-191`](BACKLOG.md) records. ⚠ **The stranding is inherited, not created here**: an *ordinary* recipient in either role strands the same way, which is [`BL-190`](BACKLOG.md) / [`BL-191`](BACKLOG.md). **What is new is that a claim spends an irreversible, once-only fill on it**, and that two ADR clauses state a boundary no predicate holds — the repo's authoring rule 5's *an exempted set that is empty today reads as an all-clear*, except here the set is not even empty. ▶ **What it needs**: either a role predicate refusing the declaration at `AddRecipientUseCase` (cheap, and it must be written as *"roles a live ceremony can reach"* rather than as a two-member deny-list, or it re-opens the day a third non-interactive role lands), or a `Status` line on §2.10 and §2.17 saying plainly that the boundary is prose. ⛔ **Do not fix this as a blanket role refusal**: BL-191 records that the in-person ceremony needs the role reachable at the **session** while refused at the **gate**, and a blanket refusal makes it unbuildable behind a guard nobody remembers is there. | [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.10 + §2.17, paired with [`BL-190`](BACKLOG.md) / [`BL-191`](BACKLOG.md) | Closed | 2026-09-30 by ADR-0073 §2.6 (`send()` refuses `RECIPIENT_ROLE_NOT_BUILT`), on `develop` at `e6a0af5e` · surfaced 2026-08-28 (post-merge audit of `feat/claimable-recipient-slot`) · discharged to Closed 2026-10-01 |
| BL-290 | **A sender's `void` racing a recipient's `decline` can leave the recipient's leg `PENDING` on the envelope while their own session reads `DECLINED`.** Both routes are independently guarded and both write; there is no single lock ordering the two across the Session→Envelope crossing the way `findLockedForUpdate()` orders a decline against a commit within one aggregate. The window is narrow (the reactor crossing is fast) and both endings converge to the same `VOIDED` result, but the recipient-facing read can disagree with the envelope-facing read for the duration. Surfaced by TASK-046's validation pass. ▶ **What would force it:** a fixture that holds the crossing open long enough to observe the disagreeing pair, then deciding whether the read needs reconciling or only needs documenting as transient. ⚑ **[ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.1/§2.2 names this row and states the race disappears** (TASK-048, merged into `develop` on 2026-09-30 at `e6a0af5e`): both the decline and the sender's void now cross `EnvelopeCommandInterface::admitDecline()`/the void path under the same envelope root lock, ordered the way a commit and a decline already were within one aggregate, so there is no longer an unordered pair of independently-guarded writes to race. Left `Open` here rather than closed — that is the maintainer's call once the branch lands, not this sweep's (⛑ the branch has since been merged into `develop` on 2026-09-30 at `e6a0af5e`; the row stays `Open`). **Updated 2026-09-28** (TASK-048 §5, `feat/task-048-how-an-envelope-ends`, merged into `develop` on 2026-09-30 at `e6a0af5e`): the prediction is no longer only what the ADR states — [`EnvelopeRootLockRaceTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Envelope/Integration/EnvelopeRootLockRaceTest.php) races a commit, a decline and a correction against a void, and a commit against a blocking decline, both orders, from a real second OS process rather than a second connection in the same test, and asserts the loser is refused with no admission written while the ending counts the winner's admitted act. ⛑ **Closed 2026-10-01, verified against `develop`:** `DeclineSignatureUseCase` asks the envelope's admission under its root lock before it writes the session, so a decline after a void is refused before the session reads `DECLINED`, and `EnvelopeRootLockRaceTest` pins both orders from a second process. | [TASK-046](tasks/TASK-046-a-recipient-may-decline.md) §6 | Closed | 2026-09-30 by ADR-0073 §2.1/§2.2 (TASK-048), on `develop` at `e6a0af5e` · surfaced 2026-09-23 (TASK-046 validation) · discharged to Closed 2026-10-01 |
| BL-296 | **On the machine OTP-send route, a session's first open and its `SigningSessionStarted` fact commit separately.** [`DbalSigningSessionRepository::startIfAbsent()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Infrastructure/Persistence/Doctrine/DbalSigningSessionRepository.php) says its append "runs on this same connection and transaction". That holds on the two bus routes (`StartSession` and the delegated commit), where `DbalTransactionMiddleware` opens the transaction. `IssueAuthChallengeUseCase` runs off the bus and, for a machine caller (`SendRecipientAuthOtpController`), calls `startIfAbsent()` before `issueAuthChallenge()` begins its transaction, so the insert and the append each commit on their own. If the process stops between them, the fact is lost for good: a later open inserts no row, so it appends nothing. The audit trail reads that fact for a party's first open. **What would force it:** a trail that lacks a party's first open. The fix is a transaction around the call, or a docblock that names the exception. | `DbalSigningSessionRepository::startIfAbsent()` and `IssueAuthChallengeUseCase` | Closed | 2026-11-13: the machine twin opens the session in a unit of its own, as the twin of the auth submit does, so the insert and its fact commit together. The lock that serializes two opens of one id needs that transaction too (`BL-517`). First found 2026-09-22 (Evidence & Audit step-4 census; the route confirmed 2026-09-25) |
| BL-312 | **An envelope that expires gets no audit trail.** Nothing records an expiry: when `settings_expires_at` passes, `Envelope` writes no `EXPIRED` status and appends no fact, so neither of the trail's reactors ([`OnEnvelopeCompleted`](../src/F5Sign/EvidenceAudit/Application/Subscriber/OnEnvelopeCompleted.php), `OnEnvelopeVoided`, since retired) runs. Promotion treats the envelope as ended all the same ([`DbalPromotableDocuments`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Infrastructure/Persistence/Doctrine/DbalPromotableDocuments.php) reads `settings_expires_at < now`), so its signed documents are conserved with no trail beside them. [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md)'s single ending, `EnvelopeCompleted` for both outcomes, would give expiry a terminal fact that the trail's reactor hears. **What would force it:** an envelope that expires after someone signed, whose tenant needs its evidence. ⚑ **Premise gone since the merge of ADR-0073 (2026-09-29)**: an expiry now closes the envelope with `EnvelopeVoided` (`void_cause: EXPIRED`), which the trail's `OnEnvelopeVoided` hears, and ends it at `EnvelopeCompleted`. No test issues the trail of an expired envelope yet, and the trail it issues stops at the close ([`BL-357`](BACKLOG.md)). **Done 2026-10-01:** an expiry ends at `ENVELOPE_COMPLETED`, which issues the trail, and the trail states the date and its extensions ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.9). No test drives an expiry to its trail; the reactor reads no cause. | [ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §3.3 | Closed | 2026-10-01 by the trail's re-cut on `feat/trail-recut` ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §9) · surfaced 2026-09-28 (Evidence & Audit pre-merge review) · discharged to Closed 2026-10-01 |
| BL-357 | **The audit trail still ends at the first `ENVELOPE_VOIDED` or `ENVELOPE_COMPLETED`, although ADR-0073 amends ADR-0072 so that the envelope ends at `EnvelopeCompleted` for both outcomes.** The two landed apart: ADR-0072 was built on `develop` against ADR-0070's two endings, and ADR-0073's package joined it at the develop merge of 2026-09-29, which kept the trail's code as built. [`EndedStream::TERMINAL_TYPES`](../src/F5Sign/EvidenceAudit/Application/Service/EndedStream.php) still holds both types, and the settled read stops at the first of them, so an unagreed envelope's trail is issued and bounded at its **close** (`EnvelopeVoided`, recorded at the decision), before the signatures admitted before the close land and before the one seal runs; its later `EnvelopeCompleted` (`outcome: NOT_AGREED`) finds the trail issued and writes nothing. What that leaves out: a signature admitted before the close and landed after it is on the document's chain but its fact is past the boundary, and the seal of an unagreed envelope is now the one seal path's, whose `ENVELOPE_SEALED` lies past the boundary too, so `TrailAssembler::sealPurpose()` states it as ADR-0070's fact-less annulment seal ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.9) when the trail sees it at all. The trail also does not state ADR-0073's new facts (`EnvelopeAgreed`, `EnvelopeClosedBySender`, `RecipientReleased`) or `EnvelopeCompleted`'s `outcome`/`end_cause`. The repair is a re-cut of the trail's boundary to `ENVELOPE_COMPLETED` alone, with the voided/agreed split read from the outcome, and the voided reactor retired or kept as a fast path; it changes published trail content, so it is its own task, not a merge fix. **What would force it:** the first unagreed envelope whose tenant needs the trail to show a signature that landed after the close, or the seal. **Done 2026-10-01:** the trail ends at `ENVELOPE_COMPLETED` for both outcomes, reads the outcome from that row, and states the facts after the close; the `EnvelopeVoided` reactor is retired ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §2.2, §2.9, §2.10). | [ADR-0073](adr/ADR-0073-step-policy-expiry-and-what-an-unagreed-envelope-keeps.md) §2.5 (the amendment) + ADR-0072 §2.2, §2.9, §2.10 | Closed | 2026-10-01 by the trail's re-cut on `feat/trail-recut` ([ADR-0072](adr/ADR-0072-the-audit-trail-is-rendered-from-the-log.md) §9) · surfaced 2026-09-29 (develop merge into the ADR-0073 package) · discharged to Closed 2026-10-01 |
| BL-251 | ⛔ **The mutation gate still cannot run through its sanctioned path on ANY branch, and since 2026-09-16 ONE environmental red stands between it and a passing suite, not two.** `composer qa` is `lint → arch → phpstan → test → infection` and `@test` returns non-zero, so the chain aborts **before** Infection; and a plain `composer infection` then refuses to start on its own account — *"Project tests must be in a passing state before running Infection"*. The red that remains is `DocumentationLinkTest` (docblock links to `CLAUDE.md`, a symlink into the workspace root repo that does not resolve inside the container). It is not a code defect and has no owner here. ⛑ **Rewritten 2026-09-16, because half of the stated cause was fixed and the other half was never the cause people repeated.** This row named two reds; the other was `StorageLifecycleScenarioTest`, described here as *"its frozen clock now leads the wall clock, so its signing step falls after the **development** DSS signing certificate's `notAfter` and the live DSS answers 500 *Expired certificate found*"*. That mechanism was right, and later records shortened it to *an expired certificate*, which was wrong — [TASK-043](tasks/TASK-043-a-refused-commit-names-its-condition.md)'s Status row carries that correction. The certificate was valid, 2026-09-01 → 2027-09-01; since `f036e4c` the scenario signs about a year and two days past the wall clock, and `DssHttpAdapter` sends that instant as `signingDate`, so with a 365-day validity the test could never pass, not even on the day the certificate was generated. `f5sign-infra` regenerated the development seal with a longer validity and changed how it is generated, and a full suite run the same day printed `Tests: 3006, Assertions: 16685, Failures: 1`, the one red being `DocumentationLinkTest`. ⛔ **The consequence is still the gate, not the test**: [ADR-0035](adr/ADR-0035-testing-standard.md) makes covered-MSI the structural strength bar, and the only way to a number is a hand-rolled run with that test excluded. It is legitimate, because the test is `#[CoversNothing]` and cannot move a mutant between the covered and not-covered populations. [TASK-042](tasks/TASK-042-no-recipient-acts-before-their-step.md) did it with two classes filtered; on 2026-09-16 it was done with exactly one test method excluded — `composer infection -- --test-framework-options=--exclude-filter=DocumentationLinkTest::every_documentation_link_in_a_docblock_resolves`, `--list-tests` 3006 → 3005, the initial run 3005 tests and 0 failures — and printed **Covered Code MSI 82.73%** against `minCoveredMsi: 79` and MSI 50.095% against `minMsi: 50` ([`BL-252`](BACKLOG.md)), exit 0 ([TASK-043](tasks/TASK-043-a-refused-commit-names-its-condition.md) Status row). ⚠ A workaround that must be re-derived per run is not a gate, and a number obtained through it is not a green `make qa`. ▶ **What would force it**: the next branch that needs a covered-MSI number and reaches for the same exclusion; or a second red of any kind, which that exclusion would not cover and which a red `make qa` could not tell apart from this one. | fold pending — the symlink is the workspace `CLAUDE.md` shape, so what remains is not this repository's to change <!-- OFFREPO: the symlink belongs to the workspace root repository; the regenerated development DSS seal (valid to 2046-09-11) and its generation change live in `f5sign-infra` (`docker/eu-dss/scripts/`), uncommitted there when this row was rewritten on 2026-09-16. The symptom each produced is restated above and is observable from here by running the suite. --> | Closed | 2026-09-22 — resolved by `DocumentationLinkTest` exempting the resolved root `CLAUDE.md` (and only that path, so a wrong-depth link to it is still reported; sabotaged by shortening one link by a `../`, red naming it). The suite's last red is gone, so `make qa` reaches Infection again and the per-run exclusion above is no longer needed · surfaced 2026-09-16 (task-validate of TASK-042, N3) · discharged to Closed 2026-09-22 |
| BL-322 | **The test that pins "no transaction id during the seal" runs the issuance with fake gathering ports.** `IssueAuditTrailTransactionTest` (P-TRAIL-154, deleted 2026-10-06) shows that the issuance writes nothing before its claim, so a slow seal does not hold back the event-log relay (`BL-234`). It runs the real registry, and every other port is a fake, so the only statements are the use case's own. The real gathering adapters never run under the probe. If one of them starts to write, the transaction gets an id during the seal, and no test fails. It holds a wider property: no transaction is open, and no transaction id is held, at any outbound request of the issuance. A liveness control and an anti-vacuity count keep its two negatives from passing vacuously. P-TRAIL-200 succeeds the first half of P-TRAIL-154, and P-TRAIL-199 is its liveness control. The second half, an id after the claim writes, has no successor. | BL-128 | **A per-delivery notification history existed in the database and nothing could read it.** Resolved by [TASK-058](tasks/TASK-058-recipient-notification-history.md) (`feat/notification-history`, pending merge): `GET` of a recipient's notification history, masked, paged, reading named columns only (never `variables`, so the plaintext-token hazard of [BL-113](BACKLOG.md) is not reachable), with `requested_at` / `settled_at` stamped on each delivery by its transitions. ⚑ **Its three items**: (a) selected columns explicitly, done; (b) per-delivery timestamps, done, with `NULL` meaning *not recorded* on pre-migration rows (§3.3); (c) `DELIVERED` is still never written, so the history reads `SENT` as *handed to the provider, not received* — carried to [BL-390](BACKLOG.md), which stays open. ⚑ **Not closed by this**: the workflow half of [TASK-025](tasks/TASK-025-recipient-delivery-failure-and-correction.md) (an envelope still waits on an unreachable recipient with no correction path) stays in that task and in [BL-118](BACKLOG.md); only its visibility bar is met. Follow-ups: BL-422 to BL-426. | [TASK-058](tasks/TASK-058-recipient-notification-history.md) | Closed | 2026-10-15 |
| BL-269 | **Recorded field values reached no document.** After [TASK-044](tasks/TASK-044-field-values-recorded-at-the-commit.md) a value lived in `envelope.field.value` and nowhere in the PDF, and [TASK-018](tasks/TASK-018-visible-signature-mark.md) F4 made stamping impossible after a signature. **Closed by [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) / [ADR-0085](adr/ADR-0085-recorded-values-are-form-fields-each-signature-locks.md):** each value is drawn as a read-only AcroForm field in a fill revision before every signature, and the signature that follows locks it, in both signing modes; the text follows the signer's layout rules (golden cases copied), a `RESERVATIONS` field draws the recipient's reservations (recorded at the admission), and the five findings this row carried were answered (original text laid out and glyphs substituted only when drawing; the page geometry, CropBox and `/Rotate` applied by our own `PageBox`; our own `/AP`, no auto-size `/DA`, no `NeedAppearances`). Still open and tracked elsewhere: [BL-366](BACKLOG.md) (placement checks), [BL-270](BACKLOG.md) (a `DATE`'s display format), [BL-268](BACKLOG.md) (a tamper-evident digest of the submitted values). | [TASK-067](tasks/TASK-067-recorded-field-values-are-drawn-into-the-signed-pdf.md) | Closed | 2026-10-21 |
| BL-462 | **The signer was told `COMPLETED` before their signature was in the PDF, and nothing on their side said when it lands.** Reported 2026-10-21. **Closed by [TASK-068](tasks/TASK-068-after-the-commit-the-signer-is-told-where-their-copy-stands.md) / [ADR-0090](adr/ADR-0090-after-the-commit-a-recipient-is-served-only-a-copy-that-carries-their-act.md) (`Accepted` 2026-10-23):** from the admission of a commit the session read and the auth descriptor answer `COMPLETED`; the session read and the commit's 200 carry `signed_copy` (`APPLYING`, `AWAITING_ENVELOPE`, `CONTAINS_YOUR_SIGNATURE`, `FINAL`), and the download serves every readable document only once the recipient's copy is ready, all or nothing. Still open and tracked elsewhere: the signer's copy after they leave the ceremony ([BL-188](BACKLOG.md)). | [TASK-068](tasks/TASK-068-after-the-commit-the-signer-is-told-where-their-copy-stands.md) | Closed | 2026-10-23 (pending merge) |
| BL-465 | **When every document was sealed early, a redelivered `ENVELOPE_READY_TO_SEAL` before `COMPLETED` records a second `ENVELOPE_SEALED`.** Accepted by the owner on 2026-10-23 as a deviation of [TASK-069](tasks/TASK-069-a-document-is-sealed-once-nobody-can-sign-it.md) (AC-4c). [`SealEnvelopeUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Application/UseCase/SealEnvelopeUseCase.php) tells that its own execution already committed by finding its own `SEAL` on a chain. When every document was sealed by `fromDocumentSeal` executions, nothing on any chain belongs to the envelope seal's execution, so a redelivery in the window between its commit and `COMPLETED` records the fact again. Nothing breaks today: `Envelope::complete()` is idempotent and the audit trail reads the first fact by execution. It is most frequent in one-signer envelopes, where the single document is sealed early. ▶ **What would force it:** a consumer of `ENVELOPE_SEALED` that counts facts, or a trail check that stops accepting two. **Fix candidates:** read the log for an existing `ENVELOPE_SEALED` of this execution before appending; a dedup key on the append (a new pattern, outside ADR-0087); or the unwritten `SEALING` status, which would make the second delivery see a status that is no longer `READY_TO_SEAL`. **Measured 2026-11-05, by the use case and a fake, not by a route:** a repeat appends a second `ENVELOPE_SEALED` and no artifact. A repeat over HTTP inside an `AT_LEAST` close wrote nothing, because that execution had sealed its own document. | [TASK-069](tasks/TASK-069-a-document-is-sealed-once-nobody-can-sign-it.md) § 7, [ADR-0087](adr/ADR-0087-a-document-is-sealed-once-it-is-final.md) | Open | 2026-10-23 (TASK-069 close) |
| BL-466 | **An early-sealed signer read `signed_copy: CONTAINS_YOUR_SIGNATURE`, not `FINAL`, until the envelope completed.** Owner decision of 2026-10-23, after integrating TASK-068: [ADR-0090](adr/ADR-0090-after-the-commit-a-recipient-is-served-only-a-copy-that-carries-their-act.md) §2.2 keyed `FINAL` on `completedAt` (`SignedCopy::of()`), while [TASK-069](tasks/TASK-069-a-document-is-sealed-once-nobody-can-sign-it.md) can seal a signer's document at their own act. The copy they were served was already sealed and no later step changed it, but nothing in the read said so. Left as is then, for TASK-071 to decide the "final copy" signal. Resolved: under `SEQUENTIAL_PADES`, `SignedCopy::of()` now also reads `FINAL` once the recipient's own act has landed, nothing is withheld from them and every document they can see is sealed, the condition that ends their part as final ([ADR-0089](adr/ADR-0089-a-recipient-is-told-when-their-part-ends.md) §2.7). The signer and integrator handoff for TASK-071 says it. | [TASK-071](tasks/TASK-071-a-recipient-is-told-when-their-part-ends.md), ADR-0089 §2.7 | Closed | 2026-10-26 by `SignedCopy::of()` on `feat/recipient-notices` (TASK-071, ADR-0089 §2.7) · surfaced 2026-10-23 (TASK-069 close) · discharged to Closed 2026-10-26 (pending merge) |
| BL-467 | **Three events that end a document's pending parties request no early seal.** ⚑ Since TASK-071 (slice 1) a decline does request the seal of what it made final, so item (1) below holds for a release only. A release and a data migration do not evaluate [`Envelope::requestSealsOfFinalDocuments`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Aggregate/Envelope.php), which runs at the end of a commit, a completion's recording and a clock; the data-migration reconcile calls it too, with no candidate documents, so it requests only when the reconcile itself secures the agreement. (1) A document whose last pending signer or field owner declines or is released is sealed early only if a later completion or the agreement evaluates it, and otherwise at the close; on the waybill, consignee C declining after A secured the agreement leaves D_C unsealed until the deadline, and the sealed route answers 404 for it meanwhile (the route's published description says so). (2) An envelope whose agreement the data migration already set gets no early request from the reconcile; it is sealed at the close. Nothing is wrong or lost: the document is sealed once, at the close. What is missing is the early copy for a recipient whose part ended that way. ▶ **What would force it:** the per-recipient notices (TASK-071) promising a final copy at the recipient's act. | [TASK-069](tasks/TASK-069-a-document-is-sealed-once-nobody-can-sign-it.md) § 6 item 3, § 7 | Open (narrowed: a decline now requests its seal, TASK-071 slice 1; a release and the data migration still request none) | 2026-10-23 (TASK-069 close) |
| BL-484 | **A documents bundle has no bound.** `GET /api/v1/signing/session/documents.zip` and `GET /api/v1/copy/documents.zip` bundle every listed document whatever their count and size: memory holds at most two documents, the disk about twice the total. ▶ **What it needs:** the owner's cap and answer code (proposed 409 `BUNDLE_TOO_LARGE`), checked from `describe()` before any read. Also: a process killed outright mid-build (SIGKILL, the OOM killer) leaves `f5s-bundle-*` in php-fpm's temp directory; a fatal error does not (shutdown function); the custodian cannot reap them, it runs in another container (libzip's own sibling temp file during `close()` is in the same case). And neither bundle route has a rate or concurrency limit: a slow or stalled reader holds an FPM worker and the unlinked archive's disk blocks until it finishes or the connection drops (TASK-072 security audit, 2026-10-27). | [TASK-072](tasks/TASK-072-the-copy-card-reads-what-the-recipient-did.md) § 6 item 4 | Open | 2026-10-27 |
| BL-486 | **The `wt-backend` lane runs out of memory under concurrent full PHPStan runs.** `php-fpm-wt` is capped at 2g and 4 CPUs; each full PHPStan run spawns workers at `memory_limit` 1536M, and two at once exit 137. TASK-072 worked around it by running PHPStan and lint on the changed files only until one full gate at the end. ▶ **What it needs:** cap `parallel.maximumNumberOfProcesses` in the lane's PHPStan configuration (infra), or serialize full runs; then run two full PHPStan runs at once in the lane. | [TASK-072](tasks/TASK-072-the-copy-card-reads-what-the-recipient-did.md) §7 | Open | 2026-10-27 |

Pre-existing, found by slice 09's review of TASK-072. ▶ **What it needs:** add the 409 response to `DownloadSigningDocumentController`'s attributes, and a check that the published set equals the emitted one. | [TASK-072](tasks/TASK-072-the-copy-card-reads-what-the-recipient-did.md) §7 | Open | 2026-10-27 |
| BL-488 | **`ZipDocumentArchiveTest` globs the shared `/tmp`, so Infection's parallel threads see each other's `f5s-bundle-*` files and the cleanup mutants flip between killed and escaped across runs.** The `ZipDocumentArchive` cleanup mutants (`unchangeAll()` and `exchangeArray()`) are killed in one run and escape in the next, because the archive tests glob the shared `/tmp` for `f5s-bundle-*` files and the parallel threads count one another's. Also, the comment above `unchangeAll()` ("would write the file again") is doubtful, since `close()` follows it. ▶ **What it needs:** a per-test `TMPDIR` for the archive tests. | [TASK-072](tasks/TASK-072-the-copy-card-reads-what-the-recipient-did.md) slice 24 (Infection, final) | Open | 2026-10-27 |
| BL-489 | **The form engine's response header has no length cap.** `PyHankoFormEngine::run` looks for the first `\n` of the child's output as chunks arrive, with no limit on the header's length, and rescans the growing head on every chunk. Cap the header (a few KB) beside BL-455's limits. | [TASK-074](tasks/TASK-074-the-form-engine-takes-the-pdf-as-binary-on-a-stream.md) §6 (security audit) | Open | 2026-10-28 (TASK-074 security audit) |
| BL-490 | **`DocumentsBundleResponse`'s docblock lists a refused bundle as answered "404 or 409".** The routes also answer 401 (the copy route, on a refused access or a receipt `REFUSED`) and 400 (a bad `with_receipt`); the list was already short before TASK-075 (the copy reader's 401) and this task widened it. Reword to "401, 404 or 409" and name the 400 where the parameter is parsed. | [TASK-075](tasks/TASK-075-a-recipients-bundle-can-carry-their-receipt.md) §6 (closing review) | Open | 2026-10-28 (TASK-075 closing review) |
| BL-491 | **The signer app names its bundle `copia.zip` (`copy.zip` in English) while the backend now names every other download by its envelope id.** TASK-076 renamed the bundle routes to `<envelope>.zip`, the trail to `<envelope>.pdf` and the receipt to `<envelope>.<recipient>.pdf`; the signer downloads through a blob and names its own files from its locale, so nothing breaks. Whether it should follow `<envelope>.zip` is the signer's decision, in its repo; the signer session confirmed no breakage and regenerates its OpenAPI snapshot after the merge. | [TASK-076](tasks/TASK-076-downloaded-files-are-named-by-their-envelope.md) §6 | Open | 2026-10-28 (TASK-076 close) |
| BL-505 | **A malformed id on `commit_delegated_signature` answers 500, where each sibling route answers 400.** `EnvelopeRef` and `RecipientRef` hold no format guard, and this route opens a session before it parses an id with a guard. The cause is by reading, not from the log line. | `refuseAPathIdThatIsNotAUuid()` in [`CommitDelegatedSignatureController`](../src/F5Sign/Session/UI/Http/CommitDelegatedSignatureController.php) | Closed | 2026-11-12: a non-UUID envelope or recipient id on this route answers 400 `INVALID_ENVELOPE_ID` or `INVALID_RECIPIENT_ID`, as on its siblings. The format guard that the two Session references still lack is `BL-512` |
| BL-513 | **A test of the lock bound hangs when the bound is missing.** Four tests hold a row lock on a second connection, and then call code that waits for that row in the same process. Only the bound under test ended that wait. So a mutant that removed the `set_config()` call from `boundTheLockWait()` made `EnvelopeRootLockTimeoutTest` wait 9.5 minutes, until it was stopped (2026-11-12). Under Infection, such a wait ends at its timeout, and Infection counts a timeout as a kill. | [`LimitsTheWaitBehindAHeldLock`](../tests/F5Sign/Support/LimitsTheWaitBehindAHeldLock.php) | Closed | 2026-11-12: each of the four tests limits the writer's wait to the bound plus 3 s with `statement_timeout`. Under the same mutant, each one fails within 9 s, kernel boot included, and names the statement timeout |
| BL-514 | **A JSON request body that is not an object was taken as `{}`, and the act ran.** `#[MapRequestPayload]` maps a JSON list or a scalar into a request DTO. For a route whose fields are all optional, the DTO is the same as for `{}`. Measured 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-06 on `void`, `access-code` and `otp/send`: a body of `[null,null]`, `[1]`, `"x"`, `123`, `true` or `null` voided a sent envelope, reset an access code or minted a one-time code, and each answered success. A void cannot be undone. Related: `BL-177`, unknown fields ignored, another cause. | [`JsonObjectBodyListener`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Http/JsonObjectBodyListener.php), [ADR-0022](adr/ADR-0022-http-controller-conventions.md) section A2 | Closed | 2026-11-12: a mapped JSON body that is neither an object nor the empty list `[]` answers 400 `BAD_REQUEST`, after authentication and before the route acts |
| BL-515 | **`app:custodian:report-runs` stayed red after one red day.** It judges each scheduled job by its latest run's exit code, and its own job is on the schedule. Its own latest exit code is its verdict of the run before on the other jobs, so one red day made every later day red, whatever the cause. Seen in production: the report exited 1 on each night from 2026-11-08, with the orphan report as the only other red job. | [`ReportCustodianRunsCommand`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Custodian/Console/ReportCustodianRunsCommand.php) | Closed | 2026-11-13: the report judges its own row by the window only. A report that stopped running is still red |
| BL-519 | **A NUL in a text that reaches the record was cut when stored.** `pdo_pgsql` passes a string parameter as a NUL-terminated C string (`BL-52`), so `Ana<NUL> Ruiz` was stored as `Ana`, with a 201. The trail and the visible mark then named the signer `Ana`, while the caller kept the whole name. `BL-52` closed the hole for field labels and anchor texts, and suspected it repo-wide. The other request texts had no guard: a signer's name (add and claim), a title, a document's name and display name, a reservation (both commits), a void reason, and an asserted subject and user agent. | [`RecordedText`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Http/RecordedText.php), [`RecordedTextCensusTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Foundation/Unit/Http/RecordedTextCensusTest.php) | Closed | 2026-11-13: each of those fields refuses a NUL with 422 `VALIDATION_ERROR` and the field's path. Other control characters still pass. A census of every string field of every request class fails on a field that neither refuses a NUL nor names its reason |
| BL-521 | **A signer's name had no length bound, and a long one lost the trail and the receipt.** `AddRecipientRequest::$full_name` and `ClaimRecipientRequest::$full_name` carried no `Length` rule (`BL-121` measured the gap). A name of about 16,400 characters or more sealed the documents, and then the renderer of the trail and the receipt failed on it, so neither was issued. | [`AddRecipientRequest`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/Request/AddRecipientRequest.php), [`ClaimRecipientRequest`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/UI/Http/Request/ClaimRecipientRequest.php) | Closed | 2026-11-13: both writers bound the name at 1,000 characters, as the title is bound. A value object for the name stays owed under `BL-121`. An envelope that already lost its trail keeps that loss |
| BL-522 | **A void after another ending without agreement answered success and recorded nothing.** `Envelope::void()` returned early on every `NOT_AGREED` envelope, so a void after a decline, a veto, a missed deadline, a member out of reach or the expiry answered 204. The sender's reason was dropped, and a consumer could read the 204 as its own void. | [`VoidRefusedException`](../src/F5Sign/Envelope/Domain/Exception/VoidRefusedException.php) | Closed | 2026-11-13: such a void is refused with 409 `ENVELOPE_NO_LONGER_LIVE`, the code that close-now and extend give for the same fact. A repeat of the sender's own void still answers 204 and records nothing |
| BL-524 | **The production image named its PHP version and kept assertions on by default only.** `docker/php.ini` set neither `expose_php` nor `zend.assertions`, so the PHP defaults applied: each response carried an `X-Powered-By` header with the PHP version, and each `assert()` ran because nothing chose otherwise. Production read both defaults on 2026-11-13. | [`docker/php.ini`](../docker/php.ini) | Closed | 2026-11-13: the file sets `expose_php=Off`, and sets `zend.assertions=1` with its reason: a failed assertion stays a 500 at the boundary that logs it. `ProductionPhpIniTest` holds both values |
| BL-526 | **A DSS 5xx left no words of DSS in the log or the exception.** `DssHttpAdapter::send()` logged and threw only the status, so a seal that parked after a 5xx read as an outage, even when DSS had said why. EU DSS answers bytes that are not a PDF with a 500 and a plain-text reason. | [`DssHttpAdapter`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Infrastructure/Pdf/DssHttpAdapter.php) | Closed | 2026-11-13: the log line and the exception carry the message of DSS, as one line of at most 500 characters. The 4xx message gets the same treatment. The quoted message names no document: EU DSS quotes the file name that the request sent, and the adapter replaces it with `<document name>` before the cap. The message can still quote a character of a signer's name. It reaches the log, the failure transport and the error tracker, and no event or response body |
| BL-516 | **A repeat of a delegated signature that was recorded got 422 `ASSERTED_DOCUMENT_MISMATCH`, not 409 `SESSION_ALREADY_COMPLETED`.** The first act moves the tip of each document that it signs. The delegated commit checked the asserted digests before the session's own refusals, so a retry with the first body, or a second press that read the digests before the first act, was refused for its stale digest. The caller then read a recorded signature as a failed one. Measured on the delegated route, 2 of 2. | [`CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/CommitDelegatedSignatureUseCase.php), [ADR-0055](adr/ADR-0055-delegated-signature.md) section 2.4 | Closed | 2026-11-13: the session's own refusals answer before the asserted digests, after the step guard. A repeat of a recorded act answers 409 `SESSION_ALREADY_COMPLETED` whatever its digest |
| BL-517 | **The loser of two concurrent opens of one session got a 500.** `startIfAbsent()` wrote `ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING`, and `session.signing_session` has a second unique index over the same identity, `signing_session_recipient_uniq`. PostgreSQL arbitrates only the index that the statement names. Two inserts that both passed its check met at the other index: the loser failed with `23505`, or the two waited for each other and one failed with `40P01`. Measured on the delegated signature route, 4 and 7 of 20 concurrent pairs on two deployments. The same statement serves the signer's open and the machine code send and auth submit. | [`DbalSigningSessionRepository::startIfAbsent()`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Infrastructure/Persistence/Doctrine/DbalSigningSessionRepository.php), [`ConcurrentSessionOpenTest`](../tests/F5Sign/Session/Integration/ConcurrentSessionOpenTest.php) | Closed | 2026-11-13: the open takes a transaction-level advisory lock on the session id before its insert, so two opens of one id never overlap. The conflict target stays the primary key, and a conflict at another unique index still raises. The machine code send now draws the transaction that the lock needs (`BL-296`) |
| BL-518 | **A log record did not carry its request's `X-Request-Id`, and php-fpm cut a record over 8,192 characters into two lines.** A support request that quotes the header found no log line, because `RequestContextProcessor` stamped the method, the path, the tenant and the client only. The production handler writes one JSON object per line, and php-fpm's `log_limit` (8192 in the base image) cut a longer record, so neither part was valid JSON. Measured on a production deployment. | [`RequestContextProcessor`](../src/F5Sign/Foundation/Logging/RequestContextProcessor.php), [`docker/php-fpm-global.conf`](../docker/php-fpm-global.conf), [ADR-0009](adr/ADR-0009-observability-at-the-boundary.md) | Closed | 2026-11-13: each record raised while the edge holds a correlation id carries it as `extra.request_id`. php-fpm writes a line of up to 65536 characters whole, in both image stages. The async half, a worker record with no request id, stays `BL-239` |
| BL-523 | **A DSS outage of about two minutes parked each signing and each seal in flight.** The fallback retry strategy of `async_events` gave every failure but a lost chain race four retries, about 80 seconds, so the signer read `SEAL_PENDING` until an operator replayed the message. The pending-seal report also said that no seal was pending while a parked one waited inside its window. | [ADR-0098](adr/ADR-0098-a-dss-outage-is-retried-as-long-as-a-lost-chain-race.md), [`ChainConflictRetryStrategy`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Infrastructure/Messenger/ChainConflictRetryStrategy.php) | Closed | 2026-11-13: a DSS outage takes the curve of a lost chain race, about three hours, then the failure transport. A refusal keeps four retries. The report's all-clear names its threshold. A storage outage keeps four retries (ADR-0098 §2.4) |
| BL-527 | **A storage outage of about two minutes parks each signing in flight.** By a reading of the code, not a run: on the signing path, an object store that does not answer reaches the worker as the Storage context's `ByteStoreException`. `CustodyStore` does not declare that type, and no type of the Storage contract means an unreachable store, so the retry strategy of `async_events` cannot name it and gives it four retries, about 80 seconds. | [ADR-0098](adr/ADR-0098-a-dss-outage-is-retried-as-long-as-a-lost-chain-race.md) §2.4, [`ChainConflictRetryStrategy`](../src/F5Sign/SignatureExecution/Infrastructure/Messenger/ChainConflictRetryStrategy.php) | Open | The fix: the Storage contract declares an unreachable store, which the adapter raises for a network error or a 5xx and not for a 4xx, and the strategy gives it the curve of a DSS outage. Until then, `app:envelope:report-pending-seals` names the envelope from two hours after its close |
| BL-528 | ⛑ **CLOSED 2026-11-04 by 631b14cd (send), b11ac931 (submit) and e07d510a (delegated commit) on `fix/unclaimed-slot-on-machine-routes` ([TASK-078](tasks/TASK-078-the-machine-routes-refuse-an-unclaimed-slot.md)).** The text below is the state before that branch. Rows already stamped stay as they are, as evidence (TASK-078 §4 D6, §7). The production query of §7 (AC-15) was not run, by the owner's decision of 2026-11-19: no known caller reaches the three routes on a slot (TASK-078 §7). **The machine auth routes do not refuse an unclaimed slot, so one API call records an opening on it and can strand it.** [`IssueAuthChallengeUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/IssueAuthChallengeUseCase.php) and [`SubmitAuthResponseUseCase`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/UseCase/SubmitAuthResponseUseCase.php) never ask `isUnclaimedSlot()`, while [`SigningTokenIssuance`](../src/F5Sign/Session/Application/Service/SigningTokenIssuance.php) and `CopyLinkEligibility` do. Measured 2026-11-04 over HTTP, with a slot given the same declaration and no machine call as the control (its gate change answered 200 and its email-only claim 204). On a sent envelope, a slot declaring `OTP_EMAIL` and `OTP_SMS` with no contact: `POST /api/v1/envelopes/{id}/recipients/{recipientId}/auth/otp/send` answers 409 `AUTH_NO_DESTINATION`, and leaves `first_opened_at` stamped and a `session.signing_session` row behind. `POST .../auth` leaves both behind too, answering 401 `AUTH_NO_OPEN_CHALLENGE`. Afterwards the gate change answers 409 `RECIPIENT_ALREADY_ENTERED` and an email-only claim 422 `RECIPIENT_CANNOT_RECEIVE_METHOD`, so a claimant with only an email is stranded, which is the deadlock [`BL-205`](BACKLOG.md) records as resolved. A claim naming both contacts still answers 204, so the way out is a phone the claimant may not own, or a void. A slot authored with an email and `OTP_EMAIL`: the same send answers 200 `minted: true` and creates an `AUTH_OTP_ACCESS` notification holding a code, with `full_name` and `claimed_at` still null. The transmission itself was not observed, because no worker ran. This also breaks the premise of [ADR-0057](adr/ADR-0057-claimable-recipient-slot.md) §2.4 (*"no session row was ever created"*) and of the deferral [`BL-176`](BACKLOG.md) records. Only a holder of a machine key can do it. The delegated commit's half is [`BL-266`](BACKLOG.md). **The fix:** refuse an unclaimed slot on both twins with `RECIPIENT_SLOT_NOT_CLAIMED`, before `recordRecipientOpening()`, and publish the code on both routes. **What would force it:** an integrator that drives the machine ceremony on a slot it has not claimed. | `TASK-078` | Closed | 2026-11-04 |
